# UFO Timeline — PURSUE releases (canonical-rooted, cluster-aware)

*full dataset: 1282 entries; 277 are clusters rooted at the canonical row, citing pages + sources from every member (each a clickable war.gov PDF link). Narrative verbatim from `DenseNarrativeSection`.*

**1833, December 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:33 p.m. On 8 Dec 1833, two reliable observers reported a bright green object over Las Vegas, New Mexico, traveling from NNE to WSW at an estimated altitude of 13,500 feet. The object left a train or trail, was described as larger than a flare, moved at a rapid rate of speed, made no sound, and faded out after about 2 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1880** — Retrospective reference to mass reports of 'airships' over the United States and Great Britain from 1880 to 1900, describing fusiform craft with searchlights, engine sounds, and occasional apparent propellers; the text argues these were unlikely to be true dirigibles given the primitive state of aeronautics at the time. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1893, May 25** — 12:00 a.m. A Nature magazine quotation from 1893 describes a recent wintry cruise aboard H.M.S. Caroline in which the officer of the watch saw unusual lights bearing north over the sea. The lights appeared alternately as a mass, an irregular line, and a formation of globes of fire that changed into a crescent of diamonds, then lost sight about midnight; the account also mentions a looming mirage, which the commentator links to the later Lubbock Lights. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 261](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1896, November 22** — 7:30 p.m. A Tribune retrospective article recounts an 1896 Oakland sighting in which passengers on a streetcar and other residents reported a brilliant aerial light or airship moving rapidly northwest over Oakland toward Hayward around 7:30 p.m., with some claims of additional nocturnal sightings over the following nights; the piece is heavily garbled but consistently describes a luminous object crossing the sky and disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1905, February 27** — On 27 February 1905, a single observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a green-white object moving from west to east, apparently parallel to the earth. The sighting lasted about 2 seconds, was silent, and the object disappeared abruptly. The observer judged it as not as fast as a meteor. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1907, November 11** — A Havana newspaper relayed a secondhand report dated 1907-11-11 about two recently arrived men from the Matamampro Mines near Havana who claimed to see a large disc in the distance for several minutes near del Rio. The object was described as larger than ordinary airplanes in that area, completely silent, and departing at very high speed toward the sea. The article gave no further description, and the file notes that no corroborating reports or follow-up inquiry were received. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1910, March 16** — 11:00 a.m. On 16 Mar at 1100 near Farmington, New Mexico, a witness reported 10 unknown objects moving from N to NE. The objects were described as bright, luminous, and like tin foil; they turned on their axes and maneuvered up and down before moving faster than conventional aircraft. Observation was discontinued after about 3-5 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1911, October 11** — 10:45 a.m. On 11 Oct at about 10:45 near Alamogordo, New Mexico, two witnesses observed a white, round dish-shaped object moving NW to SE. It was reported to appear about the apparent size of a B-29 bomber at 150,000 feet, looked 2 inches in diameter at arm's length, moved very fast compared with a falling star, lasted 10 to 15 seconds, and faded from view. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1917** — 10:00 p.m. On an evening around 1917, the witness in Roadstown, New Jersey saw a pale yellow object in the southwestern sky that appeared to fall toward Earth at a steep angle, pass behind buildings, then move horizontally, rise rapidly and straight upward, and vanish into the southeastern sky. Another older man nearby reportedly also saw it. The letter is largely a speculative theory about gravity and flying saucers rather than an investigative report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 48–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1924, October 24** — One witness in Phoenix, Arizona reported a green object seen at night moving from east to west for about 75 minutes; it was described as star-like, very slow, becoming very bright before falling apart. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1925, March 13** — 10:20 p.m. On 1925-03-13 at about 10:20 p.m. in the backyard of 1900 Graybar Lane, Nashville, Tennessee, Lt. Commander Heinth Michael Kuhn, U.S. Navy, saw a circular deep bright blue object with a slight reddish fringe on the aft end, about half the size of the moon, apparently moving northwest to southeast about 20 degrees above the horizon. It was visible for less than three seconds, made no sound, and he reported clear skies, visible stars, and a full moon; he compared it to a very high-powered spotlight on a cloud but did not think a spotlight or searchlight was present. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 132](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1930, February 24** — 7:30 p.m. On 24 Feb at 19:30 in Datil, New Mexico, a round object was seen northwest of the observer, initially white and later changing to red and green. The sighting lasted about 2 hours 30 minutes, after which the object disappeared. No sound, occupants, or other effects are mentioned. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1935, December 4** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 4 Dec 1935 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single observer reported a green, round object moving from east to west on a nearly horizontal course. It was seen for only 2-3 seconds, had no sound or trail, was described as marble-sized at arm's length, and disappeared by going out like a candle. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1935, December 4** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 4 Dec 1935, two unknown observers in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a round green object moving from east to northeast in a sloping descent. The sighting lasted about 1/5 second, was accompanied by sound, and ended when the object disappeared behind a mountain. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 51–52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1939** — In January and February 1961, FBI Newark received telephonic and follow-up interview reports from Mrs. George Doarcke/Goarcke of Franklin, New Jersey, who said she had seen unidentified objects in the sky on several occasions, but she provided no concrete description of the objects. In the later interview she speculated that UFOs might be used to photograph Boulder Dam, mentioned a teacher from 1938-39 named John Grabach, and became vague and tangential during questioning. ([FBI-UAP-D012, pp. 35–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1940, March 13** — 9:53 p.m. On 13 March at 21:53 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single witness reported observing an unidentified object moving NE to SW or SW to NE about 20 degrees above the horizon, descending slightly and visible for 2–4 seconds. It appeared bluish or greenish white, with an apparent length about twice its diameter and roughly half the diameter of the full moon. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1940, May 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 7 May 1940, a single reliable observer at Camp Hood, Texas reported a diamond-shaped object seen at an estimated altitude of 1,000 feet, moving generally north and east. The object was described as reddish greenish white, with no train or trail, and was observed for 57 seconds before dimming and going out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1940** — From 1940 to 1945, Allied and German aviators in multiple theaters reported swarms of red or green luminous balls and small metal-looking disks that followed aircraft, circled them, and at times appeared to touch wings or tail assemblies without damage. The objects were usually not seen on then-current radar and were first called "Kraut fireballs" and later "foo fighters." They became more common during the first major daylight bombing raids over Germany and were also observed from the ground; wartime authorities initially suspected a secret German weapon, while German pilots reportedly suspected a secret U.S. weapon. The report notes that secrecy may have suppressed files until 1949 and mentions other larger cigar-, disk-, and sphere-shaped sightings in both camps. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 75–76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1942, December 11** — 7:30 p.m. On 1942-12-11 at 19:30 near Hood River, Oregon, a witness reported a blue-and-white flash described as a flash of light, accompanied by a sound like thunder. No flight path, duration, or other object details are recorded in the visible table entry. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1944** — A letter dated 1944 or 1943, forwarded to the FBI on May 10, 1949, reports a second-hand story from Mrs. Frank Gardner about a supposed secret aircraft hideout and factory in or under mountains near Mexico City, Mexico. The account describes aircraft made of an almost invisible metallic material, with glass-like close-up appearance, pilots able to see out but not be seen, and thousands of rocket-type craft allegedly being prepared against the United States. The report is hearsay rather than a direct observation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 151–155](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1944, June 29** — 11:00 p.m. On 1944-06-29 at about 11:00 PM in Uniontown, Pennsylvania, Mrs. Catherine McDonald observed three luminous oval objects, apparently one at a time and at roughly five-minute intervals, moving very fast from southwest to northeast beneath the cloud base during stormy, cloudy weather. The objects were estimated at about 5,000 ft altitude, rolled/spun vertically on edge, left a short trail of light, made no sound or odor, and appeared transparent as lightning showed through one of them. The witnesses lost sight of them when neighboring houses blocked the view. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 162–163](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1944, August 15–20** — 9:30 p.m. Around 2130 MST between 15 and 20 August 1944, an intelligence officer at Rapid City Air Force Base near Weaver, South Dakota reported approximately 12 yellowish-white, brilliant elliptical objects flying in a tight diamond formation. The formation was first seen approaching from the northwest at an estimated 4 miles distance, at about 10,000 feet descending to roughly 6,000 feet, moving at 500 mph or more. The objects made no noise, produced no exhaust trails, and then turned right and disappeared to the southwest. Sketches were made, and the report notes minor discrepancies in the verbally relayed speed and altitude. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 96](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1944, September** — In September 1944, airplane pilot Horace P. Wenyon reported seeing a single projectile-like object while flying 2 to 3 miles south of Rehoboth Beach, Delaware. He described it as approximately 15 inches in diameter, flying west to east at about 1,000 ft altitude and an estimated 1,000 to 1,200 MPH, with several jets of flame spurting from it. Wenyon later stated he saw a similar projectile again in October 1946 while flying over the same beach, this time at about 1,400 ft, and assumed both sightings were some kind of rocket test. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 186–187](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1944, November** — Paul L. Peyerl reported that in November 1944, while serving as a Luftwaffe test pilot at a top-secret project in the Black Forest of Austria, he photographed a saucer-shaped radio-controlled craft about 21 feet in diameter with several jet engines mounted around its exterior and a stationary central dome. He said he retained negatives of two photographs, including one taken at 7,000 meters, and that the object was parked in a hanger in one image. The FBI summarized his account in 1967 and noted he was concerned about similar UFO reports. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 62–71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1944, December** — In December 1944, crews of the 415th Night Fighter Squadron reported being followed at night by unexplained lights that blinked, changed colors, flew in formation with their aircraft, and approached very closely. XII Tactical Air Command forwarded the report on 1945-01-16 and First Tactical Air Force replied on 1945-01-20 requesting more detail; no investigation result or conventional explanation is given in the document. ([331_120752_Numeric_Files_1944–1945_37153_German_Ar, p. 14](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/331_120752_numeric_files_1944–1945_37153_german_armament_equipment_documents.pdf>))

**1944** _(cluster ×3)_ — Wladyslaw Krasuski of 5457 Jos. Campau, Detroit, reported that in 1944, while a German POW near Gut Alt Golssen, Germany, he saw a large circular vehicle in a swamp area after tractor engines repeatedly stalled amid a high-pitched whining noise. From about 500 feet he observed a circular craft rise vertically from behind a 50-foot tarpaulin wall and then move slowly horizontally out of view. ([65_HS1-101634279_100-DE-26505, pp. 8–13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-101634279_100-de-26505.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 209–217](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1945, January 7** — 2:45 p.m. On 7 January 1945 at 14:45, a military aircraft (NG 869) reported an object over Godman Field area during radio conversations referenced in the report. The object was described as metallic, tremendous in size, directly ahead and above the aircraft, traveling about half the aircraft's speed, with estimates around 180 mph and later ascending/upward-forward at about 360 mph as the aircraft chased it. The sighting lasted apparently over 1.5 hours and reached above 20,000 ft. The final notes indicate the aircraft was ordered into the chase by Capt. Thomas E. Mattell, who later died after his last radio contact with Godman Field at 15:15. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 93–94](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1945, January 7** — 7:05 p.m. On 7 January 1945 at 1905 EST, Capt. Charles E. McGee, an assistant operations officer/pilot at Locellourne Army Air Base in Columbus, Ohio, reported a very bright white light southwest of the field. The object, seen at an estimated 1-5 miles distance and about 3000 ft altitude, appeared oval like a spotlight or floodlight, changed from white to yellowish-orange, moved slowly westward, flashed on and off, and seemed to descend and burn out. No sound was heard; bluish streaks resembling jet exhaust were reported by another observer on the squawk box. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 68–71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1945, January 18** — 11:00 p.m. Single witness reported a brilliant white object at about 35° above the horizon over the point of impact on the line between Brest and Kiev on 18 Jan 1945 at 2300. The object descended almost vertically, then appeared to bounce from north to south, was compared to planet Venus on an unusually clear night, lasted 2-3 seconds, made no sound, and disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1945, January 19** — 11:00 p.m. On 1945-01-19 at about 23:00, a single reliable observer reported a brilliant white object seen near the point of impact on a line between Brest and Kiev. The object descended almost vertically and then appeared to bounce or continue southward, with the sighting lasting only 2–3 seconds. The witness compared its apparent size to Venus on an unusually clear night and reported no sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1945, January 29** — 12:10 a.m. Multiple 415th Night Fighter Squadron pilots reported recurring nighttime "foofighters" over eastern France and the Rhine corridor in December 1944 and January 1945, including glowing red, orange, yellow, white, and amber lights, streaks of flame, and light groups that sometimes followed aircraft, formed patterns, or disappeared abruptly. The document is a compiled intelligence forwarding of sortie report extracts, with one highlighted case on 1945-01-29/30 near Weissembourg and Landau describing two amber lights off the starboard rear at Angels 2 about 1,000 ft away that followed Travel 34 until it turned toward them, after which the lights vanished. ([331_120752_Numeric_Files_1944–1945_37153_German_Ar, pp. 15–17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/331_120752_numeric_files_1944–1945_37153_german_armament_equipment_documents.pdf>))

**1945, March 1** — 10:30 a.m. On 1 March 1945, pilots reported an aluminum-colored, cylinder-shaped object about 12 feet long and 1 foot in diameter floating at approximately 9,000 feet. It appeared vertically suspended with small fins and a mast projecting from the lower end. The object was attacked and partially deflated, producing a red flame without smoke, but it did not disintegrate. A photo was reportedly taken by 107 Squadron of 67 TAC/R Group via F-5710 at 011030 hours. ([331_120752_Numeric_Files_1944–1945_37153_German_Ar, p. 9](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/331_120752_numeric_files_1944–1945_37153_german_armament_equipment_documents.pdf>))

**1945, March 1** — On 1 March 1945, pilots reportedly observed an aluminum-coloured, cylinder-shaped object floating at about 9,000 feet. It was described as about 12 inches across and 1 foot in diameter, with small fins and a mast partially projecting from one end. The object was attacked; a red flame resulted without smoke, and it did not disintegrate. A photo reference (VIC F-5710) was mentioned, and the case was forwarded through SHAEF/Air Staff to Air Ministry and other commands requesting photos and written reports of any further instances. ([331_120752_Numeric_Files_1944–1945_37153_German_Ar, pp. 7–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/331_120752_numeric_files_1944–1945_37153_german_armament_equipment_documents.pdf>))

**1945, March 27** — 6:00 p.m. At Tucumcari, New Mexico, a single witness reported an orange fire-like object moving from east to west at about 60 degrees above the horizon. The object was seen for about 15 minutes, appeared about the size of a G-47 at 10,000 feet, had a kite-tail-like feature, and then disappeared behind hills. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1945, July 30** — On 1945-07-30 Lee reported to the FBI that he and his wife had watched a large object he believed to be a flying saucer over the channel between Vashon Island and the mainland. He estimated it at about 50,000 ft altitude, 100 to 200 ft across, and thought it had lights. They observed it for about five minutes. Lee said he had served in the Air Force and had never seen anything like it before. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 19](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1945** — The document is an FBI memorandum from Mexico City forwarding a telegram written by W. R. Hanawalt in December 1966. The telegram mixes claims about a supposed laser/cobalt ray, mind control, and surveillance with a brief statement that he saw unidentified flying objects in 1945 during combat missions and that they were not the enemy. No specific UFO event details, location, date, time, or visual description are provided beyond that retrospective statement. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 53–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1945** — During World War II, aircraft pilots in both the European and Far East theaters reported unexplained 'Foo Fighters'—balls of light that flew near or with aircraft and maneuvered rapidly. The phenomena were considered possibly electrostatic, electromagnetic, or optical reflections from ice crystals, but no exact cause was established. ([CIA-UAP-002, p. 18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-002_Scientific-Advisory-Panel-on-Unidentified-Flying-Objects_Report_1952-1953.pdf>))

**1946, September 1** — Former aircraft pilot Horace P. Wenyon reported two fast-moving objects over and south of Rehoboth Beach, Delaware in 1946, one seen while flying at about 1,000 feet and another while flying at 1,400 feet; he described them to investigators as projectile-like, roughly fifteen inches across, with jets of flame and a west-to-east course, and he believed they were likely rocket tests rather than anything exotic. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 192–193](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1946, November 11–12** — In a December 1947 Air Force correspondence file concerning a "Flying Disc" report, Fourth Air Force forwarded photographs allegedly taken near Jefferson, Oregon, by Mary L. Herren between November 5 and 12, 1946. Reviewers concluded the repeated marks seen in the sky area of the photos were most likely film, paper, or camera defects rather than flying discs, and requested that no further investigation be made. ([18_100754_ General 1946-7_Vol_2, pp. 4–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/18_100754_ general 1946-7_vol_2.pdf>))

**1946, December mid-December** _(cluster ×2)_ — In mid-December 1946, professor and head of aeronautical engineering K. D. Wood observed a single object only as a white, heavy vapor trail while flying over northern Arizona near Route 66 in clear early-morning conditions. He reported no sound, no maneuvers, and no direct view of the object itself; the trail first appeared vertical and then continued west to east overhead at an estimated 20,000 to 50,000 feet and about 600 mph, ±200. He remarked that it looked like rocket or jet exhaust but not like a usual jet airplane trail. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 76–78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1946** — From February to December 1946, many witnesses across Sweden reported numerous fusiform objects, sometimes appearing as spheres or disks, flying mostly horizontally and occasionally leaving luminous trails; they were also described as capable of sudden ascents and descents. The sightings, dubbed "ghost rockets," prompted concern and investigation by Scandinavian, British, and U.S. military authorities, though no debris was officially recovered. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1947, January 6** — 3:10 a.m. A single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a brilliant green object moving horizontally from east to west at an estimated 3 to 5 degrees above the horizon around 03:10 on 6 January. The sighting lasted about 2 seconds, was silent, and the object disappeared behind a mountainous horizon at high speed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, January 7** — 10:15 p.m. At 2215 on 7 January near Corona, New Mexico, two witnesses reported a descending round ball-shaped object traveling from southwest to southeast. The object showed yellowish white, orange, blue, and green coloration, was compared to a cup 6 inches in diameter held at arm's length, and was said to move as fast as a jet fighter before disappearing behind a mountain range after about 10 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, January 16** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:30 p.m. On 16 Jan 1947 at about 22:30, a British Mosquito night-fighter, during routine night-flying practice over the North Sea roughly 50 miles from the Dutch coast and heading toward Norfolk, England, was vectored onto an unidentified aircraft at 22,000 ft. A chase lasted about 30 minutes, ending over Norfolk at 23:00. Two brief AI radar contacts were made but faded quickly, and the unknown aircraft was described as making efficient controlled evasive action and flying faster than the Mosquito. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 158–159](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, p. 73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, January 27** — 5:15 p.m. On 27 January at 1715, a witness in Scullville, New Jersey observed one unidentified object moving northwest, ascending at about a 60-degree angle. It appeared as a white streak, about half the apparent size of a fireworks rocket at close range, and faded gradually after about 30 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, January 30** — 6:54 p.m. On 30 January at about 18:54-19:00, 10 unidentified observers in Ft. Worth, Texas reported a green trail of sparking lights traveling north to south about 13 degrees above the horizon and descending roughly 30 degrees downward before disintegrating after 1-7 seconds. No sound was heard. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 14** — 6:40 p.m. On 14 Feb at 18:40 near Ganado, New Mexico, two observers reported a brilliant white object with a slightly green color, somewhat above the horizon, appearing stationary at first and then descending in a slight curve toward the west. A train or trail was noted and no sound was heard. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 15** — 3:30 p.m. On 15 Feb at 1530, a single witness at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a round object moving from northwest to west at about 45 degrees downward above the horizon. It appeared red and green, was compared to a normal marble, left a shooting-star-like trail, and faded out after about 30 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 18** — 5:10 a.m. On 18 Feb at 0510, a witness reported a white-and-orange, round-to-cone-shaped object near Holleman AFB, New Mexico. It was observed climbing for about 1 hour 44 minutes, appeared about the size of a coffee cup at arm's length, and the observer stopped watching after the object remained in view. The report appears in a declassified FBI case-file compilation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 20** — 5:30 a.m. At Holleman AFB, New Mexico, at about 0530 on 20 Feb, a witness reported a stationary white round object seen against the sky at an estimated 1,000 feet above 9,000-foot mountain terrain. It appeared about the size of a half-dollar held at arm's length, remained stationary for about 5 minutes, and then disappeared from view behind a cloud. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 24** — 1:15 p.m. On 24 February at 13:15, a single witness reported a silvery, saucer-shaped object over Los Alamos, New Mexico, moving erratically generally northeast at an estimated altitude of 20,000-30,000 feet. The object was described as about 100 feet across if at that altitude, visible for about 15 minutes, and said to have vanished while traveling as fast as or faster than sound. The report comes from a declassified FBI file reference with no additional adjudication stated in the excerpt. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:45 p.m. A single witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported an unidentified white object seen around 1345 on 24 February at an estimated altitude of 25,000-30,000 feet. The object was observed straight up, appeared to flash like a mirror in sunlight, moved very fast, turned from east to west, and then went straight up out of sight after about 20 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 24** — 1:55 p.m. On 24 Feb at 13:55 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round white object flying straight toward the W or SW at an estimated 25 ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 25** — 2:10 p.m. On 25 February at 1410, a single witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a metallic, oblong object traveling south to north at an estimated 40 mph, seen at about 1 to 3 miles distance and 30 degrees above the horizon. No lights, sounds, or other effects were reported. The object was observed for about 2 minutes and then went below the horizon. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 25** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:45 p.m. On 25 February in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a witness reported a very fast silver round object moving from northeast to southwest overhead at an estimated altitude of 4 to 10 miles. It was seen for only a few seconds to two minutes and was described as traveling with a fluttering motion, about half the size of a small airplane. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 25** — 3:50 p.m. On 25 Feb at 15:50 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a metallic object seen to the S or SW at about 12,000 feet. It was described as about as large as or larger than an average plane, moving fast, and then fading from view after a few seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 25** — 9:15 p.m. On 25 Feb at 21:15 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single witness reported a bright greenish-white tear-drop object seen toward the south at about 20 degrees above the horizon. It appeared almost vertical, about three times the size of a shooting star, moving slightly slower than a falling star, and burned out after roughly 1.5 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, February 27** — 7:00 p.m. On 27 Feb at 1900, a single reliable observer reported a green-white object seen over Los Alamos, New Mexico moving west to east on a path parallel to the earth. The object was observed for about 2 seconds, made no sound, and disappeared abruptly; it was described as moving not as fast as a meteor. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 2** — 12:10 a.m. At about 0010 on 2 March in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable observer saw a light-colored object moving very fast from north to south at a low altitude in the sky for about 2 seconds before it disappeared behind trees. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 3** — 1:59 a.m. On 3 March at 01:59, a single reliable observer reported a bright green object over Los Alamos, New Mexico moving straight down before disappearing. No additional description, sound, duration, or size was recorded in the source table. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 6** — 8:45 p.m. On 6 Mar at 20:45, a single observer at Camp Hood, Texas reported a light-colored object with an orange trail, described as a 'hound head with trail,' moving from S 81° W to S 60° W while descending from about 21° above the horizon to 6°31' above. The object was estimated to be about 10° in length and was noted as having a trail; no sound, speed estimate, or duration was recorded. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 6** — 9:00 p.m. On 6 March at 2100 hours, two unknown-reliability observers at Camp Hood, Texas reported a blue-white light moving from N 74° W to N 81° W at an estimated altitude of 6°45' above the horizon. The object was described as oblong, with a train or trail, about 2' by 1', soundless, and of unknown speed; no duration beyond the observation entry was provided. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 7** _(cluster ×3)_ — 1:13 a.m. On 7 March at 0113, a single unimpaired observer at Camp Hood, Texas reported a brilliant blue-white object moving N 40° E at an apparent altitude of 66°15' above the horizon. The object was described as having no trail, no sound, no shape beyond 'like flash bulb,' with an apparent size comparable to a flash bulb and a brief flash-bulb-like appearance. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 7** — 1:45 a.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single witness reported seeing an orange, tear-drop shaped object around 0145 on 7 March that moved on a N 60° E bearing, then dropped vertically to the ground and disappeared behind trees after about 2 seconds. No sound, trail, or additional effects were reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 8** — 6:35 p.m. On 8 March at 18:35, a single reliable observer reported an intense white, aluminum-colored elliptical object pointed at both ends moving from south to north over Los Alamos, New Mexico. It was estimated at 4,000 feet above terrain, descending at a 45-degree angle, traveled more slowly than a twin-engine plane, made no sound, and disappeared behind trees after a very short observation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 9** — A contemporaneous report summarized in an FBI file describes March 9 as the climax of a wave of "flying saucerism": a U.S. businessman traveling in Mexico claimed to have seen a streamlined disc in which a 23-inch-tall pilot had perished, while a related claim from Denver, Colorado said a similar saucer had crashed nearby and contained three little men. The excerpt also quotes the U.S. Defense Department repeating denials of flying saucers. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 91](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, March 14** — 9:00 a.m. An airplane en route from Honolulu to Canton Island reported a brief daytime sighting of an unidentified object seen about 6 ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 14** — 8:00 p.m. On 14 Mar 1947 at about 2000 hours, a single reliable observer in Vaughn, New Mexico reported a bright white, round object at an estimated 400-500 feet altitude, descending slowly in a vertical manner. The object was described as larger than a basketball, produced no sound or trail, and then exploded during its descent. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1947, March 17** — 3:00 a.m. On 17 Mar at about 0300 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round, greenish-yellow object about half the size of the full moon moving in level flight to the northeast for about 3 seconds, with a visual effect described as like a light going out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 17** — 3:07 a.m. On 17 Mar at 03:07 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single report describes a round object about one-quarter the size of a full moon moving earthward extremely fast. It was visible for about 3 seconds, made no sound, and reportedly plunged to the earth, after which two explosions were noted before it disappeared. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 17** — 3:10 a.m. At 0310 on 17 March, a witness reported a single reddish object that then turned green and appeared to fall toward the earth near Los Alamos, New Mexico. The observation lasted about one second and no additional details about the object or witness are given. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 17** — 3:10 a.m. A single witness near Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a small spherical object that seemed to climb, change from green to yellow, then descend and disappear after about 5 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 17** — 4:45 a.m. At 0445 on 17 Mar, a single witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a quite small, round green object that formed an arc and then fell toward the earth in free fall, with the sighting lasting about 5 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 21** — 1:00 p.m. On 21 March at about 1300-1330, four witnesses at Kirtland AFB, New Mexico, reported a bright silver, round object seen 40-50 degrees above the horizon moving from SE to S in a zig-zag up-and-down pattern. They estimated it as about dime-sized at arm's length and about the same size as a fast jet aircraft, with no sound, no lights, and no observable interaction before it disappeared from view. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 21** — 1:00 p.m. On 21 Mar at 1300, a single witness at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported seeing one round object moving NE to SE at an estimated 40,000-60,000 feet. It reportedly appeared shiny silver and then shady gray, was about the size of the end of a thumb at arm's length and about the same size as a jet aircraft, made no sound, and disappeared after about 30 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 21** — 1:15 p.m. On 21 March at 13:15, a single witness at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a white, round object seen about 75 degrees above the horizon toward the east. The object was described as smaller than a fist at arm's length, remained visible for about 5 minutes, and was estimated to travel around 600–700 mph before disappearing from view. The file context is an FBI declassified UFO case compilation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 22** — 11:00 a.m. On 22 Mar at 1100 near Kirtland AFB, New Mexico, a single witness reported a tan-to-brown flying wing-shaped object at about 25,000 to 30,000 feet, seen moving horizontally from the NW changing to N and appearing about the size of a golf ball held at arm's length; it lasted roughly 5 to 6 seconds before disappearing at extremely high speed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 27** — 6:00 p.m. On 27 March at about 1800-1805, a single witness in Tucumcari, New Mexico, reported an amber, long and narrow object high in the sky about 30 degrees above the horizon, moving from east to west and fading out in the distance after about 25 minutes. No sounds, effects, or other details were recorded. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, March 27** — 6:00 p.m. On 27 Mar at 1800, a single witness in Montoya, New Mexico reported an orange flame-like object moving from east to west about 75 degrees above the horizon. The object was described as long and narrow, with dimensions estimated at about one-sixth the lunar diameter in length and one-third of its length in width. It faded out in the distance after about 10 minutes. The report is from a declassified FBI case file. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 1** — 4:20 a.m. At 0420 on 1 April, a single witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico observed a white light traveling south to north at about 75 degrees above the horizon. The object was described as round, with no sound, lasting one second or less before disappearing behind a building. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 1** _(cluster ×3)_ — 11:00 a.m. In July 1947, WBAS Richmond pilot balloon observers reported a strange metallic disk seen multiple times over the previous six months, with Mr. Minczewski observing it three times through a theodolite and Miss Baron once. The last detailed sighting occurred in April 1947 during an 1100E pibal observation when the balloon was at 15,000 feet; the disk appeared below the balloon, moved on a level east-to-west track toward the far north of the station, and was followed for about 15 seconds. It was described as chrome-like, silver, and ellipse-shaped with a flat bottom and dome-like top, appearing larger in the instrument than the balloon. Weather was clear or nearly clear with good visibility. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 213–214](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 169–170](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, p. 126](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, April 5** — 10:00 p.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, one witness reported an arc-shaped object seen for less than a second at about 2200 on 5 April, apparently moving from south to north about 300 feet above the south slope of Pejarito Street. It appeared green with a red afterglow, moved at tremendous speed, and disappeared behind a mountain. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:35 a.m. On 7 April at 01:35 in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a green object about 200 yards from the top of a hill that moved very slowly and was visible for about 45 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 17** — 3:30 p.m. On 17 Apr at 1530 in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a witness reported a light green object about 2,000 feet above the horizon that maneuvered up and down and side to side for 20–30 seconds before gradually going out of sight. The object appeared bright as tin foil and was estimated as 1/16 inch at arm's length. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 18** — 1:48 a.m. On 18 April at 0148 near Flagstaff and Williams, Arizona, two observers reported a greenish-blue, ball-like object resembling a 100-watt light bulb. It was seen for 1-2 seconds, estimated at about 12,000 feet for one observer and 75-100 miles distant for the other, and then disappeared behind an obstacle. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 20** — 3:30 p.m. On 20 Apr at 15:30 in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a witness reported a bright metallic, roughly circular object about 9 feet in diameter that maneuvered up and down at an estimated altitude of 15-30 meters and moved faster than conventional aircraft before it was lost from sight. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 22** — 9:05 a.m. On 22 Apr at 0905 in Cliff, New Mexico, a single witness reported an aluminum-colored object traveling west to east at about 20 degrees and dropping slowly. The object was described as a round, flat, thin disc-shaped craft over 15 feet in diameter and was seen for about 2 minutes before disappearing behind mountains, with no sound or other effects noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 24** — 10:33 a.m. On 24 April at 1033, a white to yellow-white ellipsoidal light was seen over White Sands, New Mexico, apparently by five witnesses. It was observed for about 60 seconds at an estimated 25-29° elevation, moving at a tremendous rate of speed before disappearing due to distance. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, April 30** — In a 1947 Phoenix case involving William Albert Rhodes, the witness reported taking photographs of what he believed were flying discs and later provided negatives to the FBI, which said they were accepted for Air Force intelligence and might not be returned; the document concerns custody of the negatives and the FBI's suggested response to press inquiries rather than any detailed visual description of the objects. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 52–53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1947, May 1** — 3:10 p.m. On 1 May at 15:10 near Kirtland AFB, New Mexico, two witnesses reported a silver, lucid metallic cylindrical object moving SW to NE at apparently a few thousand feet altitude while angling downward. The object was seen for less than a second and reportedly produced a tremendous flash of brilliant white light; no sound or other effects were noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, May 5** — 3:30 p.m. On May 5, 1947, while driving between Ellensburg and Seattle, Washington, Warden John Henry and two others (Melbourne M. Hill and Mrs. Henry) saw a silver object streak across the sky around 3:30 P.M. The object made a nose dive, appeared about to crash, then disintegrated high in the air, leaving a long smoky/gaseous pillar that remained hanging and did not disperse while they watched it from the car over roughly 20 to 30 miles of travel. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 186](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, May 17** _(cluster ×4)_ — 8:30 p.m. Byron B. Savage, a 38-year-old RCA field engineer and licensed private pilot in Oklahoma City, reported that between May 17 and May 21, 1947, just after dusk, he and his wife saw a frosty white disc-like object over the south side of the city. He said it changed from appearing elliptical to perfectly round and flat as it approached, showed no protrusions or trail, seemed roughly as large as six B-29s at a comparable altitude, moved about three times as fast as a jet aircraft, and was visible for roughly 15 to 20 seconds before diminishing and departing northward. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 219–220](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 175–176](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 135–137](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, p. 81](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, May 19** _(cluster ×3)_ — 12:15 p.m. On 19 May 1947 between 12:15 and 13:15, three Pikes Peak Railway employees in Manitou Springs, Colorado, observed a small silver object approaching from the northeast. Using 4-6 power binoculars, they reported it was extremely small, too distant for size or altitude to be confidently determined, and not consistent with a conventional aircraft. The object appeared higher than Manitou Mountain, moved very rapidly westward, and repeatedly executed climbing, diving, reversal of direction, and brief hovering before departing by climbing sharply to the west. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 238–239](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 193–194](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, p. 80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June early-June** — The report is an FBI follow-up on the 1947 Tacoma, Washington flying-disc publicity scandal involving Harold A. Dahl and Fred Chrisman. Dahl and Chrisman later admitted their earlier claims about flying disk fragments from Maury Island were false and said they had instead collected strange rock formations from a gravel pit and sent them to Ray Palmer for chemical analysis. The document focuses on interviews, phone calls, and media manipulation around the alleged fragments rather than a direct UAP observation, though it references the original flying-disk story that triggered the investigation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 138–144](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, June 1** — This FBI memorandum summarizes the 1947 Maury Island flying disc story centered on Harold Dahl and Fred Crisman, reporting that Dahl claimed to have seen six discs near Maury Island and that fragments damaged his boat and killed a dog, but the FBI investigation concluded the material sent to Ray Palmer was ordinary rock formations later misrepresented as disc fragments. The report also describes anonymous calls to Tacoma reporters, a subsequent B-25 crash near Kelso, and the Bureau's view that Crisman was likely responsible for helping build up the story, though no definitive proof of the calls was obtained. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 35–41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, June 2** — 12:40 a.m. On 2 June at 00:40 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a green ball or small light descending from the east toward the north at an estimated altitude of 7,000 to 10,000 feet for about one second, with no sound noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, June 10** — On or around 10 June 1947, rumors reported from rural areas in Hungary and drifting into Budapest described silver, ball-shaped objects flashing across the daytime sky. The report notes that no accurate description or confirmation was obtained. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 116](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 11** — 8:57 p.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported observing a star-sized object traveling from southwest to northeast at about 25 degrees above the horizon; it appeared green during the flight and red at the end, lasted roughly 4 to 5 seconds, and made no sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, June 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:15 p.m. On 12 June 1947 at about 6:15 p.m., Mrs. Herbert Erickson and a neighbor in Weiser, Idaho looked westward and saw first what seemed like airplane smoke, then a tiny glistening object emitting vapor and, later, a second object that shot into the sky from near the sun. The objects moved on a south-easterly course with up-and-down shooting motion before fading to glints and vapor; the vapor lingered for over an hour and appeared cloud-like with a black streak to the horizon. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 54–57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 46–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 14** _(cluster ×4)_ — 12:00 p.m. Experienced pilot Richard Rankin reported seeing ten round, disc-like aerial objects over Bakersfield, California on 14 June 1947, first moving north in a V formation at an estimated 8,500-9,000 feet and 300-350 mph, then reappearing about 2:15 PM on the reverse course south in a reduced formation of seven. He initially believed they were Navy or Army test aircraft, later comparing them to the XF5U-1 'flying flapjack,' and only later connected them with the wave of 'flying disc' reports. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 66–69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 54–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, pp. 179–181](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 39–41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 14** — The report does not document an actual UAP sighting directly; instead it summarizes FBI reporting on newspaper claims about flying discs, the Tacoma meeting with witnesses Harold Dahl and Fred Chrisman, and the investigation into the August 1, 1947 Kelso crash of an AAF B-25 flown by Captain Davidson and Lieutenant Brown. The investigation concluded the aircraft was not carrying disc fragments and that sabotage allegations were unsupported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 31–34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, June 19** — A mother and daughter in Northeast Washington reported seeing three huge disc-like objects hurtling across the sky at high speed; the objects reportedly sounded like jet-propelled planes and vanished almost immediately after being observed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 120](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, June 21** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:30 a.m. On 21 June 1947 shortly before noon in Spokane, Washington, Guy A. Overman observed a bright shiny silvery object while watching a two-motored aircraft pass southbound overhead. He first saw one large flashing disc-like object moving more slowly than the plane and seemingly a little ahead of it, with additional smaller flashes or discs becoming visible. The objects flashed repeatedly as they moved south, slightly east of Smith, and then became less distinct. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 51–53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 42–43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 21** — In the Maury Island/Tacoma, Washington incident, Harold A. Dahl reported that on 21 June 1947 he saw five large disc-like objects over the Tacoma harbor; one allegedly wavered, collided with another, and dumped hot metal-like debris onto his boat, damaging it and killing his dog. Fred L. Crisman later claimed to have found similar material in a sand pit and to have seen a disc behave similarly, but investigators noted the alleged damage appeared inconsistent with Dahl’s claims and that no corroborating newspaper reports were found. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 14–17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, June 22** — 11:30 a.m. On 22 June 1947 at about 11:30 a.m. in Greenfield, Massachusetts, Edward L. De Rose reported a single brilliant small round object, silvery-white and like polished aluminum, moving northwest at about the speed of a fast plane or faster at an estimated altitude of 1,000 feet or more. It remained visible for roughly 8 to 10 seconds and then was obscured by a cloud bank; no sound, exhaust, or photographs were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 130–131](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 22** — 11:30 a.m. On June 22, 1947 at about 11:30 AM, a civilian in Greenfield, Massachusetts saw a brilliant small round silvery-white object pass quickly across his line of sight while he was working outdoors on a step-ladder. The object was estimated at about 1,000 feet or higher, moved very fast in a straight northwesterly course for roughly 8 to 10 seconds, and then disappeared into a cloudbank. The witness considered and rejected a weather balloon explanation, noted strong sunlight reflection like polished aluminum or silver, and said the object had an irregular round shape that did not especially look disc-shaped. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, p. 83](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 23** — 2:15 p.m. On June 23, 1947, near Bakersfield, California, experienced pilot Dick Rankin reported seeing about ten silver, almost round aerial objects while sunbathing on a lawn. He estimated they were around 9,000 feet up and traveling 300-400 mph in formation northbound, then returning southbound about 2:15 P.M. with only seven remaining. Rankin compared them to the Navy XF5U-1 "flying flapjack," though no official confirmation supported that identification. The report appears in a newspaper account later summarized in an FBI file. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, p. 180](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** _(cluster ×4)_ — 2:59 p.m. On June 24, 1947, pilot Kenneth Arnold reported seeing a chain of nine bright, disc-like objects near Mount Rainier/Mount Adams while flying from Chehalis toward Yakima, Washington. The objects flashed in the sun, appeared black as thin lines when level, lacked visible tails, and seemed to maintain formation over a distance of about five miles; Arnold later described them as likely saucer-like aircraft and submitted a detailed written account with sketches and photographs to Raymond A. Palmer and the authorities. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 159–165](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 89–114](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 93–103](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** — 3:00 p.m. On 24 June 1947 at about 15:00, pilot and dealer in fire control supplies Kenneth Arnold, flying near Mt. Rainier and Mineral, Washington, reported seeing a chain of nine mirror-like, approximately circular craft at about 9,500 ft, moving north to south on a definite course at roughly 170° and an estimated 150 mph. He observed them for about 2.5 to 3 minutes from an estimated distance of 20 to 25 miles in clear CAVU weather; they appeared sidewise, reflected sunlight brightly, swerved slightly around mountain peaks, and seemed to travel in a linked diagonal formation without tails or spin. Drawings were produced as evidence. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 36–37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** _(cluster ×3)_ — 3:00 p.m. On 24 June 1947 in the Cascade Mountains near Portland, Oregon, prospector Fred M. Johnson reported seeing a reflection and then a disc-like object (later described as 5 or 6 discs total) moving southeast while banking in sunlight. He watched one object through a telescope for about 45 to 60 seconds from roughly 1,000 ft away; the objects were estimated at about 30 feet in diameter, around 6,000 ft above sea level, round with a tail, silent, and not in any particular formation. His compass/watch reportedly reacted strangely during the sighting and returned to normal after the discs passed out of sight. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 192–193](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 145–146](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 127](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 24 June 1947 in Idaho, Lt. Governor Donald S. Whitehead reported seeing a single comet-like object hanging in the western sky. He said it remained immobile and seemed to go below the horizon only as the earth rotated; no other details, sounds, or effects were stated. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 64–67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 52–53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 1947-06-24 near Mt. Adams, Oregon, prospector F. M. Johnson reported seeing the same flying objects he believed Kenneth Arnold had described, noting that they passed about 1000 feet above him, appeared round with an oval/tapered form and bright top surface, made no engine noise, and seemed to bank on edge into a cloud. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 2–4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 37–38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** — In late July 1947 near Maury Island, Washington, Harold Dahl reported seeing four or five circular objects over the island while boating with his son, a companion named Knight, and Fred Crismon's boat. The objects were described as about 100 feet across, with a central hole and visible porthole-like features, hovering at about 1,500 feet. One object appeared damaged, another seemed to contact it, and debris was allegedly thrown from the damaged craft, falling onto the boat, killing a dog and a seagull and damaging the wheelhouse spotlight and klaxon. Crismon later reported finding fragments onshore and said he saw a disk matching Dahl's description. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 52–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, June 24** — The report uses Kenneth Arnold's June 24, 1947 sighting as historical context and quotes his description of nine shiny disc-like objects moving rapidly across the sky; the source then shifts into a general discussion of how the Air Force and FBI treated flying-saucer reports rather than providing a new, standalone incident narrative. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 84](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, June 25** — On June 25, 1947, Mr. Dahl of Tacoma, Washington reported a circular, silver, doughnut-like flying machine maneuvering over his backyard. He then observed five additional craft rotating around a seventh central object, after which the center craft shed metallic-looking debris that mostly fell seaward, with one piece landing in his yard. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 91](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, June 25** — Kenneth Arnold, a businessman and pilot from Boise, Idaho, reported on June 25 at Pendleton, Oregon, that he had seen nine unidentified objects flying in formation over Washington State's Cascade Mountains. He estimated their speed at 1,300 miles per hour and described them as shifting position "like the tail of a kite." ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 121](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, June 27** — 1:38 a.m. Single witness in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a round object, slightly larger than the brightest star and slightly more orange than a star, seen moving west to east at an estimated 30° to 90° above the horizon; it traversed about 140° in 2 minutes and then went behind a building. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, June 27** — A brief news-style item from Detroit, Michigan dated Jun 27, 1947 says observers in the West were continuing to report flying saucers, while one pilot in Missoula claimed to have captured a disc and identified it as milkweed seeds; another former B-17 navigator in Houston proposed a Nazi-style radio-controlled explanation. The excerpt is largely contextual commentary and includes no distinct firsthand Detroit sighting. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 194](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, June 28** _(cluster ×4)_ — 3:15 p.m. On 14 July 1947, 1st Lt. Eric B. Armstrong reported that while flying a P-51 from Williams Field, Arizona toward Portland, Oregon, he observed five or six white circular objects northwest of Lake Mead, Nevada. The objects were seen off his right side at about 6,000 feet while he was at 10,000 feet, flying smoothly in close formation on an approximate 120-degree course and estimated 285 mph; he judged them to be about 36 inches in diameter and not birds, jets, or conventional aircraft. The FBI interviewed him the next day and recorded his account without offering a conventional explanation. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 156–157](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 117–118](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 188–189](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 46–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:43 p.m. On 28 June 1947 at 3:43 PM, a farmer near Rockfield, Wisconsin, reported by Marion Beuscher's brother, saw about seven to ten saucer-shaped objects fly directly over the family home about 16 miles northwest of Milwaukee. The objects reportedly moved very fast, 'like blue blazes,' headed south, and made no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 126–127](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 92–93](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 28** — 3:43 p.m. Marion Beuscher of Menomonee Falls, Wisconsin forwarded to the FBI a warning letter she received from an anonymous Cincinnati postmark that referred to people talking about 'things' they were seeing and connected that admonition to the recent 'flying saucer' controversy. In her attached explanation, Beuscher cited a January 1948 Amazing Stories letter she had written about her brother seeing about seven to ten discs flying silently over their house near Rockfield, northwest of Milwaukee, on June 28, 1947 at about 3:43 PM, and she also linked the issue to another reader letter about 'Rainbow City, Antarctica.' ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 22–28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1947, June 28** _(cluster ×3)_ — 9:20 p.m. On 28 June 1947 at about 2120 Central Time over Maxwell Field, Montgomery, Alabama, four military observers reported a single brilliant light slightly brighter than a star traveling from west to east at high speed. The object was seen at great height on a clear moonlit night, made no audible sound, and moved in a zig-zag course with frequent bursts of speed, changing direction from east to south and then southwest before disappearing in the moon's brilliance after more than five minutes. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 236–237](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 191–192](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 104–105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 28** — A report dated 28 June 1947 from Illinois describes 7 to 10 unidentified discs sighted over Illinois in the afternoon. The observer was not identified; the case is second-hand, with the announcement later broadcast on WON's "The Latest News" by announcer Charles Sebastin and noted as having been taken from a later letter published in "Amusing Stories" dated Jan 1948. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 128–129](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 28** — A restricted War Department UFO checklist records an afternoon sighting in Illinois on 28 June 1947, reported via a WGN 'The Latest News' broadcast at 8:00 A.M. the next day. The summary states that 7 to 10 discs were seen over Illinois, but most descriptive fields such as distance, altitude, speed, direction, shape, color, sound, and disappearance details were left blank in the checklist. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 94–95](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 29** _(cluster ×3)_ — 1:00 p.m. On 29 June 1947 between about 1:00 and 1:30 p.m., C. H. Zohn and three other occupants of an automobile traveling northeast on Highway 17 from Las Cruces, New Mexico toward the White Sands V-2 firing grounds observed an unidentified aerial object to the right and ahead of the car. The object appeared to move horizontally northward across the highway at an unknown but rapid speed, at an estimated altitude around 8,000-10,000 feet. Witnesses reported a uniform shape with no visible wings or protuberances, specular solar reflection that changed as it receded, possible vapor trails, and disappearance after about 30 seconds under clear visibility. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 234–235](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 189–190](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 106–107](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, June 29** _(cluster ×4)_ — 4:45 p.m. On 29 June 1947 at about 16:45, Dale Bays, a former bus driver, observed two groups of unidentified objects near Clarion, Iowa while driving a bus toward Mason City. The first group contained five objects and the second 13; they flew at roughly 1,200 ft altitude, about 300 mph, in single file on opposing headings (south-southeast and north-northwest). The objects were described as dirty white, between a circle and an oval, like inverted saucers, with an estimated thickness of about 12 feet and diameters from 175 to 250 feet. Bays reported a sound like an electric motor or dynamo, and the second group disappeared into the north-northwest sky within a few minutes. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 50–51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, June 29** — 11:00 p.m. On 29 June at about 11:00 P.M. in Uniontown, Pennsylvania, Mrs. Catherine McDonald reported seeing three luminous, oval objects one at a time from her front porch. Each appeared very fast, silent, and odorless, rolled/spun vertically on edge, and left a short trail of light while flying beneath cloudy storm clouds with lightning visible through them. The objects were first seen in the southwest and traveled northeast, then disappeared behind neighboring houses after about 5 to 10 seconds each. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 183](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1947, June 29** — C. J. Zohn, who worked in the rocket-sonde high altitude research section of the Naval Research Laboratory, reported that he and three other men saw one saucer in New Mexico on June 29. He described it as a bright, silvery disc traveling at about 10,000 feet and said it was clearly visible before suddenly disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 112](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, June 30** _(cluster ×4)_ — 9:10 a.m. On 30 June 1947 at about 0910 MST, while flying a P-80 over the Grand Canyon southbound toward Williams Field at 25,000 feet, Lt. William G. McGinty reported seeing two circular, possibly light-gray objects moving straight down at inconceivable speed; he turned away as one followed the other seconds apart, and he estimated they were about eight feet in diameter and likely would have impacted roughly 25 miles south of the South Rim.
