Plummer, a former Army Air Forces lieutenant in the Signal Corps, reported seeing a circular/round, light almost-white airborne object with a dark center traveling northwest by west along the Washington, DC to Pittsburgh airline route. He compared its speed to a DC-4 passing earlier the same morning, judged it to be 3 to 4 times faster, and noted no smoke trail. The object was visible for about 20 seconds after emerging from cloud cover at roughly 12,000 feet, and he heard a loud jet-like sound as it approached and passed overhead. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 122–124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, July 20** — Over about six hours on the morning of July 20, commercial pilot Barnes and nearby pilots observed multiple unexplained lights/pips over Washington, DC; one pilot reported a light off his left wing, Barnes saw a matching radar pip, and by daybreak as many as ten peculiar pips were simultaneously present on the radar screen. Barnes later wrote that the objects were not ordinary aircraft and that neither shooting stars, electrical disturbances, nor clouds could explain the sightings. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 83](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1947, July 21** — On 21 July 1947, during a follow-up interview relayed in a Naval Research Laboratory letter dated 28 July 1947, C. C. Rockwood described seeing an unidentified flying object with Mr. C. H. Zohn. Rockwood said the object was either a silvery sphere or disc, initially seen at about 45 degrees elevation and appearing about the size of the sun. He believed it might have been a meteorological balloon except that it moved much faster than the automobile they were in, traveling roughly in the same direction at a horizontal trajectory. The car was moving about 60 mph, with little or no surface wind. He heard no sound beyond the noise of the car, saw no smoke or contrails, and noted that the object's aspect did not change before it disappeared by becoming too small to see. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 69–72](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, July 22** — This report is not a direct sighting statement but a letter from R. A. Palmer asking Kenneth Arnold to pursue the Tacoma-area 'flying disc' story. Palmer summarizes an alleged incident near Murray Island, off Tacoma, where Harold A. Dahl and two other seamen on patrol reportedly saw six discs, one apparently in trouble, then witnessed an explosion and falling debris that damaged their wheelhouse and searchlight and landed on the beach. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, July 23** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:45 a.m. At Harmon Field in Stephenville, Newfoundland, two witnesses walking outdoors at 0345Z on 23 July 1947 observed peculiar reddish intermittent flashes or a light that at first resembled a shooting star or airplane. The phenomenon reappeared over roughly three minutes, seemed to travel from south toward NNE at high speed and about 10,000 feet altitude, and was judged by the observers to be neither a falling star nor an airplane because of abrupt maneuvering and complete silence. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 119–120](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 15–16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, July 23** — 7:15 a.m. On 23 July 1947 at 0345Z (0715 local), two witnesses at Harmon Field near Stephenville, Newfoundland, observed a single unidentified object at about 10,000 feet. It was seen for roughly three minutes moving NNE at very high speed, described as faster than conventional aircraft, making abrupt darts and showing reddish flashes of light while producing no noise. Weather was reported as high scattered clouds with 15 miles visibility. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 160](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, July 24** — A witness named SAVAGE reported observing a frosty white object pass by without protrusions; as it moved away it appeared to diminish in size and speed while heading roughly 350° north. He thought he heard a faint swishing sound a few seconds after it passed, though he was uncertain whether the sound was real or imagined. His wife was called to look, but the object disappeared before she could focus on it. SAVAGE, a licensed pilot since 1934 and flying since 1929, said it was not a meteor and believed it was radically built and powered, probably atomic. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, p. 82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, July 24** — Mrs. Waitey Johnson reported that eight saucer-like objects were seen near St. Maries, Idaho, coming in at extreme speed from the south and traveling north before slowing and fluttering down into timber on a mountainside. The witnesses could see them descend but not after landing, and no effect on the trees was observed. Another report said the discs landed in full view of 10 persons, prompting an air-ground search and a patrol investigation by the Oregon Air Guard. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 116](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1947, July 25** — Kenneth Arnold reported seeing nine strange objects while flying over the Cascade Mountain Range in Washington State on 1947-07-25; an FBI interview on 1947-07-12 memorialized his account and attached his written report, while the agent noted Arnold was credible, checked his claims against aeronautical charts, and believed the distances, speed, course, and size estimates could be factual. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 138–142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, July 28** — 10:55 p.m. At Killeen, Texas, on 28 Jul at about 2255, a single witness reported a round object seen to the north-northwest about 30 degrees above the horizon. It was initially blue, then turned white, appeared about three times the size of the evening star, and went out like a light after 15-20 seconds. No sound, lights beyond the color change, or other effects were reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, July 29** _(cluster ×3)_ — 12:00 p.m. On 29 July 1947 after noon at Hamilton Field, California, 1st Lt. Ward L. Stewart (Air Reserve, former B-29 pilot) observed two milk-white unidentified objects near the field at about 6,000 ft altitude. The first was noticed while Stewart was being told of it by Capt. Ryherd; it was estimated to be following a P-80 at three or four times the jet's speed, and a second object then appeared and executed fighter-like maneuvers over the first object before both flew out of sight after roughly 15 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 154–155](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 115–116](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 84](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, July 29** _(cluster ×2)_ — 12:05 p.m. On 29 July 1947 at about 12:05 p.m., R. J. Madden and two named witnesses, Steve Herrmann and Karl Herrmann, observed a bright circular disc hovering over rural terrain about 25 miles northeast of Helena, Montana, while traveling northwest on the road from Canyon Ferry to York. Seen at an estimated distance of 2 to 3 miles, the object appeared polished nickel in color, shimmered in bright sunshine, and seemed about 3 feet in diameter and 3 to 4 inches thick from that range. It hovered and fluttered, descending and rising through roughly 50 to 100 feet before suddenly swooping northeast at tremendous speed and vanishing into clear air, described as having 'melted into thin air.' ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 182–183](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 137](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, July 29** — 12:05 p.m. On July 29, 1947, near Canyon Ferry, Montana, three civilian occupants in a moving sedan reported a bright metallic-looking disc in the northwest sky. The object hovered and fluttered about 2 to 3 miles away and roughly 3000 feet above the ground, then accelerated sharply to the northeast and vanished from view. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 172–173](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, July 29** _(cluster ×3)_ — 2:50 p.m. On 29 July 1947 at 1450 near Hamilton Field, California, Capt. William H. Ryherd, USAF assistant operations officer, observed two shiny white circular aerial objects from the ground while watching a P-30. He estimated them at about 15-25 feet in diameter and 8,000-10,000 feet altitude, moving very fast southward toward Oakland. The first flew straight and level; the second stayed close behind and appeared to move side-to-side like a guard in formation. No sound, exhaust, vapor, or trail was observed, and both objects disappeared in flight over Oakland toward the ocean. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 152–153](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 113–114](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 186](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, July 29** — On July 29, 1947, near Canyon Ferry, Montana, Karl Herrmann, Steve Herrmann, and R. J. (Bob) Madden reported sighting a flying disc/flying saucer. The FBI Seattle office forwarded copies of Madden's report and related correspondence for information only, and noted that no action was being taken. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 175](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, July 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:04 p.m. On 30 July at 22:04 near Camp Hood, Texas, a witness reported a very fast round object with a tail, predominantly white with a blue tint, moving straight from SE to SW about 30 degrees above the horizon while losing altitude; it was visible for 2-3 seconds and gradually faded out, with the observer comparing it to an evening star or planet. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, July 30** — On 30 July 1947, AFBIR-CO compiled a breakdown of 18 reported 'Flying Disc' sightings from June-July 1947, including witness backgrounds and observed characteristics. The report is an analytic summary table; one report (No. 7) lacked detail because the statement had not yet been received, and reports 2, 4, 17, and 18 were not analyzed in this section. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 74–79](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, July 30** — This report is not a firsthand sighting but an interview with attorney Dan Nelson, who claimed to have solved the 'Flying Saucer Mystery' by arguing that many sightings were reflections of bright objects on automobile or aircraft windows. He described saucer-like, disc-like objects appearing to move, rotate, dip, or multiply due to sun/glass reflections, vibration, and observer position, and stated he had not personally spoken with anyone who actually saw flying saucers. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 54–62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, July** — 10:00 p.m. In July 1947 near Bethel, Alaska, pilot Jack Peck and co-pilot Vince Daly, flying a DC-3 into Bethel Airport under clear evening skies, observed a dark, definite-shaped object off their left side at about 10 PM, just after sunset. Peck said it looked like a flying wing, roughly the size of a C-54 without a fuselage, with no visible propeller, jet exhaust, vapor trail, or radio interference. The aircraft turned about 45 degrees to avoid it, and Peck later estimated the object was traveling roughly toward Nome at about 300 mph. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 5–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1947, July** — In July 1947 at Oak Ridge, Tennessee, W. R. Presley photographed what he described as a vapor trail and then a "ball of fire" after looking up while taking family photos outside his home on Illinois Avenue. The FBI memo relays the photographs, their circulation among acquaintances, and later retrieval by Atomic Energy Commission and Army personnel; no active FBI investigation was conducted, but Colonel Gasser offered speculative analysis that the objects might have been nuclear-powered or Soviet experimental craft. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 84–88](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1947, August 1** _(cluster ×2)_ — In late July and early August 1947, Kenneth Arnold, Emil Smith, Fred Crisman, and Harold Dahl discussed alleged flying disc sightings and Maury Island fragments at the Winthrop Hotel in Tacoma. The report centers on the claimed recovery and handling of fragment material said to have come from the discs, the involvement of Army Intelligence officers, and the subsequent B-25 crash that killed Capt. Davidson and Lt. Brown. The source itself does not describe a direct visual sighting in this narrative beyond the broader Maury Island flying disc story; instead it documents interviews, phone calls, and the transfer of alleged fragments to military officers, followed by later statements that the fragments resembled ordinary slag. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 24–26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 42–49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 3** — 7:45 p.m. On 1947-08-03 in Hackensack, New Jersey, Charles Casella Jr., William Truex, and Joyce McFarland observed a small round object moving rapidly northward in a steady horizontal path about 200 yards above ground. The witnesses initially thought it might be a balloon, but reported no wind, no strings, no exhaust, and enough light to see it clearly; descriptions varied between black, silver-blue, disc-like, orb-like, and oval/flat. The object remained visible for roughly 15-20 seconds to about one minute before fading over the horizon. FBI Newark interviewed the witnesses within days and found no other local police reports. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 4–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 3** _(cluster ×3)_ — On 3 Aug 1947 in late afternoon at Hackensack, New Jersey, Charles Casella, Jr. and William Truex (U.S. Army, Ft. Dix) reported seeing a single flying disc approximately 200 yards away and about 200 yards in the air. They described it as moving rapidly while revolving slowly, and stated it was neither a kite nor a balloon. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 230–231](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 185–186](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, p. 155](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>))

**1947, August 4** — 2:00 a.m. On 4 August at about 0200 and again at 0300, two unknown-reliability observers reported a green object over North Powder, Oregon, moving from northwest to southeast in a horizontal line at an estimated altitude of 5,500 feet. It was described as softball-sized and traveling about 720 mph, with no sound, trail, or duration recorded; the object later extinguished. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, August 4** _(cluster ×3)_ — 4:00 p.m. On 4 August 1947, while flying a Pan American Constellation from Gander, Newfoundland to La Guardia Field, Captain Alpheus O. Powell and navigator Walter I. White separately observed a bright orange/gold unidentified flying object near Boston, Massachusetts at about 8,000 feet. Powell described a cylindrical, blunt-ended object about P-40 fuselage length, roughly one mile away and slightly below aircraft level, seen for about 30 seconds before being lost in cloud; White described an elliptical, blunted object about 15 feet long and 2-3 feet deep, about 3 miles away and at least 1,000 feet below, also observed for almost 30 seconds. Both men were experienced airline personnel and the incident lasted under a minute and a half total. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 202–203](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 159–160](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 1–4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>))

**1947, August 4** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 4 Aug 1947 in the evening near NW of Bethel, Alaska, Capt. Jack Peck and co-pilot Vince Daly, flying for Al Jones Flying Service, observed a black saucer-like object northwest of Bethel in a brilliant evening sky. They initially pulled up to about 1,200 ft to avoid a possible collision, then turned in behind it and followed it for about four minutes while it maintained a NW course at an estimated 500 to 1,000 ft altitude and about 520 mph, before flying rapidly out of sight. The object was described as as large as or larger than a C-54, smooth-surfaced, streamlined, and with no visible motors, wings, exhaust, or means of propulsion. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 166–167](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 125–126](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 4** — On 1947-08-04 near Bethel, Alaska, Captain Jack Peck and co-pilot Vince Daly, flying a Douglas DC-3, reported a smooth, streamlined flying saucer larger than their aircraft crossing their course at right angles at about 1,000 feet altitude. They turned to follow it at about 170 mph, but lost sight of it after four minutes; the report emphasized no visible means of propulsion and noted the witnesses were considered credible by local Signal Corps personnel. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 180–183](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 5** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:50 p.m. An FBI teletype from Portland summarized newspaper and telephonic reporting about recent flying-disc claims in the Portland area and an Army Air Forces inquiry into them. The message focused on interviews by AAF officers Capt. William L. Davidson and Lt. Frank M. Brown with experienced pilots, including Dick Rankin, Kenneth Arnold, Capt. E. J. Smith, Ralph Stevens, and Dave Johnson, and relayed a local editor's impression that the AAF was trying to debunk the reports as non-AAF in origin. No specific Portland sighting description is given in the excerpt itself. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, pp. 143–144](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:30 p.m. On 6 Aug 1947 between 10:30 and 10:45 P.M. in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, Eleanor Raddle reported from her home seeing one large white object move very fast to the south in apparently straight flight. She noted a slight buzzing sound just after it passed and a thin grayish smoke streak left in its wake; no other structure, shape, or distance was given. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 194–195](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 147–148](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** — 10:30 p.m. On 6 Aug 1947 around 22:30-22:45 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, Miss Cecelia Fine, while at 2114 Hobart St. and conversing on the steps of Miss Naddle's home, noticed Miss Naddle appear frightened; facing south, Fine did not continue to observe the object directly but reported hearing a slight buzzing sound. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 149–150](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:45 p.m. On 6 Aug 1947 around 22:45 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, insurance agent and former B-24 pilot John Snyder reported from the steps of his home that he and the neighboring Kelly family saw a single object pass quickly across the sky from northeast to southwest. Snyder described a bluish-white flame, a hissing sound, and either a smoke or condensation trail that lingered for about two seconds; he estimated the object at 1,000-3,000 ft altitude and 400-500 mph. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 196–197](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 151–152](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** — 10:45 p.m. On 1947-08-06 at about 22:45 in Philadelphia, a reliable witness group interviewed by the FBI reported a bluish-white flaming object at an estimated altitude of 1,000 feet or more moving from northeast to southwest. The object left a short-lived smoke trail and was accompanied by a buzzing or missing sound; one witness was a former Army Air Corps pilot who did not think it was a jet. The FBI teletype relayed the Philadelphia office's summary of the newspaper-reported incident. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 119–122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** — 10:45 p.m. On August 6, 1947, several apparently independent Philadelphia witnesses reported a fast-moving white or bluish-white object crossing the sky in a southwesterly/southerly direction, leaving a short smoke or condensation trail and in some accounts a fiery or bluish-white flame, with buzzing or hissing sounds. The reports came from Miss Eleanor Naddle and Miss Cecelia Fine near Hobart Street, and from John Snyder and retired police officer John J. Kelly near Oxford Circle; all accounts roughly corresponded. FBI follow-up checked with local Army and Naval Intelligence, a newspaper, and the Charles Lennig Chemical Company, but no military testing explanation was found and the office closed the matter unless advised otherwise. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 153–156](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** — 10:45 p.m. On 6 Aug 1947 around 22:45 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, retired police officer John J. Kelley and his wife observed a large object that looked to him like a giant firecracker pass quickly overhead/southbound, silently, leaving a fiery trail about 100 feet long before disappearing in a split second. The reporting form notes clear weather and no sketch or photograph. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 153–154](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 6** — FBI teletype reports that Red Hatfield, a foreign navy pilot and airport manager in Myrtle Creek, Oregon, saw a mysterious flying object on two occasions on the evening of August 6, 1947 while flying at about 5,000 feet above Myrtle Creek. He was to be interviewed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, p. 167](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>))

**1947, August 7** — 11:30 p.m. On the evening of August 7, 1947, John Bartlett of Agate Beach near Ocean Lake, Oregon reported seeing a bright disc for a very short time. He said the object was moving so fast that he could only observe it briefly before it disappeared from view. The FBI teletype notes that Bartlett was to be interviewed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 117–118](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 7** — FBI Seattle teletype dated 1947-08-12 summarizes interviews about the Tacoma, Washington flying disc affair involving Fred Chrisman, Harold Dahl, Kenneth Arnold, and pilot Emil J. Smith. Smith reported earlier sightings of discs or similar objects, but the Tacoma incident itself is described in the report as a false story or hoax told by Chrisman and Dahl; the document focuses on investigative leads, phone calls, and the deaths of Davidson and Brown after they apparently carried alleged fragments. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 146–148](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 8** — 3:30 a.m. Mrs. Robert Little of 6327 Harper Avenue, Chicago, reported seeing a flying disc at about 3:30 AM over Lake Michigan off Jackson Park Beach, as summarized in a contemporaneous FBI cable that also notes her father-in-law said she had seen a disc over a month earlier and that she was 18 years old. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 10** — 12:10 a.m. On 10 Aug at 00:10 near Killeen Base, Texas, a witness reported a blue oval object, about head-size, moving east to west at roughly a 30-degree downward angle before suddenly disappearing after 3 to 4 seconds. The report is preserved in an FBI declassified case file, but no further investigative conclusion is provided in the excerpt. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, August 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:00 p.m. On 10 Aug 1947 at about 9 o'clock well after dark near Silver Springs, Ohio, Lt. Col. F. L. Walker Jr. of the Scientific Branch, Research Group, observed a single low-flying object moving horizontally from north to south. It appeared over a barn, was bright orange like flame, left a long narrow white streak similar to a tracer bullet, and vanished in mid-air after 3-4 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 188–189](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 141–142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 10** — 9:00 p.m. On 10 August at Camp Hood, Texas, a witness reported four round objects seen at several times between 2100 and 2250, moving at great speed and appearing white with orange coloration and trailing light. The objects were estimated at 40°-70° above the horizon, with one nearly vertical and the others almost horizontal, each visible for about one second before going out like a light. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, August 10** — 10:07 p.m. On 10 August at Camp Hood, Texas, a single witness reported a white, round object with a tail moving from west to east in level flight about 30° to 45° above the horizon. The object was seen for only a few seconds, appeared larger than a flare, moved faster than a plane, and then burned out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, August 11** — In an oral statement recorded by Captain Harry D. Black on 11 August 1947, Colonel Gilkey, Commanding Officer at Muroc Army Air Field in California, said he saw an object that he believed at the time was paper and not significant enough to report. He indicated there was nothing seen clearly enough to justify any further reporting. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 12** — 2:20 p.m. On August 12, 1947, in Seattle, Washington, Bruce Armstrong and several Boeing research engineers saw a small dark triangular object about 200 feet up passing south over Boeing Field runway; witnesses compared it to a black leaf or tar paper, and a separate witness, M. A. Nichols, reported a reflective cylindrical object over his home later that afternoon that was ultimately attributed by investigators to weather balloons or burnt paper. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 178–180](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 12** — In Seattle, a newspaper report of two flying-disc sightings by Bruce Armstrong of Boeing Aircraft Company and M. A. Nichols was investigated by the FBI and both were explained conventionally: Armstrong's object was identified as a piece of burnt paper seen by the Boeing Field control tower, and Nichols's object was identified as a weather balloon. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 115–116](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** — 9:00 a.m. On 1947-08-13 around 09:00 near the Redmond post office in Redmond, Washington, three civilian witnesses reported two very bright, noiseless objects moving northeast at extreme speed, one slightly behind and above the other, maintaining equal pace for about 5-8 seconds before disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 143–145](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** — 9:30 a.m. On August 13, 1947, while fishing in isolated country about 40 miles southwest of Twin Falls, Idaho, County Commissioner and ex-sheriff Al W. Hawkins and Idaho State Warehouse Inspector J. H. Brown reported seeing two very high-altitude objects that looked like discs. They said the objects moved very rapidly, each appeared about six feet in diameter, and were accompanied by a roar like a motor truck before quickly disappearing from view. The FBI was notified by teletype on August 15 and further investigation was said to be underway. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 85–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** _(cluster ×5)_ — 1:00 p.m. On 13 Aug 1947 at about 1300, A. C. Urie, a farmer and operator of the Auger Falls Trout Farm near Smoke River Canyon/Twin Falls, Idaho, and his two sons observed a single sky-blue, oblong disc-like object moving east-to-west down the Snake River canyon at roughly 1000 mph. It was seen momentarily about 300 ft from the witnesses and approximately 75 ft above the canyon floor, following the canyon contours and disappearing behind a hill/tree; the report notes a swishing sound, a red tubular fiery glow/exhaust at the side of the top or hood, and a sketch was made. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 161–162](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 205–206](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_130, pp. 42–45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_130.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 123–124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** — 1:00 p.m. On August 13, 1947, at about 1300 hours, a farmer at his fishing camp looked for his two sons and saw a sky-blue object about 300 feet away and 75 feet above the ground, moving hedge-hopping along the canyon contour. The object was roughly 20 feet in diameter and 10 feet thick, with side pods emitting flames; it made a swishing sound and reportedly agitated nearby trees as it passed. His two sons, aged 8 and 10, also saw it, but none of the witnesses observed it for more than a few seconds. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 95](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** — 1:00 p.m. On 1947-08-13 at about 1:00 PM near Twin Falls, Idaho, A. C. Urie and his two sons reported seeing a flying-disc-like object about nine miles northwest of Twin Falls moving down the Salmon River at very high speed. They described it as light sky blue, roughly 20 feet long, 10 feet wide, and 10 feet thick, with flames coming from its sides and a loud swishing sound as it disappeared. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 29–30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** — 1:00 p.m. A witness described a close-up daytime sighting of a flying saucer moving down Snake River Canyon near Anger Falls Trout, seen from the south side looking north. The object was observed by the main witness and two boys; it passed at roughly 75 ft above the canyon floor, about 300 ft away from the main observer, with the boys seeing it from a different angle across the river in a boat. It was light sky blue with a red tubular fiery glow on the side of a hood, made very little noise like a swish, and appeared to ride up and down over the rough canyon terrain as if instrument-guided. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 133–134](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 13** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 13 Aug 1947 at Salmon Dam, County Commissioner Hawkins and J. H. Brown reported seeing two circular objects at high altitude, estimated around 4,000 to 6,000 ft and several miles away, traveling at great speed and reflecting light; both witnesses also heard a sound described as an echo or roar resembling a motor. They doubted the objects were planes. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 207–208](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 163–164](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, August 14** — 10:40 a.m. Three American enlisted men of the 147th Airways and Air Communications Service Squadron reported two unidentified flying objects over Harmon Field, Guam, at 1040 hours on 1947-08-14. They described the objects as small, crescent-shaped, moving west on a zig-zag course at roughly twice fighter-plane speed and at an estimated altitude of 1,200 feet; the objects entered clouds, and a few seconds later a similar object emerged and continued west. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 14** — 3:15 p.m. An FBI Seattle teletype dated August 14, 1947 summarizes a report of 'flying discs' sighted by Fred Chrisman and Harold A. Dahl in Tacoma, Washington. The message focuses not on the sighting itself but on investigative follow-up, noting that Dahl later said he would describe the story as a hoax if questioned by authorities, and that the Bureau believed Chrisman or Dahl may have made an anonymous phone call to generate publicity and potentially secure a deal with Fantasy magazine in Chicago. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, p. 119](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>))

**1947, August 14** _(cluster ×5)_ — 4:00 p.m. On 1947-08-14 at about 4:00 PM, Ray A. Switzer and his wife, while driving on Cedar Ravine Road 4 to 5 miles southeast of Placerville, California, saw a north-to-south vapor trail that resolved into a shiny metal object at roughly 500-1000 feet altitude moving very fast. They described it as more rocket-shaped than disc-shaped, about 5 feet long, tilted in a semi-circle, and said it struck the ground with a puff of black smoke on a hillside ahead of their car; a canyon prevented immediate inspection. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 190–191](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 143–144](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, p. 73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 14** — An FBI office memorandum from San Francisco forwards copies of Army Air Forces reports concerning additional alleged 'flying disc' sightings in Oregon and Arizona, noting that the inquiries were conducted by Hamilton Field personnel before the FBI issued its own instructions to investigate such reports. The memorandum itself does not describe the objects, witnesses, or circumstances of any single sighting in detail. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 14** — This FBI memorandum concerns the re-interviewing of Dahl and Chrisman in the aftermath of the Maury Island-related hoax investigation; it discusses claims that anonymous phone calls were made while witnesses believed recovered fragments were parts of a flying disc, but the document itself does not describe a direct UAP observation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 15–20** — 9:30 p.m. Major Elmer H. Hammer, Jr., intelligence officer at Rapid City Air Force Base, reported seeing about twelve brilliant yellowish-white elliptical objects over Weaver, South Dakota, between 15 and 20 Aug 1947 at 2130 MST. The formation approached from the northwest in a tight diamond pattern, descended from about 10,000 to 6,000 feet, made a wide right turn, climbed sharply, and disappeared to the southwest after roughly one minute; no sound or exhaust trail was observed and sketches were made. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 103–107](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1947, August 19** _(cluster ×3)_ — 9:30 p.m. On 19 Aug 1947 at about 9:30 PM in Twin Falls, Idaho, Mr. H. H. Hedstrom, his wife, a neighbor (Mrs. Shultz), and Detective Richard A. Frazier observed multiple luminous objects in the night sky for a few minutes under an overcast sky. The objects appeared in repeated triangular formations, first as about ten traveling rapidly northeast, then peeling off and regrouping, followed by additional groups of 3, 5/6, and finally 35 to 50 or more; later similar objects were seen returning southwest. The witnesses described the lights as glowing like electric light, seemingly internally illuminated, moving in level flight with no flames, and changing direction/course between NE and SW. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 184–185](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 139–140](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, pp. 177–179](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, August 21** — 9:15 p.m. On 21 Aug at Nogales, Arizona, a witness reported a dull orange wafer-shaped object seen at low level around 45° and high level around 90°, moving horizontally to the earth's surface before disappearing into space after about 10 seconds. The object was described as about the size of a volleyball and traveling roughly 10 times faster than jet planes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, August 25** — This FBI-linked Air Corps memorandum from Wright Field in late August 1947 does not describe an actual aerial sighting; it reports analysis of alleged 'flying saucer' specimens, concluding that several recovered articles were ordinary manufactured or reworked parts, including coils of wire, a metallic electronic filter condenser box, and a metallic magnetic ring, with no connection to AMC projects. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 165–168](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, August 25** — The report is not a direct witness sighting but an FBI/Army Air Forces inquiry into alleged flying-saucer debris found near Danforth, Illinois, in August 1947. Laboratory and Air Forces officials examined the fragments, compared notes with Mrs. Whedon, Major Hopkins, Wright Field, and Special Agent Reynolds, and concluded the material had no connection to Operation Mogul or any classified project and was most likely a hoax; the described items included plaster/ceramic fragments with resistance wire, an old-type magnetic speaker diaphragm, and bakelite coil forms. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 162–164](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, September 3** — 12:00 p.m. On 1947-09-03 at about noon near Oswego, Oregon, Mrs. Raymond Dupuis and two children saw approximately two dozen round silver objects high in the sky. She described them as platter-shaped, white-metallic, larger than a nearby cub-type airplane, grouped in an arc near the sun, and apparently stationary for 3-4 minutes; some seemed to rotate rapidly, and one detached object slowly fell until hidden by trees. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 94](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 3** _(cluster ×3)_ — 12:15 p.m. Mrs. Raymond Dufui of Oswego, Oregon reported seeing twelve to fifteen round silver objects at high altitude over Oswego at about 12:15 PM on September 3, 1947, while she was watching a local airplane overhead; the FBI noted she would be interviewed. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 151](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 112](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 92](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 6** — Mrs. G. H. Stockton reported a flying disc sighting at Shouns, Tennessee, on September 6, 1947, later confirmed by her son-in-law High Jennings. Jennings described a football-shaped object moving south at a constant altitude of about 2,000 feet, turning end-over-end, and visible for about five minutes against an overcast sky. He said it moved about as fast as a Cub plane and thought it may have been a loose meteorological balloon rather than a disc. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:30 p.m. On 8 Sept 1947 at about 10:30 p.m., Mr. and Mrs. Norman Hall reported seeing a group of flying objects over Logan, Utah, moving clockwise above the city in a formation that changed as they traveled. The objects were described as similar to moving stars, approximately star-sized, and much faster than birds. Weather was cloudy; no sound, sketches, or photographs were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 178–179](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 135](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, September 8** _(cluster ×4)_ — 10:30 p.m. In Logan, Utah, multiple civilian witnesses reported seeing groups of small flying objects on the evening of 1947-09-08, mainly around 10:30 p.m. and up to about 11:10 p.m. The Salt Lake City FBI office interviewed several parties: the Andersons and Caldwells described roughly a dozen white, illuminated objects flying in formation at a few thousand feet, circling the city in one to two minutes and seeming faster than birds; Mrs. Thunell and others described five groups of 35 to 60 small yellowish-white objects circling quickly at several thousand feet. Other newspaper-named witnesses, Keith Hughes and Joan Crookston, denied observing anything. The FBI concluded the information was indefinite and did not pursue further investigation. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 180–181](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 133–134](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 169–171](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, September 8** — 10:40 p.m. Eight different people in Logan, Utah reportedly saw three separate flights of 'flying discs' between 10:40 and 11:10 PM on Monday, September 8, 1947; the FBI Salt Lake City office noted that an investigation was being conducted based on the newspaper report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 152](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 11** — 5:15 p.m. Portland Chief of Police Leon V. Jenkins reported seeing a weather-balloon-sized object over Portland, Oregon, on 1947-09-11 at about 5:15 PM. He described it as appearing to be made of aluminum or another bright metal, moving rapidly northwest to southeast at an estimated 10,000 feet, then veering south and disappearing in about one minute. The object was also seen by Patrolman H. S. Raney and possibly other Portland police officers; a newspaper item in The Oregonian appeared the next day and an investigation was underway. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 120](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 11** — 5:21 p.m. On 1947-09-11 over Portland, Oregon, multiple police officers and the Chief of Police observed a bright silver object in the sky that was reported by District 18 and then watched by District 14 and others. Witnesses described it as round, egg-shaped, or elliptical depending on viewing angle, moving rapidly across the city, turning gradually, and disappearing to the south/southwest after about 30 seconds to 2 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 2–4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1947, September 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:15 p.m. On 12 Sept 1947, about 45 minutes after sunset, Pan American pilot Capt. Stanley S. Griffin and 2d Officer Edward B. Polhemus observed a single intense white light beyond Necker Island on the Midway Island to Oahu route. The light appeared like an approaching aircraft light but far brighter, held an estimated altitude of 9,500-10,000 ft, then abruptly veered from about 350° to 109° true, split into two reddish lights, and burned out about 30 miles ahead after roughly six seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 168–169](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 127–128](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, September 12** — At Los Alamos, New Mexico, construction worker Lee Robinson and eight coworkers reported a strange blinking aerial object over the highly restricted atomic-energy area while they were eating lunch on September 12. Robinson said it was not a balloon or conventional aircraft, estimated it at about 20,000 feet, and observed it alternately flashing bright and black for 3 minutes 40 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, September 15** — 12:25 a.m. At Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single witness reported seeing a round object about the size of a baseball appear approximately 30 degrees above the horizon. It was yellow-red in color, was visible for about 6 seconds, and then burst and appeared to disintegrate. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, September 16** — 2:30 a.m. On 16 Sep at 0230, a single witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported an orange ball-shaped object traveling horizontally from west to east at about 3,000 feet. It was described as larger than a falling star, moving faster than an airplane but slower than a falling star, and dimming before disappearing completely after about 15 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, September 17** — 3:40 p.m. At 3:40 PM on September 17, 1947, A. Courtney Parker, superintendent of schools in South Royalton, Vermont, reported seeing two noiseless objects while halfway up Rix Ledges north of Lebanon, New Hampshire. He first noticed a floating white object about the size of a tennis ball, cotton-batting in appearance, approaching rapidly from the east; closely behind it was a second object resembling a saucer, maintaining a constant angle of inclination and fixed separation. He watched them for more than a minute before losing them in the sun glare, and compared the sight to Venus and Saturn moving together at high speed near Earth. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_002, p. 100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 17** _(cluster ×3)_ — Two Army officers at Fort Richardson, Alaska, reported witnessing a fast-moving airborne object traveling due south below scattered clouds above 10,000 feet. Both described it as metallic and silver or dull metal in finish, spherical rather than saucer-shaped, with no vapor trail and no apparent spinning or reflection. One estimated it at about 2-3 feet across, the other at roughly 10 feet across, and both judged it to be moving far faster than any plane. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 200](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 157–158](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 150–152](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, September 20** — This FBI memorandum summarizes inquiries into Richard F. Shaver of Lily Lake, Illinois, whose flying-disc theories were being promoted through Amazing magazine. No specific UAP sighting by the named witnesses is described; instead, the report records that local officials had no reports of flying discs in Lily Lake and discusses Shaver's speculative explanation that discs are "space ships" from subterranean Lemurians. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_003, pp. 147–149](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_3.pdf>))

**1947, September 24** — This document is not a firsthand sighting report but a 24 Sept 1947 Air Materiel Command memorandum requesting all available information about an alleged 'Flying Saucer' sighting made by a radar station in Japan; it notes the incident was mentioned by Dr. Charles Carroll during a 5 Sept 1947 conference in Brig Gen Schulgen's office. ([18_100754_ General 1946-7_Vol_2, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/18_100754_ general 1946-7_vol_2.pdf>))

**1947, September 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:30 a.m. On 27 Sep at 0130 near Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single witness observed one dark blue object low over the horizon moving from south to north. It appeared about 20 degrees above the horizon, made a gentle arc toward the earth, looked like a skyrocket, had a tail about twice its diameter, traveled about 50 degrees in 2 seconds, and then burnt out after roughly 2 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, September 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 a.m. On 27 Sep at about 0300, a witness at Sandia Base, New Mexico, reported a single round yellow object to the north about 10 degrees above the horizon. It was described as baseball-sized at 25 yards, moving at the same rate as a shooting star and dying out after 3 to 4 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, September 30** — 10:57 p.m. On 30 Sep at 2257 near Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single witness reported a yellow-orange object traveling from south to west at about a 55-degree angle to the horizon. It was described as slightly larger than a shooting star, visible for 2-3 seconds, with no sound or other effects noted, and an estimated speed of 200-300 mph. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 2** — 9:10 p.m. On 2 Oct at 2110, a single witness reported a bright green object near Los Alamos, New Mexico at an estimated altitude of 20,000-30,000 feet. The object reportedly went up and then down, and the sighting lasted about 3 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947** — 5:45 p.m. On 6 Oct at 1745, a single witness in Mescalero, New Mexico reported a dark green round object, described as a big ball, about 15 degrees above the horizon. It descended in a slight arc, lasted about 30 seconds, and then disappeared behind hills. No sound or other effects were reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 8 or 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 8 or 9 October 1947, Thomas L. Moore, a Capt. in the USAFR and former pilot, observed a single fast-moving aerial object from the northwest outskirts of Las Vegas, Nevada. He initially thought he was seeing a sky-writing trail, but noticed no visible craft at the head of the steadily forming trail. The trail was almost white, cloud-like in density, and persisted for roughly 15 to 30 minutes before disintegrating. He estimated the object's speed at about 700 to 800 mph and described a turn radius of roughly 5 to 15 miles as it curved east to west in clear, nearly cloudless weather. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 198–199](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 155–156](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 10** — 1:07 a.m. At 0107 on 10 Oct, a single witness at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a round, bluish-green object traveling slowly from west to east at about 45 degrees above the horizon. The object was estimated to be fist-sized at arm's length, was visible for about 15 seconds, and then died out. The report is embedded in a declassified FBI UFO file. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 10** — 4:06 a.m. At 0406 on 10 Oct, a single observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a small brilliant-white object moving from southeast to northeast at an estimated 3,000 feet above the observation point and parallel to the earth's surface. It was visible for about 4-5 seconds, appeared slower than a meteor, and then disappeared. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 12** — 11:15 a.m. On 12 October at 1115, three witnesses in Roswell, New Mexico reported a white-silver round or probably elliptical object moving from south to northeast in a smooth arc at an estimated altitude of 3,500 feet. The object was observed for about 45-60 seconds, made no sound, appeared faster than jet aircraft, and continued beyond the range of vision. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 12** — 11:15 a.m. On 12 Oct at 1115 in Alamogordo, New Mexico, three witnesses reported a round, white or aluminum object that appeared from the south, veered to the northeast in a smooth arc, and faded from view after about 45-60 seconds. The object was estimated to be at 35,000 feet, roughly B-29-sized by comparison, about 4 inches in diameter as seen, and traveling around 1,500 mph. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 12** — 1:40 p.m. On 12 Oct at 13:40 near Tucson, Arizona, four witnesses reported a round white or silver object moving from northeast to southwest at about 30,000 feet. It was estimated at 50 to 100 feet in diameter, traveled horizontally at about 1,000 mph, and faded from view after roughly 15 seconds. The report is preserved in an FBI declassified case file. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 13** — 5:00 a.m. On 13 Oct 1947 between 5:00 and 5:30 a.m. in Kansas District, three corroborating witnesses reported a single strange object moving slowly west to east. It was described as a flaming luminous mass about the size of a baseball, with a black bell-like shape, flames, and an orange-tinted wake of smoke and fire; the account was reported by the Kansas Times. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 109–110](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 13** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:30 a.m. On 13 Oct 1947 at about 5:30 a.m., Judge Heaney and C. D. Bates were driving north toward Winnipegosis about 14 miles north of Dauphin, Manitoba, when they saw a single object traveling west to east in a clear pre-dawn sky. It was first about the size of a large grapefruit, grew to a large football, moved eastward at meteoric speed at roughly 10 degrees elevation, and exploded like a sky-rocket into a brilliant reddish cone with bluish light fragments; no sound or tail was observed. Later notes in the report state a scientific group concluded the object was probably a meteor. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 99–100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 13** — 5:30 a.m. On 13 October 1947 at about 05:30 near the highway between Grosse Isle and Warren, Manitoba, Dr. W. R. Giesbrecht and F. W. Sawatsky observed a single green object crossing the sky ahead of them. They estimated it to be about two miles distant and roughly 1,000 feet up, with the size and shape of a soccer ball. It was silent, appeared to trail green sparks, and seemed to dip toward the ground before going out and disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 101–102](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 13** — 5:30 a.m. On 13 October 1947 at about 0530 near Passois Lake, Canada, witness Wilburt Speers, an apprentice undertaker from Dauphin, Manitoba, observed a single round orange-yellow object moving rapidly south of west at an estimated altitude of about 8000 ft. for 30-40 seconds in clear weather with little wind. He reported no sound, a flame or tail with sparks at the rear, and said it seemed to disappear rather than explode. A companion, Robert Smith, was reportedly with Speers, and the report notes the object was not considered a flare, rocket, comet, or falling star. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 103–104](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 13** — On 13 Oct 1947 in Saskatchewan District, Alden Frederickson, while duck hunting, reported seeing a reddish object in the eastern sky moving toward him and apparently growing larger. The object changed to a greenish color and then broke up, with a trail of light following it. Two others, Don Adams and Charlie Irving, also reportedly saw the strange light. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 107–108](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 14** _(cluster ×2)_ — 12:00 p.m. On 14 October 1947 at about noon, mine owner and pilot J. L. Clark reported with his partner Mr. Anderson a single aerial object seen from a mine about 11 miles north-northeast of Cave Creek, Arizona. The object moved in a straight line northwest to southeast at an estimated 350 mph, at an apparent altitude of about 8,000 feet and at roughly a 25-degree viewing angle, remaining visible for 45 seconds to 1 minute. It made no noise and appeared about 3 feet across, resembling a flying wing with no tail visible; its color seemed reddish against blue sky and black against white cloud background. The report noted no change in elevation or direction and no sketches or photographs. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 114–115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 84–85](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 14** — 2:10 p.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, on 14 October at about 1410-1415, a single witness reported a green-and-silver object with a pale blue appearance moving south to north. The object was described as about 1/8 the size of a full moon, with no sound, visible for 3-4 seconds, and estimated at 500 mph or more. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:00 a.m. On 20 Oct 1947 at about 1100 hours near Xenia, Ohio, Ruth Atkinson reported seeing a single silver, round object about 12 inches in diameter at an estimated altitude of 1000 to 1500 feet. It moved fast on a straight southwest course, made no sound, and appeared to lack propellers or a dome. The report notes two witnesses. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 48–50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:20 p.m. On 20 Oct 1947 at 13:20, B. C. Britton reported seeing two cigar-shaped objects over David Road in Dayton, Ohio, flying very fast from west to east slightly northward in a straight, level course. The objects appeared high, reflected sunlight brightly, emitted no sound, and left a faint steam-like exhaust trace that vanished immediately; they were seen one behind the other about a city block apart. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 45–49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 38–39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, October 21** — 9:30 p.m. On 21 Oct at 21:30 near Roswell, New Mexico, a single witness reported one very bright white unknown object moving from southeast to northwest at about 30 degrees above the horizon, visible for roughly 20-30 minutes; no sound, size, shape, or other details were recorded. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October 22** — 2:28 a.m. On 22 October at 0228 hours near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single railroad witness reported a bright green object traveling northwest to southeast on the horizon. It was seen for about 2 seconds, estimated at four times the size of a flare and moving about 150 mph before disappearing behind a hill. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, October** — Around October 1947 in San Francisco's Golden Gate Park, John E. Pruitt, a student at the Maj Rockwell School of Aviation, reported seeing a mysterious light that illuminated the ocean and felt like an electric arc; he described it as seeming very near and fading away, with no time, size, shape, color, or motion details recorded. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 18–19](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1947, November 1** — FBI Albany forwarded a report about A. Courtney Parker of South Royalton, Vermont, who said he saw 'flying discs' and referred investigators to a letter he had already sent to the Bureau on September 17, 1947. Parker stated he believed he saw them because of his unusually good eyesight, and he thought they may have been beyond normal visual range. He told the FBI no one else in South Royalton, to his knowledge, saw the discs. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1947, November 2** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:30 a.m. On 2 November 1947, just before daybreak at their home on Anderson Road in Houston, Texas, Mr. and Mrs. Elmer L. Brimberry saw a single round fireball-like object with a bright light and a tail, descending earthward and spinning before it suddenly went out just before reaching the ground in an open vacant field adjacent to their residence. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 250–251](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 205–206](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, November 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 12 Nov 1947, early in the day, Second Officer Claude Lee Williamson reported from the tanker Ticonderoga at sea about 20 miles offshore and 40 miles south of Cape Blanco, Oregon, seeing two ball-shaped flying discs trailing jet-like streams of fire. The objects came from the north horizon, traveled at an estimated 700 to 900 MPH, and curved westward in a long, low arc before disappearing after roughly 45 seconds. A second witness, Ordinary Seaman James S. Lynde, also said he saw the discs. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 111–112](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 81–82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, November 23** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:00 p.m. At about 2200 hours on 23 November in Vaughn, New Mexico, a single reliable witness reported a round, bright-white object larger than a basketball descending slowly in a vertical manner from an estimated altitude of 400 to 500 feet. No sound was heard, and the object was observed to explode. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, November 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:49 p.m. On 27 Nov at 17:49 near Socorro, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round, quite large object moving from east to west at an estimated 10° to 40° above the horizon. It appeared pale green to pale blue, was seen for about 5 seconds, moved slower than a meteor, and faded out gradually with no sound or other noted effects. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, November 27** — 6:00 p.m. On 27 Nov at about 1800 in McIntosh, New Mexico, a single witness reported a vertical object descending from less than 2,000 feet; it was green, shaped like a flare, and described as the same as a signal flare or falling signal flare, with the sighting lasting about one second and no sound or other effects noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, November 3 or 4** — Colonel Hayes reported three nighttime observations near Vaughn, New Mexico in 1947-1948 of a bright ball or globe of light descending vertically, then bursting or disintegrating silently into red sparks before reaching the ground; in the earlier 1947 event he stopped his car on Highway 60 and watched fragments fall beyond a fence, while the two 1948 sightings occurred while he was driving east and west of Vaughn under clear skies with no aircraft seen and no sound heard. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 123](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1947, December 5** — 7:30 p.m. On 5 December at about 19:30-19:45, three witnesses in Carrizozo, New Mexico reported a blue-green teardrop-shaped object seen about 40 degrees above the horizon, descending in a dive, moving very slowly, and then disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 5** — 9:33 p.m. On 5 Dec at 2133, two reliable observers reported a whitish-orange object over Las Vegas, New Mexico, moving from west to east slightly above 9,000 feet on a horizontal, slightly descending course before disappearing after a few seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 5** — 10:40 p.m. On 5 December at 22:40 near Tularosa, New Mexico, a single witness reported a streak of light moving from east to west in a smooth downward arc. The light was described as blue with a yellowish-red tail, lasting about one second and appearing slightly longer than a pencil held at arm's length. The report says it appeared to hit the ground near Tularosa. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 6** — 10:55 p.m. At 2255 on 6 Dec, a single unknown observer at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a green, round object traveling E to W with a slight falling arch and leaving a train or trail. The object was seen for about 2-3 seconds, made no sound, was estimated at about one-third the apparent diameter of the moon, moved rapidly, and then vanished. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 9** — 1:30 p.m. On 9 Dec at 1330 in Farmington, New Mexico, a single witness reported an unknown object seen dropping vertically from about 500 ft and moving straight down; it was described as resembling a charred parachute or cargo net and then disappeared. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:05 p.m. On 12 Dec 1947 at about 22:05-22:08 in Oslo, Norway, the daughter of MA Kai E. Rasmussen observed a single meteor-like object from the ground for 2-3 seconds. It was intensely white with a greenish-yellow tail, appeared to travel fairly close to the ground, moved from SSE-SSW toward NNW in an arcing, highly curved path, and disappeared when trees obscured the view. No sound, odor, or cloud effects were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 99](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 92](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1947, December 20** — 8:54 p.m. On 20 Dec at 20:54, four reliable observers reported a pale green or bluish-white ball moving from west to east over Los Alamos, New Mexico at great height and descending at a 20-degree angle to the horizon. The object was seen with a train or trail, had the apparent size of a basketball, traveled at high speed for about 1.5 seconds, made no sound, and disappeared behind a mountain. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 28** — 4:31 a.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, one observer reported a white, star-like object descending from high altitude to about 6,000 feet in a vertical path toward the south, seen for only several seconds and ending in a greenish flash. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947, December 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:25 p.m. On 30 Dec 1947 at about 19:25 PST, a motorist on U.S. Highway 40 roughly 30 miles west of Lovelock, Nevada reported seeing a single object at an estimated altitude of 1200 to 1300 feet above ground; the object was later reported to have exploded in the air. The Fairfield Control Tower relayed the report from Hamilton Field Flight Control and noted the sighting may be connected with nearby Incidents 94, 95, and 97. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 246–247](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 201–202](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, December 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:25 p.m. On 30 Dec 1947 at 1925 PST, a C-47 crew from Sacramento Air Depot #7488 reported a single object near Rosedale, California, flying at low altitude and very high speed. The object trailed several colors of flame, with red and green predominating, and disappeared eastward toward hills. A tower follow-up later described a small fire at roughly 38°50' N, 120°53' W that grew larger, burned in a triangular shape, and showed bright blue-green flames at two points. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 242–245](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 199–200](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, December 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:25 p.m. On 30 Dec 1947 at 1925 PST, the pilot of United Airlines Flight 191 reported seeing a single flash while flying between Medford, Oregon, and Mt. Shasta, California, just north of the aircraft's position; it was presumed to be an explosion. The report contains no detailed description of the object beyond the flash. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 248–249](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 203–204](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, December 30** — 7:26 p.m. On 30 Dec 1947 at 19:26 PST near 42 ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 242–243](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1947, December 30** — 7:26 p.m. On 30 Dec 1947 at 19:26 PST, two USAF pilots, Lt Col W.W. Jones and Maj A. A. Andrae, observed from a plane at 13,000 ft a single unidentifiable object at about the 2:30 position, descending vertically at very high speed. The object was seen for roughly 2 seconds while en route from Great Falls, Montana, to Fairfield-Suisun AFB and was described as trailing intensely bright green and blue flames and seeming to slow slightly near the ground. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 197–198](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1947** — 5:00 a.m. Multiple witnesses in Cincinnati reported orange-red, silent, disc-shaped objects around 300 feet above the city; the report describes a wave of similar sightings, often in formations of three, and includes a separate Sunday sighting of three discs by a neighbor of Leonard H. Stringfield. The source frames the reports as part of a broader international 'flying saucer' wave, with Stringfield suggesting a possible magnetic fault line explanation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))
