**1947** — 8:00 p.m. In the latter part of 1947, a lone reliable witness in Vaughn, New Mexico reported a bright white object seen around 20:00 at an estimated altitude of 400 to 500 feet. The object was described as larger than a basketball, descending slowly in a vertical manner, and there was no train or trail. The report indicates sound was present and the object was said to explode. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947** — At Holloman Air Base near Alamogordo, New Mexico, during the flying-saucer scare, an astronomer studying solar phenomena first mistook weather balloons for saucers in the morning, then that evening observed two hazy bluish disk-like objects near the rising moon. The objects could not be brought into clear focus, appeared elevated like 'flying' lights, and faded away after the witness and driver stopped the car; the witness reported the sighting to the Air Force but offered only hypotheses, not a definitive explanation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 256–257](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1947** — Rev. Joseph Brasky of St. Joseph's Church in Grafton, Wisconsin, heard a swishing and whirring noise followed by a thud and mild explosion, then investigated and found a warm sheet-metal disc about 18 inches across with a central hole containing gadgets and wires. He notified the FBI, and an FBI officer said an official report might be at the office, but the object was not immediately identified. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 117](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1948, January 1** — 1:25 a.m. On 1 Jan 1948 at about 1:25 A.M. and again at 1:30 A.M., Mr. A. Schroeder observed a bright blue-green, bell-shaped flare-like glow low on the horizon due west of Abilene, Texas. The object appeared stationary, produced no sound, showed no apparent speed or flight, and was seen for about 2 seconds each time in a clear sky, with a fan-shaped glow surrounding it. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 135–136](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, January 3** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 3 Jan 1948, a press-reported sighting near Vaasa, Finland described a brightly shining object with a long tail moving west to east and visible for about 30 seconds. The report was relayed through the U.S. Military Attache in Helsinki from Helsinki press coverage dated 10 Jan 1948. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 252](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 207](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 5** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 5 January 1948, a shining object was reported near Pietarsaari, Finland, and attributed in the official source to information from the Military Attache in Helsinki. The object was seen for about 10 seconds moving from north to south; eyewitnesses reported flames being ejected and grey streaks left in the sky. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 253](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, p. 208](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 6** — 3:10 a.m. At 0310 on 6 Jan, a single reliable observer in the Los Alamos, New Mexico area saw a brilliant green object moving horizontally from east to west at high speed, appearing 3 to 5 degrees from the observer, for about 2 seconds before it disappeared behind the mountainous horizon. No sound or trail was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:10 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at 1310 in Madisonville, Kentucky, an unidentified object was reported by a Madisonville observer using a Finch telescope and described as cone-shaped, estimated 100 ft tall and 43 ft across at about 4 miles altitude, moving southwest at about 10 mph. No sound, exhaust, or other construction details were reported. The file also notes a Kentucky State Police relay of an unusual circular object seen by unknown persons, estimated at 250-300 ft and moving westward, and cross-references related incidents in the broader July 1948 traffic of reports. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 95](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 79–80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:20 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at about 1320 CST, PFC Stanley Oliver, duty in the control tower at Godman Field, Ft. Knox, Kentucky, observed a single unidentified object southwest of the field at over 15,000 feet. The object was described as resembling an ice-cream cone topped with red, apparently white with red above, and it was reported to have gone behind clouds; its motion could not be determined. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 85–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 69–70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:45 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 between about 13:45 and 13:50, T/Sgt Quinton A. Blackwell, the Chief Operator in the Godman Field control tower at Fort Knox, Kentucky, reported a single huge silvery/metallic object over the south side of Godman Field climbing north to south at apparently greater speed than a P-51 and continuing upward above 15,000 ft. The object was first relayed to him by military and state police reports as a circular craft 250-300 ft across seen over nearby Kentucky towns, and later multiple officers and four P-51s attempted to identify it; the flight leader reported it directly ahead and above, still climbing and appearing metallic and tremendous in size. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 81–82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 65–66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — 2:00 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 shortly after 1400 CST at Godman Field, Ft. Knox, Kentucky, Lt. Paul I. Orner observed from the control tower a small white object in the southwest sky that appeared stationary, partially parachute-shaped and round, with bright reflected sunlight and a faint red light around its lower part. Military Police had first reported the unknown object near Ft. Knox; it was later reportedly seen over Marysville, Irvington, and Owensboro. Three P-51s were sent to pursue it, one pilot reporting it at '12 o'clock' traveling about half his speed before that aircraft vanished from radio contact. A later sighting by Orner around 1735 CST described a round bright light at azimuth 240° and 8° elevation that remained visible through haze until it set below the horizon, and was tracked with a weather-station theodolite. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 67–68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:07 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 after 14:07 at Godman Field, Kentucky, Capt. Cary W. Carter, Operations Officer, observed from the control tower a single round white object, sometimes appearing cone-shaped, very high in altitude and visible through cirrus clouds for about 3 to 4 minutes. He later summoned senior officers and arranged for nearby P-51 aircraft to intercept; one aircraft piloted by Mantell pursued the object while radio relays reported increasing speed and climb, after which Mantell blacked out and crashed. The report notes the object was whiter than the clouds and that some later explanations suggested Venus or a comet, though the case file itself records the sighting as unidentified. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 89–90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 73–74](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:20 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at Godman Field, Kentucky, Capt. J. F. Duesler Jr. observed a bright silver unidentified aerial object from the control tower beginning about 1420 CST. Additional military witnesses and Col. Hix joined the observation, and a flight of four P-51s was vectored toward the object; the lead pilot reported it was bright, climbing away, and later estimated at the same speed as his aircraft, about 360 mph. The object was seen for over an hour, later appearing after dark from the Godman area at about 234° and moving west before descending, with a round/fluid form, no tail, and color changes from white to blue to red to yellow with a black center spot before it was obscured by clouds. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 87–88](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 71–72](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — 2:45 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at 1445, Col. Guy G. Hix observed a single white, round object from the control tower at Godman Field, Kentucky, after multiple earlier reports placed it south of the base. Seen through 8-power binoculars and aligned with the sighting bar, it appeared about one-quarter the size of a full moon, near the sun, and seemingly stationary for roughly 1.5 hours before disappearing behind a cloud; three P-51 aircraft were reported pursuing it on a 215° course. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 75–76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — 3:15 p.m. On 7 January 1948 near Godman Field, Kentucky, Capt. Thomas F. Mantell (NG 869), a Flight Leader, investigated a single metallic object reported by Godman Field Tower. Radio traffic described the object as directly ahead of and above the aircraft, moving about half the aircraft's speed, at a tremendous size and metallic appearance. Estimates in the checklist placed it above 15,000 ft and later at 20,000 ft, with an approximate speed of 500 mph and a bearing of about 210° from Godman Field. The encounter ended when Mantell ceased radio contact; he later crashed and was killed after the chase. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 77–78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:15 p.m. On 7 January 1948, tower operator Mr. Boudreaux at Lockbourne AAB in Columbus, Ohio watched a single glowing object for about 15 minutes from the tower. Initially resembling a star against an overcast sky, it was seen over the Lockbourne Power Plant southwest of the base and moved southwest while bobbing up and down. The object appeared white to amber, then changed from amber to red before going out. Through field glasses it was described as enormous, with a cone-shaped streak to the right and bluish streaks like a jet effect extending from the right side. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 72–75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 58–59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:20 p.m. On 1948-01-07, between 7:20 and 7:55 P.M., multiple control tower operators and alert crew members at Clinton County Air Base in Wilmington, Ohio, reported a single red cone-shaped aerial phenomenon with a gaseous green mist trail. It was seen in the southwest sky for about 35 minutes, intermittently hanging suspended, then gaining and losing altitude in bursts of terrific speed before disappearing over the horizon beneath heavy cloud cover. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 134–135](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 96–97](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:20 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at 1920 EST, Corporal James H. Hudson observed a single triangular object from the control tower at Clinton County Army Air Field, Wilmington, Ohio. Seen at an estimated 15 to 20 miles for about 35 minutes, it appeared white at first, then red, then white changing to red and green, with a cone or upside-down triangle appearance and a green mist-like trail when ascending and descending. It hovered at spots, moved with great speed on a southwest heading, changed orientation on climb/descent, and finally disappeared to the southwest around 1955 EST with no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 138](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 100–101](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:20 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 between 19:20 and 19:50 at Clinton County Army Air Field, Wilmington, Ohio, witness Mr. Ralph L. Stirr observed a single fire-colored light/object apparently near 4,000 ft, moving very slowly westward and fluctuating in altitude like a parachute flare. It looked like a bright flame-colored flare or aircraft in trouble, remained visible through scattered clouds, left no trail, and disappeared over the western horizon. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 104–105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:25 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at about 19:25, I/Sgt LeRoy Ziegler observed a single very large, starlike circular object over Clinton County Air Field/Base near Wilmington, Ohio. The object was high in the cloudy sky, appeared brighter than a star and sometimes shifted to a reddish hue, hovered stationary for a time, then made rapid vertical and lateral movements with a faint exhaust trail before accelerating southwest and disappearing over the horizon around 20:00. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 136](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 98–99](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — 7:25 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at 19:25 EST near Lockbourne/Commercial Point, Ohio, VHF/DF operator Albert R. Pickering observed a single amber round-or-oval object, larger than a C-47, about 3-5 miles away and around 10,000 ft altitude. It hovered, vanished briefly into overcast, then reappeared to execute repeated circular maneuvers and vertical climbs/descents, leaving an amber trail about five times its length before leveling off and disappearing into the clouds heading east. The object was visible for about 20 minutes and produced no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 70–73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — 7:25 p.m. On 7 January 1948 at 1925 EST, Capt. Charles E. McGee observed a very bright white oval light southwest of Lockbourne Army Air Base near Columbus, Ohio. From runway 23 overhead approach, the object appeared about the size of a floodlight, gave no noise, flashed on and off, shifted from white to yellowish-orange, and seemed to fade, descend, and move westward in a hovering manner at roughly 3000 ft. The witness reported solid overcast conditions and stated the object did not resemble a celestial body. ([38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 56–57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:30 p.m. On 7 Jan 1948 at about 19:30, S/Sgt John P. Haag observed from the Clinton County AAF control tower near Wilmington, Ohio a single bright object southwest of the base, initially resembling the wing of an aircraft on fire. Over roughly 25 minutes it appeared to gain and lose altitude rapidly with little or no forward motion, changed from red to green and back again, at times passed behind overcast while its light remained visible through the cloud layer, then moved away on heading 210° and disappeared over the horizon around 19:55. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 140–141](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 102–103](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:40 p.m. On 7 January 1948 around 19:40, Mr. Eisele, a DF Operator in the Lockbourne Control Tower at Lockbourne AAB near Columbus, Ohio, observed a single circular object low in the western sky under high overcast. It first appeared as a ruddy red light that shifted to amber-yellow at 1-2 second intervals, seemed larger and brighter than any star, and was compared to a runway light seen from 500 ft. The object was estimated at about 5 miles away and around 5,000 ft altitude, though reports from Godman Field, Clinton County Airport, and a pilot over Columbus suggested a similar phenomenon farther away. It remained motionless for about 10 minutes, then moved in an elliptical counter-clockwise path, descending to the horizon, hovering briefly, returning to its original position, and finally fading and lowering into the horizon at 19:55. No sound, odor, or clear structure was noted; a thin tail-like wisp extended from the object. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 74–77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 60–61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:53 p.m. On 7 January 1948 at 19:53, Lt. C. W. Thomas, a pilot on a cross-country flight from Dayton to Washington near Columbus, observed a single large amber light to the west while flying at about 3000 feet. He and Lt. Sims reported the object to Columbus Airways, which relayed the sighting to Lockbourne. The object appeared stationary, with no maneuvers, sound, exhaust trail, or visible construction, and was described as looking like a large star or planet about 15 miles away in dark overcast conditions. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 78–80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 63–64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — 10:15 p.m. Two observers reported a round, ball-shaped object over Corona, New Mexico on 7 January at 2215, moving from southwest to southeast while descending behind a mountain range after about 10 seconds. The object was described as yellowish white, orange, blue and green, with a train or trail, no sound, and an apparent size comparable to a cup held at arm's length. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 7** — On 1948-01-07 near Fort Knox, Kentucky, an unidentified flying disc was sighted and intercepted by four fighter planes; Capt. Thomas F. Mantell closed to near the object, radioed that it looked metallic and very large and that he was climbing after it at about 360 mph, then crashed and died minutes later. The report is embedded in a 1950 press account summarizing a Wright-Patterson Air Force investigation that listed many domestic and foreign flying-disc reports, some explained conventionally and others left unresolved. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 150](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, January 9** — 10:25 p.m. On 9 Jan at 22:25, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a greenish-white round object high above the horizon, moving in a straight line with no sound or trail for about 3 seconds before it disappeared behind the horizon. The object was described as small in apparent size, comparable to -4 to -5 relative to Jupiter, and traveling rapidly. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:26 p.m. On 9 Jan at 22:26, a single reliable observer near Los Alamos, New Mexico, reported an incandescent green oval with a trail, seen at an estimated 80° to 40° above the horizon on a horizontal course. The object was observed for about 2 seconds, made no sound, and disappeared behind trees. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 49–51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:30 p.m. On 9 January 1948 at about 23:30, pilot Hugh DuBose observed a single light sky-blue circular object from an aircraft near Cartersville, Georgia, moving on a 175° magnetic course at an estimated 400 mph and around 3,000 feet altitude. As it crossed his path he saw only an intense blue flame, circular with a flat top quarter and pot-shaped appearance; it then sputtered, turned earthward, and extinguished before any impact was seen. A second witness, purser Arthur Porter, also saw the flame only. The report notes the object did not resemble a wingless missile's curved trajectory and DuBose later compared it to a 'Buzz Bomb.' ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 72–73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 70–71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, January 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 9 Jan 1948, Mr. and Mrs. Markham reportedly saw a pencil-like object flying very high around Danville, Kentucky, while driving in their car. The object moved west in a straight course and left a long trail of smoke or exhaust, which shortly afterward exploded into a beautiful display of sparks. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 144](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 106–107](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:00 p.m. On 1948-01-10 at about 10:00 PM EST in Wildwood, New Jersey, Mrs. Ethel Evans reported a single lamplight-sized ovular/teardrop object of yellow, red, or amber color moving shoreward at a slow speed in clear night conditions. The object was visible for only 5-8 seconds, descended slowly, split into two pieces, and then fell into the sea. The file later adds multiple subsequent alleged 'saucer' sightings by Mrs. Evans in 1948, but the primary checklist incident is the Wildwood ocean fall. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 146–147](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 108–109](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:00 p.m. On 10 Jan 1948 at about 10:00 PM EST in Wildwood, New Jersey, Mrs. Krueger, a housewife, and other nearby witnesses observed two small orange lights/disc-like objects at about 500 feet over her Pine Avenue residence. The objects reportedly floated slowly across the horizon, separated from one another, descended vertically in a slow steady manner, and then disappeared below the housetops and into the sea after only 5-8 seconds of visibility. Witnesses reported no sound, no smoke, and no exhaust trails; they described the lights as distinct, oval-to-round, and resembling an old-fashioned gas or lamplight in yellow-red-amber tones. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 148–149](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 110–111](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 11** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:30 p.m. On 11 Jan 1948 at 1630 in Hartford, Connecticut, two USAF Air Transport Command captains, Kenneth M. Helton and James J. Pargoe, observed a single disc-like object resembling a shooting star with a bluish center and red edges. It was seen shooting toward the earth at about a 45-degree angle, making no sound and leaving no tail or trail, then dropped out of sight north of Mt. Tom near Holyoke, Massachusetts. ([DOW-UAP-D087, p. 240](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>); [38_143685_box7_Incident_Summaries_1-100, pp. 195–196](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, January 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:00 p.m. On 12 Jan at about 1900 near Holloman, New Mexico, a witness saw a star-like object to the west that changed altitude erratically and changed color from white to green and red. The object was about the apparent size of Venus and was watched for about five minutes before the observer discontinued watching. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 13** — 6:05 a.m. At 0605 on 13 January, three observers near Holloman AFB, New Mexico, reported a star-like white object about the apparent size of Venus moving from east to west while erratically rising and falling. The object showed no trail or sound, changed color from white to green and red, and disappeared with daylight after a short observation. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 19** _(cluster ×3)_ — 7:30 a.m. On 19 Jan 1948 at about 7:30 AM, an observer of unknown background reported three rocket-like objects over North Jutland near Hjerrin, the Scaw, and Hjerup. The objects were seen flying east to west; they were described as ball- or saucer-shaped with a green light tail, and their motion was said to be rocket-like. The objects allegedly suddenly stopped, exploded, and disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 145–146](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 126](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, January 27** — 5:15 p.m. A single observer reported a white streak near Scullville, New Jersey, on 27 January at 1715, seen for about 30 minutes while ascending at roughly a 60-degree angle toward the northwest before fading gradually. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 29** — In a January 29, 1948 letter from Covington, Ohio, N. J. Marshall speculated to the FBI that the 'flying discs' were likely self-propelled bombs or tracer projectiles, not an ordinary UFO; his letter references a circular/elliptical motion and claims the discs seen in Texas and elsewhere may have been test devices dropped into the Gulf or Lake Erie. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 29–33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, January 30** — 5:54 p.m. On 30 Jan at 1754, approximately 200 observers in the El Paso, Texas area reported a green ball-shaped UAP moving northwest to southeast at an estimated 3°-5° above the horizon on a horizontal course; the object made no sound and then broke into pieces. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 30** — 6:00 p.m. At about 1800 on 30 January near Alamogordo, New Mexico, roughly 200 observers reported a green ball-shaped UAP moving from north to south at an angle of elevation of 15° to 30° and making a gentle descent before seeming to fizzle out after 3 to 15 seconds; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, January 30** — 6:54 p.m. On 30 Jan at about 1854-1900, ten observers in Ft. Worth, Texas reported a green trailing-sparks phenomenon moving from north to south at about 13° above the horizon and descending about 30° from the horizon before disintegrating after 1 to 7 seconds; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — Around 7 Feb 1948, three men field laborers working in Gadbjerg near Vejle, Denmark, reported a single shining object moving at high speed across the sky from southeast to northwest at an estimated altitude of about 200 meters. It remained visible for roughly three seconds. No verification was received by the investigator. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 70–71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 68–69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 14** — 6:40 p.m. On 14 February at 18:40, two unidentified observers reported a brilliant white object slightly green in color near Canado, New Mexico. It appeared somewhat above the horizon, was stationary at first, then fell away in a slight curve to the west. A train or trail was noted and no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 16** — 1:00 a.m. On 16 Feb 1948, around 0100 hours, James Amaral, a former Air Force pilot, was driving between Parrott, Georgia and Richland, Georgia when he saw a brilliant blue-white streak of light in apparent level flight on a heading of about 250 degrees. The object was seen for 5 to 10 seconds at an estimated altitude of 20,000 to 35,000 feet, moving at roughly 3,600 mph. After it disappeared, a several-mile-long trail of sparks remained visible for about 45 seconds to 1 minute. Amaral described it as rocket-like and said it did not seem celestial. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 138–139](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 17** — Two separate aerial incidents, on 17 February and 27 March, were reported from the Albuquerque and Tucumcari-Clevis areas of New Mexico. In both cases observers described a red/orange/pink/amber elongated, apparently solid but flexible, self-luminous object that maneuvered with climbs, dives, and turning motions before disappearing in the distance. No sound was heard, and investigators found no evidence of a jet-propelled craft; witness credibility was assessed as above average. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 81](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 17** — On 17 Feb in Albuquerque, New Mexico, approximately 100 observers reported a round object that shifted to an ellipse, brilliant white changing to peach in color, traveling west to east and disappearing after about 6 minutes with no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 18** — 5:10 a.m. On 18 Feb at 0510 near Holloman AFB, New Mexico, five reliable observers watched a round-to-cone-shaped white-and-orange object for 1 hour 44 minutes. The object was seen to climb, had no train or trail, made no sound, and the observers stopped watching rather than report a disappearance or resolution. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 18** _(cluster ×4)_ — 3:00 p.m. On 18 February 1948 at about 1500 MST, two B-29 aircraft crew members observed a huge multi-colored ball of fire with a dense smoke cloud and vapor trail while flying near Green River, Utah, reportedly about 100 miles away and at roughly 20,000 feet. The object was described as moving at very high speed, heading southeast of Limon, Colorado. No sound could be determined because of B-29 engine noise, and no photographs or sketches were taken. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 2–16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 17–18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 18** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:01 p.m. On 18 February 1948 near Norcatur, Kansas, multiple witnesses described a bright sky event that looked at times like a rocket or fireball, leaving a bluish-white smoke trail, followed by a large mushroom cloud, one big explosion, and several smaller explosions. The incident was later argued by some investigators to be a meteorite fall, with recovered fragments and a large stone reportedly found in a field, though several witnesses also gave rocket-like descriptions and one witness claimed a close low-level pass over a farmyard before takeoff. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 1–14](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 3–16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 20** — 5:30 a.m. On 20 Feb at 0530, two unknown observers at Holloman AFB, New Mexico, reported a stationary white round object seen against a cloud-covered sky at about 1,000 feet above the 9,000-foot mountains. The object appeared about the size of a half-dollar at arm's length, made no sound and left no trail, remained visible for about 5 minutes, and then disappeared from view behind a cloud. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:00 a.m. On 20 Feb 1948 at about 0700 hours in Asuncion, Paraguay, Mrs. Agustina Vargas de Paula reported seeing a single oval object moving from west to east. She described it as having a bulky forward section, a very thin tail about 3 feet long, and a highly colored wake; the interior gave off green, yellow, and orange colors. No sketch or photograph was obtained. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 62–65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 63–64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:00 a.m. On 20 Feb 1948 at about 07:00, a rancher and laborers near Villa Hayes, about 10 miles NNE of Asuncion in Paraguay, observed a single disc-shaped object over a ranch house in the Chaco. It was estimated at roughly 1,000 ft altitude, moved west to east at a speed less than that of a plane, and reportedly gave off an intense green color with a green aureola along its edges. No sound, duration, or other physical effects were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 68–69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 66–67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 20** — 7:00 a.m. On 20 Feb 1948 at 0700 in Aregua, about 10 miles east of Asuncion, a Bank of Paraguay employee reported an oval object crossing the sky from west to east. The report notes that many other people saw it as well; the witness judged it to be a meteor, and later residents east of Aregua claimed to have seen a star fall into hills. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 64–67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 20** — 7:00 a.m. On 20 Feb 1948 at 0700, an unidentified witness withheld by name and employed by the Bank of Paraguay reported seeing a single oval object in Aregua, about 10 miles east of Asuncion. The object moved from west to east; no distance, duration, altitude, speed, sound, or other physical details were recorded, and no sketches or photographs were provided. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 20–26** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:30 p.m. A restricted Department of War checklist records multiple unidentified flying objects seen in Norway, Denmark, and Sweden during 20-26 Feb 1948, usually around 2130 hours. The reports describe objects coming from the direction of Peenemunde, flying at altitudes from tree-top level up to 20,000 feet, at an estimated speed of one to two miles per second, with green tails and green exhaust trails. The form, size, sound, and other particulars were largely not specified. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 93](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, February 24** — 1:15 p.m. On 24 Feb at 1315, a single reliable observer reported a silvery, saucer-shaped object over the Los Alamos, New Mexico area moving erratically generally northeast at an estimated altitude of 20,000-30,000 feet. The object was seen for about 15 minutes, made no sound and left no trail; it was judged to be about 100 feet across at that altitude and moved as fast as or faster than sound before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:45 p.m. Single observer at Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a white object at 25,000-30,000 feet that flashed like a mirror in the sun, traveling very fast east then turning west, before going straight up out of sight after about 20 minutes. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 24** — 1:55 p.m. Single witness reported a white, round object over Albuquerque, New Mexico on 24 Feb at 1355, seen traveling to the west or southwest at an estimated 20° to 23° above the horizon in straight flight for about 1.5 minutes before disappearing. The object left no train or sound and was described as about the apparent size of the upper dark portion of the moon as it rises in the east. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 24** — 7:30 p.m. A single reliable witness reported a round white object over Datil, New Mexico, on 24 Feb at about 1930. It was observed for 2 hours 30 minutes moving toward the northwest at an estimated rate of 1 degree per 2 minutes, with a train or trail and no sound. During the sighting the object changed color from white to red and green and then disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 25** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:00 a.m. On 25 Feb at about 0200 near Datil, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round object seen toward the northwest that appeared white and changed to red and green before disappearing behind a mountain. The object was observed for about 30 minutes and was described as moving at roughly 1 degree per 2 minutes. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 54–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1948, February 25** — 3:45 p.m. On 25 Feb at about 1545-1555, 14 reliable observers in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a round silver UAP moving NE to SW overhead at an estimated 4 to 10 miles altitude. It was seen for a few seconds to two minutes, fluttering as it traveled very fast, and then disappeared from view without sound or trail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 54–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 25** — 3:50 p.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported a metallic unidentified object seen at about 12,000 feet moving S or SW. It was about as large as or larger than an average plane, observed for only a few seconds, made no sound, left no trail, and faded from view. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 53–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 25** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:55 p.m. On 25 Feb at 16:55 in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a shiny silvery round object moving slowly from east to west for about 10-15 seconds before disappearing behind a tree. The object was described as about the size of a B-25 fuselage, with no sound or other effects noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 55–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, February 25** — 9:15 p.m. On 25 Feb at 2115, a single unknown observer in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a tear-drop-shaped object traveling toward the south at about 20 degrees above the horizon on an almost vertical course. It appeared bright greenish white, had a train or trail, lasted about 1.5 seconds, made no sound, was estimated at about three times the size of a shooting star, moved slightly slower than a falling star, and seemed to burn out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 53–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 1** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:30 a.m. Around 1 Mar 1948 at about 09:30, a Norwegian ONL pilot and his co-pilot, flying en route from Aalborg, Denmark to Oslo, Norway, observed a single projectile-like object over the Swedish coast at roughly 20,000 ft. It was seen for about four seconds, appeared to follow the earth's surface southeastward, passed along the coast and disappeared over Norway without leaving a trail, while emitting a bluish-green flame. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 145–148](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 129–130](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 2** — 12:10 a.m. At about 0010 on 2 March, a single reflexive observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a very fast light moving from north to south at low altitude in the sky. The object was seen for about 2 seconds and then disappeared behind trees; no sound or trail was noted. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 3** — 1:59 a.m. At 0159 on 3 March, a single reflexive witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico, reported a bright green UAP descending straight down before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 5** _(cluster ×3)_ — 4:10 p.m. On 5 March 1948, George L. Buchner reported seeing two objects from the ground in Bakersfield, California, looking southwest toward Buena Vista Lake. The objects, about 10-12 miles away and seen between 1610 and 1655, were described as similar to falling aircraft, seemingly on fire, with black and red smoke and brownish-white debris trailing as they moved earthward before disappearing behind a water tower. Later Air Force investigation material also noted related Bakersfield sightings and suggested the activity could have been marker flares. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 24–27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>); [18_6369445_General_1948_Vol_1, pp. 15–16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/18_6369445_general_1948_vol_1.pdf>))

**1948, March 6** — 8:45 p.m. At 2045 on 6 March, a single unknown observer reported a round-headed light-colored object with an orange trail near Camp Hood, Texas, moving from S 81° W to S 60° W and descending from about 21° above the horizon to 6°31' above. The object was noted to have no sound and disappeared from view after a short observation. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 6** — 9:00 p.m. On 6 Mar at 2100, two observers at Camp Hood, Texas, reported an oblong blue-white light moving from north-northwest to north-northwest at an apparent altitude of about 6°45' above the horizon. The object was seen as a blue-white light with a trail, no sound, and no known speed or duration was recorded. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 7** — 1:15 a.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single witness observed a brilliant blue-white object around 0115 on 7 March, moving on a bearing of N 40° E at an estimated altitude of 66° 15' above the horizon. The object was described as like a flash bulb/flash bulb, with no sound or trail noted, and it disappeared without further detail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 7** — 1:30 a.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a lone observer reported a bright blue-white object at about 27°30' above the horizon traveling on a N 16° W bearing at 0130 on 7 Mar. The object was described as basketball-sized, shaped like a flash bulb, with no sound or trail, and it appeared as a fixed flash before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 7** — 1:30 a.m. On 7 March at Camp Hood, Texas, two unidentified observers reported a bluish-white ball-like flash moving S 20° W at about 26° above the horizon. The object was described as resembling a flash bulb, with no sound and no trail, and the observation lasted from about 0130 to 0200. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 7** — 1:45 a.m. At 0145 on 7 March, a single unknown observer at Camp Hood, Texas reported a small orange tear-drop-shaped object about 2 feet by 1 foot that dropped vertically to the ground on a course N 60° E, made no sound, and disappeared behind trees after about 2 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:55 p.m. On 7 Mar 1948 at 20:55 local time near Smyrna AAF, Tennessee, three USAF personnel observed a single yellow-orange oval object about six miles away and just above the western to northwestern horizon. It appeared flame-like and flare-like, moved very slowly away from Smyrna without sound or exhaust, remained visible for about 45 minutes, and gradually faded into the horizon. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 17–19](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 20–21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:03 a.m. On 8 March at 0103, a single observer at Camp Hood, Texas reported a pale white light moving in an arc from south 58° east to south 54° east, descending from about 58° above the horizon to 54° above. The object was described as round-ish with a hazy smoke trail; no sound was heard and the observation was of unknown duration. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:30 a.m. On 8 March 1948 at about 08:30 in Bakersfield, California, H. B. Nix reported a single orange-red ball of fire seen from the ground at an estimated distance of five miles. It was described as larger than a small airplane, appeared static for about 30 seconds, then drifted eastward and split/burned before moving into the hills east of Bakersfield. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 29](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:00 a.m. On 8 Mar 1948 at about 1100, Mr. A. C. Morrison, a Duke Power Co. superintendent, observed from the ground near Belmont, North Carolina, a single bright silver/metallic round object, possibly spherical, moving easterly in a straight line at constant speed, estimated around 600 mph, for about 10 seconds before it disappeared behind a small white cloud. No sound, exhaust trail, or change in altitude or direction was noted, and altitude could not be estimated. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 17–21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 22–23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 8** _(cluster ×3)_ — 6:36 p.m. On 8 March at 18:36, a single reliable observer reported a bright white object with a greenish tint moving south to north over Los Alamos, New Mexico at an estimated 12,000 to 15,000 feet. It was seen horizontally for only 1–2 seconds, made no sound, and was judged to be traveling about 800 mph before it either went out or disappeared behind cloud. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 8 March 1948 in Bakersfield, California, Mr. Denio, a Pacific General Electric Company employee observing from the ground, reported two unidentified objects. One object was seen as if afire, with red and black smoke trailing, moving earthward before falling to the ground. No other descriptive details were recorded. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 20–26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:30 a.m. On 9 March 1948 at 08:30 in Bakersfield, California, J.E. Johnson (a dentist) and Mrs. Callie R. Mason (a secretary) observed a single object from the 5th floor of a business building, estimated 7-10 miles away and about 1,000 feet altitude. It appeared like a very small airplane, moved due north of Bakersfield in a zig-zag pattern, looked orange-red flame colored, and trailed considerable black smoke for 4,000-5,000 feet. No sound, odor, or other details were reported, and no sketches or photographs were taken. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 26–28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:00 p.m. On 10 March at about 18:00-18:30 in Phoenix, Arizona, six witnesses reported an oval or oblong aluminum/quick-silver object at an estimated 40,000-50,000 feet moving upward at a 60-degree angle before disappearing after roughly 10 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 11** — 1:00 a.m. At 0100 on 11 March, a single reliable observer reported a ping-pong-ball-sized object near Holloman AFB, New Mexico, flying on an apparent 270° heading about 30° above the horizon at an estimated 50-mile distance. The object moved in straight flight for about 5 minutes, changed color from light orange to blood red to amber to light green, and then disappeared from view without sound or trail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 11** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 11 Mar 1948 at night near the shores of James Bay, observer V. J. Pratt, an employee of the Hudson Bay Co. at Moose Factory, Canada, reported a single meteoric object: a big blue flame about the size of a football streaking earthward with no sound, exploding near the community and briefly lighting the area brighter than daylight. The object disappeared, then a second orange streak shot upward from the last seen point and vanished within a second. The report also notes a similar sighting by Mrs. Charles Giles describing a downward-plunging object in the northern sky, and ties the event to contemporaneous meteor reports near Cochran. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 58–59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 57–58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 13** — 2:30 p.m. On 1948-03-13 at about 14:30 MST near Benkelman, Nebraska, the witness saw a fast-moving white streak high in the sky, first noticed after hearing a distant motor/train-like rumbling. The object traveled east to west at very high altitude, maintained a straight connected streak-like appearance without changing shape or direction, and vanished while still far above the horizon. The witness's son also observed it, and the witness compared it to a vapor trail, a streamlined train, or a rocket, while noting it did not resemble a smoke trail or the bright fireball of a meteor. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 46–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, March 13** — 9:53 p.m. At 2153 on 13 March, a single unknown observer in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a bluish or greenish-white object moving NE to SW or SW to NE at about 20° above the horizon, descending slightly, with a train or trail visible. The object was described as ball-shaped with an apparent length about twice its diameter, roughly half the diameter of the full moon, and the sighting lasted 2-4 seconds; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 14** — 9:00 a.m. A lone reliable witness aboard an airliner en route from Honolulu to Canton Island reported a bullet-nosed unidentified object at about 6°-12° above the aircraft and 5,000 feet relative altitude. The object was observed for about 10 seconds, made no sound, and appeared to move horizontally at roughly 65° in 9 seconds before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 29–30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 16** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:00 a.m. On 16 Mar at 1000, ten observers in Farmington, New Mexico reported a bright aluminum oval/oblong object moving NE at an apparent altitude above 20,000 feet, on a skyward course of about 60°-50°. They watched it for about 30 minutes; it made no sound, had no trail, appeared smaller than conventional aircraft at arm's length estimate, moved faster than conventional aircraft, and gradually disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 16** — 10:00 a.m. On 16 March at 1000 hours near Farmington, New Mexico, a witness reported a bright aluminum-colored oval/oblong object high in the sky, estimated at over 20,000 feet and seen in the skyward direction at 50 ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1948, March 16** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:00 a.m. On 16 Mar at about 1100 near Farmington, New Mexico, ten observers reported a bright luminous object like tin foil that moved from N to NE, turned on its axis, and maneuvered up and down for 3-5 minutes before they discontinued observation. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 17** — 3:00 a.m. At 0300 on 17 March, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a small round greenish-yellow light moving on a level course to the northeast. It was seen for about 3 seconds, had no trail and no sound, and vanished like a light going out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 17** — 3:00 a.m. At 0300 on 17 Mar near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported a round greenish-yellow object traveling in level flight to the northeast. It appeared about one-quarter the size of the full moon, had no trail or sound, lasted about 3 seconds, and vanished like a light going out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 17** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:10 a.m. On 17 Mar at 0310, a single reliable observer reported a brief 1-second sighting over Los Alamos, New Mexico, of an unidentified object moving toward the earth, appearing reddish and then green, with no trail or sound, and disappearing as it seemed to fall to the ground. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 21** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:00 p.m. At Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single unknown object was observed around 1300 on 21 March, moving NE to SE at an estimated 40,000-60,000 feet for about 30 minutes. The witness described it as round, changing in color from shiny silver to shady gray, with no train or trail, no sound, and disappearing from view while traveling about the same speed as a jet aircraft. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 58–59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 21** — 1:00 p.m. On 21 Mar, between 1300 and 1330, four unspecified observers at Kirtland AFB, New Mexico, reported a bright silver round object moving SE to S at roughly 40 ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 22** — 11:00 a.m. At Kirtland AFB, New Mexico, 11 observers reported a tan-to-brown flying-wing object at about 25,000 to 30,000 feet moving from NW to N at extremely high speed. The sighting lasted only 5 to 6 seconds, with no sound or trail, and the object disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 58–59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 23** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 a.m. On 23 March 1948, a confidential female observer active in American Legion activities in Baltimore (Hamilton), Maryland reported hearing peculiar sounds in the sky on several occasions between 3:00 and 5:00 A.M. over the prior six to eight weeks. The sounds were described as coming from distant airplanes but unlike the normal motor drone of American aircraft, and her husband thought they resembled foreign-type airplane engines. No object was visually sighted. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 31–32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 27** — 6:00 p.m. A single unnamed observer reported an amber, long and narrow UAP over Tucumcari, New Mexico on 27 Mar, seen high in the sky about 30 degrees above the horizon traveling east to west with no trail or sound; it faded out in the distance after roughly 25 minutes. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 27** — 6:00 p.m. A single observer reported an orange flame-like UAP over Montoya, New Mexico, seen about 75 degrees above the horizon traveling east to west for roughly 10 hours, with no sound or trail; it was described as long and narrow and eventually faded out in the distance. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 29–30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 27** — 6:00 p.m. On 27 Mar at about 1800, a single observer in Tucumcari, New Mexico reported an orange fire-like object moving E to W about 60 degrees above the horizon. It was described as having a kite-tail-like trail, lasting about 15 minutes, making no sound, and disappearing behind hills. The apparent size was compared to an O-47 aircraft at 10,000 feet. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, March 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:30 p.m. On 28 Mar 1948 at 16:30 in Berlin Lichtenrade, Kirchbachstrasse 2, Dr. Ormans, a former guided missile expert at Exeinmetall Borsig, and Mrs. Ormans reported seeing a single white point traveling south to north at an estimated altitude of about 14 kilometers. The object was described as rather fast, with no sound and no exhaust trail; no photographs or sketches were available. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 60–63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 61–62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, March 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:50 p.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single reliable observer reported a spherical fire-red-to-white object about the apparent size of a basketball, seen at an estimated altitude of 2,000 feet moving southwest with a visible train or trail. No sound was reported, and the object later disappeared; the report is part of a broader Department of War / AFSWP and USAF collection of 1948-1950 New Mexico-area UAP documentation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 1** — 4:20 a.m. At 0420 on 1 April in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported a white round light moving from south to north at about 75 degrees above the horizon; the light had no train or trail, lasted 1 second or less, produced no sound, and disappeared behind a building. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 1** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:55 a.m. On 1 April 1948 at 0955 hours, 1st Lt. Robert W. Meyers, a P-47 pilot leading a flight of four aircraft over the Philippine Islands at 124° 3' East, 12° 52' North, sighted a single silver unidentified object about three miles east of his position at roughly 1,000 feet altitude. He described it as a half-moon or flying-wing type craft with a turtle-back and barely perceptible dorsal fin, estimated at 30 feet wingspan and 20 feet length. As Meyers began a left turn to intercept, the object made a sharp 90° left turn, leveled on a 270° heading, accelerated rapidly, and vanished from view in about five seconds; no sound or exhaust trail was observed. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 31–32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 33–34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 5** — 10:00 p.m. At about 2200 on 5 April, a single reliable witness near Los Alamos, New Mexico, saw a green object with a red afterglow moving from south to north at an apparent altitude of about 300 feet above the south slope of Pejarito Rock. The object followed an arc, made no sound, lasted roughly half to one second, and vanished behind a mountain, appearing to the witness to move at tremendous speed. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 5** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 5 April 1948 at Holloman Air Force Base, trained balloon observer Mr. Olsen watched a single large grey-white, rounded and indistinct object from the ground for about 30 seconds under CAVU conditions. It was very high, moved faster than any known aircraft, made no sound, executed an erratic maneuver involving a large loop after descending, then climbed and disappeared suddenly. The report notes it was definitely not a balloon and was apparently unmanned. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 72–75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 72–73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 5** — On 5 April 1948 in the afternoon at Holloman Air Force Base, trained balloon observer Mr. Johnson watched a single very high object from the ground for about 30 seconds. He reported it as dime-sized against a moon-as-orange comparison, rounded and indistinct, resembling but definitely not a balloon, moving west at tremendous speed. The object made three vertical loops and then disappeared suddenly in a large arc to the west. He reported no sound, no signal noise on the 400-meter receiver, and no odor. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 74–77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 5** — On 5 April 1948 at Holloman Air Force Base, trained balloon observer Mr. Chance reported seeing two very high, golden, indistinct objects from the ground for about 30 seconds. Both moved faster than any known aircraft; one went straight up then veered right and descended out of view, while the other arced west at terrific speed and disappeared suddenly. He described the shape as roughly like a major's insignia, irregularly rounded and slightly concave on top. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 76–79](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 6** — 12:05 p.m. On 6 April at 12:05, a single reliable observer reported a very fast unidentified object near Los Alamos, New Mexico, seen heading southeast at an estimated altitude of about 15,000 feet for 3 to 5 seconds. The object was described as between dark and light green, with no sound or trail noted, and it disappeared from view. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 7** — 1:00 a.m. On 7 April at 0100, a single reliable observer reported a green object near Los Alamos, New Mexico, moving slowly from south to north for about 5 seconds with no sound before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 7** — 1:35 a.m. At 01:35 on 7 Apr, a lone reliable witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico, saw a green object moving very slowly westward about 200 yards from the top of a hill. The observation lasted about 45 seconds, produced no sound, and no trail was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:00 p.m. On 8 Apr 1948 between 1400 and 1430 at Ashley, Ohio, two civilian witnesses on the ground reported a single oblong, long-and-narrow silver object, like a silver streak, moving slowly and steadily southwest parallel to the ground in clear weather. They could not estimate its altitude or size, heard no definite sound from the object itself, saw no maneuvers, exhaust trails, or disappearance details, and one witness initially thought a low-flying plane was making unusual noise but could not locate it. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 31–37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 35–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:00 p.m. On 8 April 1948 between 14:00 and 14:30 in Ashley, Ohio, newspaper reporter Mrs. Eelah Stephens observed a single opalescent, long cylindrical object high in the sky moving slowly southwesterly. It first appeared as a streak and was initially mistaken for sky writing; after the nearby airplane passed and disappeared, the object became clearly outlined, resembling a stick of firewood and possibly drifting with the wind. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 36–39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 37–38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 8** _(cluster ×4)_ — 2:30 p.m. On 8 April 1948 at 14:30, Rev. James B. Stephenson, a pastor in Delaware, Ohio, observed one white object from the ground moving slowly southwesterly in clear weather. He first thought it was a plane on fire because it was surrounded by vapor, then after a few seconds saw a clearly outlined vertical cylinder, larger at the bottom and narrow at the top, with vapor streamers attached. It looked like a white cloud but retained its shape, moved steadily in a straight course against the wind, and made no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 41–42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 8** _(cluster ×4)_ — 2:30 p.m. On 8 April 1948 at 14:30 in Delaware, Ohio, Pastor Rev. John H. Bergen observed one very white, spherical object with a small protrusion above it, resembling concentrated cloud or vapor streaks. Seen from the ground for about ten minutes at several thousand feet, it moved in a straight line almost due west into the wind at not too fast a speed, made no sound, did not maneuver, and disappeared behind trees. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 44–45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 43–44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:30 p.m. On 8 April 1948 at 14:30 in Delaware, Ohio, Mrs. H. S. Kirkpatrick, a housewife, observed from the ground a single white spherical object moving steadily southwest in a clear blue sunny sky. She said the object was not large enough for a person to be inside, maintained the same shape and altitude, made no sound, and left no exhaust trail; her son Robert Cochran later remarked that it looked unusual and like a rainspout. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 46–49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 47–48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:10 p.m. On 9 April 1948 at 15:10 over Montgomery, Alabama, Lt. Col. Robert B. Hughes, a USAF pilot, observed a single silver object from the air for about five seconds during clear weather. He first saw it off his left wing while completing an 180 ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 50–52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 49–51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 11** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:30 p.m. On 11 April 1948 at about 11:30, Col. W. F. Siegmund, former Air Base commandant and then sales manager for Weestern Cartridge Co. in Alton, Illinois, observed a single creature-like object while riding four miles north of Alton from ground level. He described it as bigger than an airplane, resembling a huge fowl rather than an aircraft, and said it flapped its wings. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 76–80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 74–75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 15** — 4:30 p.m. On 15 Apr at 1630, a single observer in El Paso, Texas reported a very thin grayish object seen moving straight up at about 30 degrees, leaving a thin smoke trail. The observation lasted about 15 minutes and the object was said to dissipate; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 20–30** — 3:30 p.m. On 17 Apr at 1530 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, ten reliable observers reported a bright light green object, bright as tin foil, seen about 2,000 feet above the horizon. It maneuvered up and down and from side to side for 20 to 30 seconds with no sound or trail, and then gradually went out of sight. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 58–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 18** — 1:48 a.m. On 18 April at 0148, two observers reported a greenish-blue, ball-like light seen over the Flagstaff and Williams, Arizona area. The object was described as about the apparent size of a 100-watt light bulb, seen for 1–2 seconds, with no sound, and it disappeared behind an obstacle while heading northwest. One observer estimated an apparent altitude of about 12,000 feet; to the other it appeared 75–100 miles distant. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 18** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:06 p.m. On 18 Apr 1948 at 1306 hours, 1st Lt Aytch Johnson reported a single silvery, round, flat object about 8 inches in diameter seen over Fairbanks, Alaska, roughly 1 mile north of the city at an estimated altitude of 2000-3000 ft. It was observed for a few minutes in clear weather with unrestricted visibility, moved northeast toward southwest at an estimated 250-300 mph, and rapidly oscillated from a horizontal to a vertical plane.
