No sound or trails were noted, and the report suggested it may have been sunlight reflecting off aircraft wings because multiple aircraft were in the area at the time. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 50–51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 52–53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 18** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 p.m. On 18 April 1948 at 1500 GCT, a weather station patrol in the North Atlantic at 62°00'N 33°00'W reported a radar contact on air search radar. One target was tracked from 6,500 yards to 18,000 yards at an estimated 30 mph. The target was searched visually in clear weather (CAVU) but no clouds or weather fronts were found, and no surface radar indications were present. The contact faded at 18,000 yards and the incident was filed as a radar sighting. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 82–83](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 76–77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 19** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:15 p.m. On 19 Apr 1948 at 1615, two USAF 1st Lieutenants at Greenville AF Base, South Carolina, observed three white elliptical objects over the base at an estimated 15,000 to 20,000 ft. The objects were first seen in a semi-stationary hovering state, then another appeared and the group moved in trail formation to the N/NNE, rapidly accelerating and climbing until they disappeared in about two minutes total. Witnesses initially considered them balloons, but rejected that explanation due to the rapid acceleration; no exhaust, condensation trail, or sound was reported, and two sketches/photographs were noted. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 53–57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 54–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 20** — 3:30 p.m. On 20 April at 15:30, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a roughly circular, bright metallic object about 9 feet in diameter. It maneuvered up and down for an estimated 15-30 minutes, made no sound or trail, and was seen at what was apparently an undefined altitude before the witness lost sight of it. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 59–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 22** — 9:05 a.m. On 22 Apr at 0905, a single observer near Cliff, New Mexico reported a round, flat, thin disc-shaped object, described as aluminum-colored, traveling from west to east at about 20 degrees and dropping slowly. The object was seen for about 2 minutes, made no sound, had no trail, was estimated to be over 15 feet in diameter, and disappeared behind mountains. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 24** — 10:33 a.m. On 24 April at 10:33, five observers at White Sands, New Mexico reported an ellipsoid white-to-yellow light traveling north at an apparent altitude of 25–29 degrees. The object was seen for about 60 seconds, made no sound, and vanished by fading into distance; the reported speed was tremendous. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 25** — 6:30 a.m. On 25 Apr at about 0630 near Springer, New Mexico, two observers reported four groups of silvery-white round objects moving in opposite directions, with two groups going west and two groups going east, well above 30,000 feet. The objects were described as very small, very fast, and moving well above the speed of sound, with no trail or train and a total observation time of about 20 seconds before they disappeared from view. A sound was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, April 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:15 a.m. On 30 April 1948 at 10:15 over Anacostia Naval Air Station, Lt. Commander Marcus L. Lowe observed one yellow/light-colored spherical object at about 4,500 feet and roughly one mile away. He estimated it at 25-40 feet in diameter, moving north at about 100 mph on a constant altitude. The pilot thought it resembled a balloon, but noted no external fittings or attachments and said its south-to-north track was contrary to upper winds from the north-northwest. He did not pursue it further as it was heading toward prohibited airspace near the U.S. Capitol and White House area. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 79–80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 84–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, April 30** — 10:15 p.m. At 2215 on 30 Apr, a single observer in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a small round blue-green UAP moving from east to west at about 59 degrees above the horizon. The object was seen for roughly 2 seconds, appeared about a tenth the size of the moon, left no trail, made no sound, and then went out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 1** — 3:10 p.m. At Kirtland AFB, New Mexico, two observers reported a silver lucid metallic cylindrical object moving from southwest to northeast at an estimated altitude of a few thousand feet. It was seen for less than a second while angling downward, had no trail or sound, and vanished in a flash of brilliant white light; the report notes tremendous speed. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 2** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 2 May 1948 at night in St. Louis, Missouri, a ground observer reported one phosphorescent object after dark shaped like a bird. No other descriptive or motion details were recorded, and the report refers the reader to Incident #123 for additional information. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 84](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 3** — 9:26 p.m. On 3 May in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported two brief aerial sightings at 2126 and 2140. The first was a white object seen 10 ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 3** — 9:43 p.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported a bright white light moving rapidly from south to north about 10 ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 5** _(cluster ×2)_ — Around 5 May 1948, near Adapazari, Turkey, a single shining object was reported over a village at an estimated altitude of 650 feet (also described as 200 meters) before it exploded in the air. Debris reportedly fell into a nearby village, killing a sheep dog, and authorities stated the object was found to be a rocket; experts were to inspect the debris. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 197–200](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 173–174](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 6** — 1:05 a.m. At Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single reliable witness reported a round green object moving from north to south at about 6 degrees above the horizon, descending at an angle of 30 to 35 degrees. The object was observed for a fraction of a second, emitted no sound, left no train or trail, and disappeared west of the Jemez Mountains. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:05 a.m. On 6 May 1948 at 0905 GCT, Capt Floyd Barnes, a USAF pilot, observed a large white ball of fire ahead of and slightly above his aircraft while flying between Kwajalein Island and Hickam Field near 19°08'N, 164°05'E. The object was seen only for a split second at an estimated distance of 4 to 5 miles and altitude of about 10,000 ft, then appeared to explode like a shell and disappear. Barnes reported no sound, no exhaust trails, and no visible streamers or tail; later he saw a single white light on the water surface and additional lights farther north, which he believed were from a surface vessel. About 10 minutes later, an aircraft was observed on a reciprocal course, and Barnes attempted radio contact without receiving acknowledgment. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 194–195](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 168–169](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 p.m. On 7 May 1948 between 1500 and 1700 hours over Memphis, Tennessee, Miss Jean Bray and additional witnesses, including the Kaiser family, reported seeing about 50 to 60 very small shiny objects at extremely high altitude moving easterly at a speed faster than any aircraft they had ever seen. Most traveled in straight lines, though some zig-zagged slightly, no sound was heard, and a few appeared to leave silvery tails suggesting exhaust. Witnesses initially thought the objects might have been meteors, but later commentary said meteor and weather balloon explanations were unlikely; investigators consulted astronomers about a possible meteor shower. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 60–61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 59–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 7 May 1948, a press dispatch reported residents along the Lake Doiran area on the Yugoslav-Greek frontier saw a single flying disc from ground level. The object was described as moving southward from Yugoslavia at about 3,000 feet altitude and making a sound like an artillery shell with a shrill whine. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 85–88](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 81–82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 10–20** — 9:30 a.m. On 8 May near Tucson, Arizona, four observers reported a metallic circular object seen from about 0930 to 1100. It was white, had no train or sound, appeared motionless at first, then moved faster than a jet, made a 90-degree turn to the north, and climbed at a 45-degree angle before disappearing out of sight. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 8 May 1948 at night near Middleport, Ohio, New York Central System car inspector Ben Rupe observed one or more round, phosphorescent objects from the ground, appearing about 6 to 8 miles high, moving with great speed on an eastward heading (90°) and leaving a phosphorescent trail in the sky. The checklist notes similar sightings by Earl Roush, Bob White, and patrolman C. K. Hite; Hite reported disc-shaped objects traveling west to east in succession at roughly two-minute intervals, occasionally hook-backing along the same path and sometimes bursting and disintegrating. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 87–90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 83–84](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 8** — On the night of 1948-05-08 in Hobson, Ohio, witnesses reported round, phosphorescent 'flying discs' moving at great speed across the sky, apparently around 6 to 8 miles altitude, with a phosphorescent trail; the Eleventh Air Force summarized the FBI-sourced report and stated it had no further information and was not initiating an investigation. ([18_6369445_General_1948_Vol_1, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/18_6369445_general_1948_vol_1.pdf>))

**1948, May 12** — 9:15 p.m. Single witness reported at Alamogordo, New Mexico, seeing two fuzzy star-like objects about 3° or 4° above the horizon, white with a greenish tinge, with no train or trail, observed for about 4 minutes before they vanished. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 15** — Reverend Charles C. Barnes of McMinnville, Oregon reported that one afternoon in May 1948 he saw four narrow beams of light crossing the sky from northwest to southeast and converging over the Cascade Mountains, where small clouds formed within the beams and an explosive-looking effect appeared against the mountains. He estimated the display lasted at least 10 minutes and said the apparent explosion rose to about 10,000 feet. He suggested it might have been a scientific or military experiment related to radio beams and rainfall. ([FBI-UAP-D011, pp. 1–4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D011_DFBI-Correspondence-Referral_1949.pdf>))

**1948, May 16** — 5:00 p.m. On 16 May at about 1700 in Tucson, Arizona, a single observer reported a black, round, solid-flat object traveling east to west at an estimated 5,000 feet altitude. The object was seen for about 8-10 seconds, measured roughly 3-4 feet in diameter, and was estimated to be moving at 800-1000 MPH before disappearing behind an aircraft hangar. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 34–35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, May 17** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:30 p.m. On 1948-05-17, W. A. Bonneville, a B.F. Goodrich territory manager, reported watching a bright white ball-like object at night between Plevna and Miles City, Montana while driving west on Route/U.S. Highway 212/12. He said it appeared to move about twice as fast as Northwest Airlines aircraft, altered course in a broad arc turning west then slightly south and back west several times, at times seeming to vanish into a dark cloud bank and reappear, and was seen for about twenty minutes. He described a long bright light shooting from beneath it, no sound, and an appearance unlike reflected moonlight or a conventional airplane. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 91–96](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 87–90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 p.m. On 28 May 1948 at about 1500 EDT, 1st Lt. Alexander Kokolonis reported seeing five disc-shaped, silvery-golden objects from a C-47 passenger aircraft about six miles east of Monroe, Michigan. The objects, initially three and later five, were seen for 10 to 15 seconds at roughly 7,500 to 8,000 ft, moving southwest at well over 500 mph with abrupt right turns and a stepped, line-abreast/line-of-stern pattern. They had hazy outlines, left no exhaust or trail, and disappeared into haze. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 101–102](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 94](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 p.m. On 28 May 1948 at about 1500 EDT, M/Sgt Ernest Davis, Jr. reported seeing two unidentified round, shiny brass objects while en route from MacDill Field to Selfridge Field, about 6 miles due east of Monroe, Michigan. He estimated them at about 8,000 ft altitude, moving southwest in a straight course at roughly 400 mph, visible for about 30 seconds from 25 to 30 miles away. He noted a clear sky with high overcast and broken thin stratus, and said he perceived only the topside of the objects. A sketch was made. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 103](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 95](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, May 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:20 p.m. On 31 May 1948 at 13:20 in Wilmington, North Carolina, Mrs. H. D. Alspach and two other women reported a mysterious oblong object, about as large as an arm and roughly 3 feet long, flying very high and fast. It was seen from the ground, initially coming from the direction of Bluethenthal Field over the Cape Fear River, then veering back toward Wrightsville slightly to the west while emitting smoke. A Civil Aeronautics Administration office later rejected a suggestion that it was a P-30 aircraft, stating no such plane was in the sky there at the time. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 91–92](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 85–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 2** — 12:40 a.m. At 0040 on 2 June, a single reliable observer reported a green ball of light over Los Alamos, New Mexico, moving from east to north while descending at an estimated altitude of 7,000 to 10,000 feet. The object was seen for about 1 second, had no train or trail, and was described simply as a ball of light. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, June 11** — 8:57 p.m. A single reliable observer reported a brief UAP over Los Alamos on 11 Jun at 20:57, traveling SW to NE at about 25° above the horizon. The object was seen for 4–5 seconds, left a trail, and appeared green during flight then red at the end; it was described as about the size of a star and disappeared without further detail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, June 20** _(cluster ×3)_ — 10:30 p.m. On 20 June 1948 at 2230, Lt. Col. Wm. T. Hull, a pilot and Supervisor of Flying Training at Scott Air Force Base in Belleville, Illinois, observed a single white light/object below 6,000 ft under complete overcast. The object, described as about six or eight inches in diameter and approximately round, moved at an estimated 500 mph on a zigzag course, first on a 295° heading south of the base and then 340° when it reached the south end of Scott AFB. No sound, exhaust, or aircraft traffic in the vicinity was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 97](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 91](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 27** — 1:39 a.m. At 0139 on 27 June in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported a round object moving from west to east at an estimated altitude of 30° to 90° above the horizon. It appeared similar to a star, slightly more orange in color, left a trail, and was seen for about 2 minutes before going behind a building. No sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, June 29** — 11:00 p.m. On 29 June 1948 at about 11:00 p.m., Sgt. Chas E. Schuh of the Uniontown Police Department and his daughter reported seeing one luminous semi-circular object over the west end of Uniontown, Pennsylvania, near Oakland Ave. and West Main St. The object was seen from the front porch of 38 Kensington Circle, appeared at roughly 6,000 ft, moved straight and level to the northeast for about 5 to 7 seconds (roughly 8 to 900 feet), made no sound, and left no reported trails or other visible effects. Additional named witnesses were Mrs. McDonald and Mrs. Hollar. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 185–188](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 29** — 11:00 p.m. On 29 June 1948 at about 23:00 in Uniontown, Pennsylvania, Mrs. Margaret Hollar and other witnesses observed three luminous oval objects, one at a time, moving very fast from southwest to northeast beneath storm clouds. Each object was seen for about 10 seconds, appeared about platter-sized, rolled vertically on its edge, seemed to be spinning end-over-end, and left a short tail of light. One witness reported a lightning flash visible through a passing object, making it appear transparent. The objects were silent and vanished from view as houses obstructed the witnesses' line of sight. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 183](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 30** — 11:25 a.m. On 1948-06-30 between Watertown and Webster, South Dakota, chemical engineer and amateur astronomer Norman Pfutzenreuter observed a single polished-aluminum, changing-shaped cluster of parts apparently stationary at an estimated altitude of about 150 miles. Over slightly more than two hours it slowly altered form, shed a large chunk, then disintegrated into smaller pieces that formed a triangle and drifted apart while leaving very faint vapor trails before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 123–124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 30** — 11:25 a.m. On 30 June 1948, near Hecla, South Dakota, amateur astronomer and chemical engineer Norman Pfutzenreuter observed a single object from about 11:25 A.M. until shortly after 1:27 P.M. while between Watertown and Webster, S.D. The object appeared as a cluster of parts, remained nearly stationary for about two hours, then slowly changed shape, shed a large chunk, and progressively disintegrated into smaller pieces that formed a triangular arrangement before fading away. Very faint vapor trails were reported from the disintegrating mass, and the witness noted the parts seemed to be moving away from the earth. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 110–111](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 30** — 4:30 p.m. A single reliable observer reported a dull grey circular object over Seligman, Arizona on 30 June at 16:30. It was seen about 30 degrees above the horizon, appeared roughly 1.5 diameters in apparent size at an estimated 10,000 feet, had no train or trail, made no sound, and was observed for 8 seconds before disappearing in the distance. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 34–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, June 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:40 p.m. On 30 June 1948 at 21:40, Mr. Tryus W. Setliff reported seeing a single orange, ball-shaped object from S. Knoxville, Tennessee, while observing from Knoxville. The object was seen for about 3 seconds, estimated at 30,000 feet and 1,000 mph, traveling westward and disappearing over the western horizon. The checklist notes no sound, no sketch or photograph, bluish exhaust trails, and an apparent construction of fire. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 110](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 99](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, June 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:40 p.m. On 30 June 1948 at about 2140 in South Knoxville, Tennessee, Mrs. Whitehouse reported a single orange ball of fire with a bluish streamer trailing, moving west at very high speed and disappearing over the horizon without sound. The object was estimated by one witness to be about 30,000 ft altitude and 1,000 mph, with visibility lasting anywhere from 3 seconds to about 3 minutes depending on the observer. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 108–109](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 97–98](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 1** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:00 p.m. On 1 July 1948 between about 2100 and 2200 hours in Gahanna, Ohio, Jack Taylor reported seeing a single bright yellow-white luminous object moving rapidly from northeast to southwest at an estimated altitude of 3000 to 4000 feet. The object traveled in a flat line for about 2 seconds under clear, bright moonlit skies, made no sound, and then ceased abruptly. Additional interviews found Mrs. Howard Enslow, Mr. Enslow, Mr. and Mrs. Chester Taylor, and their sons Harold and Jack also reported a similar golden saucer-like object with a silver rim, though Mrs. Taylor did not observe it. An investigator later noted tree cover may have limited the witnesses' line of sight. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 121–122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 108–109](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 1** — 9:00 p.m. On 1 July 1948, between 21:00 and 22:00, Mrs. Howard Enslow observed a single golden saucer-shaped luminous object from the ground in Gahanna, Ohio. It moved northeast to southwest, remained visible for about 3 seconds, and was described as glowing like a golden saucer with a silver halo or rim in clear moonlit conditions. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 117–118](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 4** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:15 p.m. On 4 July 1948 at about 11:15 P.M. OST in Dravasburg, Pennsylvania, John Jannicky Jr. and his wife saw a star-like light moving across the sky. The object was the same size and color as a star, made no noise, hovered, then moved on at an undetermined speed, stopping three times before continuing and eventually disappearing. A vague sketch was made. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 172–173](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 154–155](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:14 p.m. On 7 Jul 1948 at about 21:14-21:15 in Chapel Hill, North Carolina, Capt. H. W. Daniels, Infantry Reserve, observed three unidentified objects in clear weather for approximately one minute. The objects were reported at extreme altitude, moving high-speed east northeast, and the only noted sound was a jet-like noise; no exhaust trails were visible and no sketches or photos were taken. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 111–113](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 101](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:14 p.m. On 7 Jul 1948 at about 21:14-21:15 in Chapel Hill, North Carolina, Lt. James Eaves, a U.S. Naval Reserve mathematics instructor at the University of North Carolina, observed three cigar-shaped objects for approximately one minute moving east-northeast at extreme altitude and high speed in clear weather. He reported a jet-like sound, no visible exhaust trails, and stated the objects appeared about the size of an airplane in the sky, with no wings or apparent means of propulsion. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 110–112](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 7** — On 7 July 1948, ground observer Chas N. Tasker of West Bridge, New Hampshire, reported an unidentified object sighted on Rt. 202 near West Bridge, first attracting attention by curls of smoke. The checklist form contains no filled-in measurements or descriptive details beyond the smoke cue, and the incident summary notes that investigation was continuing. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 99](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, July 8** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:20 a.m. On 8 July 1948 at about 0920 hrs in Columbus, Ohio, Mrs. Wilda Zittek, a housewife observing from the ground, reported two silver, cigar- or torpedo-shaped objects about 3000 feet up moving fast and bobbing/undulating as if in air currents. She first saw them with the naked eye, then used M-3 6x30 binoculars. They were in view for about four minutes, made no sound, left no exhaust trail, and initially headed northeast before turning north. Air Force investigators later concluded the objects were highly likely to have been two F-80 aircraft seen from a three-quarter rear aspect, which could distort their appearance. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 114](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 102–105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 8** — On or about July 8, 1948, in McKeesport, Pennsylvania, two housewives, Mrs. Nell Veway and Mrs. Elizabeth Goltz, observed a single noiseless silver object shaped like a plate or disc moving overhead toward Kennywood Park. Mrs. Veway estimated it at 12 to 15 inches in diameter. The object disappeared into a cloud bank, reappeared on the far side, and was then tracked until it went out of sight. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 172–175](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 8** — Two civilian witnesses in McKeesport, Pennsylvania reported seeing a noiseless, silver, plate-like disc that zoomed overhead toward Kennywood Park, disappeared into a cloud bank, reappeared on the far side, and then continued out of sight as the witnesses followed its direction. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 157](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 12:05 p.m. On 9 July 1948 at 1205 AST, two photo reconnaissance observers near Fielding Lake, Alaska saw about 20 grayish-black dot-like objects immediately below the clouds, estimated at 5,000+ ft and moving west-northwest to east-southeast at over 500 mph. The formation jockeyed back and forth as if in a shotgun-blast pattern but held a straight course. A loud roaring sound was heard before sighting and sharpened into a buzz as the objects passed. No exhaust trails were seen; a sketch was made and no photographs were available. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 131–138](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 121–122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:47 p.m. On 1948-07-09 at 21:47, 1st Lt. Clarence Glasebrook reported from the ground at the north end of Osborn, Ohio, seeing a single pale yellowish-white luminous object moving east to west at an estimated 3,000 to 4,000 feet and 500 to 600 mph. The object appeared three times for 1 to 2 seconds each, with about 3-second pauses between appearances, then disappeared suddenly at an angle of about 70 degrees to the horizon. The sky was clear with a quarter moon in the east-southeast; there was no sound, trail, or observable structure, and the light seemed to glow rather than beam. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 114–119](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 106–107](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 11** — 9:10 p.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single observer saw a pale red ball-like object, not perfectly circular, about 30 degrees above the horizon and moving W by SW. The object was seen for roughly 2 seconds and then disappeared as if a flashlight were turned off; no sound or trail was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 35–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, July 17** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:50 p.m. On 1948-07-17 at about 16:50 near San Acacia Dam, roughly 65 miles south of Albuquerque, two Air Force noncommissioned officers observed seven metallic, aluminum-colored objects flying northward in formation at an estimated 20,000 feet. The formation shifted from V to L to Q and then became irregular; when directly overhead the objects appeared circular, resembling a cluster of balloons, and after passing zenith they became indeterminate in shape. No sound, exhaust trail, or other visible effect was reported, and the objects disappeared heading between north and northwest. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 131–140](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 123–124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:30 p.m. On 20 July 1948 at about 13:30 local time, observer A. D. Otter, Chief Investigator of the Court of Damage, reported a single object near Obrechstreet in Arnhem/The Hague, viewed from 52°30' N, 4°30' E. The object was seen intermittently four times through clouds at very high altitude, moving at high speed comparable to a V-2 and producing a similar sound. It appeared as a wingless aircraft with two decks. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 194–196](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 170](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 22** — 2:45 a.m. On 1948-07-22 at about 02:45 EST, co-pilot John B. Whitted and Capt. C. S. Chiles, flying a DC-3 near Montgomery, Alabama at 5,000-5,500 feet, observed a large cigar-shaped object with red fire trailing from its tail pass on the right at high speed. The object was estimated at about 100 feet long, with two rows of large square windows glowing white/orange-red, no visible wings, and no noise or turbulence. The crew later asked whether the Army had any aircraft in the area; Lawson Field reported none. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 130–136](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 22** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:30 p.m. On 22 July 1948, about an hour before dark, Capt. Henry Glover and his wife observed a single round, probably spherical object almost directly overhead in Van Nuys, California, while dining in an open-air market patio. The object initially resembled a weather balloon at about 2000 ft but showed no bobbing, remained steady despite gusty ground winds, and traveled slowly through a vertical arc of about 25 degrees over roughly an hour from east toward the sun. It displayed a constant bluish luminescence like a fluorescent lamp that gradually shifted to yellow-orange at dusk before disappearing instantly like a light being switched off. The outline was sharp and symmetrical, with no sound, exhaust, or apparent construction. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 176–182](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 160–161](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:30 a.m. At about 0230 on 24 July 1948, pilot Louis Feldwary observed from the air near Blackstone, Virginia, while en route to Raleigh-Durham, a trail on the distant western horizon that appeared to be a jet or rocket trail. The object leaving the trail was traveling at terrific speed on a southwesterly course (estimated 230°), with the trail covering an 80° to 90° lateral arc; the trail never crossed the flight path and was seen about 20° above the horizon. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 127–129](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 115–117](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:30 a.m. At about 0230 on 24 July 1948, Capt. Perry R. Mansfield and C. Kingsley, flying between Blackstone, Virginia, and Greensboro, North Carolina, observed a brilliant object they believed resembled an unusual meteor. It was seen for about 3 seconds, moved horizontally slightly above the horizon on a southerly course, then faded out in the distance; the sky was clear with unlimited visibility. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 130–132](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 119–120](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:45 a.m. On 24 July 1948 at 02:45, pilot Clarence Shipe Chiles, co-pilot John B. Whitted, and Mr. McKelvie observed a single cigar-shaped object while in the air about 20 miles south of Montgomery, Alabama. The object was seen for 5 to 10 seconds at an estimated 5,000 ft altitude and 500 to 700 mph, moving southwest toward Mobile and New Orleans. It reportedly pulled up sharply with a tremendous burst of red-orange flame and blue fluorescent glow beneath it, then disappeared into a cloud. No sound or odor was detected; two sketches or photographs are referenced. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 126–127](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 114](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:45 a.m. On 1948-07-24 at about 02:45 A.M., Eastern Air Lines Capt. C. S. Chiles and John Whitted sighted an unidentified aircraft while flying on trip 576 about 20 miles southwest of Montgomery, Alabama. The object, seen for roughly ten seconds in clear moonlight, appeared jet-like with no visible wings, two rows of glowing windows suggesting upper and lower decks, a bright rear flame trail extending about fifty feet, and a blue glow underneath. It passed to the right and slightly above their aircraft, then climbed into broken clouds and disappeared; no prop wash or rough air was felt, and a passenger awake at the time saw only the fire trail. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 129](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 118](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** — 3:40 a.m. At 0340 on July 24, 1948, a DC-3 pilot and copilot observed an object approaching their aircraft. It passed to their right and slightly above, then entered a steep climb and disappeared into clouds after about 10 seconds. A passenger briefly saw a flash of light as it passed. The object was described as jet- or rocket-powered with a trail of fire extending about 50 feet behind it, and it reportedly had no wings or protrusions plus two rows of lighted windows. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 91](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** — On the night of 1948-07-24 aboard an Eastern Airlines DC-3 near Atlanta, Georgia, passenger C. L. McKelvie saw a sudden bright streak of flame moving in a straight line across the airway above the aircraft toward the southeast under a full moon. He briefly lost sight of it, then saw it again; he reported no physical shape, no sound, and said the plane's vibration was unusually strong. The pilots later appeared excited and nervous about the episode. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 128–130](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 24** — This report is a memo about the 1948 New Mexico green fireball investigations, arguing that copper particles found in dust collections from the fireballs are significant evidence and recommending further airborne and ground dust sampling along fireball paths, especially after the July 24, August 6, January 30, July 27, and August 8-9 incidents. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, July 25** — On the night of July 24-25, 1948, Eastern Air Lines captain C. S. Chiles and copilot J. B. Whitted reported a large, wingless black craft with a fiery tail and rows of lit square windows passing near Montgomery, Alabama at about 5,000 feet. A passenger, C. L. McKelvie, later said he saw only a continuous light speed past. The newspaper article notes that Army and Weather Bureau sources suggested the object was likely an air radar weather observation balloon with reflective tinfoil boxes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 179–180](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, July 26** — 8:00 p.m. On Monday night in Atlanta, Georgia, multiple witnesses reported a bright fire-like aerial object described variously as a flying floor lamp, cantaloupe-sized ball of fire, or bluish streak of light. Witnesses said it banked, rolled, climbed, and trailed square sections or a long colored tail while moving over the city between about 8:00 p.m. and 9:45 p.m. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 181](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, July 26** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:00 p.m. On 26 July 1948 at about 21:00, Doyle L. Avery and others at the observation tower of Atlanta Naval Air Base in Chamblee, Georgia, reported a single blue-white light moving very fast southeast from the northeast. It looked like a shooting star, initially maintaining a uniform altitude and speed, then appeared to gain altitude and turn sharply south. Additional area residents later reported the same light; another witness group described a separate sighting of a green light with a silver tail traveling southeast and fading out. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 185–191](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 164–165](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 26** — On July 26, 1948, boardwalk crowds in Asbury Park, New Jersey, reported seeing a high-flying "silver cylinder" overhead, described as being "way up in the air" and resembling a blimp but blunt at both ends; officials asked Fort Monmouth personnel to attempt an identification. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 182](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, July 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:35 a.m. On 27 Jul 1948 at Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single reliable observer reported a flat, round object seen for about 10 minutes between 08:35 and 08:45. The object was described as showing 'Duramium-mini-reflected light,' with no sound, stationary at times, and disappearing by a reported evaluation note. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1948, July 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:00 p.m. On 27 July 1948 at about 2200 in Springfield, Ohio, Mrs. H. H. Gibson, a housewife, observed a single round object at great height for about three seconds. It appeared as a huge ball moving south-to-north in a straight line at about jet-plane speed, trailing a bright red tail of fire and making no sound. She reported it flowed out of sight; thunder and lightning were present, but the witness thought the object was something entirely foreign. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 132–142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 125](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:00 a.m. On 28 July 1948 at about 04:00 in Springfield, Ohio, Mrs. Rose Henry reported from 826 Dibert Ave. seeing a single round, basketball-sized intensely yellow light toward the northeast from her bedroom window. The object hovered nearly stationary, was visible for about 5 minutes, then faded away, reappeared, remained visible for about 15 minutes, and faded away completely. She reported no noise from the object itself, though she thought she heard a plane-like sound before seeing it. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 137–144](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, p. 127](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 28** — 10:58 p.m. A single observer reported a round UAP over Killeen, Texas on 28 Jul at 2258, moving from north to northwest at about 30 degrees above the horizon. It appeared initially blue, then turned white, showed no trail or sound, lasted 15-20 seconds, and went out like a light. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, July 28** — 10:59 p.m. On 28 Jul at 22:59 in Killeen, Texas, a single observer reported three round objects moving from south to north at about 30 degrees above the horizon; two were predominantly white and one had an orange glow at the tail. All three left a train or trail, appeared tennis-ball sized, were silent, and faded out after brief sightings. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, July 29** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:55 a.m. On 29 July 1948 at 09:55 in northeastern Indianapolis, Indiana, two witnesses, James W. Toney and Robert Huggins, saw a single shiny aluminum object shaped like a broad, short propeller fly just above the treetops at an estimated 30 ft altitude. It moved south at about 25-30 mph in a left bank of roughly 20 degrees, made no sound, showed no exhaust trail, and appeared to descend slightly before going down into a wooded area where it could not be relocated despite a search by USAF officers. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 147–149](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 131–134](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 29** — 9:55 a.m. Two men working for a rug-cleaning firm saw a shiny, metallic object glide across the road ahead of their car while crossing a bridge at 0955 hours on July 29, 1948. The object was estimated at 6 to 8 feet long and about 2 feet wide, flew at roughly 30 feet altitude in a flat glide with a moderate left turn, and after only a few seconds appeared to descend into a wooded area where no trace was found. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 88](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1948, July 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:15 p.m. On 30 July 1948 at about 20:15 in Worthington, Ohio, John A. Felton reported watching a single silver cylindrical object about 3,000 ft up moving very slowly southwest for roughly 15 minutes. He described no wings, tail surfaces, standard marker lights, engine noise, or other nearby aircraft; his wife also independently saw the object. Weather was clear with high scattered clouds and good visibility. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 168–171](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 152–153](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 30** — 9:36 p.m. Single observer at Camp Hood, Texas, reported a blue-white object moving from southeast to northwest at about 35 degrees above the horizon, climbing in an arc like a rocket tail and fading out after 2 to 2.5 seconds; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, July 30** — 10:04 p.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single witness reported a very fast round object with a tail, appearing about 30 degrees above the horizon and traveling SE to SW in a straight descending path before gradually fading out after 2-3 seconds; it was predominately white with a blue tint and gave no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, July 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:25 a.m. On 31 July 1948 at 08:25 in Indianapolis, Indiana, Mr. and Mrs. Vernon Swigert reported a single white, cymbal-shaped object with smooth, nonshiny surfaces passing west to east over south-central Indianapolis at an estimated 2,000 feet. The object was seen for about 10 seconds, appeared to shimmer as if spinning, made no sound, left no exhaust trail, and was estimated at roughly 20 feet in diameter and 6 to 8 feet high in the center. An intelligence officer later investigated by phone and noted good visibility from the witnesses' windows; no other reports were received. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 151](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 135–137](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 31** — 8:25 a.m. An electrician at his home witnessed a smooth, featureless object for about 10 seconds at 8:25 a.m. on July 31, 1948, first from a bathroom window and then while pointing it out to his wife from the kitchen; it traveled in a straight, level line from horizon to horizon moving west to east. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 94](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1948, July 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:30 p.m. On 31 July 1948 at 16:30 in Columbus, Ohio, Mrs. Edward G. Watson reported seeing approximately six or seven gold, starlike objects very high and far away in clear daylight. They appeared first like large stars, formed a group resembling the Big Dipper, hovered motionless or nearly so, and were described as glittering spots in the blue sky, about the size of an ash tray from the observer's perspective. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 161–163](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 144–145](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:00 p.m. On 31 July 1948 at about 8:00 PM, Mrs. Martin Price and Mrs. Mary Rippetoe observed a large oblong black object resembling a huge lantern near the WOSU radio tower on Tremont Road by the University Golf Course in Columbus, Ohio. It was seen in clear weather and daylight conditions, moved very slowly from west to east without changing course or altitude, passed close to the tower, emitted black smoke from the top, and showed a dull enclosed light on its lower left side. The object made no noise and no wings or openings were visible; another car nearby reportedly saw the event as well. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 164–165](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 146–147](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, July 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:00 p.m. On 31 July 1948 at about 8:00 P.M. in Columbus, Ohio, observer Robert C. Goshorn, a teletype operator at the State Highway Patrol Radio Station, saw a single object near a golf driving range and the Ohio State Student Housing Project. He reported it as a white crepe-paper, balloon-like object about 2 by 3.5 feet, round/"County Fair" balloon shaped, floating down from around 100 feet, then landing in a nearby field where it was retrieved and investigated. It was smoking when first seen, apparently due to a large ball of waste material fastened underneath; no markings or writing were observed. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 164](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 148–149](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 1** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:00 p.m. On 1 Aug 1948 at about 2:00 PM near Groveport, Ohio, student Harold A. Yantis observed a high-altitude black smoke/exhaust streak west of town while standing about a mile west of Groveport. He reported one object, but the object itself was never seen; only a dark cloud or trail appeared, stretched into a wide streak with smaller vertical streaks, twisted and turned, disappeared, and then reappeared about 20 degrees west of the first position. The witness, familiar with aircraft vapor trails and anti-aircraft shell bursts from wartime experience, said the pattern looked like a large exhaust trail or smoke burst moving westerly against a light wind. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 166–169](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 150–151](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 2** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:45 p.m. On 2 Aug 1948 at about 19:45 in Columbus, Ohio, attorney Charles Saunders observed a single object for 10 to 15 minutes from the yard of 1337 Clifton Ave. The object was estimated at 20 to 30 feet in diameter, about 1,500 to 2,000 feet altitude, moving south from the north at a constant slow speed of roughly 15 mph. It had a constant grey-black perimeter with a transparent center, made no sound, and at one point hesitated, emitting a thin trail of smoke from the opposite direction of travel. Its apparent form changed from a parallelogram side-view to a circular shape and then back again. Saunders produced four sketches. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 156–159](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 140–141](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 2** _(cluster ×3)_ — 7:50 p.m. On 2 Aug 1948 at about 7:50 PM in Columbus, Ohio, Mrs. Jaymes Saunders observed a single dark object in a cloudless sky after neighborhood children pointed north; her husband Charles Saunders also witnessed it. The object was first oblong, later circular for much of the sighting, and repeatedly changed from oblong to circular and back while traveling slowly and regularly from north to south, then toward the southwest over roughly 15 minutes. It appeared about a half mile high, about the size of a small plane, emitted no sound, and on one occasion trailed smoke from its rear. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 160](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 142–143](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 3** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 3 Aug 1948 at sunset, about 25 km northwest of Moscow, a ground-based American newspaper correspondent saw one long, narrow, wingless, apparently metallic object at very high altitude. It shone brightly, likely from reflected sunlight, moved southwest-to-northeast at high but not excessive speed, made no sound, and was later discussed as possibly a rigid airship, jet, conventional airplane, or dirigible. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 201–202](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 175–176](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 4** — 2:00 a.m. At North Powder, Oregon, two observers reported a green UAP seen on 4 August between 0200 and 0300, traveling from northwest to southeast at about 5500 feet along a horizontal path. The object was described as softball-sized, moving at roughly 720 mph, and it extinguished/disappeared without sound or trail noted. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 4** — 2:30 a.m. On 4 Aug 1948 between 2 and 3 A.M., J. B. McCullough, driving south on U.S. Highway 30 about 2.5 miles north of North Powder, Oregon, observed a single green fireball-like object in the sky. He described it as moving northwest to southeast on a horizontal line, then arcing downward over an estimated 5.5-mile trajectory at about a 90-degree arc, with a fluorescent glow and little or no meteor-like tail, before extinguishing itself. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 133–134](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, August 5** — 1:00 a.m. On 5 August 1948 at about 0100 hours, James Amaral, a former Air Force pilot, was traveling by automobile between Parrott, Georgia and Richland, Georgia when he observed a brilliant blue-white streak of light moving in apparent level flight on a heading of approximately 250 degrees. The object was estimated at about 20,000 to 35,000 feet altitude, lasted 5 to 10 seconds, and was followed by a several-mile-long trail of sparks that persisted for roughly 45 seconds to 1 minute. Amaral described it as rocket-like and said it resembled a burst of power applied to a rocket or rocket ship. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 156](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 12:10 a.m. At about 0010 on 10 Aug, two observers at Killeen Base, Texas, reported an oval blue object with a head-sized apparent size and a train or trail, moving from east to west on a 30-degree downward angle. The sighting lasted about 3 to 4 seconds, made no sound, and then disappeared suddenly. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 8:30 p.m. On 10 Aug at 20:30, a single unknown-reliability observer at Killeen Base, Texas reported a white object resembling a comet, with a train or trail, moving from north to south at about 30 degrees above the horizon. The sighting lasted about 5 seconds, made no sound, and the object disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 8:35 p.m. On 10 Aug at Camp Hood, Texas, a single unknown observer reported a white round object with a tail, resembling a large flare, moving west to east at about 30 ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 38–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 8:40 p.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single observer watched an orange object with a white tail move north-to-south about 30 degrees above the horizon in a mostly horizontal path with a slight arc for 3 to 5 seconds; it looked like a rocket or 20 mm tracer ammo, had no sound, and gradually dimmed before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 39–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 9:00 p.m. At Killeen Base, Texas, a single witness at about 2100 on 10 Aug observed a bright orange, reddish ball with a long fiery tail moving southward about 30 degrees above the horizon. It continued climbing at roughly a 30-degree angle until it disappeared after about 5 seconds; no sound was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 9:00 p.m. One observer at Camp Hood, Texas reported four brief nighttime observations on 10 August at 2100, 2120, 2215, and 2250 of round objects with orange-white coloration and orange trails moving at great speed, three with an almost horizontal course and one nearly vertical, then going out like a light. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 37–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 10** — 10:40 p.m. On 10 Aug at 2240 near Camp Hood, Texas, a single unknown-reliability observer saw a round light-red object with a jagged trail travel straight from north to south at 8,000 feet or more on a gradual incline for about 2-3 seconds. It was much larger than an evening star, made no sound, changed color from light red to light yellow, and burned up and disintegrated. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 11** _(cluster ×2)_ — 12:00 p.m. On 11 Aug 1948, at a back yard in Hamel, Minnesota, two child witnesses reported a small dull-gray round metallic object descending from the northwest, settling gently on the ground a few feet from them, spinning and making steam-whistle-like noises, then shooting straight up, halting, and maneuvering around tree branches and telephone wires before accelerating northeast. The object reportedly left a dented spot on the ground and was later covered by a washtub. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 174–177](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 158–159](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 11** — 8:30 p.m. On 11 August at Camp Hood, Texas, seven observers reported a white flash-like object moving from north to south about 45-60 degrees above the horizon in generally level flight. The object was star-sized, had a train or trail, made no sound, lasted only 3-5 seconds, appeared faster than any plane observed, and went out like a light. The report is part of a Department of War / AFSWP-era compilation of late-1940s UAP cases. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 12** — 12:10 a.m. At Killeen Base, Texas, two unknown observers reported a bluish-white object around 45 degrees above the horizon moving due west in an arc, with straight-and-level segments combined with ascent and descent. The object left a train or trail, was described as a broken circle resembling a signal flare, lasted about 10 seconds, made no sound, and vanished suddenly. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 14** — 9:35 p.m. On 14 Aug at 21:35, a single reliable observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a round reddish-orange object larger than Venus that appeared at about 25° altitude and disappeared at 12° while moving 10-15° off vertical. The object was seen for about 2 seconds, left no sound, and was described as producing a train or trail before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 19** — 10:50 a.m. On 19 Aug 1948 at 1050 Eastern, Lt Col John Waugh reported from Godman Air Force Base, Kentucky, a bright silver, spherical object appearing stationary and reflecting sunlight. The object was estimated at 30,000 to 40,000 feet, moved slowly southwest, and was tracked visually by theodolite for about two hours. An F-51 from Standiford AFB was dispatched but could not locate it; later the same day Maj. Mitchel relayed that the object was identified as Venus, a conclusion reportedly verified by MCI. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 33–34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, August 21** — 9:15 p.m. On 21 Aug at Nogales, Arizona, five observers reported a dull orange wafer-shaped UAP moving horizontally over the Earth's surface at low level and then high level, with an apparent size like a volleyball and a speed estimated at ten times that of jet planes. The object was seen for about 10 seconds, made no sound and left no train or trail before disappearing in space. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 26** — 1:45 p.m. On 26 Aug at 1345, a single reliable observer reported a brownish hazy triangular object with round edges over Davis-Monthan AFB, Arizona, moving from SE to NW at about 50,000 ft for roughly 60 seconds. It was seen as a small object against the sky, had no train or trail, made no sound, and faded from view while traveling at a terrific rate of speed. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 41–42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, August 29** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:30 a.m. On 29 Aug 1948 at about 05:30 in Maplewood, Ohio, farmer Mr. Faby Niswonger reported watching a large silver sphere emerge from behind nearby woods and float across his farm. While airborne, it appeared to unravel or shed a silver substance that drifted downward; under observation it disintegrated in mid-air. No photo or sketch was obtained. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 196–198](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 171–172](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, August 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:00 p.m. On 30 Aug 1948 at about 20:00 in Los Angeles, S/Sgt Leroy H. Estes observed a single silver object traveling west to east in level flight over the city. He described it as rocket-like and shaped like a V-2 rocket fuselage but much larger than a B-29, at an estimated altitude well over 20,000 feet and speed well over 700 mph. The object made no sound and left a blue exhaust trail; no photos, sketches, or other effects were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 192–193](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 166–167](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, September 5** — 2:02 p.m. Three crews of the 307th Bomb Group reported an unidentified aircraft off the west coast of Holland at 1402Z on 1948-09-05. The object was seen at about 30,000 feet, initially cruising at normal jet speed on a heading of 120 degrees, then producing smoke and condensation trails before making a sudden acceleration and climb. Observers generally agreed it appeared to be a single jet-propelled aircraft, possibly with rocket assistance and greater-than-normal reserve power, but it never came within identification range. ([341_110448_Records_Relating_to_the_Collection_and_, p. 4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/341_110448_records_relating_to_the_collection_and_dissemination_of_intelligence_1948-1955-ts_cont_no.2_2-5300-2-5399.pdf>))

**1948, September 5** — 3:00 p.m. On 5 Sept 1948 between 3:00 and 3:30 P.M., Mrs. Elma McDaniel and her daughter Mildred reported an object they could not actually see in Kentwood, Louisiana; they noticed only an unusual sound and believed something very high and moving west at terrific speed was overhead. Subsequent notes say nearby neighbors heard nothing unusual and later characterized Mrs. McDaniel as having a vivid imagination. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 14–15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 9** — A 1948 Project SIGN correspondence package and FBI Laboratory report describe soil taken from a depression allegedly caused by a 'flying saucer' about two feet in diameter and one foot thick that reportedly settled gently, rebounded to about twenty feet, and continued on its way; FBI testing later found only ordinary pebbles, sand, and dirt with no unusual metals, heat damage, or radioactive substances. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 56–57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, September 15** — 12:25 a.m. At 00:25 on 15 Sep in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single unidentified object was seen about 30 degrees above the horizon for roughly 6 seconds. It was round, baseball-sized, and yellow-red in color, made no sound, and then burst and appeared to disintegrate. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, September 16** — 2:30 a.m. At 0230 on 16 Sep, a single reliable observer reported an orange ball-shaped object over Los Alamos, New Mexico, moving west to east at about 3,000 feet. The object was seen for roughly 15 seconds, left no train or trail, and was described as larger than a falling star and moving faster than an airplane but slower than a falling star. It dimmed and then disappeared completely; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, September 18** — 5:00 p.m. On 18 September 1948 at about 1700 CST, a ground observer in Shreveport, Louisiana reported a single aluminum-colored, round metallic object seen with binoculars about 3 miles southeast of the city. The object was estimated at roughly 12 feet in diameter, laterally about 2.5 miles away and 50 degrees above the horizon, drifting south in smooth horizontal flight at an estimated 100–150 mph for 10–15 seconds, with no sound, no exhaust trail, and no visible clouds below it. The report notes a possible connection to a raincoat release from Nashville Field, Louisiana, and suggests the sighting may have been that released object at altitude. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 19** — 9:00 a.m. On 19 Sep at 0900, two unknown observers in Tucson, Arizona reported a grayish-white object moving north at an apparent altitude of about 4000 feet. They estimated it to be 2 to 3 feet across, traveling around 600 mph, with no sound or trail, and it faded from view after about 2 minutes. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 42–43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, September 22** — 3:30 p.m. On 22 Sept 1948 about 1530 EST, Lt. Martin G. Rubisch, serving as co-pilot on a C-47 en route from Eglin Auxiliary Field #2 to Turner AF Base near Albany, Georgia, observed a single metallic, shiny object approaching at an angle while the aircraft was at about 2,000 ft. He estimated the object at 5-10 ft in size, about 500 ft lower and 1/2 mile away, traveling roughly 250 mph on a level path with no visible wings or rudder. No one else aboard saw it; the case file notes the witness had excellent ability to judge size, speed and color but also records comments from superiors that he tended to exaggerate. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 37–38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 23** — 9:30 a.m. On 23 September 1948 at 0930, ground observer Mr. John C. Fairchild, a building superintendent in Santa Fe, New Mexico, reported a single bright silvery oblong/egg-shaped object with a bluish streak or exhaust-like appearance to the rear, moving on a straight NE-SW course at an estimated 700-800 mph and about 30,000 feet altitude. He first noticed it by a glint of light, described a possible sunflower-like feature near the tail, heard no sound, and said it faded from view in clear CAVU weather. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 8–9](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 23** — 9:45 a.m. On 23 Sept 1948 at about 09:45 local time near Santa Fe, New Mexico, an experienced pilot and neon sign manufacturer observed a white, perfectly flat-looking circular object in daylight while watching another aircraft. He first noticed it glinting in the sun and judged it to be at roughly 25,000 to 30,000 feet altitude and about 100 to 150 feet in diameter, appearing like a dime or coin seen edge-on/at a distance. The object showed very little movement, seemed to remain in nearly the same position for 15 to 20 minutes, and may have traced a semi-circular path from south to east to northeast, though this was noted as possibly an optical illusion. No sound, exhaust, projections, or cloud effects were reported. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 6–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 23** — 12:00 p.m. On 23 Sep 1948 around noon near Castro's Ranch east of San Pablo, California, retired Army colonel and meat inspector Horace S. Makins observed an unknown object while watching a low-flying Army bomber. The object was seen above the bomber moving nearly due east at very high speed. He described it as about the size of a four-engine bomber, with an undulating amoeba-like outline, a spherical center, and 3 forward and 2 aft appendages that appeared to flap or oscillate while the center remained stable. It was translucent dirty gray, looked partly like canvas, made no sound, left no exhaust or trail, did not reflect or emit light, and disappeared from sight very quickly. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 10–11](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 23** — 12:30 p.m. On 23 Sep 1948 around 12:30, Mr. Sylvester Bentham observed a single buff/gray rectangular object with sharp corners and darker vertical rib-like lines flying very high over Castro's Ranch, about 4 miles east of San Pablo and 4.1 miles south of Pinole, California. Col. Eakins/Eskins drew Bentham's attention to it; both watched for at least 3 minutes as it traveled northwest, then turned north and gradually faded from view without noise, exhaust, or trail. Bentham compared it to a translucent vegetable crate and estimated it at roughly 2 x 1 x 1 inches from his viewpoint. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 12–13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, September 27** — 1:30 a.m. At Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single observer at 0130 on 27 Sep reported a dark blue object traveling from south to north about 20 degrees above the horizon. It was described as conical with a tail about twice its diameter, resembling a sky rocket, leaving a trail, making a gentle arc toward the earth, and burning out after about 2 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 42–43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))
