**1948, September 27** — 1:30 a.m. At Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single observer reported a green, round object seen about 45 degrees above the horizon moving tangent to the earth; it lasted about one second, had no train or sound, appeared fist-sized at arm's length, and burnt out after moving roughly 20 degrees in that second. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, September 27** — 3:00 a.m. At Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single unknown witness reported a round bright blue-to-white object traveling from the southeast about 45 degrees above the horizon, apparently moving tangent to the earth. The object was observed for about 4 seconds, showed no trail or sound, and was about softball-sized at an estimated 300 yards before it was obscured by a building. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, September 27** — 3:00 a.m. Single witness reported a round yellow object near Sandia Base, New Mexico, at about 0300 on 27 Sep. The object was seen to the north at roughly 10 degrees above the horizon, had a train or trail, lasted only 3 to 4 seconds, made no sound, and died out while moving at about the same rate as a shooting star. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, September 30** — 10:57 p.m. At Sandia Base, New Mexico, a lone observer reported a yellow-orange aerial object traveling from south to west at about 55 degrees to the horizon. It was slightly larger than a shooting star, left no trail, made no sound, lasted only 2 to 3 seconds, and was estimated to be moving at 200 to 300 mph. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 1** — 5:40 a.m. On 1 Oct 1948 at about 0540, E. B. Williams reported a single cone-shaped, white-hot object over Ascension Parish near New Orleans, Louisiana, moving east in straight and level flight. He described it as about the length of a passenger aircraft, appearing encased in flame with red flames toward the rear, silent, with no trail, no visible means of propulsion, and no lighting effect on the ground; it was observed for roughly 10 seconds before disappearing behind trees. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 3–5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 1** _(cluster ×4)_ — 9:00 p.m. On 1 Oct 1948 at about 2100 hrs, pilot George F. Gorman observed a small clear white round light over Fargo, North Dakota, while flying an F-51 in the Fargo vicinity. The object remained in view for 27 minutes, performed several left turns, one right turn, dives, climbs, and repeated head-on passes, and was described as moving faster than the F-51. At closest approach it appeared as a flat but apparently deep white light about 6 to 8 inches across, with no sound, no exhaust, and no visible structure; it later climbed steeply out of sight. Investigators noted possible conventional explanations had been discredited, and Gorman believed the maneuvers showed intent. ([DOW-UAP-D088, pp. 208–209](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_101-172, pp. 177–178](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, October 1** — 9:00 p.m. On 1 Oct 1948 at about 2100 hrs, Manuel E. Johnson, an assistant airport traffic controller on duty at Fargo Airport control tower, observed a single clear white light object from the tower, roughly 1-2 miles away at an estimated altitude of 2000-2500 ft. The object was seen for several seconds in CAVU weather, moved extremely fast on a NNW heading, made one turn, and disappeared in a straight line; no sound, trail, or definite outline was noted. ([DOW-UAP-D088, p. 207](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D088_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_101-172.pdf>))

**1948, October 2** — 9:10 p.m. On 2 Oct at 2110, a single reliable observer near Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a bright green UAP at an apparent altitude of 20,000-30,000 feet that moved upward and then downward, left a train or trail, and was visible for about 3 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 41–44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 4** — 2:30 p.m. On 4 October 1948 near Dayton, Ohio, Maj. C. M. Smartt, a pilot and ordnance officer flying a C-47 at about 6,000 feet around 1430 local time, reported seeing three or four small teardrop-shaped metallic objects, silver or silvery white, moving very fast in a general easterly direction and gradual descent. They appeared one at a time at intervals of three or four minutes, came close enough to force the pilot and co-pilot to duck to avoid collision, and disappeared under the wing of the aircraft after about a second in view. The report notes no sound, no clear exhaust, and only a slight tail on one object, with the investigator highlighting discrepancies between this later account and an earlier report that mentioned more objects and differing flight directions. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 21–22](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 6** — 5:45 p.m. At 1745 on 6 October, a single observer in Mescalero, New Mexico, reported a dark green round object described as a big ball, seen about 15 1/2 degrees above the horizon. It descended in a slight arc and disappeared behind the hills after about 30 seconds; no sound or trail was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 42–44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 6** — 5:50 p.m. On 6 Oct at 17:50, a single reliable observer in Albuquerque, New Mexico, reported a greenish-white object moving E to W in a curved descent approaching vertical. It resembled a flare, had no train or trail, lasted about 1 second, and disappeared abruptly. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 3–4** — 5:50 p.m. On 6 October at 1750, a single reliable observer reported a greenish-white round object seen near Wagon Hound, New Mexico, moving from east to west for about 3 to 4 seconds. It appeared about 1.5 to 2 inches in diameter at arm's length, was described as traveling at about meteor speed, had no trail, made no sound, and went out like an electric light. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 6** — 5:58 p.m. On 6 Oct at 1758, a single reliable observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a circular bluish-white object, possibly with a light green tinge, moving from east to west in a straight angular descent. The object was seen for about 4 seconds at an apparent size about three times Jupiter or Venus, with no train, trail, or sound, and then disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 6** — 5:58 p.m. A single observer reported a brief nighttime sighting over Albuquerque, New Mexico, of a brilliant green, teardrop-shaped object traveling NE to SW for about 10 seconds. The object had no trail, was seen to arc over at the end of its course, and then seemed to burn out and disappear. The report is part of a larger Department of War collection of late-1940s New Mexico UAP sightings. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 6** — 6:00 p.m. On 6 Oct at about 1800, a single unknown-reliability observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a green, round UAP seen for 5 to 8 seconds at an apparent altitude/course noted as 4°20' to 7°5'. The object was described as baseball-sized at arm's length, with no train or trail and no sound; no disappearance details were recorded. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:07 a.m. At 0107 on 10 Oct, a single uncertain-reliability observer at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a round green object traveling from north to northeast about 45° above the horizon. The object was seen for roughly 15 seconds, appeared fist-sized at arm's length, had no train or trail, made no sound, moved slowly, and then burned out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 45–46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 10** — 1:07 a.m. At 0107 on 10 Oct, a lone observer reported a greenish-blue round object with red sparks trailing near Sandia Base, New Mexico. The object was seen about 45 degrees above the horizon, moving SW to NE and executing dives before fading out after roughly 4 seconds. No sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 10** — 4:06 a.m. A single reliable observer reported a small, brilliant white unidentified object over Los Alamos, New Mexico, traveling from southeast to northeast at an estimated 3,000 feet above the observation point. The object was seen for about 4–5 seconds, had a train or trail, made no sound, appeared slower than a meteor, and then disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 11** — 1:00 p.m. On 11 October 1948 at Neubiberg Air Force Base, Germany, six military personnel of the 86th Fighter Wing observed a single silver, very shiny round object in daylight for about 45 minutes. It appeared stationary on a 50° heading from the base, at an estimated altitude of 40,000 ft, and seemed to grow smaller as if rising. An AF-80 aircraft passing between the object and the ground observers appeared small by comparison. The object vanished behind a thin layer of clouds. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 39–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 12** — 11:15 a.m. On 12 Oct at 11:15, three observers at Roswell, New Mexico reported a white/silver round, probably elliptical object at about 3,500 feet moving from south to northeast in a smooth arc for roughly 45–60 seconds. They judged it faster than a jet aircraft; it left no trail, made no sound, and went beyond visual range. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 46–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 12** — 1:40 p.m. On 12 Oct at 13:40 near Tucson, Arizona, four reliable observers reported a white or silver round object traveling from northeast to southwest at an apparent altitude of 30,000 feet. It was seen for about 15 seconds, estimated around 50-100 feet in diameter, moving horizontally at roughly 1,000 mph before fading from view. No sound or trail was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 13** — 5:25 a.m. On 13 October 1948 at about 5:25 a.m., while duck hunting about one mile south and west of Rivers, Manitoba, Mr. G. W. Chapman and his two brothers observed a small ball of fire moving west to east for roughly 12 to 15 seconds. The object appeared low in the sky, grew to about the size of a small football, left a short sparking trail, then seemed to drop slightly and explode into glowing fragments. The witnesses concluded it was probably a meteor, though they noted the flight path and duration seemed unusual. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 105–106](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1948, October 14** — 2:10 p.m. On 14 Oct at about 1410-1415, a single unknown observer reported a brief sighting over Los Alamos, New Mexico of an object moving from south to north with a green-and-silver front and pale-blue rear, leaving a train or trail and disappearing after only 3-4 seconds; no sound was heard. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 47–48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 14** — 2:20 p.m. On 14 Oct at 1420, three reliable observers near Los Alamos, New Mexico, saw a round object or trail moving west to east at an estimated altitude of 20,000 feet in level flight just above the horizon. It appeared as a 12-inch disc with a greenish blue-white trail, lasted about 2 seconds, made no sound, and burned out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 45–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 15** — On 15 Oct 1948 at night, an F-61 pilot and radar observer near the Fukuoka area, Japan, reported one possibly two unidentified aircraft detected first on airborne radar and also seen visually in moonlight as silhouetted forms. The object(s) showed high-speed evasive behavior, rapid acceleration, and near-vertical climb or dive beyond radar elevation limits, repeatedly disappearing from airborne radar while apparently tracking the interceptor's position. No exhaust flames or trails were seen. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 25–26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 16** — On the morning of 1948-10-16, while deer hunting, Mr. Fred Nash heard a steady throbbing/purring sound and looked up to see a flattened football-shaped object about 500 feet overhead traveling in a straight northerly course slightly west toward Salt Lake City, Utah. He described it as silver and black with a silver stripe down the center, roughly 9 inches by 6 inches by 3 inches in apparent size, with a rear opening and a paddlewheel-like motion, sounding as if jet propelled but unlike any aircraft or gasoline engine. The object remained visible for about 4 seconds or longer. Investigators noted Nash appeared generally reliable but his distance estimation was faulty. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 17** — 4:10 p.m. On 17 Oct 1948 at 16:10 near Battery Point in Crescent City, California, Mrs. Elise Hanggi (also spelled Henggi), a housewife, observed a small silver elliptic object reflecting brilliant sunlight-like flashes while flying very fast southerly at an estimated high altitude. She first noticed it as a glint of light, watched it for about 2 minutes, and saw it make a sharp 45-degree bank to the right before resuming course and disappearing out to sea. No sound or exhaust trail was reported; the object was noted for its brightness and presumed metallic appearance. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 65–66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 17** — 4:10 p.m. On 17 Oct 1948 at about 16:10, Ernest Haley, a dry cleaner in Crescent City, California, watched a small silver metallic object resembling a small plane move southerly southeast of Battery Point at an estimated altitude between 20,000 and 25,000 feet. A glint of reflected sunlight drew his attention; the object remained visible for 4 to 5 minutes, made a 45-degree right bank and turn, flashed brightly as sunlight struck its topside, then turned sharply south and gradually disappeared. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 63–64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 17** — 4:10 p.m. At about 16:10 on 17 Oct 1948, fisherman Andrew N. Siler heard what he first took to be a plane and looked up to see a silver-colored, egg-shaped object resembling a small blimp moving south at an estimated 300 mph. He reported no visible wings, tail, or smoke, and no sound from the object itself; the sound that drew his attention came from a nearby small Piper Cub-type plane. He did not continue watching to see where the object went. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 17** — 4:10 p.m. On 17 Oct 1948 at about 1610Z near Crescent City, California, ground observer Edgar W. Allen reported a single silver, egg-shaped object with fins/tail, resembling a nickel-plated bullet or plane with no visible wings. He first noticed it by a glint of light, estimated it at about 10,000 ft altitude, moving southerly at roughly 300 mph, and watched it for 2-4 minutes as it made one short bank before resuming course and fading from view. No sound, smoke, exhaust trail, cloud effects, or environmental impacts were reported. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 59–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 18** — 5:05 p.m. On 18 October 1948, Major Robert C. Drum, a rated USAF pilot in Honolulu, observed a bright silver, round-or-elliptical metallic object moving steadily northeast at about 200 mph between cloud layers, intermittently sighting it four times over roughly 10 minutes with the longest view lasting about 10 seconds; no lights, flame, exhaust, or sound were noted, and Drum stated it was not a conventional craft. His wife and 8-year-old daughter reported the same observations. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 16–17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 20** — 5:50 a.m. On 20 October 1948 at about 0550 hours, Lawrence A. Hansen and Harold Oliver reported a single orange flame-colored round object with a tail over Minneapolis, Minnesota, seen from two locations. The object was described as about 6 feet in size, moving south-southwest to north-northeast at roughly three times jet aircraft speed, maintaining a mostly straight horizontal course at an estimated altitude of 5,000 to 10,000 feet before disappearing behind a cloud bank to the north-northeast. Witnesses noted a blurred front as if enveloped in smoke, a fiery orange main body, and a tail resembling boiling sheets of flame; there was no sound. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 29–30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 20** — 6:00 a.m. On 20 Oct 1948 at about 0600 near Winona, Minnesota, Dr. F. T. Benoit and Paul Zenk reported a single slowly moving object seen from the ground. They described it as about 25 feet long and 8 feet wide, appearing as a 'bunch of sparks with a tail,' with a nature of luminosity described as sparks. It reportedly broke up into numerous bright particles and disappeared in that manner. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 27–28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 21** — 9:30 p.m. On 21 Oct at about 2130, a single witness near Roswell, New Mexico reported a very bright white unidentified object moving from southeast to northwest at roughly 30 degrees above the horizon. The object was observed for 20-30 minutes, made no sound, left no train or trail, and its manner of disappearance was not recorded. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 22** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:28 a.m. At 0228 on 22 Oct, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico, saw a bright green object moving NW to SE on the horizon for about 2 seconds. It was described as about four times the size of a flare, traveling at an estimated 150 mph, and it disappeared behind a hill without sound or trail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 46–48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:45 p.m. On 24 Oct 1948 at 1545 CST, Capt. Glenn Huber, the Base S-3 Officer at Fort Riley, Kansas, observed an unidentified metallic object from a ground position about 10 miles from Junction City. He reported it moving westward at roughly aircraft speed, at an estimated altitude above 7,000 feet, then turning south and accelerating to a speed he judged greater than any aircraft he had ever seen. Visibility was excellent and the wind was south-southwest. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 24** — 5:45 p.m. On 24 Oct 1948 in the early evening around 5:45 P.M., L. O. Sanders and his wife, traveling by car on U.S. Highway #52 near Moorhead/North Fargo, Minnesota/North Dakota, observed a single light moving east to west in good visibility. Sanders first noticed it to his right and pointed it out to his wife, who also clearly saw it. They estimated it moved horizontally at two to three times the speed of a plane, with a slight rise in its path, and concluded it was not a meteor because it remained visible too long and did not descend. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 43–44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 24** — On 1948-10-24 at night, a single unverified observer reported a green, star-shaped light/object over Phoenix, Arizona traveling east to west at a very slow pace for about 75 hours (as transcribed in the source table). The object reportedly became very bright and fell apart/disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, October 29** — 8:12 a.m. On 29 Oct 1948, a radar operator at AAF Goose Bay, Labrador reported an unidentified object on Ground Control Approach scope at 0812Z, 0819Z, and 0916Z. The object was plotted in the Hamilton River area and later near Lake Melville/Dock Area, appeared aircraft-like and aligned with runway approach paths, moved at an estimated 25 to 30 mph, and was seen clearly at about two miles distance. The operator brought in a second operator to verify the returns; no sound, color, or altitude could be determined. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 35–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 30** — 2:30 p.m. On 30 Oct 1948 at 1430 PST, Major Morris and Lt. Kunzman, flying an F-82 near Grays Harbor, Washington, reported seeing a compact group of 10 to 20 translucent, egg-shaped objects 15 miles northwest of the coast. The objects first appeared yellow, then white, moved in a westerly line, later changed course northward and disappeared by seeming to dissolve; no sound, exhaust, aggressive behavior, or clear estimate of size or speed was obtained. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 57–58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 31** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:30 a.m. On 31 Oct 1948 at approximately 0230Z, PFC Robert R. Anderson, standing on the ground at Army Air Base in the Azores, observed a single orange, round object larger than a star overhead after it was pointed out by Staff Sgt. Joseph J. Barnett. The object was seen moving northerly at an estimated 800 mph, apparently swinging forward and backward while still maintaining forward motion, with no sound and no visible vapor trail, before disappearing behind a cloud after about 8 minutes (the summary elsewhere notes about 2 minutes). ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 69–70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 31** — 3:00 a.m. On 31 Oct 1948, at about 0300Z-0330Z, a USAF ground observer at Army Air Base, Ameres, reported one light-like object overhead, white with a yellowish tint and shaped like a light bulb. It was seen moving northerly at an estimated 25-30 mph while steadily gaining altitude, then disappeared behind a cloud; no sound or vapor trail was noted. An A-2 review later judged it to be a weather balloon with a light swinging from it. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 71–72](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October 31** — 3:45 p.m. On 31 October 1948 at 1545Z, a GCA operator at Goose Bay, Labrador observed a single target on PPI Scope No. 1. The blip appeared about 3 miles south of the field, traveled southwest at approximately 25 mph, and eventually went into ground clutter at about 6-8 miles. The sighting was verified by Base Commander Col. C. D. Jones and Group Commander Capt. Verner, both present in the unit. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 49–50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October** — 12:00 p.m. In October 1948, between 1200 and 1430 hours, men at Neubiberg Air Force Base reported a single black unidentified flying object seen from a housing project about 2 miles west of the base. The object was observed in daylight, appeared to be moving on a southwest course, and was described as round in visible circumference though its overall shape and size could not be determined. Witnesses said it seemed faster than an F-47 type aircraft and remained in sight for about 2 minutes. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 45–46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, October** — 10:20 p.m. In October 1948, while the S/S Gulfport was at sea, the ship's master/captain observed a single bright round object about one-quarter the size of a full moon at 74°40' West, 36°42' North. The object had a distinct bright center, gradually darkened as daylight faded, traveled southeast, and was tracked by bearing changes from 350° at 2220 GMT to 358°40.18 at 2230 GMT and 050°57.05 at 2254 GMT before disappearing at 2310 GMT. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 23–24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 1** — 9:45 p.m. On 1 Nov 1948 at 2145Z, two GCA operators at Fort McAndrew, Newfoundland reported a single target appearing on the GCA radar scope about 3.75 miles from the station on a bearing of 123°. The blip traveled on an approximate heading of 249°, covered about 4 miles in 4 minutes 7 seconds, and disappeared at about 7.5 miles on a bearing of 182°. The operators estimated a speed of roughly 60 mph (about a mile a minute) and stated the object seemed too large to be a bird but too small to be an aircraft. The report is radar-based, with no visual description beyond size comparison and no identified conventional explanation. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 51–52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 3** — 10:40 a.m. On 3 Nov 1948 at 10:40 A.M. near Andrews Field, a Coast Guard pilot in aircraft #37821 and two 82d Fighter Wing pilots observed what was first reported as an unidentified object, later determined to be a cluster of eight MIT balloons used for cosmic-ray research. The object was seen at high altitude, resembled an inflated intestine, appeared roughly 5 to 6 times higher than wide, moved at an estimated 2 mph on a heading of about 45°, and the case was ultimately closed after definitive identification. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 47–48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 3 or 4** — 10:00 p.m. On 3 or 4 November 1948 and again on 23 November 1948, around 2200 hours, Col. W. P. Hayes reported seeing a single bright, fireball-like object while driving on Highway 60 about 10 miles east of Vaughn, New Mexico. He described it as a small ball about a foot in diameter, glowing like a miniature sun, descending slowly from roughly 500 feet to 100-200 feet above the ground before bursting in a spray of reddish fragments that fell like sparks and extinguished. No sound was heard despite the close approach. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 3 or 4** — 10:00 p.m. At about 2200 on 3 or 4 November, a single reliable observer in Vaughn, New Mexico reported a bright white, round object larger than a basketball at an estimated altitude of 400 to 500 feet. It descended slowly in a vertical manner, produced no sound, and then exploded/disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 4** — USAFE intelligence summarized recurring flying saucer reports and cited one recent observation of an object hovering over Neubiberg Air Base for about thirty minutes. The report also relayed a Swedish intelligence discussion in which a technical expert allegedly saw an object crash or land in a lake, after which a salvage team found a previously uncharted crater on the lake floor. ([341_110448_Records_Relating_to_the_Collection_and_, p. 5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/341_110448_records_relating_to_the_collection_and_dissemination_of_intelligence_1948-1955-ts_cont_no.2_2-5300-2-5399.pdf>))

**1948, November 5** — 4:35 a.m. On 1948-11-05 around 04:35-04:55 in Richmond, Indiana, sports editor Dale Stevens observed a brilliant white round light larger than a star, first called to his attention by telephone reports from a woman and then a man who described a flare-like light in the east. Stevens watched the object from The Palladium-Item building, first from a window and then from the roof, where it appeared stationary for about 20 minutes, flickered, and dimmed as low clouds moved in. He later photographed the light; investigators compared the event against possible stars, parachute flares, and a comet, but no firm conventional identification is stated in the report. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 53–54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 6** — 11:57 a.m. On 6 Nov 1948 at 11:57 over the radar site at Wakkanai, Japan, a ground-based radarscope operator saw a radar target immediately above the station that remained in the ground clutter area for 1 hour 5 minutes. The target was never seen visually because dense overcast prevented observation, but on radar it sometimes appeared as a single aircraft and at other times as two aircraft, giving the impression of two fighter planes dog-fighting while continuously changing course. It was estimated to travel between 160 and 240 mph and was later evaluated by the reporting authority as likely Soviet electronic reconnaissance, possibly a Soviet 'Ferret.' ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 55–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 8** — 4:40 p.m. Weather-observer Solera in Panama reported that at 1640 on 8 Nov 1948, while making a GCT weather report at sunrise, he saw what appeared to be a comet in the southeast sky: a long cone of light ending in a star-like object larger than Venus. The phenomenon remained visible to the naked eye for about 40 minutes, fading as sunrise brightened until it was obliterated, and the tail reportedly showed about four grades of intensity. The report was later compared against a contemporaneous article about the newly discovered comet observed in Australia and in the southern/western United States. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 77–78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 8** — 6:50 p.m. On 8 Nov 1948 at 18:50 EST, civilian dispatcher and former USAFR Capt. Edmund J. Cisek observed a single pale luminous disc-like object from ground level at Newark AF Base, New Jersey. The object, about moon-sized and one-third the moon's brightness, with little or no depth, moved rapidly in a shallow arc from north-northwest toward south-southeast, passed out of sight over another hanger after about one second, and was heard to make no sound. Its altitude was estimated at 5,000 to 6,000 feet under clear moonlit conditions. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 73–76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 10** — 5:00 a.m. Around 5:00 A.M. on approximately 10 Nov 1948, a ground observer in Boston reported three fast-moving, single-engine-plane-like objects over the Charles River Basin. The objects were first noticed because they were flying fast, with the middle one weaving back and forth. No lights were seen, but city lights made them visible; no sound was heard. They were described as looking like single-engine planes and traveling east to west. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 93](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 11** — 7:30 p.m. On 1948-11-11 at 19:30, a witness in Hood River, Oregon reported a blue and white flashing object described as a flash with a like shunt. The report notes sound: yes, with a flash-type sound, but provides no further detail on direction, altitude, course, duration, or number of observers. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1948, November 12** — 1:00 p.m. On 12 Nov 1948 between 1300 and 1400, Sgt. Fredrick M. Wright of the 18th Maintenance Sq, 18th Main & Supply Gp, while on the ground at the northeast portion of Clark Air Force Base in the Philippine Islands, observed a single snow-white object that appeared first as a white speck and then lengthened into a plane-like craft with no apparent tail assembly. He reported intermittent views through cloud cover as it flew around the base with no definite heading, seeming to enter and break out of a large cloud formation six times, leaving what looked like a white exhaust trail or 'sky writing' and producing a single roar. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 81–82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 14** — 10:43 a.m. On 14 Nov 1948 at 1043, an unidentified aircraft was observed over Army Observation Post No. 1 in South Korea at about 5,000 feet, moving east to west. The same aircraft was also sighted from Army Observation Post No. 2 and additional listed observation points; it circled post #2 and then headed north. The object was described as twin-engine bomber-like in shape and construction, and the document states it was believed definitely to have been Soviet. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 91–92](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 16** — 1:18 p.m. On 16 Nov 1948 at 1318 hours, Marie S. Green, a housewife in Demarest, New Jersey, reported seeing a single ball-like object about 1880 feet up moving north to south for approximately one minute from her house. She described it as aluminum or silvery with mixed dark coloration, metallic in apparent construction. No sound, lights, or other effects were noted, and the case file states no further investigation was made because only one person witnessed the phenomenon. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 137](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 17** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:18 p.m. On 17 Nov 1948 at 1418 Z, while a crew flying from Edmonton toward Kittigazuit, N.W.T. at 7,000 ft MSL over Peace River, Alberta, observed a single bright flaming orange, egg-shaped object with a tail. The object was estimated at about 18,000 ft, appeared momentarily above broken stratus/overcast clouds, moved southwest in a shallow dive, emitted no sound, and briefly illuminated the sky behind it. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 74–75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>); [342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 121](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1948, November 17** — 2:30 p.m. On 17 Nov 1948 at 14:30, two Army fighter pilots at Clark Air Force Base saw a single white object over the eastern edge of the base that looked like an irregular flak-burst or an aircraft explosion leaving a white smoke column. The object appeared stationary or nearly so at about 30,000 ft, with a white exhaust/smoke trail extending roughly 1 to 10 miles that formed a complete circle or corkscrew loop, then dissipated in about three minutes. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 89–90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 18** — 9:45 p.m. On 18 Nov 1948 at 2145 hours, 2d Lt. Kenwood W. Jackson reported a single oval object with no wings or tail surfaces circling Andrews Air Force Base, Camp Springs, Maryland. Observed from an aircraft, the object showed a dull glow when the pursuing plane switched on its landing light, climbed vertically when approached, dropped behind the plane, and continued circling for about 15 to 18 minutes before heading northeast and climbing rapidly out of sight. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 83–84](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 18** — 10:00 p.m. On 18 Nov 1948 at about 2200 hours, 2d Lt. Henry G. Combs, USAFR, observed a lone moving lighted object east over Andrews Air Force Base and directly over Camp Springs, Maryland. He pursued it in aircraft for about 10 to 12 minutes, reporting an oblong, wingless, tail-less metallic-looking object smaller than a T-6 aircraft, dull gray with one continuous glowing white light and no exhaust flame. The object executed tight turns, near-vertical climbs, and rapid speed changes, repeatedly evading Combs over a circular route between Andrews and the Washington, D.C. lights before heading east toward the coast. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 85–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 18** — On 18 Nov 1948 at Andrews Air Force Base, Camp Springs, Maryland, S/Sgt John J. Kachner (also referred to as Kushner in the narrative) reported hearing a noise and then seeing a shining, light-like object cross the night sky over the base from the direction of the 2238th Engineering Hangar toward Hangar #1. It was described as not very high, with no visible exhaust, sounding similar to a P-47 but about twice as loud; the witness said it did not resemble an aircraft. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 87–88](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 19** — 9:52 p.m. On 19 Nov at 2152, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a vertically moving UAP that appeared green and then yellow, lasted about 2 seconds, and made no sound before disappearing. The report is embedded in a larger Department of War collection of 1948-1950 New Mexico UAP sightings, but this row itself contains only the brief case summary. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 23** — 10:00 p.m. At about 2200 hours on 23 November in Vaughn, New Mexico, a single reliable witness reported a bright white, round object larger than a basketball descending slowly in a vertical manner from an estimated altitude of 400-500 feet. The object made no sound, was observed for an unspecified duration, and then exploded. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 23** — 10:20 p.m. On 23 Nov 1948 at 22:20, Capt. Hugh Slater, an F-80 pilot at Furstenfeldbruck, Germany, observed one possibly two reddish-star-like lights/object moving south across the Munich area, then turning slightly southwest and southeast while climbing and circling to an estimated 27,000-40,000 ft at 200-500 mph. Racecard DF Station initially had no scope contact but later reported an unidentified object matching the sighting; Capt. Darwin R. Addis verified the account. Weather was clear with moonlight, no cloud layers, and ground fog. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 114–115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 27** — 5:30 p.m. A single unknown witness reported an egg-shaped, bright blue-white object over Winslow, Arizona, traveling from east to west about 30 degrees above the horizon. The object carried a visible train or trail, was estimated at arm's length size, and was observed for only 3 to 4 seconds before dwindling out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 27** — 5:49 p.m. On 27 Nov at 17:49, a single reliable witness reported a round UAP over Socorro, New Mexico, moving from east to west in an arc about 10° to 40° above the horizon. The object appeared quite large and pale green to pale blue, left a train or trail, was silent, and faded out gradually after about 5 seconds; it was described as slower than a meteor. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 27** — 5:49 p.m. A lone witness reported a brief nighttime sighting over Albuquerque, New Mexico, of a round blue-white object traveling east to west at a low apparent altitude, descending slopingly and then blinking out, reappearing, and going out again within 1–2 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 27** — 6:00 p.m. A single unknown observer reported a green object over McIntosh, New Mexico, descending vertically from less than 2,000 feet at about 1800 on 27 November. The object lasted about one second, was shaped and sized like a signal flare, made no sound, left no train or trail, and disappeared in the same manner as a falling flare. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, November 29** — 8:00 p.m. On 29 Nov 1948 at about 8:00 PM, a 14-year-old ninth grade student, Doris Croke, standing with two friends at Concord St. and 1st Street in Newburgh, New York, saw a red ball of fire with a long tail traveling horizontally from east to west in the north sky. She described it as a large red ball speeding through the heavens, visible for about two seconds before it went below the horizon, and thought it was a blue-tailed comet. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 108–109](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, November 29** — 9:13 p.m. On 29 Nov 1948 at 21:13 in San Francisco, California, a ground-based observer reported a single yellow flash of light moving rapidly across the sky. The light traveled due north, then turned north-northwest, lasted about 7 seconds, and was judged too fast for a plane; the witness did not think it was a comet or shooting star. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 110–111](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 1** — In early December 1948, Nathan Smith and others at a private airport in the Mojave Desert near Helendale, California found what was initially reported as a flying disk. They photographed it, removed portions, and retained samples, but the object was later traced to an experimental large model kite developed by Claude Leroy Wolfert for Olsen and Rice Manufacturing Company. The FBI was contacted and planned to obtain photographs and samples. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 66–70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 3** — 5:13 p.m. On 3 Dec 1948 at about 17:13 during twilight, George H. Hoffman, riding in a car with Roy E. Statzer near the Dayton, Ohio city limits on Siebenthaler Ave. by Stillwater Bridge, saw a small irregular white-yellow light like a star or rocket pulse appear in the northwest sky, vanish after about half a second, then reappear in an apparently ascending path and vanish again. The observer estimated it about 10 miles away and 15 degrees above the horizon, with no sound, no exhaust, and clear sky around the object. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 95–96](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 3** — 5:15 p.m. On 3 Dec 1948 at about 17:15, Lt. Higar S. Miller, U.S. Navy, observed a single bright yellow-orange flame directly ahead from his car while driving west on Harshmanville Road near Needmore Road/Wagner Ford Road on the outskirts of Dayton, Ohio. The object was seen intermittently over time, appeared to climb at a fairly high rate, and seemed to follow a definite horizontal-and-vertical motion pattern before disappearing overhead, presumably into clouds. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 97–98](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 3** — 8:15 p.m. On 3 December 1948 at about 2015 PST, personnel in the Fairfield-Suisun AFB control tower observed a single brilliant white round light, about 30 inches across and resembling a runway/searchlight, appear roughly 2 miles north of the tower at low altitude. It moved NNW to SSE at high speed, slowed as it approached, passed within about 500-800 yards, made a near-vertical climb to around 3,000 feet, hesitated, and then accelerated almost vertically to an estimated 20,000 feet before disappearing in the clear night sky. The object produced no sound, exhaust, radio effects, or physical evidence, and no radar contact was reported. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 99–114](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1948, December 3** — 8:15 p.m. On 3 Dec 1948 at about 20:15, control tower personnel at Fairfield-Suisun Air Base, California, observed a single bright circular light north of the field through 8-power binoculars. The object was described as 1 to 2 feet in diameter, initially at about 1,000 to 2,000 feet altitude, then climbing erratically southeastward to about 20,000 feet at an estimated 400 to 500 mph, with no sound and no exhaust trail. The light was so intense it obscured any silhouette, and a weather balloon launched 35 to 45 minutes earlier was later noted as lost after release. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 100–101](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 7:30 p.m. On 5 Dec at approximately 19:30-19:45, three observers reported a blue-green tear-drop shaped object over Carrizozo, New Mexico, at about 40° above the horizon in a dive. The object was seen for a very short time, made no sound, and disappeared; its apparent speed was described as very slow. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 49–50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 7:30 p.m. On 5 Dec 1948 and subsequent dates in New Mexico, multiple independent witnesses reported green lights or fireballs moving generally north-south or northeast-southwest, including military pilots, civilians, students, and personnel at Sandia Base and Los Alamos. The entries suggest a cluster of repeated sightings over several months, mostly described only by color and course, with a few observations of white or red-white lights. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 73–76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 9:05 p.m. On 5 December 1948, Captain A. Goade reported seeing a green, flare-like object twice in New Mexico: first at about 2105 hours west of Las Vegas and again at about 2127 hours near Albuquerque on the east slope of the Sandia Mountains. He was flying a C-47 at 18,000 feet and described the object as similar in shape and luminosity to a flare, rising from the ground to an estimated altitude of about 500 feet. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 116–117](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 9:35 p.m. On 5 December 1948 at about 2135 hours, Capt. Van Lloyd of Pioneer Air Flight #63, flying a commercial C-47 en route near Las Vegas, New Mexico, saw a single flare-like luminous object west of Las Vegas that appeared first as a bright flash/green light, then as a pale green to white/orange light with a pale green trail. It seemed to come straight toward the aircraft, prompting an evasive swerve, then arched or trailed downward toward the ground and disappeared after a few seconds. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 118–119](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 9:35 p.m. On 5 Dec at 21:35, two reliable observers reported a whitish-orange unidentified object over Las Vegas, New Mexico, traveling west to east slightly above 9,000 feet and on a horizontal, slightly descending course. The object was seen only for a few seconds and then disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 10:00 p.m. At about 2200 on 5 December near Albuquerque, New Mexico, two apparently reliable observers reported a green, round UAP seen at an estimated altitude of 10,500 feet. The object showed no apparent direction of flight, followed a parabolic curve, had a train or trail, lasted about 2 seconds, made no sound, and faded out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 5** — 10:40 p.m. A single observer reported a brief nighttime sighting near Tularosa, New Mexico, of a streak of light moving from east to west in a smooth arc downward. The object was blue with a yellowish-red tail, left a train or trail, and was seen for about one second before it appeared to hit the ground near Tularosa. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 6** — 10:55 p.m. On 6 December 1948 at about 22:55, while driving west on D Street at Sandia Base, Albuquerque, security worker Joseph B. Toulouse saw a definite greenish flare-like light almost directly overhead, appearing in the upper right of his windshield slightly to the northwest. The object was about one-third the apparent diameter of the moon, traced a slight arc from east to west with a flaming tail, and vanished after roughly 2 to 3 seconds. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 124–125](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 6** — 10:55 p.m. On 6 Dec at 22:55, a single observer at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a green round object moving east to west in a slight falling arch with a train or trail visible. The object was seen for 2-3 seconds, made no sound, and vanished rapidly; it was estimated at about one-third the diameter of the moon. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 6** — In December 1948, Houston machinist Lonnie Edward Noack heard from acquaintance R.C. Person in Los Angeles that a flying disk had been seen in a desert area near Hollywood. Noack, Person, and Nathan and Mrs. Smith then drove into the desert near Lone Pine, California, where they located the object and photographed it with movie and still cameras. The FBI reported the film showed a disk-shaped object with wing-like extensions and small cylinders at the wing tips; the materials were treated as authentic evidence and were coordinated with OSI representatives. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 74–78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 7** — 5:30 p.m. Lonnie Edward Noack reported that on December 7, 1948, he and several companions drove to an area near R.C. Person's private property/airport in the desert northeast of Pasadena, California, where they found a crashed flying disc or flying wing lying at about a 45-degree angle with part embedded in the ground and apparently having slid 300-400 feet. The object was described in detail as an aluminum central disc with wings, struts, an elevator, and rocket-like cartridges on the tips, with Noack taking 8 mm film and photographs; no radio equipment was seen and the object was later turned over to authorities by him or another witness. The report is a first-hand witness statement made to FBI/OSI representatives on December 28, 1948, with photos/film shown to coworkers and family afterward. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 95–100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 8** — 6:33 p.m. On 8 December 1948 at 18:33, while flying a T-7 aircraft about 20 miles east of Las Vegas, New Mexico, two Special Agents and rated pilots observed a brief, brilliant intense-green light/flare-like object at about 13,500 feet MSL, roughly 2,000 feet above their plane. It approached rapidly from the left on a mostly flat, nearly parallel-to-earth trajectory, then its path dropped off sharply, it disintegrated after about 2 seconds, and reddish-orange glowing fragments fell toward the ground. The investigators noted it resembled a flare or meteor but was much more intense and larger than a normal flare. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 120–121](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 8** — 6:46 p.m. On 8 December 1948 near Chanute Air Force Base, Illinois, two USAF weather observers, Sgt. James E. Doty and Sgt. Eugene E. Montag, reported a bright white round object moving rapidly from the northeast toward the south-southwest at about a 15-degree upward angle. Seen in good visibility under scattered clouds, it resembled a skyrocket or meteor, traversed roughly 35 degrees of sky in about 2-3 seconds, left a trail, and then disappeared. No sound, sketches, or photographs were available. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 137–140](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1948, December 8** — 6:46 p.m. On 8 December 1948 at Chasute AFB, Illinois, two weather observers on the ground watched a single white, round object with a glinting light for about 3 seconds. It was seen heading northeast to south-southwest, climbing at a shallow angle, estimated over 15,000 ft and over 350 mph, leaving a trail and then flying out of sight with no sound. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 102–103](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 9** — 1:30 p.m. On 9 Dec at 1330, a single unidentified observer in Farmington, New Mexico reported a vertically dropping object at an apparent altitude of 500 feet. The object was described as a charred parachute or cargo net, with no sound, no train or trail, and it disappeared. The table appears within a larger Department of War collection of 1948-1950 New Mexico UAP reports. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 9** — 3:20 p.m. On 9 Dec 1948 at 15:20, while Col. J. K. Brown and Capt. E. G. Mulling were flying a C-45 from Washington, D.C. toward Patterson Field near Pittsburgh, they observed a single perfectly round, chalky white object against broken cloud and blue sky about 8-12 miles southeast of Pittsburgh. It appeared about quarter-sized on the windshield at first sighting, shimmered or jittered horizontally, moved in a straight course on a southwest/270-250° heading, and was last seen 10° above the horizon after about 7 minutes, when it became too small to see. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 104–105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 11** — 10:45 p.m. On 11 December 1948 at 2245E, a C-45F flying at 9,500 ft and 150 mph reported an unidentified object approximately 20 miles ahead at the 12 o'clock position while traveling 40 miles northwest of Martinsburg, West Virginia. The report provides only the sighting geometry and aircraft parameters, with no description of the object's appearance or behavior. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1948, December 12** — 9:00 p.m. On 1948-12-12 around 21:00 local time near Bernal, New Mexico, Dr. La Paz and companions observed a very bright green fireball-like object, roughly ball-shaped with an apparent 5-minute angular diameter, traveling east to west in an almost perfectly horizontal path low on the horizon. It was visible for about 2.1 to 2.3 seconds, showed a slight downward curve just before ending, brightened slightly, and broke into three or four smaller bright green fragments that vanished almost immediately. No sound, trail, or cloud effects were noted; the report discusses it as an unusual low-altitude fireball rather than a conventional meteor. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 126–129](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 12** — 9:02 p.m. On 1948-12-12 a green fireball was independently tracked from near Starvation Peak/Bernal, New Mexico and from the Los Alamos reservation, allowing triangulation of a nearly horizontal 25-mile path at about 8-10 miles altitude and roughly 8-12 miles per second; the report argues the object was non-meteoric based on its low altitude, horizontal trajectory, lack of noise, lack of sparks/trail, vivid green color, and other differences from ordinary meteors. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 13** — 3:53 p.m. On 1948-12-13, Capt. O. M. Legg of the USAF Air Rescue Service reported a single object seen directly in his flight path while in flight, about twenty miles ahead of the aircraft at an estimated 10,000-11,000 feet. The object, observed for about one minute in clear sky with unlimited ceiling, appeared as a brilliant flare with colors shifting red to blue to red, resembled a parachute flare, and seemed stationary before fading from view like a flare burning out. No smoke, exhaust, sound, radar contact, or photographs were reported. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 123–135](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1948, December 13** — On 13 Dec 1948, a ground observer in South Bay, Florida, viewed an unidentified luminous object through binoculars. The report is meager and provides only a sketch-like description: a circle or halo of stars surrounding a blazing star, with colors noted as red, blue, and yellow. No further investigation details, movement data, weather, duration, or outcome were recorded. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 131](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 14** — On 14 Dec 1948, a ground observer at Rivera Beach, Florida, using binoculars reported a silver object with a large white spot, shaped like a large kite and estimated at about 20 feet in size. The object allegedly dodged, skidded, and then sped up. The initial report was meager and the incident was not fully investigated. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 132](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 20** — 8:54 p.m. On 20 Dec at 20:54 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, four reliable witnesses reported a pale green or bluish-white ball or fireball-like object at great height descending from west to east at about 26 degrees above the horizon. They said it had a trail, appeared basketball-sized, moved at high speed, made no sound, and disappeared behind a mountain after about 1.5 seconds. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 20** — 8:54 p.m. On 1948-12-20 at about 20:54, four Los Alamos-area observers—AESE Inspectors William D. Wilson, Buford G. Truett, Clifford E. Strang, and Security Inspector George S. Skipper—reported a greenish-white fireball seen under poor observing conditions and later triangulated from two observation points near Los Alamos, New Mexico. Lincoln LaPaz concluded the object descended on a steep path to point C, then levelled off and traveled nearly horizontally to disappearance at point E, with no sound heard; he stated the object was not a falling meteorite. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 20** — Two December 1948 green fireball sightings in New Mexico were notable for each having two red lights trailing the green light, with observers saying the three lights kept a constant relative position. Both incidents also reportedly involved a sharp change of direction in the vertical plane, unusual among the green fireball reports. One sighting on 20 December was seen by Atomic Energy Security Service inspectors in the Los Alamos area; the other on 13 December was seen southwest of Las Vegas by a truck driver and his wife. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 23** — Incident 221 was reported from Midland, Michigan and entered on an Incident Summary Sheet, but the page contains no substantive observation details; the summary explicitly notes 'No Info Rec' and refers to an attached page not present here. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 112–113](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1948, December 27** — In late December 1948, Lonnie Edward Noack and companions found an object in the desert near Helendale, California, initially described as resembling a flying disc. FBI investigation quickly determined it was not a UAP but a model tow target or kite used for flight tests by Ohlson and Rice Manufacturing Company and inventor Claude Leroy Wolford. Witnesses reported pieces had broken off the object at the landing field, and Henry T. Rice later confirmed the model crashed during testing and was abandoned. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 123–124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 27** — On 1948-12-27, attorney Fuller Blackwell contacted the FBI Houston office on behalf of his friend Lonnie Edward Noack, a machinist at Humble Oil and Refining Company, who reported seeing a large flying-disc-like object near a desert location near Las Vegas, Nevada. Noack said the object was about eight feet in diameter, had wings and a tail, appeared jet-propelled, and that he had filmed it on 8mm movie film. He later brought the film to Houston, along with an aluminum cylinder he said had detached from a wing tip; both items were surrendered to investigators and forwarded to the FBI Laboratory. The report reflects an active FBI/O.S.I. inquiry rather than a resolved explanation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 89–94](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 27** — A complainant reported finding a flying-disc-like object in the desert near Helendale, California, but FBI investigation concluded it was a model tow target or kite abandoned after unsuccessful tests; portions of the target were positively identified by Henry T. Rice as part of the model. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 28** — 4:31 a.m. A single reliable observer reported a white, star-like object over Los Alamos, New Mexico at 0431 on 28 Dec that moved from north to south, descended vertically from high altitude to about 6000 feet over several seconds, and vanished with a greenish flash. The witness judged it slower than a falling star and heard no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948, December 28** — 10:00 a.m. On December 28, 1948, an FBI office memorandum reported a call from SAC Willis in Houston about photographs and witness statements concerning an unidentified object seen near Lone Pine, California. According to Fuller Blackwell, his friend Lonnie Noack, while flying in a private plane from California to Houston with another man, noticed a strong reflection from an object on the ground about 30 miles east of Lone Pine. After landing, they found a disc-like object about 8 feet in diameter, with wings about 4 feet long, roughly 1 foot thick, and a gyroscope-like structure in the center; the wings were said to have some kind of jet propulsion. The men photographed the object, found no identifying marks, and Army Air Forces and FBI personnel were arranged to interview Noack and inspect the object. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 72](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 28** — 3:30 p.m. On December 28, 1948, FBI headquarters in Washington handled a Houston Office report concerning a supposed 'flying disc' linked to photographs, film, and a recovered part. After agents were directed to locate the witnesses and secure the material, Nathan Smith was reached and explained that the object was not a flying disc but a tow kite with a disc center and wings, manufactured in Los Angeles by Olson and Rice Manufacturing Company. The manufacturer confirmed making the kite, said they had tried and failed to fly it, and had abandoned it. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December 28** — FBI Laboratory Division correspondence from Houston dated January 25, 1949 concerns 8mm film and fragments submitted by complainant Lonnie Edward Noack regarding a supposed flying disc; prior to laboratory examination, the object had already been identified as a tow target and later as a toy kite, so no testing was conducted and the materials were returned. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 101–102](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1948, December** — 9:02 p.m. Five reliable observers reported a very bright green ball seen about 15 miles south of Las Vegas, New Mexico, traveling horizontally from east to west at an estimated altitude of 3 to 10 miles for about 2.1 to 2.3 seconds. It made no sound and then broke into 3 or 4 small fragments before disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1948** — The report contains conflicting archival references about a 1948 Antarctica sighting attributed to Capt. Augusto Orrego: one quoted account says he and others saw "flying saucers" during a bright Antarctic night, one above the other and turning at tremendous speeds, with photographs said to exist; another later communication from Capt. Orrego states he had not seen any UFOs over Antarctica in 1948 and that the requested photographs do not exist. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1948** — Between 1948 and 1951, a Los Alamos-affiliated correspondent recalled multiple sightings of green lights, usually between 21:00 and 23:00 in the Jemez Mountains, including instances of lights weaving in and out of mountain peaks; the same source also recalled one afternoon event where five objects flew over Los Alamos from southeast to northeast in formation, observed by several Protective Force personnel. ([DOE-UAP-D002, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOE-UAP-D002_JamesTuck_Correspondence.pdf>))

**1949, January 1** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:00 p.m. On 1 Jan 1949 at about 1700, Mr. and Mrs. Tom Rush observed a single dark blue or black cigar-shaped aerial object from a Stinson civilian aircraft near Jackson, Mississippi, while inbound to Dixie Airport. The object, initially resembling a tow target or rocket with possible short wings, crossed in front of their aircraft at an estimated 500 feet, then turned about 50 degrees to the southwest, appeared wingless, accelerated from roughly 200 mph to 400-600 mph, climbed, and disappeared after 10-12 seconds. No photograph was taken and no clear explanation was found in the available investigation notes. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 132–136](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>); [38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, pp. 138–139](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1949, January 1** — 5:00 p.m. On 1 January 1949 at about 1700 local time, three witnesses in a private aircraft approaching Dixie Airport near Jackson, Mississippi, reported a single cigar-shaped dark blue or black object about two miles east of the city. The object was estimated at roughly 60 feet long, 10 feet in diameter at the front tapering toward the rear, initially moving west at about 200 mph before accelerating to 400-500 mph while turning south-southwest and climbing gradually to 1500-1600 feet. It produced a roar but no exhaust trail. The sighting was documented by the 26th Weather Squadron with a weather report and sketch attached. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 116–120](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, January 4** — 2:07 p.m. On 4 January 1949, Capt. Paul R. Stoney of Hickam Field reported an unidentified aerial object first seen about 25 miles east of the base at 1407 local time and visible for roughly 28 minutes. He described it as a large, flat, round white object about the size of an AT-6, oscillating, with a dark non-reflective topside, an extremely white underside, blinking whitish reflections, limited maneuverability, no audible sound, and no exhaust trail. It circled the area, climbed steadily, and then departed on a 25-degree magnetic heading. Later staff correspondence suggested the object may have been cardboard or light metallic debris carried by unusual updrafts over Hickam, and weather service checks noted updrafts existed but were not strong enough to explain the described motion. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 90–98](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, January 4** — 6:02 p.m. On 1949-01-04 at 18:02 Z, personnel in the Indian House Lake, Canada area reported an unidentified object flying overhead and emitting bright lights; the light reportedly moved exceedingly fast and the object itself was only partially seen. Local personnel later suggested it may have been a shooting star or meteor, and the light or object was not seen again. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, January 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:30 p.m. On 6 Jan 1949 at about 1730 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single observer reported a bright white diamond-shaped object flying horizontally from southeast to northwest at an estimated altitude of 1,500 to 2,000 feet. The object was described as approximately 2 feet long, moving much faster than a jet, making no sound or trail, and then disappearing. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, January 6** — 5:30 p.m. On 6 January 1949 at about 1730 hours, Pfc. Meredith J. Everitt reported seeing a bright white diamond-shaped object moving rapidly across a clear sky from southeast to northwest while he was guarding a C-97 airplane near the landing strip adjacent to the Ordnance Area. He estimated it was 1500-2000 feet up, about two feet long, brighter in the center than at the edges, and traveling much faster than jets he had observed; no smoke, vapor, sound, or other visible trail was noted, and the object disappeared before the other guards could see it. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, January 24** — This FBI memorandum dated 1949-01-24 summarizes information relayed by Colonel C. D. Gasser about 'flying discs,' but it does not describe a specific witnessed sighting; instead it relays theories that the discs may be man-made missiles, possibly powered by atomic energy, and notes reports of similar objects approaching from the north and returning over the North Pole. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 103–105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, January 25** — 7:50 p.m. Multiple Japanese weather stations reported a pale glow or fireball-like phenomenon crossing the sky over central/northern Japan on 1949-01-25 around 19:30-19:50 local time, moving generally northwest-to-southeast or east-southeast to west-southwest, followed by thunderlike sounds and, in some places, trembling doors and a loud explosion-like report. The Japanese Central Meteorological Observatory collected station telegrams and judged the event to be probably a meteor or meteor explosion. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 82–89](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, January 30** — 5:54 p.m. On January 30, 1949 at 5:54 p.m. MST, a very bright anomalous fireball traversed New Mexico and Texas on a nearly horizontal north-to-south path; LaPaz’s follow-up report, dated February 21, 1949, summarizes interviews with more than 100 eyewitnesses initially and several thousand total observers, field surveys across multiple towns, and a search by ground and air parties. The object was seen as a brilliant green fireball by many, though some nearby Texas witnesses described blue, orange, red, or purple coloration; no meteor detonation, long-lasting train, or recovered fragments were found, and only possible Udden noises were reported in a few distant locations. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 81](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, January 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:55 p.m. On 1949-01-30 at 17:55, approximately 200 observers in Roswell, New Mexico reported a blue-green aerial object traveling west to east at about 2000 feet, moving slowly on a horizontal course. Several witnesses said it disappeared, and to some it disintegrated into a shower of smaller lighted fragments. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 25–26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, January 30** — 10:55 p.m. A January 1949 Army message from Kirtland AFB, New Mexico references previous reports of an unknown aerial phenomenon and states that an identical object was sighted at 2255Z on 30 Jan 1949 by approximately 30 people, with an estimate of at least 100 total sightings from multiple New Mexico and Texas locations including El Paso, Albuquerque, Alamogordo, Roswell, and Socorro. The office planned a full investigation to determine whether there was an impact point. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 106](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, January 31** — FBI memorandum from San Antonio summarizing reports of unidentified aerial phenomena, especially a cluster of sightings near the AEC installation at Los Alamos, New Mexico, in December 1948 and January 1949. Witnesses included Special Agents, airline pilots, military pilots, security inspectors, and civilians; descriptions ranged from brilliant green lights to red, white, blu-white, and yellowish-green lights, sometimes with red/orange flashes, and daytime appearances resembling jet exhaust. The memo notes no sound, no debris, estimated high speeds, and speculation that the phenomena may have been man-made or a natural phenomenon, while emphasizing the security concern for vital installations. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 115–117](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, February 6** — 4:30 p.m. Captain McBride of the S. S.
