Antigua reported that officers aboard the vessel saw three unidentified aerial objects near the San Andres Islands while the ship was en route to the Canal Zone from New Orleans. The first, sighted at 1630 on 1949-02-06, initially appeared disc-shaped, then as a round silver sphere passing overhead, changing color from silver to yellow to red before disappearing in dusk; two additional objects were seen about 30 minutes later, with no luminosity or visible propulsion. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 75–77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, February 7** — 2:50 a.m. At Godman AFB near Fort Knox, Kentucky, around 0250E on 7 February 1949, four military witnesses on weather duty observed a bright unidentified object to the southwest, tracked it with a theodolite, and reported it as rotating and showing bright green, yellow, and red colors before it dimmed and disappeared to the southwest after a series of flashes around 0310E. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 79–81](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, February 11** — 6:55 a.m. On 11 February 1949 at 06:55 local time near Peeper's Crossing and Curling East, Newfoundland, William Welts and Jim Burnes observed a burning object traveling southeast at about 1,000 feet altitude while waiting for a bus; the bus driver also reportedly saw it. The object came up over the horizon, passed behind a 2,000-foot hill, was visible for 5-10 minutes, glowed when first illuminated, faded, then showed a second burst with an estimated 200-yard flaming exhaust. No sound was heard, and whether it landed was unknown. Police considered Welts reliable but inarticulate, and he reported it because he thought it might be an aircraft in trouble. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 71–73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, February 15** — 3:30 p.m. On 15 February at 1530, a single observer at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a round object traveling from northwest to west at an estimated 45 degrees downward above the horizon. The object appeared red and green, left a trail described like a shooting star, made no sound, and faded out after about 30 minutes. The case is part of a larger 1948-1950 Sandia Base collection of UAP reports from military personnel and others near the base. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, February 16** — At a 16 February 1949 Los Alamos conference on Project Grudge and the green fireball phenomena, investigators discussed numerous reports of fast, silent yellow-green fireballs seen since about 5 December 1948 across the Los Alamos, Las Vegas, and West Texas triangle. Dr. LaPaz described one personally observed event, the 'Starvation Peak Incident,' as a bright yellow-green light traveling essentially horizontally at constant angular velocity for about two seconds with no noise; Mr. Teller argued that if the objects were material they would have produced an audible shock wave, so the group considered photometric/photographic follow-up and broader scientific participation. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 89–90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, February 17** — 5:30 a.m. On 17 February 1949, Captain Neef reported from Sandia Base a blue fireball visible at 05:30 and a yellow-orange cigar-shaped light visible from 17:59 until 18:06. The report appears in a Secret 18 February 1949 memorandum discussing continued unexplainable phenomena near sensitive installations. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 93](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, February 17** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:00 p.m. On 17 Feb 1949 at about 13:00, a single unreliable witness reported a white oval object over Grants, New Mexico moving southward. The object reportedly climbed vertically, then leveled off and continued in a gradual ascent, had a train or trail, made no sound, and then disappeared. The report comes from an FBI declassified case file context. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 26–27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, February 19** — 6:40 p.m. A report relayed from Crystal 1, Fort Chimo, Quebec, stated that on 19 Feb 1949 around 1840 hours a sound like slow-running aircraft engines was heard from a general southwesterly direction at very high altitude. The sound persisted for about 15 to 20 minutes, but no object or lights were seen. The message noted there were no Canadian or USAF aircraft from Goose Bay in the vicinity at the time and said a written report would follow. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 60–64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, February 25** — 2:10 p.m. On 25 Feb at 14:10, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico, reported a metallic oblong UAP traveling south-to-north at an estimated altitude of 1 to 3 miles and about 30 degrees above the horizon. The object was seen for roughly 2 minutes, had no trail or sound, and was estimated at 10 to 15 feet in size. It moved at about 40 mph and then went below the horizon. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, February 28** — 8:30 p.m. On 1949-02-28 at about 20:30 over Sandberg Pass, roughly 40 miles south of Bakersfield, California, 1st Lt. John T. Smith reported that his Air Force T-11 on a round-robin flight from Fresno to Los Angeles was circled by a jet-propelled blimp-like object. He estimated it at about 9,000 feet altitude and 500 mph, after which it disappeared heading 180 degrees. Weather was thin scattered clouds with 10-mile visibility, though local remarks noted rapidly changing fog and drifting stratus. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, March 1** — This source appears to be an illustrated overlay or exhibit tied to Camp Hood, Texas, showing marked azimuth/elevation ray lines and a legend for March 1949 sightings; the visible narrative labels green as objects sighted and identifies object types such as moving oblong fire body without trail, fixed flash, moving fire ball, and moving oblong fire body with trail, but it does not provide a single complete incident description or observation context in the extracted text. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 109](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:05 a.m. On 1949-03-06 at 01:05 near Camp Hood, Texas, a single witness reported an unidentified object traveling in an arc from north-northwest to south-southwest at about 15 degrees above the horizon, described as a pale reddish nose with a whitish-red trail and lemon-like shape with a tail before it disappeared. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 29–30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 6** — 7:30 p.m. FBI memorandum from San Antonio summarizes military intelligence discussions about 'unidentified aircraft'/'flying discs' and reports a cluster of green fireball-type sightings in the Los Alamos, Las Vegas, and West Texas triangle, including a March 6-7, 1949 pair of flare-like lights seen near Killeen Base at Camp Hood, Texas, near a vital installation; the document notes the objects' bright yellow-green light, horizontal path, constant angular velocity, short duration, and lack of sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 120–121](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, March 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:20 p.m. On 1949-03-06 at 20:20, two observers reported a blue-white light over Camp Hood, Texas, seen at about 59 degrees above the horizon moving on a bearing of N 40° E. The object was described as a ball-like flash, about basketball-sized, with no trail, no sound, and a 'fixed flash' appearance. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 27–28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 6** — 9:00 p.m. On 6 March 1949 at about 2100 hours, two military security witnesses at Camp Hood, Texas, observed a single oblong pale blue-white light/object traveling from N 74° W to N 81° W over Site Baker/Q Area. The object was reported as about 2 ft by 1 ft, climbed from roughly 6° above the horizon to 45°, made no sound, and showed no deviation from course; one witness reported no trail while the other saw a faint pinkish-red trail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 102–103](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 7–8** — 1:03 a.m. On 7-8 March 1949 near Camp Hood, Texas, multiple military witnesses reported several brief nighttime lights around the Q Area and nearby observation points, described as fixed flashes, a teardrop, and a lemon-shaped light with tails or smoke trails. The lights were seen against clear weather, moved along arcs or dropped vertically, and then vanished after seconds, with no sound and no exhaust beyond smoke/light trails. Investigators noted the lights formed a rough circle about the Q Area and considered but did not confirm explanations involving trip flares. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 7** — 1:15 a.m. At 0115 hours on 7 March 1949, Pfc. Robert Gardner Black of the 42nd AIB, Company A at Camp Hood, Texas, observed a single brilliant blue-white object from observation point #1. He described it as flash-bulb-shaped and flash-bulb-sized, fixed in place with no speed or maneuverability, heading N 40° E at about 66°15' above the horizon, and producing no sound or exhaust trail. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 104](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:10 p.m. On 7 March 1949 at 18:10, a single observer in Window Rock, Arizona reported a red fire-like object shading to blue at the edge, seen through a window for 6 to 8 seconds moving north at an estimated 40-45 degrees above the horizon. The object was described as a round spherical ball about 3 feet in diameter, trailing a train or trail, traveling about 200-300 mph, and it disintegrated from view without sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 29](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 8** — On 8 March 1949, an AESS Inspector on duty at Los Alamos reported an unidentified object that looked like an elliptical, stubby aluminum body surrounded or covered with billowy flame. The observer thought it resembled the aft portion of a wartime German aircraft and noted possible stubby control surfaces or wings, though this was uncertain. The object moved on a slightly descending path behind the clouds with no noise reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 16** — 8:45 p.m. At about 2045 hours, Pvt. Harold D. Moore of the 42nd AIB, Company A at Camp Hood, Texas, observed a single roundish object with a light-colored head and orange trail moving steadily on a southwesterly course with no deviation; it appeared to pass over the Q area in overcast conditions and made no sound. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 103–104](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 17** — 7:52 p.m. On 17 March 1949, eight moving lights were observed over or near the 'Q' area at AFSWP, Camp Hood, Texas, by military observers using tank sights; the alert guard intended to measure azimuth/elevation and fire flares, but the lights appeared first. Captain McCullough personally saw three of them. The lights were reported in two groups bracketing the Q area, with one burst into a green cluster and others described as reddish, white, or like Very Pistol flares. The report says no one in the Q area was responsible, pyrotechnic missiles had not been issued or used in months, and the area was placed on alert while ground crews investigated. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 97–100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 18** — 6:19 p.m. On 18 March 1949 around Camp Hood/Killeen Base, Texas, multiple military patrols reported several unidentified lights in different areas, including three yellow and one red light near 915.26 - 855.19, another yellow light near 910.41 - 855.10, and blinking lights near the Rock Quarry at 905.82 - 855.61. The lights were seen by different patrols and at least one individual, with no noise, no estimated altitude, and no physical trace found after immediate investigation by base personnel and the Alert Force. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 95–96](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:13 p.m. On 27 Mar 1949 at 18:13, a single reliable observer reported a bright orange, long and narrow unidentified object near Tucumcari, New Mexico, traveling from east to west at an apparent altitude of about 46 degrees above the horizon. The object was observed for about 15 minutes, made no sound, left no train or trail, and eventually faded out of sight in the distance. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, March 31** — 11:50 p.m. At 11:50 P.M. on March 31, 1949, 1st Lt. Frederick W. Davis of Company C, 12th Armored Infantry Battalion, observed a reddish-white light the size of a basketball with a fire trail moving rapidly southwest of Killeen Base near Camp Hood, Texas. The object was estimated at 6,000 feet, flew parallel to the ground directly over him and almost over the air strip, remained visible for 10 to 15 seconds, then suddenly disappeared high in the sky without descending. No sound or odor was detected, though Davis reported static or electrical interference on the telephone line when he tried to call headquarters immediately afterward. The report also notes earlier unexplained lights seen on March 6, 7, 8, and 17 by military personnel around the base. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 145–147](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, March 31** — The document is a Sandia Base headquarters transmittal dated 31 Mar 1949 forwarding three Fourth Army summaries about unidentified lights reported in the vicinity of Camp Hood, Texas, and referencing a 16 Feb 1949 Los Alamos conference on reported unnatural phenomena. No single underlying sighting is described in detail in this page. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, April 1** — In early April 1949, three employees of the Los Angeles Department of Recreation and Parks reportedly observed an object in the sky over Griffith Park that appeared to be a flying disc. One witness, identified as De Jarnett, first noticed the object while working in the park; two coworkers half a mile away also saw it, and they later compared notes and concluded they had seen the same disc-like object. The observation was relayed through C. A. Atkins and then forwarded by the FBI to the Air Force with no FBI investigation conducted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 139–141](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, April 4** — 10:20 p.m. On 1949-04-04 near Merced, California, William H. Parrott reported hearing a sustained clicking noise and then seeing a single solid object blank out stars as it moved slowly west or northwest, made a roughly 90-degree left turn, and returned on a south/southwest heading before disappearing overhead. He described it as dull, light-colored metallic in reflections, curved underneath, about 4-5 feet in diameter, and below 1000 feet altitude; his dog also appeared to react to the object and sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 157–159](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, April 5** — 9:00 a.m. On 1949-04-05 around 09:00 local time, multiple witnesses in northern Utah reported silver-colored objects and apparent aerial explosions: a guard at the Army General Supply Depot near Ogden saw a flash in the sky followed by a falling silver object; Lt. Ron Hatfield in Logan saw two puffs of smoke and a silver object falling west of Logan; Utah Highway Patrolman L. N. Jeppson near Mantua saw a silver object high in the air approaching the mountains near Sardine Canyon and apparently explode in a flash of fire; residents in Trenton also reported two aerial explosions and a falling object. FBI Salt Lake City relayed the information to headquarters, and OSI at Hill Field later suggested the phenomena were probably caused by two B-29s from Wendover Field practicing high-altitude bombing, producing vapor trails. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 126](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, April 8** — 8:30 p.m. On 8 April 1949, multiple witnesses at and near the U.S. Naval Operating Base, Kodiak, Alaska, observed a luminous object crossing from west to east over the base area near Old Woman Mountain, Anton Larson Bay, and Chiniak Bay. Descriptions varied among a flaming greenish-blue object, a reddish ball of fire, and a bright blue object with a pinkish streamer; witnesses estimated altitudes around 2,500 feet, speeds from 1,500 to 2,500 mph, and durations of about 15 seconds. The report concluded the event was probably real as observed, but the nature of the phenomenon remained undetermined and no final explanation was given. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 51–57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, April 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:30 p.m. At 19:30 on 1949-04-12, a single witness in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a round white object traveling from southeast to northwest at about 20 degrees above the horizon. The object was seen for roughly 8 seconds, appeared about one-eighth the size of the moon, moved about 15 degrees during the observation, made no sound or trail, and then extinguished. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, April 13** — 9:05 p.m. On 1949-04-13 at about 2105C, 1st Lt Robert A. Francis and Capt Stanley Borowski of the Carswell Flight Service Center observed a very fast silver spherical object over Fort Worth, Texas, shortly after returning from a local flight and standing by their aircraft. In clear weather with about eight miles visibility, Francis saw it appear on the north-northwest horizon and travel horizontally toward the south-southeast, leaving a silver exhaust-like trail of streaks and star-shaped figures before suddenly disappearing without sound. The report states no photographs were available and no jet aircraft were logged in the area at the time. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, April 16** — 5:25 p.m. On 1949-04-16 at about 5:25 p.m. in Fort Smith, Arkansas, post office employee Wade H. Harrison reported seeing a very bright, mirror-like object moving southeast while he was stopped at a traffic light at North Eleventh Street and Grand Avenue. He estimated it was about 2 miles high, watched it travel several miles until it was hidden by trees, heard no noise from it, and later spoke with an Army officer who mentioned a similar sighting by the officer and his wife the previous day near Fort Smith. The FBI memo records no further local evidence and says no additional inquiries would be made. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 133–136](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, April 19** — This is a US Air Force OSI summary of unidentified aerial phenomena reported in the New Mexico–West Texas area, describing recurring fireball-like sightings with green, yellow-green, blue-green, or greenish-white appearance, horizontal movement, no sound, no persistent trail, and brief visibility of 1-5 seconds. The specific sighting record is part of a larger tabulation compiled from OSI interviews and reports by Dr. Lincoln LaPaz, with no conventional cause identified for the reported incidents. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, April 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 5:45 p.m. On 28 Apr 1949 at 17:45 near Tucson, Arizona, three witnesses reported a silver cigar or sausage-shaped object moving NE to SE or SW. It was observed for about 12 to 40 minutes, estimated at roughly B-29 size, traveling around 300 to 600 mph before fading from view. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, May 2** — 11:40 a.m. On 1949-05-02 near Elko, Nevada, radio operator Marvin L. Small reported seeing three flying discs traveling southwest. He fetched field glasses and estimated them at about 30 feet in diameter at roughly 14,000 feet altitude, moving at an estimated 300 to 400 mph and making a left turn before departing ahead of a United Air Lines aircraft. The report notes clear weather and no sighting by the airline pilot. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 43–44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, May 5** — 2:30 p.m. On 1949-05-05 at about 14:30 in Tucson, Arizona, a single witness reported a silvery round, flat object moving from southwest to northeast. It was seen for about 6-10 seconds, estimated at 25 feet in diameter and traveling roughly 750-1000 mph, then faded from view. The FBI case file context indicates this report is part of a broader declassified UFO records collection, but no further investigation details are provided in the excerpt. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, May 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:40 p.m. On 1949-05-06 at Camp Hood, Texas, a single reliable observer reported a round object seen between about 2040 and 2130 that moved west and north, appeared to descend from roughly 1200 feet to 440 feet, changed color from alternating pinkish to green, made no sound, had no train or trail, and faded from sight after about 50 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, May 9** — 2:30 p.m. On 1949-05-09 at about 14:30, a single reliable observer in Tucson, Arizona reported a silvery, round-and-flat UAP moving from southwest to northeast. The object was estimated at about 25 feet in diameter, seen for 6–10 seconds, and reported to travel at roughly 750–1000 mph before fading from view; no trail or sound was noted. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, May 12** — 5:00 p.m. On the night of May 12, 1949, multiple reports from Virginia and Tennessee described a long, cigar-shaped object with a bright red nose, fire, and trailing smoke moving over the southwest horizon. The first account cited was from Roanoke around 5:00 PM/early evening, where two witnesses said it looked like a streak of fire, rose steeply, then leveled off and headed east. Lynchburg tower personnel reported no unusual objects, and CAA officials had no explanation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 191](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, May 12** — An FBI office memorandum from Richmond merely encloses two newspaper clippings about a 'flying disc' reportedly sighted at Radford, Virginia on 1949-05-12. The memorandum states the office had no information beyond the news items, that the matter was not reported directly to the office, and that no investigation was opened or witnesses interviewed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 187](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, May 14** — 7:15 p.m. On 1949-05-14 over Bristol, Virginia and nearby towns, multiple witnesses reported long, cigar-shaped or streak-like objects moving rapidly across the sky, including a whitish cylindrical mass seen by Rex Rainey around 7:15 p.m.; other reports described a white-smoke trail with a blue flame, a silver streak about 100 feet long, and similar flaming objects seen in Kingsport, Christiansburg, Roanoke, and Pulaski. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 193](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, May 18** — In New Orleans, three flying disc sightings were reported over the prior ten days (May 18, 19, and 23, 1949). Witnesses described a single saucer-shaped object in straight flight generally northward in late afternoon, about the size of an observation plane, with two witnesses saying it traveled end over end. The Navy considered weather balloons as a possible explanation, noting the sightings were outdoors by unglassed witnesses in good weather, but said the objects would likely have been out of sight within minutes if they had been balloons. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 169](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, May 19** — An FBI inquiry reviewed an ash sample from a fire near Scranton, Pennsylvania, after Frederic Marquardt suggested the blaze may have been caused by a military flare or a "flying saucer." The underlying incident, according to Marquardt, involved a house roof struck by a large object during a storm, a loud noise, and a fire that burned for about twelve hours; the material submitted for analysis was ash residue rather than an observed airborne object. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 199–202](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, May 24** — 5:00 p.m. On May 24, 1949 at about 1700 hours, an employee in the supersonic laboratory of an aeronautical laboratory and several coworkers saw a metallic-looking object over a river about 2.5 miles from its mouth. Seen first with the naked eye reflecting sunlight and then through 8-power binoculars, it showed no glare, appeared to have dirty skin and a fin or rudder, and flew horizontally away with gradually increasing speed until it seemed jet-like before disappearing; no propulsion was visible. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 96](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1949, May 25** — 7:45 p.m. In Nobleboro, Maine, Elizabeth E. Beston reported that on May 25, 1949, near sunset she and several family members watched a small bright object in the sky that appeared angular at first, then changed shape repeatedly as it drifted northeast with the wind. Through glasses it sometimes looked like a double balloon or double bloom with something trailing below, later seeming like a marionette-like form spinning while keeping its altitude. The FBI file notes the report was vague and that a weather balloon was considered the most likely explanation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 189–198](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, May 26** — This FBI memorandum concerns a 1949 flying discs matter triggered by Walter Winchell forwarding a telegram from Robert Ripley claiming he had "the only authentic Japanese flying saucer ever recovered in this country." FBI inquiries to the Army Intelligence Division and Colonel William E. Carpenter, OSI-USAF, produced no corroboration; both services reported no authentic information that any Japanese flying saucer, or any flying saucer of any kind, had been recovered in the United States. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, pp. 160–162](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, June 8** — 11:09 a.m. An RB-29 weather reconnaissance aircraft from the 514th Reconnaissance Squadron reported two separate incidents in June 1949: an experienced radar operator observed radar interference over Guam during a test hop, and on 1949-06-08 two crew members saw a white billowing substance at high altitude that appeared to move rapidly in a straight line before disappearing after about one minute. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 46–50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, June 10** — 8:45 a.m. On 10 June 1949 at 0845E, a USAF T-6 aircraft flying about 20 miles southwest of Boston, Massachusetts, reported a single white tubular object about 6 miles ahead and above the aircraft, apparently at 30,000 feet and moving on a 330-340 degree course at about 150 mph. The observer reported no formation, no maneuvers, no exhaust, no lights, no sound, and the object faded from view. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 37–41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, June 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:45 p.m. On 24 Jun 1949, around 15:45-16:30, five steel-gray disc-like objects were reported over Mesa, Arizona. The witnesses described the objects moving to the southeast, east, northeast, and one vertically, with four appearing horizontal and one vertical. One was estimated at about 30 meters altitude and another at about 25 seconds of observation; the objects reportedly faded from view. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 35–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, June 28** — 5:50 p.m. On 28 June 1949 between 17:50 and 17:55 Eastern time near Fairfield, Ohio, Major R. L. Long and an unidentified civilian observed a single unidentified flying object described as a long tail of flame accompanied by a long tail of smoke moving rapidly from west to east at very high altitude; no photos or sketches were available. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 35–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, July 1** — 1:00 p.m. A newspaper article dated 1950-04-05 reports a political eyewitness claim by Rep. Charles A. Engel of Michigan, who said he saw a flying disc about 1 p.m. one day the previous summer at Elsie, Michigan; he said several sober and well-regarded citizens saw it too, and two of them chased it in a plane but it was too high and too fast to catch. The article juxtaposes Engel's account with repeated denials from military and government officials, but provides no technical description of the object beyond 'flying disc.' ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 80](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1949, July 9** — 9:30 a.m. On 1949-07-09 near Boston, the captain and crew of a Trans-World Airlines Paris-New York flight reported a large white disc-like object flying a parallel course near their aircraft at about 9:30 a.m. EDT, above the high clouds and about 10 miles north of Boston. After landing, the pilot received a message stating a weather balloon had been released from Grenier Air Force Base and was over Boston at 9:19 a.m., which the pilot disputed as a cover-up; the report was supported by the copilot, flight engineer, and reportedly by several Logan Airport tower employees. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1949, July 13** — 8:00 p.m. On the evening of 1949-07-13 in Radford, Virginia, multiple local witnesses reported a strange black, cigar- or stick-shaped object moving from the southwest horizon eastward across the sky shortly after sundown. Accounts described a bright front light, long fire or vapor/smoke trails, a slight curve in its path, and either a faint motor/roaring sound or no sound at all; some observers thought it resembled a comet, meteor, or airplane exhaust. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 192](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, July 14** — 4:55 p.m. While on vacation at Lake of the Woods near Kenora, Ontario, Canada, Clive G. Matthews observed an unconventional aircraft resembling a flying disc. He later provided full information to U.S. Air Force Intelligence at Fort Crook, Nebraska, and the FBI note says the matter would not be reported to the Bureau unless advised otherwise. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 203](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, July 19** — 9:35 p.m. On 19 July 1949 at about 2135E, a Columbus photographer, Mr. Neinkirchen, reported a single luminous flying-wing-shaped object over Columbus, Ohio. He described swept-back wings, a tail, no noticeable nose, a size too small for one-man aircraft, very fast straight-line eastbound travel at 3,000 to 4,000 feet, and a humming noise with no visible exhaust under clear skies. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 32–34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, July 20** — An FBI memorandum dated July 20, 1949 recounts a report relayed by Ernest Cuneo about a letter Walter Winchell received from a Los Angeles individual claiming he saw a flying saucer leave the ground within his view and speculating it might have been a landing from another planet. The Los Angeles Office attempted to locate and interview the supposed writer, Peter Cameron Jones of 164 West 37th Street, Los Angeles 7, California, but the current owner and former manager had no knowledge of him and further investigation failed, leading the Bureau to conclude it may have been a prank. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 213](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, July 21** — This FBI memorandum does not document a UAP sighting event itself; it reports that several citizens in Alexandria, Louisiana had recently claimed to see flying discs/saucers, prompting local newspaper coverage and public interest in a proposed 'flying saucer seers' convention. The memo notes concern that publicity could encourage false claims of sightings. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_004, p. 205](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_4.pdf>))

**1949, July 24** — 12:05 p.m. On 24 July 1949 at 1205 MST, Harry Clark, the airport manager at Ritchie Field in Nampa, Idaho, observed seven dark, delta-wing objects in a V formation about 10 miles northwest of Mountain Home, Idaho. He was flying a Cub Cruiser from Burley to Nampa when he saw the formation slightly below and left of his aircraft at roughly 8,500 to 10,000 feet. The objects were larger than an F-51, appeared between light gray and dirty white without markings, had a dark circular bulge beneath the cockpit area, produced no sound or exhaust, and were estimated at least 600 mph. The formation made an unexplained 180-degree turn without banking and then moved out of sight. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 28–31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, July 24** — 8:26 p.m. Researchers at the New Mexico School of Mines reported a fireball over the Socorro, New Mexico area at 8:26 p.m. on 1949-07-24 and then conducted airborne particle collections beginning the next morning to see whether copper-bearing material could be associated with the event. The report is primarily an investigation of unusual airborne particles rather than a direct visual UAP encounter; it notes several copper-positive particles, a few cobalt-indicating spheres, and some nickel indications, but concludes the evidence was negative or inconclusive. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, July 26** — 8:20 p.m. On 26 July 1949 at 2020 PST over Spokane, Washington, civilian commercial pilot Bill Miller reported hearing a sound like a formation of jet aircraft and then observing eight dish-shaped objects traveling west in the same direction as a B-29 but at much greater speed. He estimated they were about 1,000 feet above the B-29 and around 14,500 feet altitude, and said each object was larger than a B-29. The report was forwarded through Spokane Radio and Seattle Air Route Traffic Control Center to McChord Flight Service Center; the B-29 pilot reported no unusual sighting. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 15–17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, July 28** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:46 p.m. At 20:46 on 28 Jul 1949, a single observer at Killeen Base, Texas reported a round object with a tail traveling from north to south at about 30 degrees above the horizon. It appeared white with a blue-green tint, left a train or trail, lasted only 3-5 seconds, made no sound, and faded out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, July 30** — 9:00 p.m. On 30 July 1949, multiple witnesses near Portland, Troutdale, and Mount Hood, Oregon reported an unidentified flying object seen in clear, unlimited visibility between 2100P and 2130P. Captain Thrush, a Northwest Airlines pilot, first observed an object with two red lights and one white light while approaching Portland from the east; he tracked it from about five miles west of Stevenson, Washington at 6,000 feet, then attempted to intercept it after hearing Portland Tower mention an unidentified flying object dropping flares. He said the object turned southeast and pulled away easily. Civilian instructor pilot H. W. Penhallegan also reported seeing the object over Troutdale while in the traffic pattern. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 25–27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, August 1** — 7:00 p.m. On 1 August 1949, CAA controllers at Columbus Tower reported a single unidentified object over Columbus, Ohio, tracked visually for about two hours from 1900E to 2100E. The object was seen at an estimated 30,000 feet or higher, moving northwest on a straight course, with no sound and no reported speed estimate. It was described as having no discernible shape, impossible to size, appearing white and turning yellowish-red near the horizon, and leaving an exhaust trail before a white light. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 22–24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, August 6** — 8:00 p.m. On 6 Aug 1949 at about 20:00 in Las Cruces, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round, bluish-green object, described as bigger than a falling star, moving east to west before disappearing behind a building after 1 to 2 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, August 6** — 8:30 p.m. On 1949-08-06 at about 20:30 in Alamogordo, New Mexico, a single witness reported a small round white object with a bluish tint moving on a constant slight earthward curve before going out after about one second. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, August 6** — 8:30 p.m. At Alamogordo, New Mexico, on 1949-08-06 at about 20:30, a single unknown-reliability observer saw a small round white object with a bluish tint moving north in a constant slight curve toward the earth. It had a short train or trail, lasted about one second, made no sound, was described as smaller than a clenched fist and similar to a falling star, and then went out. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 8** — This report is not a UAP sighting itself but a memo discussing an attempted airplane collection of meteoritic material after the August 6, 1949 event. A B-25 from Kirtland Field was fitted with collecting equipment and flown on August 8, about 41 hours after takeoff, reaching 23,000 feet to sample air beneath the meteor trajectory over distances of several hundred miles. The authors note that a few copper indications were found but appeared to be of surface origin, and no nickel or cobalt indications were detected; they conclude that more upper-air data would be needed before deciding whether the flight actually intercepted material from the trajectory. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 9–12](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:35 p.m. At Camp Hood, Texas, a single reliable observer reported a yellow-to-orange light resembling the headlight of an old car moving from north to south in level flight at about 2035 on 10 Aug 1949. The object left a train or trail, was seen for only 1 to 2 seconds, made no sound, and cut out from view after apparently covering about 60 degrees in 2 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 37–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 10** — 10:00 p.m. On 10 Aug 1949 at about 2200-2230 hours, a single unreliable/unknown witness at Camp Hood, Texas reported an oval white object with a yellow trail moving from south to north at roughly 30 degrees above the horizon in level flight. The object was seen for 1-2 seconds, made no sound, was estimated as the size of a shooting star and at 300 mph or faster, and then faded out. The report is part of a Department of War/U.S. military collection of UAP sightings. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 10** — This document is not a sighting report but a laboratory letter dated 10 Aug 1949 discussing chemical examination of eighteen collections from a Monday-Tuesday trip near Socorro, New Mexico; it concludes that no widespread significant dispersion of copper-bearing particles was present, and that one heavy copper collection was probably of local origin. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 10** — On 10 August 1949, personnel associated with Medford Airways, Medford Tower, and Medford Radio in Medford, Oregon observed between one and seven shiny unidentified objects at very high altitude. Witnesses used binoculars and a theodolite; tower personnel could make out wings, but no further identification was possible. The objects flew in formation, then broke off and re-formed in a trail formation, disappeared briefly, and reappeared in the same general area, finally heading west. They were visible to the naked eye only when reflecting sunlight and produced no sound or exhaust trail. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 19–21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, August 12** _(cluster ×2)_ — 4:45 a.m. At 0445 on 12 Aug 1949, a lone observer at Killeen Base, Texas, reported a reddish object or fireball moving from SE to NW about 30° above the horizon and headed downward. The object left a trail of flame, made a sound, appeared as a fairly long streak of flame, lasted only 2–3 seconds, and then faded away. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 41–42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 15** — 7:30 p.m. On 1949-08-15 at 1930 local time, two witnesses in Greenville, South Carolina reported a single unidentified flying object moving northeast at an undetermined speed. The object was described as round, fairly large, grey, silent, and apparently steady in altitude and speed at an estimated 5,000 feet or less. No photos or sketches were obtained. A later investigation by the 316th Troop Carrier Wing concluded the object was likely a Geological Weather Service balloon released in Tennessee. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 14](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, August 17** — This report is not a direct sighting statement but an investigative memorandum by Lincoln LaPaz on green fireball fallout investigations in New Mexico. It describes collection efforts for suspected volatilization products (notably copper particles) after the Jan. 30, July 24, and Aug. 6, 1949 green fireball events near Socorro, Vaughn, and related routes, with Dr. William Crozier conducting dust sampling on the ground and by aircraft. The memo concludes the results were negative or at best inconclusive, though copper residues were repeatedly found in dust samples. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 9–12](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, August 22** — 6:45 p.m. On 22 August 1949, three military controllers in Seattle reported a circular shiny aluminum object seen over the city around 1845P, moving north to south at an estimated 500-600 mph and about 10,000 feet altitude without maneuvering or leaving exhaust. Later that evening, two air traffic controllers west-northwest of Seattle-Tacoma Airport reported what looked like an F-84 heading south to north at 4,000-5,000 feet, again with no sound or exhaust; both accounts were relayed through McChord Flight Service Center. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 9–13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, August 30** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:00 p.m. On 30 Aug 1949 at about 2300 in Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported an object falling almost straight down overhead at about 90 degrees, appearing as a bright green fireball with a reddish tail. The event lasted only 1–2 seconds, and the object was noted to burn out; the witness said it was much larger than a meteor. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 42–43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, September 19** — 4:00 p.m. Hundreds of people in Poplar Bluff, Missouri reported a strange spherical object crossing the sky for five to six hours on the afternoon of September 19. CAA workers in Malden tracked its southeasterly course from 4 p.m. until dark, and four military planes were sent up to intercept it. Pilots on two F-51 fighters and one F-80 jet reported being unable to get near the object, which appeared motionless and remained far above them at altitudes of 31,000 to 40,000 feet. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1949, September 20** — 9:06 a.m. On 20 September 1949 at 0906E, a C-45 flown by Lt. Colonel Phillip J. Kuhl and Major Earl Goodrich observed a single cylindrical, silvery object about thirty miles to the right and rear near Griffiss Air Force Base, Rome, New York. The object was estimated at about 20,000 feet, descending slowly into overcast until it disappeared at 7,000 feet, leaving an orange-and-white flame more than twice the object's length. No sound, lights, or radar contact were reported. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 3–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1949, September 27** _(cluster ×2)_ — 3:00 a.m. At 0300 on 27 Sep 1949, a single witness at Sandia Base, New Mexico observed one round, bright green object traveling from SE to NW about 15°-20° above the horizon in an arc toward the earth. It was compared in apparent size to a perimeter fence light at about 200 yards distance, brightened, and then went out after about 2 seconds, covering roughly 45° in that interval. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 41–44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, October 6** — 6:00 p.m. A single unknown witness reported a round green object over Mescalero, New Mexico on 6 Oct 1949 between 1800 and 1845. It was seen about 18° above the horizon, appeared about half the size of a thumb at arm's length, moved slowly for 7-9 seconds, made no sound or trail, and disappeared behind a hill. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, October 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:20 p.m. On 7 October 1949 at 21:20 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round object, about half the apparent size of a moon, that appeared behind the Sandia Mountains. It was seen at roughly 35 degrees above the horizon, colored yellow to green, and dropped straight down about 15 degrees in one second before vanishing; no sound, lights, or other effects were noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, October 14** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 14 Oct 1949, two witnesses in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a round red-orange object moving north to south in a horizontal path. The object was visible for about 10 seconds, appeared about one-third to one-quarter the size of the moon, then broke into two pieces and disappeared. The report is drawn from a declassified FBI file cover summary. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 46–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, November 19** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:52 p.m. On 19 Nov 1949 at 21:52, a single reliable observer reported a green unidentified object seen over Los Alamos, New Mexico, moving vertically for about 2 seconds with no sound before it disappeared. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, December 3** — 6:05 p.m. On 3 Dec 1949 at 18:05 in Alamogordo, New Mexico, a single witness reported a circular object, somewhat larger than Venus, moving from east to west in an arc downward and seen for about 2 seconds before disappearing behind a building; it appeared as a green fringe of orange light and no sound was reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949, December 3** — 6:05 p.m. On 3 Dec 1949 at 18:05, a single reliable observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a circular UAP moving E to W in an arc downward. It appeared as a green fringe of orange light, with no trail or sound, lasted about 2 seconds, and disappeared behind a building; it was described as somewhat larger than Venus. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 49–50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1949, December 13** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 1949-12-13 in Alamogordo, New Mexico, a single circular object was observed stationary at about 5,000 feet before moving slowly downward and to the right; it showed white, amber, red, and green colours, then took on a brilliant green colour, increased speed, and faded from view. The report appears in an FBI-declassified UFO case file. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 49–51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1949** — Colonel MacLaughlin, described as a missile expert, stated in 1949 that he had many times seen flying discs following and overtaking missiles in flight at the experimental base at White Sands, New Mexico. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 87](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1950, January 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:30 p.m. On 1950-01-06 at 22:30, seven reliable observers in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a star-like white object that changed to green and red, moving from east to west while making up-and-down and horizontal course changes over about 45 minutes before stopping observation; it was described as slightly larger than Venus and left no sound or trail. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 50–51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, January 6** — On the night of 1950-01-06, two spherical unidentified objects were reported over Kansas City and Olathe, Kansas. They appeared motionless over Olathe for 10 to 15 minutes, then departed very rapidly to the southwest. The witness report described brilliant white objects with orange and red flashes, no sound, no exhaust trail, and an estimated altitude of 7,000 to 8,000 feet. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1950, January 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 10:20 p.m. On 1950-01-09 at 22:20, a single reliable observer in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a bluish-white pointed object moving due west on a straight course about 60 degrees above the horizon. It appeared as a point, left a visible train or trail, lasted about 2 seconds, made no sound, and then vanished when its luminosity stopped suddenly. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, February 7** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:45 p.m. On 1950-02-07 between 19:45 and 20:00, two reliable observers reported a white fireball traveling on a flat trajectory between Tucumcari and Kirtland AFB, New Mexico. The object was seen for 2-4 seconds, reportedly trailed something, made no sound, and faded out suddenly at an estimated speed of over 1,000 mph. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, February 7** — 8:15 p.m. On 7 Feb 1950 at 20:15 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a witness reported two reddish-green objects or lights moving horizontally from SSE to E about 40 ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, February 7** — 8:15 p.m. On 7 February 1950 at about 20:15, two reliable observers in Albuquerque, New Mexico, reported a reddish-green round elongated object with a train or trail, seen 40 ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, February 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:15 p.m. On 24 Feb 1950, between about 1315 and 1400, a single witness reported a rather large spherical object of aluminum appearance over Los Alamos, New Mexico, at considerable altitude. The object reportedly circled and then headed east, with erratic movement and no sounds or other effects noted. The sighting comes from an FBI declassified case file context rather than a dedicated field report. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, February 24** _(cluster ×2)_ — 2:00 p.m. At about 1400 on 24 Feb 1950 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a lone witness reported a bright white, round object about 20 degrees above the horizon traveling very slowly to the east by southeast. The object was compared in apparent size to a weather balloon as it receded into the distance and then faded out of sight after roughly 20 to 30 seconds. No sound, lights, or other effects were reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, February 24–25** _(cluster ×2)_ — A photograph of an unknown aerial phenomenon was taken at Datil, New Mexico on 24-25 Feb 1950 by Cpl. Lertis E. Stanfield of Holloman Air Force Base. Dr. Lincoln LaPaz later analyzed the image and concluded the perfectly round luminous object had an angular diameter of about 1/4 degree and moved at greater than half a degree per minute, ruling out the moon, Venus or another planet, and a bright fixed star. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, February 25** — 3:45 p.m. On 25 Feb 1950 at 15:45 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a very high object traveling north to south-west by south, appearing white to silver and circular, about half the size of a 50-cent piece at its apparent height. The object moved very fast for about 30 seconds and disappeared into the glare of the sun. The report is preserved in a declassified FBI case file. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, February 25** — 3:45 p.m. Single witness reported a circular white-to-silver object over Los Alamos, New Mexico, flying N to SWS at very high altitude and very fast speed for about 30 seconds before disappearing into the glare of the sun. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, March 9** — A Grand Blanc pizza maker, Joe Perry, provided the FBI with a color photograph he had taken of the full moon showing a saucer-like object silhouetted against it; he described the object as round, much smaller than the moon, dome-topped, and leaving a green trail, and said it appeared nearer to Earth than the moon. The FBI sent two agents from the Flint office to examine the photo and other slides, taking the image for further review. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 134](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1950, March 11** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:00 a.m. On 1950-03-11 at about 0100, a single reliable witness at Holloman AFB, New Mexico reported a UAP seen about 30 degrees above the horizon at an estimated distance of 50 miles. The object moved in a straight course and changed color from light orange to blood red to amber to light green before disappearing from view after about 5 minutes. No sound or trail was reported, and the object was compared in apparent size to a ping pong ball held at arm's length. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, March 17** — 3:07 a.m. On 17 Mar 1950 at 0307 in Los Alamos, New Mexico, one witness reported a straight-down descent of an orange-and-green object lasting about 2 to 2.5 seconds, followed by a tremendous explosion on impact with the earth or possible disintegration. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, March 19** — The source is not a firsthand sighting report but a 1950 letter and attached newspaper clippings from Miguel Angel Garcia Macias proposing speculative "stratospheric aerostats" or flying-saucer-like devices, accompanied by an article about photographed "flying saucers" in Durango reported by Horacio Robles Jr.; the document discusses imagined propulsion and stability concepts rather than documenting a confirmed UAP event. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_220, pp. 1–15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_220.pdf>))

**1950, March 20** — 9:26 p.m. On March 20, 1950, at 21:26, a Reserve Air Force captain and an airline captain aboard a commercial flight observed a fast-moving disc-like object approaching from the south, passing in front of the aircraft and exiting to the right after about 25-35 seconds. The object was estimated to be about 100 feet in diameter, with a blinking brilliant light on top and a bottom ring of 9 to 12 oval/circular portholes emitting soft purple light; it flew straight and was estimated at over 1000 mph. ([CIA-UAP-015, pp. 97–98](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1950, March 21** _(cluster ×2)_ — 1:20 p.m. On 21 Mar 1950 at 1320 near Sandia Base, New Mexico, a witness reported two silver round objects traveling from east to southwest at about 45 degrees above the horizon. They were seen for about 10 minutes, appeared about the size of a dime at arm's length, and gradually faded from view without noise or other effects. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 70](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, March 22** _(cluster ×2)_ — 12:10 a.m. At 0010 on 22 Mar 1950, three observers at Sandia Base, New Mexico reported a brief fireball-like object moving SE to NW in a line parallel to the earth's tangent. The object had a blue center with an orange exterior, no trail, no sound, and was described as round like a ball from a roman candle; it lasted only a few seconds before burning out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 59–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, April 10** — On an afternoon near Preston, England, three girls walking along the Preston-Lancaster main road saw an oval, silvery object in the east that looked brighter than a star and moved with a peculiar swinging motion, apparently against the wind. An Air Ministry official suggested it may have been a meteorological balloon blown over from Northern Ireland, and the report also referenced a prior balloon explanation for London 'flying saucer' sightings. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 79](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, April 10** — This is not a direct sighting report but a newspaper column in which Henry J. Taylor argued that U.S. Air Force denials of 'flying saucers' were useful to Soviet propaganda. He described a secret U.S. helium balloon/cosmic-ray instrument vehicle launched near Minneapolis and elsewhere, noting that it was unmanned, translucent plastic, about the size of an eight-story building when fully expanded, glowed at sunset, drifted to very high altitude, and later broke up or exploded, scattering plastic fragments. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 78](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, April 20** — 3:00 a.m. A 1950 press report from Madrid and London describes a photographed luminous trail or 'flying saucer' seen over the Balearic Islands at about 3 a.m., allegedly captured by newsreel cameraman Enrique Hausmann Muller after he and assistants heard a loud noise. The same article also mentions a separate Texas anecdote in which Ira Maxey photographed two groups of objects near Fort Worth, but that remark is explicitly skeptical ('more like flying bananas'). ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 77](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, April 21** — This newspaper article reports official denials of flying saucer claims rather than a single firsthand sighting. The Air Force stated that recent reports could be explained as misinterpretations of conventional objects, mass hysteria, or hoaxes, while the article also notes continuing public sightings in Tipp City, Ohio, and Elizabeth City, Illinois, described by witnesses as bright lights or disc-like objects moving rapidly and disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 81](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, May 23** — This report summarizes the green fireball problem in New Mexico rather than a single discrete sighting, arguing that most observed green fireballs were meteorite falls while a minority may have been U.S. guided missiles; the author notes a strong evening concentration of sightings, a secondary early-morning peak likely contaminated by misidentified ordinary meteors, and proposes that some incidents could be missile tests near sensitive installations. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, May 23** — This is a 1950 memorandum from Lincoln LaPaz of the University of New Mexico's Institute of Meteoritics summarizing anomalous luminous phenomena, especially bright green fireballs observed in New Mexico between 1948 and 1950. The report compares these objects to ordinary meteors and notes recurring unusual features: low altitude, horizontal or vertical downward paths, unusually long duration, vivid green color, lack of sound, lack of sparks or dust, and a tendency to appear from the north half of the sky. The document is an analytical summary of a series of sightings rather than a single observation. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 61–64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, May 24** — On May 24, 1950, personnel of Land-Air, Incorporated, at observation posts near Vaughn, New Mexico, reported sighting 8 to 10 unusual aerial objects described in the context of the Air Force's 'Project Twinkle,' which was established to photograph and measure green fireballs and discs. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, May 25** — This is a covering USAF summary memo, not an individual sighting report. It states that since December 1948 the Kirtland AFB Office of Special Investigations had been collecting and reporting unexplained aerial phenomena in New Mexico, compiling sightings mostly from December 1948 through May 1950 and classifying them as green fireball phenomena, discs or variants, or probably meteoric. No single event details, date, time, or location are given in the excerpt. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 20](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, July 8** — The source is a brief FBI/file cover note mentioning an Air Force report of another object described as appearing to be a guided missile, said by the Alaska Air Command to have passed over Fairbanks, Alaska, on Saturday night; no direct witness narrative, observation details, or outcome are included. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 76](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, July 12** — On the night of July 12, 1950, two Navy training planes near Osceola, Arkansas, were reportedly paced by a shiny round object that passed in front of them and moved away too quickly for pursuit; an electronics technician flying with one pilot said he tracked it on radar for about eight miles and described it as helmet-shaped with glare obscuring the center. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, July 14** — 3:07 a.m. On 1950-07-14 at about 03:07 CST, Fargo Weather Bureau employee Ray Wilson and three other airport-area observers reported five bright, moon-like objects flying in formation south of Fargo, North Dakota. Wilson watched them until 03:41, when clouds obscured his view; the group later said only one object was visible to the naked eye, while binoculars and telescopes revealed two smaller objects on each side of the main 'thing.' The objects were described as white, flame-like, slow-moving, and maneuverable. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 72](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, July 30** — Near Springfield, Illinois, pilot Jim Graham reported that a blue, sausage-shaped object trailing yellow fire passed slightly above his aircraft, dove into his propeller, and 'exploded like a bomb' without damaging the plane; several Springfield residents and a woman on her porch also reported seeing it, and weather bureau observers suggested it may have been a meteor. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, July 31** — A CIA-translated article by Dr. Eduard Ludwig of Santiago, Chile, speculated that the reported 'flying discs' could be explained by advanced aerodynamics, especially boundary-layer effects and a turbine/rotor-driven design, rather than by extraterrestrial origin; the document contains no firsthand sighting, only technical argumentation about the phenomenon. ([CIA-UAP-005, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-005-GERMAN_SCIENTISTS_ ARTICLE_ON_FLYING_DISCS.pdf>))

**1950, August 11** — A 1950 press article by G. Tilghman Richards argues that many reported flying saucers may be disc-type aircraft, citing clear photographs and consistent descriptions of white or silver rotating objects that dart, vanish, hover, and show brilliant lights or flames; the piece also discusses earlier experimental disc-wing aircraft and secret U.S. Navy development as a possible explanation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 74–75](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, August 13** — 11:20 a.m. Drew Pearson's column on 1950 flying-saucer reports describes multiple Air Force investigations that all ended in conventional explanations, including a supposed Venus midget story, a fake crashed saucer near Warren, Minnesota, photographs later matched to high-flying F-84 jets over Great Falls, Montana, and a radar blip at Wright Field later identified as a charged cloud. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 68](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, September 26** — Late on 1950-09-26, two Philadelphia police officers on Vare Boulevard near 26th Street observed a round, parachute-like lavender object about six feet in circumference slowly descend and land in a field. The material was described as dewy, like soap suds, evaporated within 15-20 minutes, and left only a slight sticky residue when touched. The incident was reported to the Philadelphia Police, publicized in the press as 'Flying Saucer Just Dissolves,' and referred by FBI personnel to Air Force Intelligence/OSI with no further FBI action. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, September 26** — On September 26, 1950, two Philadelphia Police Department officers cruising in a scout car reportedly saw an object descending slowly to the ground at tree-top level, initially resembling a parachute. It was described as about six feet in diameter, landed in a nearby field, glowed purplish like a mist, could not be picked up without breaking, left a slight odorless residue, and disintegrated over roughly 25 minutes without bending the weeds or grass on impact. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, September 28** — Four Philadelphia policemen reported seeing a saucer-like object land in a field. Before FBI men could join them, the roughly six-foot gadget had vanished. One policeman who touched it said the part he handled dissolved immediately, leaving a sticky, odorless residue. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, October 12** — 11:25 p.m. On 1950-10-12 at about 23:25 EST, USAF radar at Knoxville detected indications of 11 or more unidentified objects crossing the controlled area over the Oak Ridge atomic energy installation. The returns varied in altitude and density and were estimated to be moving 100-125 mph from south-southeast toward southeast. A fighter was scrambled and obtained radar contact, but the pilot reported no visual object and no contact on the aircraft radar; AEC patrols also saw nothing. The FBI teletype states no reasonable explanation had yet been developed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 92](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, October 12** — An FBI office in Knoxville forwarded OSI Air Force reports describing radar detections of unidentified objects over Oak Ridge, Tennessee on October 12, 15, and 16, 1950. The memo says the objects in the airspace over the Oak Ridge vital installation remained unexplained at the time, and that any additional information from OSI or CIC would be passed along. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, October 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — 6:15 p.m. On 1950-10-20 at about 6:15 P.M. in New Haven, Connecticut, chemical engineer Aaron L. Hitchens and his wife and daughter saw a steady golden-orange sphere directly overhead in a clear night sky. The object, estimated at 10,000-20,000 feet or higher, moved west toward New York City, then veered south as if crossing Long Island, maintaining roughly the same apparent size, making no sound, leaving no trail, and showing no attachments. Hitchens estimated its speed at 400-700 mph and reported that the object appeared to back up and go forward during the turn. The FBI relayed the report to the Air Force and noted Hitchens as a reliable, technically knowledgeable witness. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 83–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))
