**1950, November 5** — 11:00 p.m. In late evening reports across Devon, multiple independent witnesses from Woolacombe, Exeter, Cullompton, Exmouth, Sidmouth Junction, Paignton and Torquay described fast-moving silent aerial objects, often circular or disc-like, many with bright bluish-white or silvery-blue light and long red or fire-like tails. Several observers reported two objects traveling in formation, appearing to disintegrate or fizzle out, and all sightings were said to have occurred around 11 p.m. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 66–67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, November 16** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 16 Nov 1950 at Los Alamos, New Mexico, a witness reported two bluish-green objects that were stationary and then disappeared from view to the north with speed compared to a meteor. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 47–48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**1950, December 3** — 4:00 p.m. On 1950-12-03 around 4 p.m., thousands of people across Britain reported a fast-moving strange white flash or light crossing the sky; at Chard, Somerset, spectators at a football cup match and nearby rugby match described a diamond-flash/peardrop-shaped white object that seemed to melt or dissolve, leaving smoke or vapor, while similar flashes were reported from North Petherton, Portishead, Weston-super-Mare, Llandaw/Cardiff, Easton-in-Gordano, Amersham, Launceston, and other places. The Air Ministry said no aircraft was missing and could not explain the phenomenon. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 64–65](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1950, December 12** — 9:02 p.m. On 12 December at 21:02, five reportedly reliable observers near Las Vegas, New Mexico, saw a very bright green ball traveling horizontally from east to west at an estimated altitude of 8-10 miles. The object was observed for about 2.1-2.3 seconds with no sound, then broke into 3 or 4 small fragments and disappeared. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, December 15** — 3:00 p.m. On 15 Dec 1950, Air Command reports that military jet pilots near Fairbanks, Alaska, while over Weeks International Field at 8,000 ft, observed a yellow flash high above them, followed by a dark brown smoke column rising at a 40-degree angle and an object appearing at the leading edge. The object was described as cigar-shaped or a wingless fuselage, dark in color, with no sunlight reflection, and was pursued by the pilots until it disappeared near Clear after climbing to an estimated 50,000-55,000 ft. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 97](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, December 18** — 8:27 a.m. On 18 December 1950 near Oak Ridge, Tennessee, multiple military and civilian witnesses traveling by car on the turnpike within the controlled area observed a bright white circular light through the windshield for about thirty seconds. The object appeared west to northwest of the townsite, low above the horizon, traveling northwesterly and diminishing in apparent size; some witnesses described it as a sun reflection from a metallic surface. Radar logs from McGhee Tyson Air Force Radar Station recorded related tracks and a fighter interception attempt that failed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 101–105](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950, December 20** — 12:47 p.m. On 20 December 1950 at 1247 hours, personnel of the 663rd AC&W Squadron at McGhee Tyson Airport logged a very slow-moving small object or point in the Oak Ridge Controlled Area near Oak Ridge, Tennessee. The radar entry says the target made a perfect intercept with an F-82 fighter aircraft and then orbited around a small smoke cloud. The report was submitted on 20 January 1951 by military personnel to help identify numerous unidentified objects sighted over Oak Ridge. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 106](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1950** — A military aviation observer reported a brief daylight sighting from a flight on the first leg from Edmonton, Alberta, to Kittigazuit, N.W.T., while flying at 7,000 ft MSL above an overcast. The object was momentarily seen above broken cloud layers and described as a bright flaming orange object that illuminated the sky behind it, appearing to be in a shallow dive with no sound and leaving an exhaust trail; an altitude of approximately 18,000 feet was noted. ([342_HS1-416511228_319.1 Flying Discs 1949, p. 141](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/342_hs1-416511228_box186_319.1-flying-discs-1949.pdf>))

**1951, January 16** — 9:45 p.m. On 16 January 1951 near Oak Ridge, Tennessee, radar personnel, AEC patrols, police, and 663rd AC&W Squadron controllers reported two bright star-like objects: one east of McGhee Tyson Airport showing intermittent multicolored glows, and another west that was tracked visually through a spotting scope as descending slowly for about an hour before disappearing behind trees. An aircraft scrambled to intercept one object, but observers later concluded they were likely seeing stars and atmospheric refraction; the later official report also noted brief radar scope interference near the observed area. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 108–110](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1951, January 20** — 8:20 p.m. On January 20, 1951 at about 2020 hours, two tower operators saw a light over a city airport and asked the pilots of a commercial aircraft taking off to investigate. At 2026 hours the pilots observed an object that flew abreast of their climbing turn, blinked lights resembling running lights, then turned toward the plane, crossed its nose, appeared instantly on the other side flying the same direction, and slipped under them after 2-3 seconds. They described it as airplane-like with a cigar-shaped body and straight wings, somewhat larger than a B-29, with no engine nacelles visible. ([CIA-UAP-015, pp. 89–90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1951, January** _(cluster ×2)_ — In January 1951 near Oakridge, Tennessee, two Air Force aircraft attempted to intercept an unidentified object and obtained a radar lock. At about 7,000 feet, the target was estimated by radar to be at 10 to 25 degrees elevation. The pilots made three passes, but each time their radar led them first upward and then down toward a specific point on the ground, prompting a temperature-inversion reflection interpretation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 73](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1951, February 10** — 12:55 a.m. On 10 Feb 1951 at 0055Z, the crew of Navy 6501 flying near 49°50' N, 50°03' W over the North Atlantic reported an unidentified object that first appeared as a distant heavy light like city lights on the surface, then approached rapidly, brightening into a very large yellowish fire-colored semi-circular form. As it neared the aircraft it made an abrupt near-180-degree turn and vanished rapidly over the horizon as a small ball. The five-man crew, all experienced North Atlantic fliers, watched it for about seven to eight minutes from a plane at 10,000 feet. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 117–118](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1951, February 13** — This is not a specific sighting report but a press article summarizing Dr. Urner Liddel's explanation that many 'flying saucers' were actually Navy cosmic-ray balloon 'skyhooks' launched since 1947. The article describes huge plastic balloons, their appearance at dusk, and notes Liddel's review of 2,000 reports, concluding that reliable observations were attributable to the balloons. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1951, July 6** — A witness with a long personal interest in flying saucers reported seeing an orange disk-like globe hovering for four minutes over the Lynchburg College administration building in Lynchburg, Virginia, on the morning of 1951-07-06. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1951, September 10** _(cluster ×2)_ — 11:10 a.m. Between 10 and 11 September 1951, multiple radar operators at Fort Monmouth, New Jersey tracked several fast-moving targets exhibiting unusual speed, maneuverability, and very strong radar returns. One low-flying target southeast of the base was initially mistaken for a ship, then seen to move too fast and change azimuth rapidly. Later targets were tracked north and northeast of Fort Monmouth at extreme elevation angles and ranges up to 32,000 yards, with operators resorting to manual tracking because the targets exceeded the SCR 584's aided tracking capability. One target near Navesink appeared stationary or hovering before rising nearly vertically and then accelerating southward. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 124–128](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1951, December 14** — 4:05 p.m. Radar operators at McGhee Tyson Airport tracked a group of unidentified targets for about three hours on 14 December 1950/1951 over the Oak Ridge atomic energy project area near Knoxville, Tennessee. The targets blanketed the radar scopes, could not be identified from the radar image, and a fighter interception attempt produced negative results. Lt. Robinson photographed the scope readings for documentation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 107](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1951** — Donald Slayton, a Mercury astronaut, described a 1951 daytime sighting while test-flying a P-51 fighter over Minneapolis at about 10,000 feet. He first thought the object might be a kite, then a weather balloon about three feet across and gray, but as he closed in it appeared disk- or saucer-shaped. He reported it suddenly pulling away, executing about a 45-degree climbing turn, accelerating, and disappearing. ([USG-UAP-D001, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1951** — A secondhand report describes a respected farmer near Circleville, Ohio, who allegedly saw a lighted object dancing and flying around him in a pasture late at night about two years before the 1953 letter; the letter itself mainly challenges rumors that FBI agents had discussed captured flying saucers from Mars with the farmer and notes the story was circulating in the community and had appeared in a local newspaper. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 149–153](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, March 6** — 9:00 a.m. Chicago artist Karl Nyquist reported that on 1952-03-06 at about 9:00 AM he observed a white, bluish-tinged circular disc over Fullerton Avenue from his home window facing south. He estimated it at roughly 7,000 feet altitude, saw it emerge from an eastern cloud, pause motionless briefly, then fly due south at very high speed and disappear in about three seconds. He reported no sound, lights, or exhaust, believed it may have been radio controlled, and made a sketch that was forwarded with the report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 129](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, March 13** — 10:20 p.m. On 1952-03-13 at about 10:20 p.m., Lt. Commander Melvin Michael Kuhn, U.S. Navy, observed from the backyard of 1900 Graybar Lane in Nashville, Tennessee, a small circular blue object low in the southwest sky. He said it was about half the size of the moon, vivid deep blue with a slight reddish fringe on the aft end, moving from northwest to southeast, silent, and visible for only a few seconds under clear, starry conditions with a full moon. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 131](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, March 29** — 11:20 a.m. On 1952-03-29 at 11:20 a.m. over Northern Japan, Lt. David C. Brigham reported a small metallic disc-shaped object on a controlled sweeping pass near an F-84 Thunderjet; he observed it at very close range for about 10 seconds on a bright, cloudless day, describing it as thin, shiny like polished chromium, and leaving no exhaust or vapor trails. The object reportedly caught up with the jet, hovered briefly, and then shot out of sight. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, March 29** — 10:45 p.m. On March 29, 1952, around 10:45 p.m. near the Harness Track on Ritchie Highway in Maryland, Donald F. Stewart and George Tyler saw a slowly moving sky object after a vacuum-cleaner-like sound drew their attention. The object, estimated as large as a two-motored transport plane and about 200 feet up, hovered over the highway and their car for about two minutes, during which the car engine died; a nearby yellow Pontiac reportedly had the same problem. The object then turned perpendicular with a whirring sound and shot away rapidly like a rocket. Investigators noted the witness appeared credible but could not identify the object, speculating it may have been 'ours'. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 164–166](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, March 29** — 10:45 p.m. On 29 March 1952 at about 10:45 PM near Ritchie Highway south of Baltimore, Maryland, Donald Stewart and George Tyler reported a disc-like aerial object while driving north in a 1949 Anglia Vampire. Stewart described a flat silver disk with a central dome/cupola, porthole-like detail, and bright edge lights, hovering roughly 200 feet above the car for about three minutes, making a vacuum-cleaner-like sound and allegedly killing the engine. A later Air Force Office of Special Investigations inquiry found Tyler recanted and said he had not actually seen the object, and the investigation concluded there was no further inquiry contemplated. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 152–163](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, March 29** — 10:45 p.m. On March 29, 1952 at about 10:45 P.M. near Ritchie Highway by the harness track outside Baltimore, Maryland, George S. Tyler III and a friend, Donald Stewart, reported a slowly moving object hovering over their car as the engine seemed to die and the vehicle drifted to a stop. Tyler described a round, slightly tilted craft with yellowish-green light that pulsed in intensity, a possible St. Elmo's fire-like glow around the edges, wavering motion while hovering, and a short yellowish-green exhaust as it climbed and departed at extreme speed to a lower-than-airliner altitude. He also later said the car engine appeared to restart on its own. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 167–169](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, April 15** — 4:30 p.m. On 15 April at 16:30 in El Paso, Texas, a single witness observed an unknown grayish object straight overhead at about 30 degrees elevation. The object was seen for about 15 minutes and reportedly left a very thin smoke trail before dissipating. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, April 20** — 2:30 p.m. Louie Masonick, Jr., Representative for Minnesota, reported sighting a round dull gray object northeast of Long Prairie, Minnesota on April 20, 1952 at about 2:30 p.m. The object traveled east to west in a straight path at an estimated 250 mph, about 5,000 feet altitude, remained visible for roughly 45 seconds, and produced a low hum as it ascended. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 123](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, April 20** — 9:15 p.m. On 1952-04-20, a naval aviation student, his wife, and several other witnesses at a drive-in movie observed about 20 groups of two to nine aerial objects passing overhead between 2115 and 2240 hours. The objects traveled in straight lines and made turns like conventional aircraft, but were described as shaped like conventional aircraft and each was surrounded by a red glow, with the objects themselves also glowing on a cloudless night. ([CIA-UAP-015, pp. 88–89](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1952, May 10** _(cluster ×3)_ — 10:45 p.m. On May 10, 1952, four DuPont employees at the Savannah River Plant near Ellenton, South Carolina reported a series of eight disc-shaped objects passing near the '400 area' from multiple directions. The objects were described as about 15 inches in diameter, yellow-gold in color, moving at high speed at high altitude without noise, weaving left to right but generally holding course; one flew so low it had to rise over tall tanks. The Savannah FBI office forwarded the report but stated it was not actively investigating and was sending the information to headquarters for whatever action deemed advisable. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 140–142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, May 25** — 8:50 p.m. On May 25, 1952 at about 8:50 PM EST, three women in Ashland, Kentucky saw strange oval objects low in the sky for two or three minutes. They were described as floating like clouds, looking like large oysters with fish tails, coming from the north, circling over Ashland, and then departing in the opposite direction. The report notes the observers thought they could have been balloons. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 150](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, June 6** — 8:42 a.m. A Flight Sergeant observed a disc-like object over an Air Force base in Korea at 0842 hours on 1952-06-06. The object displayed repeated spinning and tumbling, moved erratically, briefly stopped, shot straight up, resumed level flight, changed course, disappeared into the sun, then reappeared and flew back and forth across the sun; an F-86 passed between the observer and the object, and another man also saw it near the sun. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 93](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1952** — A newspaper feature about Oskar Linke’s 1950 sighting near the Soviet zone in Germany describes him and his 12-year-old stepdaughter observing a 50-foot saucer with a 10-foot conning tower and two metal-clad figures in a forest clearing; the article emphasizes that the craft and its occupants took off shortly after the sighting. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, pp. 177–178](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, July 14** — A Chicago newspaper service item reported that O'Hara Air Force Base officers said flying-saucer reports had increased in the Chicago vicinity that week, with the Public Information Office receiving 16 reports of mysterious objects in the sky. Officers denied any special alert, said jet patrols were always on alert, and noted that some reports may have come from an orphanage picnic where 5,000 toy balloons were released. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, July 14** — Two veteran Pan American Airways pilots, W. B. Nash and W. H. Fortenberry, reported seeing eight huge glowing orange-red disc-like objects near Norfolk, Virginia, from their DC-4 at night. The six first appeared in echelon formation below the aircraft, then turned sharply west, banked upward almost vertically, and were joined by two more before the lights flickered out and the objects vanished. The pilots judged the objects intelligently controlled and estimated extreme speed and maneuverability. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 59–60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, July 16** — 9:35 a.m. The Coast Guard released a photograph taken over its Salem, Massachusetts air station showing four brilliant white, ragged-edged round objects in V-formation with shaft-like extensions of light across each center. The image was made by Coast Guard photographer Shell Alpert at 9:35 a.m. on July 16 through an open window of the station photo laboratory, and Coast Guard experts said the negative showed no retouching or fakery. The negative was later sent to Dayton, Ohio, for Air Force investigation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, July 16** — Four Florida pilots, including Air Force and World War II veteran Capt. John Baldwin, reported seeing a flying saucer hovering over the Hanford Atomic Plant near Richland, Washington. Baldwin said the object was a perfectly round, white, almost transparent disc with small vapor trails like tentacles, seen below wispy clouds at about 9,000 feet. The object appeared to back away, change shape from round to flat, gain speed, and then vanish quickly. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, July 19** — 12:00 a.m. On July 19, 1952 at midnight, a part-time farmer and a hired hand were curing tobacco when they looked up and saw two cigar-shaped objects. One hovered while the other moved east and then returned; after this both ascended until disappearing from view. The objects were described as transparent, internally illuminated, with an exhaust at one end and no projections. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1952, July 27** _(cluster ×3)_ — 10:00 a.m. On 1952-07-27 at about 10:00 A.M., Dr. R. E. McIndoo reported that while fishing in the back waters of Thessalon Lake, Ontario, he saw a formation of 16 to 20 bombing planes flying south at an estimated altitude of four to five miles. He said the planes suddenly dropped objects he first thought were parachutes; the objects fell straight for a short time, then emitted vapor and sped off to the southwest with single bluish smoke trails, while the planes themselves had twin bluish smoke trails. He and companion Paul Gaddis were the only named witnesses, and no further explanation was developed in the file. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 192](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, July 27** — 9:08 p.m. On the night of July 27, 1952, Washington, D.C. was reported to receive another visit from unidentified aerial objects, with at least a dozen glowing lights seen within a 30-mile radius and simultaneously picked up on radar at Washington National Airport. Two F-94 jet fighters were scrambled at 11:25 p.m.; pilots saw the lights but could not close to within about 7 miles. One pilot later tracked four lights near Andrews Air Force Base and a steady white light east of Mount Vernon, but the objects remained hard to follow and eventually faded or were lost. Airline, private, and military pilots also reported the lights, which were variously described as blue lights, a cigarette ember, or orange-red clusters, while radar operators plotted speeds between 38 and 90 mph and noted clear blips consistent with solid targets. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 58](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952** — On July 27 in the evening, Frank Ryan, a court operator in Lake City, Seattle, observed a single white object from the ground over the north side of Lake Washington. He and his wife watched it for about 10 minutes as it appeared to speed across the evening sky, passing directly overhead; a news photograph was taken. Ryan described it as a shiny ball at great height with a stained-looking center/bill. ([DOW-UAP-D087, pp. 36–37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DOW-UAP-D087_US-AirForce_Analysis-of-Flying-Objects-in-the-US_1-100.pdf>))

**1952, July 28** — 2:15 a.m. Harry H. Winchester reported by telephone that during a severe electrical storm in the early morning of 1952-07-28 at his residence in Jersey Shore, Pennsylvania, he observed seven unusual flashes in the sky, each accompanied by a loud report. The FBI memorandum notes the complainant appeared under the influence of intoxicants and that his call was prompted by recent newspaper stories about similar phenomena. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 212](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, July 28** — 4:48 a.m. On the morning of 1952-07-28 over Franklin, Indiana, police officers and other witnesses observed three round, flat, saucer-like objects at about 15,000 feet. One larger bright object with a white-yellow light appeared to keep track of two smaller objects that glowed orange and red; the smaller pair executed barrel rolls, loops, spins, and abrupt 45- and 90-degree turns at very high estimated speeds, repeatedly moving south out of sight and returning, while the larger object circled and eventually moved west out of sight after apparently enveloping the smaller two. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 123](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, July 28** — This FBI office memorandum is not a firsthand sighting report but an administrative note about coordination between FBI and Air Force Intelligence after reports of observed aerial objects described as "Flying Saucers." It requests that future FBI-received reports of such observations be immediately relayed to OSI at Bolling Field. No specific object description, witness account, place, or observation details are provided. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 190](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1952, July 29** — On the night of July 29, 1952, five or more residents of the Navy Homes area in Pasco, Washington reported a brilliant, very fast circular object flashing mostly green lights as it streaked west-to-east across the sky. Multiple witnesses, including adults and children, independently described it as round like a saucer or a giant pinwheel, spinning, ringed with lights, and moving so fast it seemed to cover miles in a fraction of a second. A follow-up news item later noted additional 'flying saucer' reports from the Kennewick-Pasco area, including disc-like and triangular-at-times objects, but those appear to be separate sightings in the same local wave. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1952, July 30** — 3:00 a.m. Press accounts from July 1952 describe a Washington-area radar and public sighting episode involving multiple unidentifiable dots on Civil Aeronautics Administration radar, with no corroborating visual confirmation from airline pilots or nearby Air Force radar. The accompanying newspaper analysis then argues the broader saucer phenomenon is likely explained by natural causes and atmospheric refraction, citing Harvard astrophysicist Donald H. Menzel and Air Force intelligence statements. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 55–56](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, July 31** — 10:15 a.m. On 31 Jul 1952 in the 200 block of Brooks Avenue, Passaic, New Jersey, John H. Riley and George J. Stock reported photographing a greyish saucer-shaped aerial object seen around 10:15 a.m. in clear, hot weather. The object was said to hover about 200 feet up, move southeast, stop overhead, tilt as if observing the ground, extend a brief antenna-like projection, make no sound, and then depart rapidly to the southeast; investigators later doubted the photos' authenticity and requested background checks on the witnesses and photographer. ([FBI-UAP-D012, pp. 5–12](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1952, July 31** — On 1952-07-31, civilian photographer George J. Stock allegedly photographed an unidentified aerial object near Passaic, New Jersey, while John R. Riley witnessed the event and later developed and printed the film. The FBI Newark office investigated the background of both men at the request of the USAF Inspector General/OSI to assess reliability, but did not conduct a field investigation of the sighting itself. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, August 1** — On 1952-08-01, a Coast Guard photographer reportedly took a daylight photograph over Salem, Massachusetts showing four bizarre lights burning brilliantly in the sky. The next day, a Harvard astrophysicist dismissed the photograph as scientifically worthless because it lacked supporting data such as temperature distribution and altitude. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 84](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, August 2** — A Jackson, Minnesota farmer's family reported a steady white light circling their farm on a Sunday morning, and later found a round pile of shiny tinsel-like material in a pasture with burned clover beneath it and three similar burned patches arranged in a triangle. The witness believed the material had not fallen from a plane because it was loose and not in a container, and asked the FBI to analyze the sample, which was later described as shredded thin strips of tin foil. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 27–29](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, August 8** — 9:30 p.m. On August 8, 1952 at about 9:30 PM EST, two employees of the E. I. Du Pont Company reported seeing a blue light with an orange fringe shaped like a saucer fly over the 400 Area of the Savannah River Plant (AEC). The object was described as moving at a high rate of speed and heading northeast. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, August 10** — A Washington City News Service article reports physicist Noel W. Scott explaining that recent radar-spotted 'objects' and flying 'things' may be natural 'anode glows' created by ionization of thin upper-atmospheric air. He said he produced similar balloon-like blobs of light in a vacuum jar at Fort Belvoir, could move them at will, and that such glows might appear on radar; the piece also notes five unidentified objects were spotted by radar at Andrews Air Force Base shortly before midnight. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, August 11** — A Martinsburg, West Virginia woman found a mysterious five-foot-square aluminum-covered object on her farm, prompting flying-saucer speculation until Andrews Air Force Base identified it as an Air Weather Service balloon-borne device used to measure wind direction and speed by radar. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, August 25** — 5:35 a.m. At 0535 on 1952-08-25, a radio station musician driving to work saw a large hovering craft about 10 feet above a field near the road. He stopped and watched from the road, approximately 100 yards away, unable to approach because of an artificial leg and rough terrain. The object rocked slightly, emitted a deep throbbing sound, then ascended vertically through broken clouds with a sound likened to a covey of quail flushing; vegetation was blown about near the ground. The craft was described as a dull aluminum, smooth object with windows, blue lights, a visible seated humanoid figure in front, internal activity in the midsection, and a ring of fast-spinning small propellers around its outer edge. ([CIA-UAP-015, pp. 99–100](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1952, August 25** — A Philadelphia Inquirer article reports that Berks County 'flying saucers' observed on Monday were explained by Air National Guard and CAA tower personnel as vapor trails from a B-36 bomber and a jet flying at 40,000 to 45,000 feet; the same article also notes an unrelated eyewitness report by Herbert Long of a close road-level object near Maxatawny and mentions Harry Feinsuer's report of a plane seeming to release a saucer-like object. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 49–50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, August 29** — On or before August 29, 1952, a scoutmaster in West Palm Beach, Florida, Mr. D. S. Desvergers, reported seeing an unidentified flying object in a desolate area. He alleged that a roughly 30-foot object hovered over his head and fired a red blob, after which he lost consciousness. The event left holes burned in his cap and singeing on his arm hair, prompting an FBI examination of the cap for residue and burn-pattern analysis. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, September 13** — On September 13, 1952 in Melbourne, Australia, a young woman reported seeing a noiseless green ball moving so fast that she thought it could not be a plane or meteor; she also said it smelled like rotten eggs. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, September 13** — On the night before the Sep 13, 1952 newspaper report, witnesses across Washington and five eastern states reported a fast-moving fiery object crossing the sky, described variously as a greenish fireball, a rocket-like body with a long flame tail, a bright sun-colored object, or a star-like shape. Authorities at the Naval Observatory, Weather Bureau, and National Airport leaned toward a meteor explanation, while some witnesses thought it might have been a flying saucer or flare-like craft. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, September 14** — 5:30 p.m. A former Air Force B-29 test mechanic and scientific lecturer, Robert G. Garner of San Francisco, reported that he and his wife saw two silver-grey objects flying erratically over San Francisco Airport at about 5:30 p.m. on the day before the report. He said the objects looked like cross-sections of a cone clipped off at both ends, were about 150 to 200 feet in diameter, flew at roughly 12,000 feet altitude, and were traveling at an estimated 1,800 mph. Garner said he was convinced they were not of earthly origin. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, September 20** _(cluster ×2)_ — Law enforcement officials in Montana investigated a strange white object that reportedly streaked across the sky for about 100 miles late at night, with sightings reported from Bozeman to Helena and also over Butte and Coulder; CAA officials said no plane in the sky could account for it. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, September 20** — During RAF Exercise Mainbrace near Topluffe, York, England, on 1952-09-20, RAF pilots reported a white, silver circular object at about 15,000 feet that moved slowly forward, then descended swinging like a pendulum, followed the aircraft, revolved on its own axis at times, and eventually departed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, September 22** — 9:08 p.m. Alan K. Stazer reported sighting a disc-shaped object east of Los Angeles at about 9:08 p.m. on 1952-09-22. He described it as yellowish white, stationary for about 4 seconds, about 12 degrees above the horizon, approximately 50 feet in diameter, and traveling due north. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 123](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 3** — 10:30 p.m. Two persons in Mayaguez, Puerto Rico reported seeing strange red objects cruising east at about 10:30 p.m. on October 3, 1952. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 6** — Several people observed a 'flying saucer' over South Seattle that hovered motionless for nearly five minutes before streaking away at very high speed and disappearing within seconds. Arthur Owley, a World War II anti-aircraft artillery major, and two friends watched it through binoculars and said it appeared to have four sources of light. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1952, October 7** — On 1952-10-07 in Southern France, two Air France pilots reported sighting a flying saucer over the region. The provided source text is a brief datelined mention embedded in an FBI declassified file context, with no further descriptive details about the object or flight behavior. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 13** — In October 1952, reports from Norway and Sweden described strange saucer-like objects in the sky. The Norwegian Government stated that a strange object resembling a saucer had landed on Norwegian soil, while Stockholm, Sweden, also reported sightings of strange objects. German experts were quoted as claiming the devices were of Russian origin. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 16** — 7:33 p.m. On 1952-10-16 at 19:33 over International Airport, New York, an eyewitness reported a blue flame that flashed overhead and appeared as a fiery ball-like object. A Hayden Planetarium official suggested it may have been a fiery meteor. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 17** — About a dozen people in Oloron, France, including a schoolmaster, reportedly watched multiple flying saucers surrounding a long cigar-like object moving through a clear sky at about 6,000 feet on October 17, 1952. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 23** — 10:30 p.m. Mrs. Nelvar Spurgeon reported that around 10:30 P.M. on 1952-10-23, while standing in front of the Douglas Hotel, she observed an unknown object moving from overhead toward an easterly position. She described it as resembling a falling star except that it suddenly changed course, appearing as a round diffused light. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 9](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1952, October 29** — On 1952-10-29 in Gaillac, southern France, about 20 townspeople watched a formation of white circular objects with slightly swollen centers spin across the sky around a larger cigar-shaped object. As they passed overhead, they dropped bright white thread-like material that settled on trees and telephone lines and reportedly melted like ice when handled; a police officer described it as resembling glass wool. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October 29** — On 1952-10-29 at the western front of Korea, U.S. troops reportedly observed about a half-dozen mysterious spark-throwing 'cartwheels' in the sky. The objects were described as looking like 18-inch circles and moving within a roughly 15-foot circle. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, October** — In Long Island, New York, in October 1952, a terrific air explosion occurred over a small area, breaking windows, cracking sidewalks, and causing general panic. The report states that no planes were in the area or scheduled at the time. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 115](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1952, November 18** — This item is not a sighting report but a newspaper/FBI-file clipping explaining that reported November lights would be meteor showers, specifically Taurid meteors around the 10th and Leonid meteors around the 16th. The text explicitly says the lights are not flying saucers and attributes them to solid particles from outer space burning in the atmosphere. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1952, December mid-December** — A retrospective account in Harold T. Wilkins' 1954 book describes two unidentified humanoid visitors allegedly hired in Los Angeles around January 20, 1953, after they claimed to have arrived from another planet in a small flying saucer that landed on the Mojave Desert in December 1952. The men were described as exceptionally tall, emaciated, bluish-green, and odd-eared, and one reportedly dented a steel filing cabinet top with a curved hand, leaving an impression later said by a metallurgical chemist to require extraordinary force and containing unknown elements. FBI agents were said to have investigated, but the visitors vanished by mid-February 1953. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 254–258](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1952, December 22** — 7:30 p.m. On December 22, 1952 at about 19:30, an instrument technician driving from a large city toward an Air Force base observed a UFO from his car, stopped to watch it, and saw it rise toward the zenith in spurts, move leftward at about a 45-degree angle, then travel level at high speed while changing from mostly white to showing a red side during what appeared to be three rolls and a brief period of no light. It later held position south of Jupiter at high altitude and darted left and right before the witness stopped watching after about 15 minutes. ([CIA-UAP-015, p. 92](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-015-Project_Blue_Book_Special_Report_No_14.pdf>))

**1952, December 23** — A December 1952 memorandum recounts British UFO activity, noting that a Yorkshire incident drew RAF officials and pilots who reportedly saw a “perfect flying saucer” during a demonstration at an RAF field. The report also mentions the British standing committee on flying saucers, discussion with R. V. Jones, and reference to the Tremonton film and earlier Swedish incident. ([CIA-UAP-014, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-014_British_Activity_in_the_Field_of_Unidentified_Flying_Objects.pdf>))

**1952** — This source is a book jacket and FBI file context page, not a primary sighting account. It describes Gray Barker's 1952 West Virginia saucer experience and his later research into civilian flying saucer investigators, while the FBI file note mentions a broader 62-HQ-83894 UFO case file containing investigative records, eyewitness testimonies, photographs, proposals, and media coverage from 1947-1968. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_403, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_403.pdf>))

**1953, January 10** — A newspaper report from Santa Fe, New Mexico, speculated that forthcoming Defense Department disclosures about guided missile research at White Sands Proving Grounds might explain some 'flying saucer' sightings over southwestern skies. Col. M. G. Hendricks explicitly denied that any flying-saucer-like device would be demonstrated and said the work was strictly missile research. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, January 14** — The report is not a direct sighting account but a newspaper-style discussion from Santa Fe, New Mexico, saying guided missile research at White Sands may soon explain some 'flying saucer' reports seen in southwestern skies; Col. M.G. Hendricks explicitly denied any flying-saucer demonstration or connection to the research. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, January 17** — This is not a specific sighting report but a draft memorandum discussing the broader U.S. UFO situation; it notes that since 1947 approximately 1,500 official sightings had been received and about 20% remained unexplained, and recommends expanded scientific research by the Department of Defense. ([CIA-UAP-002, pp. 41–42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-002_Scientific-Advisory-Panel-on-Unidentified-Flying-Objects_Report_1952-1953.pdf>))

**1953, February 10** — 10:10 a.m. On 1953-02-10 at about 10:10 A.M., Mrs. Wiskerken of Seattle reported seeing a very large, very bright silver object high in the sky directly above and somewhat west of Sand Point Naval Air Station. She described it as like a large parachute and said it faded away to the north after about two minutes. A second report from Kathleen Carter described a bright object high over Sand Point Naval Air Station on 1953-02-09 at about 15:08 while riding a bus over the University Bridge; no size, shape, or speed could be given. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 10](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1953, March 4** — A Chilean correspondent in Valparaiso wrote the FBI on 1953-03-04, arguing that reported electrical failures, vehicle stoppages near Berlin, and a newspaper account of a plane encountering a flying saucer might all reflect the same unknown force associated with Dr. Julius Linke; the letter is speculative rather than a first-hand sighting report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 135–138](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, March 9** _(cluster ×2)_ — 9:05 p.m. At 9:05 p.m. on March 9, 1953, a man identifying himself as John Bailey telephoned the FBI and reported that he had just seen a flying saucer near Bowie, Maryland. He gave no usable details about the object, his residence, or where he was calling from, and the Bureau noted that no investigation was being conducted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 131](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, April 8** — 11:30 a.m. On 1953-04-08 at 11:30 AM, five witnesses at Fort Buchanan, Puerto Rico—three U.S. Army captains, a sergeant, and one civilian—reported a bright star-like or ball-of-fire object at great height moving rapidly overhead. The FBI later relayed a report from the Wing Intelligence Officer at Ramey Air Force Base, noting that two RB-36 aircraft were also in the area, but the witnesses could not further describe the object. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, May 7** — 9:25 p.m. On May 7, 1953 at about 9:25 PM near a school parking lot in Watertown, Massachusetts, Mrs. Robert H. Davisson reported seeing a round orange object traveling horizontally at a slow speed for a few seconds before disappearing. Another student was present and also saw it. The witness later tried to report the sighting to Civil Defense, the Air Force, and police, but was unable to get clear guidance. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 147–148](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, June 1** — Bruce Stevenson of Rural Route 2, Circleville, Ohio reported that a saucer-shaped object had landed on his farm sometime before June 1953 and the account reached local newspapers. The FBI later checked rumors surrounding whether Stevenson had been interviewed by an FBI representative; Stevenson said he was instead visited in June 1953 by Jack W. Grant of Columbus, who claimed to be a television-antenna salesman and saucer researcher, denied any official government connection, and mentioned he had once been 'checked' by the FBI. No prior FBI interview of Stevenson was found in Cincinnati files. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 164–166](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, July 8** _(cluster ×3)_ — 3:45 a.m. On July 8, 1953, near Mableton, Georgia, Edward E. Waters and two companions reportedly encountered three small animal-like beings while driving on Bankhead Highway; Waters said the creatures were believed to have come from a flying saucer, two escaped into it, and a third was struck and killed by the automobile. A reporter, Tom McRae of the Atlanta Constitution, later viewed the killed animal and described it as monkey-like, about 21 inches long with a pointed head, large ears, and pinkish skin; a veterinarian was said to have examined it, but no scientific identification is recorded in the report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 154–156](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, July 9** — This report does not describe a UAP sighting; it concerns a dead animal examined by a veterinarian, with discussion about missing hair, pallor at death, a missing tail possibly cut off in an accident, and blood around the mouth resembling human blood. The Bureau notes the matter is being furnished for information only and no investigation is being conducted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 163](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, July 9** — Edward Watters and two friends reported seeing a flying saucer on U.S. Highway 78 near Atlanta, Georgia, with several small creatures running toward it; they said they ran over one creature, while the others entered the saucer and it departed in a blaze of light. A later comment in the newspaper noted that an anatomy professor identified the exhibited 'man from Mars' as a skinned monkey. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, p. 159](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1953, July 15** — 2:30 p.m. On 1953-07-15 near Richland, Washington, Robert T. Jaske and three guests observed two bright shimmering specks at very high altitude over the AEC reservation area; one departed rapidly west, while the other was tracked in a 30x telescope and appeared as a tiny transparent disc-like object with four helicopter-like arms and a thin vaporous ring, hovering briefly before accelerating eastward and disappearing in about 10 seconds. An FBI memo later relayed AEC/5th AAA Group information that a meteorological balloon launched at 2:05 PM and tracked to 25,000 feet likely explained the sighting. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 11–15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1953, August 2** — 9:20 p.m. On 1953-08-02 at about 9:20 p.m. over the Hanford Reservation area near Kennewick/Richland, Washington, two witnesses reported two very brilliant reddish-orange balls of fire traveling south by west at very high speed and disappearing over Rattlesnake Mountain. The report was relayed to FBI Seattle by AEC Security Division personnel and later discussed with airport and AEC officials, who found no radar testing or aircraft reports at the time and suggested the lights may have been fog banks illuminated by the Pasco airport beacon. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 16–17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1953, August 23** — An Associated Press article reported that Air Force "Moby Dick" helium balloons, launched routinely to study the upper atmosphere, had been mistaken for flying saucers. The piece said more than 90 percent of saucer reports coincided with the balloons' logged ascents and flight paths, and described one example where SAC units in Texas chased a glowing balloon at about 90,000 feet before B-36 crews and jet fighters abandoned the pursuit. It also cited other balloon misidentifications over San Francisco and Dayton. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, December 16** — A private plane pilot near Bowling Green, Ohio reported seeing an unidentified object brighter than the sun for about five seconds while cruising about 80 mph at roughly 2,000 feet, two miles west of the city. He described it as a brilliant white ball of fire, like molten steel, which turned blue and moved straight west; he first saw it traveling south. He said the sky was clear, no other planes were in sight, and the object appeared about a foot in diameter at approximately his altitude. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, December 17** — 12:00 p.m. A Swedish passenger airliner crew reported a disc-shaped metallic object over southern Sweden near Haessleholm shortly after noon on 1953-12-17, prompting the Swedish Royal Air Force to order a full investigation and collect radar station reports. The captain described it as symmetrical, circular, and faster than sound, flying southward above a low cloud layer before vanishing toward the Baltic shore near Peenemunde. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, December 17** — This memorandum is a status summary of the Air Force/OSI UFOB project rather than a single incident report; it notes that most sightings were handled by ATIC/ADC via reports, field checks, meteorological/astronomical/aircraft/balloon comparisons, and filing, with about ten percent of sightings unsolved over the prior year. ([CIA-UAP-007, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-007_Current_Status_of_Unidentified_Flying_Objects_UFO_Project.pdf>))

**1953, December 28** — Press article discussing the Air Force's unresolved flying-saucer investigations and the disputed Utah film analysis; it does not describe a single firsthand sighting with a specific observer, but summarizes claims that a group of unknown round luminous objects moved in circular or elliptical patterns at high speed and could not be explained as birds, balloons, known aircraft, or conventional objects. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 36–37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, December 30** — 9:05 p.m. At Quantico Marine Base, a moving blinking red light was first seen by Pfc. Norman Viets on 1953-12-30 near the Tank Park/Camp Barrett area and was repeatedly observed over the next several nights by Marines and officers. Witnesses reported it moving along tree lines, dropping straight down, rising straight up, hovering, and at times appearing as multiple lights; Navy/Marine officials later suggested it was likely a new aircraft navigation light, though many witnesses remained unconvinced. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 31–32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1953, December 31** — This report is not a firsthand sighting account but a discussion of Truman Bethurum's alleged flying-saucer encounters described in a 1953 Daily Breeze article and referenced in an FBI file; the text says Bethurum, a truck driver from Redondo Beach, California, claimed an encounter in the Nevada desert with a crew of space explorers under a woman commandant and said he was aboard flying saucers on eleven occasions. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 6–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1953, December 31** — On New Year's Eve near Quantico, Virginia, two Marine sentries at Camp Barrett reported seeing an object in the sky that they described as a flying saucer and said it had landed; a helicopter was dispatched from Quantico, but by the time it arrived the object had risen and disappeared. Marine officials later confirmed a report had been made and said the matter was under higher authority investigation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1954, January 5** — At Quantico Marine Base, Marines reported a mysterious reddish blinking or revolving light over the reservation on the nights of December 30 and January 1, 2, and 3, 1954. After a field look by Marine officers in the Guadacanal area of the base, authorities concluded the lights were commercial airliners outfitted with newly installed flashing red navigational lights on the tail, which created an illusion of nearness. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1954, April 12** — The report is not a direct sighting account but an FBI memorandum enclosing translated letters from Alois Pivec/Adolph Dornig, who claimed he had discovered a new aerodynamic principle and that the invention known as a 'flying saucer' was his own, describing experimental flying machines allegedly developed before and after the war, with claimed speeds of 250-300 km/h and 2600-2800 km/h and fatal test flights in South America. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 169–181](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1954, May 1** — 12:00 a.m. West of Logan, Utah, on 1954-05-01 around midnight, residents reported a violent ground explosion preceded by a mysterious streak/fireball-like light in the sky, after which a 16-foot-wide, 6-foot-deep crater and scattered sod were found; University and geological experts investigated and concluded the crater was not produced by a conventional meteorite fall, but the source of the explosion remained unidentified. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, June 3** — On the evening of 1947-07-07, Tacoma police officers Stan Johnson and Evan Davies reported seeing a saucer-like object near The Narrows while cruising the Jackson-Alexander Road; in the related 1954 newspaper account, Oliver Tiedeman said he and passenger Phil Raboin saw one of several fast-moving objects while driving from Doty toward Chehalis. The objects were described as moving northward at very high speed, stopping briefly, then accelerating eastward or southeasterly, with multiple independent civilian/police witnesses mentioned in the press and later USAF/FBI correspondence noting no official Air Force report and no investigation. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 21–24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, June 6** — 11:35 p.m. On 6 June 1954 at 11:35 PM, Gale Peck, supervisor of a local ground observer group in Pasco, Washington, reported seeing two white half-moon shaped objects slightly north of Pasco at an estimated 1,000 feet altitude. The objects were described as about six times the size of a half moon, traveling together at a 5-degree angle to the horizon, and during about ten minutes of observation one object transformed into a large fireball while the other split into two fireballs. No distance or speed could be estimated, and the sighting was later relayed through Air Force and FBI channels. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 22–25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, June 7** — This FBI memorandum concerns publicity and investigative follow-up around Truman Bethurum's claimed flying-saucer encounter, not a firsthand UAP sighting described by the reporting office. The document relays that Thomas Eickhoff showed magazine articles about Bethurum's alleged experience with space explorers and said Bethurum had reportedly been aboard flying saucers on eleven occasions. The FBI advised it had no jurisdiction over flying-disc matters and that the Air Force handled such investigations. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 192–198](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1954, June 8** — 12:00 a.m. At midnight on 8 June 1954, at least seven people in Tacoma, Washington, watched an orange glow described as a ball appear over the city, shrink from grapefruit size to orange size, and disappear. The first observer was Tacoma transit driver Richard K. Hirst, who was making a 6th Ave. run and stopped near Union; his three passengers also saw it, and three more people at a restaurant reportedly joined in observing the phenomenon. Hirst estimated it was about 1,000 feet high; McChord AFB tower reported no contact. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 26–29](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, June 8** — 6:30 p.m. On 1954-06-08 at about 18:30, a complainant reported that he and neighbors watched two metallic-luster objects move westerly across the sky for about 1.5 hours, through binoculars, from above his home toward the horizon. He estimated they were about 7 to 10 degrees apart, roughly 30 miles high and about 1000 feet in diameter, with little puffs of white smoke ejecting every 15 minutes; he also presumed a gondola-like structure beneath them and noted jets in the sky at the time. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 20](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, June 21** — 3:00 p.m. A complaint relayed information from CAA employee William Flener, who heard from a reliable pilot near McMurray, Washington, that an unidentified aircraft—believed by the pilot to be a helicopter—appeared on several occasions in the early morning hours, hovered over the highway in the vicinity of McMurray, had no running lights, remained about 10-20 minutes per visit, and then departed. The pilot considered the repeated low-level hovering suspicious because the engine sound remained constant rather than varying like a normal aircraft throttle change. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 34–35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, June 21** — The provided document is not a sighting report but a June 21, 1954 letter to the FBI from Joseph Gunderson asking whether the book "Flying Saucers Have Landed" and George Adamski's Venus contact story were factual. No actual UAP event, observation, date of sighting, or location is described. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_007, pp. 204–205](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_7.pdf>))

**1954, June 22** — 7:30 p.m. On 22 June 1954, radar at Neah Bay picked up an unidentified target northwest of the station, and two F-86D interceptors scrambled from McChord Field tracked a blip that split into two targets before both faded from scope; no pilot visually observed the objects, and the incident was forwarded through FBI/OSI channels for intelligence review. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 29–33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, July 2** — 12:30 p.m. On 1954-07-02 near Utica/Walesville, New York, a silvery balloon-like object was reported by residents across a wide area and a Mohawk Airlines pilot at about 20,000 ft; Air Force personnel at Rome described it as a plastic balloon roughly 40 ft long and partially deflated, while separate press accounts describe an F-94C jet on an intercept mission that crashed near Walesville after its cockpit overheated and the pilot and observer bailed out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, July 14** — 6:50 p.m. On 1954-07-14 near Mobile, Alabama, Brookley Air Force Base reported an unidentified flying object that was brilliant and silver-colored and tracked on radar over Mobile and the Alabama-Mississippi Gulf Coast. Maj. James Zicherali said it appeared like a jet-type aircraft of new design with short stubby wings. Radar operators reported that it made no sound, left no trail, and showed evidence of deliberate maneuvering. Five other residents of Alabama and Mississippi also reported seeing the object. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 29](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1954, July 14** — 10:30 p.m. At a July 15, 1954 meeting, George Adamski introduced Clarence O. Trout of East Outer Drive, Detroit, who reported having seen a flying saucer the previous evening, July 14, 1954 at 10:30 p.m. The available report is a brief FBI declassified file cover reference and contains no description of the object beyond the witness's claim. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, July 23** — 2:40 p.m. On 1954-07-23 at about 2:40 p.m., Carl R. Keyser and his wife, while at their residence two miles east of Milford, Ohio on U.S. Highway 28, reported seeing a silver circular or spherical object in the sky to the southeast about 45 degrees above the horizon. The report was relayed to Colonel J. M. Smith, Deputy Director of Civilian Defense for Greater Cincinnati and Hamilton County, and then referred to the U.S. Air Force. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 11](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, July 29** — This is not a direct UFO sighting report but an FBI interview summarizing claims made by John Hutson about Frances Swan's alleged telepathic communications with beings from 'outer space.' Hutson described messages about flying saucers, two large space ships (M-4 and L-11), and scheduled future contacts, but the account is hearsay and no physical object was directly observed in the report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 12–16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, July 30** — 10:25 p.m. A complainant reported that on 1954-07-30 between 10:25 and 10:30 PM he saw two very bright white objects in the northeast sky from his residence. They looked like two stars following one another, were not blinking, moved at high speed one behind the other toward the south (toward town), and after about a minute disappeared into a cloud; only one then appeared to emerge before also disappearing. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1954, August 8** — At sea about 80 miles east of New York, officers aboard the Dutch liner Groote Beer observed a circular gray object near sea level that brightened like the moon, shot straight upward with great speed, and disappeared into clouds at about 5,000 feet. Captain Jan P. Boshoff and several other officers watched it through binoculars and said it did not appear to be a meteor or anything supernatural. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1954, August 16** — 5:00 p.m. On 1954-08-16 in Antananarivo, Madagascar, several hundred Air France personnel and other witnesses observed a large green ball in the sky moving at high speed. It disappeared behind a hill and then reappeared as a metal rugby-ball-shaped craft with a detached green lens-shaped front section and sparks issuing from the rear. The object flew about 50-100 meters above the city, passed over observers, caused shop lights to go out, and frightened animals; a similar craft was reportedly seen minutes later about 150 km away over a farm school. The source notes an alleged scientific commission was assembled afterward, but no archive trace of the investigation was found. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 21–22](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1954, August 19** — At an August 19, 1954 meeting, Mrs. Mooney said the gathering consisted mostly of people reporting that they had seen saucers. John Hoffman, an advertising man, and Mr. Trout of Continental Motors reported sightings, and Mr. Reeves, an engineer, commented on each reported sighting. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, August 27** — 4:15 p.m. On 1954-08-27 in Woodside, California, a woman motorist reported seeing a "circle of fire" along Pertola Road, after which firemen and local officials found scores of small white-hot metal pellets burning the pavement and shoulders over an area about 70 by 250 feet. The metal burned holes in the road, started grass fires, and baffled metallurgists, Air Force officials, and local responders; a deputy sheriff said the scraps had irregular markings and appeared to have come from a cylindrical object, while an Air Force spokesman suggested jet exhaust pipe fragments, though no military jets were reported in the bay area that day. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, September 5** — 10:35 p.m. On 1954-09-05 in Rockford, Illinois, amateur astronomers Peter Bartkus and Theodore McColm were observing the Moon through a 6-inch Cassigranian reflector telescope between 10:35 and 11:15 P.M. when they saw a dull, spherical object apparently ascending from the Mare Humboldtianum area and moving across the same field as the Moon for about 40 minutes before disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 46–47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, September 8** — This report is a secondary, conflicting account about alleged 'earth satellites' rather than a direct witness sighting. It describes a Pentagon scare over two previously unobserved objects orbiting the earth, later publicly explained in Aviation Week as natural satellites identified by Dr. Lincoln La Paz, but La Paz then denied in a letter that Aviation Week's account was accurate or that he had confirmed any such identification. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, September 18** — On 1954-09-18 over Rome, Italy, multiple newspaper callers, radar operators, and Ciampino Airfield technicians reported a cigar- or cone-shaped object that seemed to descend, pause in mid-air, then shoot upward rapidly and vanish, leaving a milky white exhaust trail; the radar at Rome reportedly tracked it for 35 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, September 30** — 4:30 a.m. A report states that a saucer landed at 4:30 a.m. on September 30, 1954, at Rotunda Drive and Southfield in Detroit, with strange greenish men wearing brown uniforms associated with the event. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 70–71](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1954, October 16** — On 1954-10-16 near Po Di Gnocca in the Po River marshlands, several score of villagers reportedly watched a silver disc approach at great speed, land gently in the marsh, pause briefly, then rise vertically and depart eastward; afterward, villagers found a shallow 20-foot crater and scorched poplar trees at the apparent landing site. The report appeared amid a broader wave of Italian flying-saucer stories, while Air Ministry radar reportedly showed no registered objects. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 61](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1955** — 10:00 p.m. Sergeant Gordon Richardson of the Kennewick, Washington Police Department reported that about a year before the June 1, 1956 FBI interview he saw a large, shiny, metallic spherical object over Kennewick at about 10:00 p.m. while on night shift near 1st and Washington. He estimated it at about 30 feet in diameter, flying in from the west at low altitude, leaving a trail of fire, stopping abruptly after traveling a few blocks east of Washington Street, remaining motionless for several minutes while emitting a chugging engine sound, and then accelerating away so fast it disappeared within seconds. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 39–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1955, June 27** — 8:50 p.m. On 1955-06-27 at about 8:50 PM, Raymond Kent, standing in front of the Virginus Hotel at 804 Virginia, reported seeing an unidentified object in the sky heading south parallel to the coast at about a 45-degree elevation. He described it as having no definite form and a silver glow. He first tried to report it to Boeing Field Air Force personnel, who allegedly treated it as a joke and told him to call the FBI. ([FBI-UAP-D013, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1955, October 4** — 4:30 p.m. On 4 Oct 1955 at about 16:30 local time, a US tourist traveling by slow train from Baku to Tiflis in Azerbaijan SSR reported that, from the train window, he and three other US nationals saw a triangular object near an airfield between the train and the Caspian Sea coast. A searchlight on the field illuminated the object as it was launched from the ground, spiraled several times, then climbed very rapidly at roughly a 45-degree angle to high altitude. Witnesses described it as jet-fighter sized, squat, equilateral-triangle shaped, with three lights at the points; the observer believed this was a missile-like launch rather than an ordinary takeoff and noted a second launch in rapid succession. ([CIA-UAP-006, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-006_Sighting_of_Unconventional_Aircraft.pdf>))

**1955, October 4** — 7:10 p.m. On 4 Oct 1955 at about 19:10, three highly reliable U.S. observers traveling by rail in the Trans-Caucasus region of the USSR reported two disc-shaped unconventional aircraft taking off almost vertically one minute apart near the ATJATY-ADDILJABUL rail segment. Each object was seen from the left side of the train, rose slowly to roughly 6,000 feet, showed a slowly revolving outer surface with two stationary lights near the top, emitted sparks or flame, then accelerated sharply northward in level flight. The witnesses believed they had seen genuine flying saucers; the report was later debriefed in Prague by USAF attaché personnel and documented with notes and sketches. ([341_110677_Numerical_File, pp. 1–10](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/341_110677_numerical_file_5-2500.pdf>))

**1955, October 18** — 7:10 p.m. Senator Richard B. Russell and three accompanying party members reported a night-time sighting while traveling by train from Baku to Tiflis in Russia/Georgia region on the evening of 1955-10-18. Russell first saw a small greenish-yellow glowing ball rising rapidly; shortly afterward the others observed a second object. Debriefings recorded mixed descriptions: two eye-like lights, a shadowy body with a light at the top and rotating lights at the base, and an impression that the object may have been rocket-sized or comparable to a U.S. jet fighter. CIA and FBI-associated memoranda later concluded the evidence most likely could be explained by conventional aircraft, missile, exhaust, or a helicopter, though Colonel Hathaway's testimony remained the strongest support for an unconventional craft. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 119–122](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1955, October 27** — On 27 October 1955, Dr. Francis Clauser and an interviewer spoke with Senator Richard B. Russell about an observation during a train trip between Baku and Tiflis in Russia. Russell reported seeing a greenish-yellow glowing ball rise rapidly from the ground while he was resting in a dark compartment; after alerting others, a second similar object was seen and the party became convinced. No object shape was discerned beyond the glowing ball, and no trail was observed.
