The report also mentions a separate motor trip near Moscow where Russell saw radar and military installations, but the memorandum concludes the sightings do not support saucer-like aircraft and may have been jet exhausts in steep climb or possibly short/vertical takeoff aircraft. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 123–124](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1955, October 27** — Senator Richard B. Russell reported that while riding a train from Baka to Tiflis in darkness, he and later three other companions observed two separate greenish-yellow round lights ascending rapidly outside the window. Russell said the objects appeared as balls or round lights with no visible structure, trail, smoke, or noise, and seemed to climb steeply upward before disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 125–126](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1955, November** _(cluster ×2)_ — In November 1955, a Hungarian correspondent in Budapest reported via letter that so-called flying saucers or rockets had kept people nervous for several weeks and had kept scientific groups busy; the letter also cited an estimated speed of 12,000 km per hour and included a sketch of the reported formation and suspected course between Budapest and Moscow. ([CIA-UAP-013, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-013-Report_of_Unusual_Flying_Object_Sightings_and_Attendant_Scientific_Activity.pdf>); [CIA-UAP-018, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-018_Report_of_Unusual_Flying_Object_Sightings_and_Attendant_Scientific_Activity.pdf>))

**1955** — Around 1955, an aircraft worker named Leland Jesse Gash (also given as Jesse Leland Gash) reportedly saw an unidentified object in the sky near his home in Fort Worth, Texas and filmed it with a movie camera; the film was later shown to his nephew Donald Keith Gash in Kansas City and described as very good, about fifteen feet long. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 197](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, February 12** — This record is not a sighting report but a 1956 FBI correspondence file in which 15-year-old Richard E. Guertin of Springfield, Massachusetts asked J. Edgar Hoover whether a claimed 1953 Los Angeles 'Venusian' incident described in a flying-saucer book had ever been reported to the FBI; Hoover replied that no such matter was ever reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 127–130](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, April 6** — 5:00 a.m. On April 6, 1956, near Henderson, North Carolina, Sylvia L. Richards and her fiance Joseph L. Morris Jr. saw a very bright, oval/round low-flying object approaching their car from straight ahead at high speed before passing overhead, accelerating, and veering away out of sight; she later described it as light blue, spinning, and silent, and noted cloudy skies with rain beginning about 30 minutes later. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 131–135](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, April 25** — 6:30 a.m. Two groups of very small, silvery-white round objects were seen at Springer, New Mexico, moving well above 30,000 feet; the objects were described as very fast, traveling well above the speed of sound, and disappearing from view. The observation lasted about 20 seconds and involved four groups total. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1956, May 4** — 11:35 p.m. On May 4, 1956, Thomas Gamble (later identified as Billy Thomas Gambill) reported seeing a bright yellow light/object over Oxford, Pennsylvania, traveling toward Baltimore at about 2,000 feet altitude. He described it as visible from all directions, brighter and larger than an automobile headlight or the evening star, silent, and moving fast enough that he could not keep up with it while driving 80 mph. Two companions, Cecil Pew and Raymond Dixon, reportedly saw it as well. FBI records show the case was forwarded to Air Force OSI for information and no FBI action was contemplated. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 136–142](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, June 12** — This report is not the original sighting account but a 1956 Air Force/FBI-style correspondence noting that Sgt. Gordon Richardson of the Kennewick, Washington Police Department had previously reported an unidentified flying object on June 12, 1956 and was seeking further contact with the Air Force to provide additional theories or information about the same incident. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1956, June 12** — Sergeant Gordon Richardson reported seeing a ball-shaped object that hovered still for several minutes and then departed at extreme speed, vanishing in seconds without fire or smoke. He speculated about an advanced propulsion or anti-gravity principle and believed it was too low for radar detection and too fast for interception. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1956, July 16** — A 1956 FBI memorandum reports an Air Force inquiry about Tod Kittredge of Van Nuys, California, who allegedly said a spherical vehicle landed in his backyard, split a large tree, returned later, and was followed by the appearance of 'little green men' who invited him to ride in their ship; he supposedly traveled with them for about 20 minutes. The document notes the reporting officers had little confidence in the story and that the CBS newscast at the time also treated it skeptically. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 143–145](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, July 30** — 12:00 a.m. Seven reports of a flying saucer were received by State Police at Bridgeport Post between midnight and 7 a.m. on 1956-07-30, originating from Saginaw, Midland, and Gratiot counties west of Saginaw. The first report, from Burt Ground Observer Corps station, described a roughly 12-foot object with red and blue lights moving swiftly at an estimated altitude ranging from 1,200 to 25,000 feet; one officer said he also saw the object himself. Midland police received one call but could not spot it, and later Air Force investigating planes found nothing in the Cadillac area where similar reports had been made. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 19](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1956, August 13** — 10:55 p.m. During the night of 1956-08-13 to 1956-08-14 at the RAF/USAF Lakenheath-Bentwaters area in the United Kingdom, radar operators, control tower personnel, and a military transport pilot reported a bright light and an unidentified object moving rapidly over the bases, with later radar-confirmed maneuvers, abrupt stops, and pursuit by RAF Venom night fighters. The case was later reviewed by Condon Commission-related researchers and discussed as one of the more credible and disturbing UFO incidents, with official USAF documents describing radar and ground visual observations and concluding the object was unidentified. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 16–17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1956, September 13** — 5:21 a.m. On 1956-09-13 a Coast Guard cutter, Sebago, in the Gulf of Mexico about 200 miles south of Louisiana reported a brilliant mystery object in the southern sky. Crew said it looked like a brilliant planet or Venus, was seen visually for only a few seconds by four crew members, but was tracked on radar for 27 minutes and intermittently appeared on and off the screen. The object was estimated to be about 1,000 mph, at about 2,000 feet, came as close as two miles to the vessel, and briefly appeared stationary/hovering before resuming erratic flight. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 10–11](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1956, September 20** — This FBI/NARA-CIA document does not describe a UAP sighting event itself; it concerns administrative correspondence about the A.P.R.O. Bulletin, a publication containing articles about flying saucers. Harlan N. Wold of St. Louis reported that he and co-workers subscribed to it after seeing an advertisement, and he criticized the publication as slurring the U.S. Air Force. The Bureau noted no further action was contemplated and that the matter was being referred to the Air Force for information. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 146–149](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, September 28** — 4:30 p.m. On 1956-09-28, unidentified objects were reported near Cape Disappointment and later checked by the U.S. Coast Guard and FBI Seattle; by 1956-09-29 the duty office said the objects had been determined to be Mark Six aircraft flares, with similar reports at Willapa, Point Granville, and Neah Bay, and demolition squads had destroyed the recovered objects except those at Neah Bay. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1956, October 5** — The report is not a firsthand sighting but a 1956 letter to FBI Director J. Edgar Hoover from Esther D. Fillion of Stamford, Connecticut, enclosing a lecture titled 'Whirling Wheels' by Rev. John Miller. The enclosed text argues that biblical passages describe UFOs or 'space-ships' and discusses unidentified flying objects in a speculative religious context rather than documenting an observed incident. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 150–163](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1956, November 14** — 10:10 p.m. On 1956-11-14 at about 10:10 p.m. near Mobile, Alabama, Captain W. J. Hull, a veteran Capital Airlines pilot, and his co-pilot saw what they first took to be a brilliant meteor while flying a Viscount at about 10,000 feet above the clouds. The light abruptly halted in front of the aircraft, remained intensely blue-white and far brighter than Venus, then performed violent, sharp maneuvers including instantaneous 90-degree directional changes before zooming away at a steep angle and disappearing. Hull noted the plane was above the clouds, ruling out reflections from searchlights below. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1957** — Robert J. Sroka reported that while driving with two companions on back roads near Rabberbishopsheim, Germany, he saw a strange disc-like object high in the sky to their front. He said it rapidly approached, descended slowly, paused its horizontal motion about a mile away, continued a slow oscillating descent like a parachute, then dropped in a spiral motion. He stopped the jeep to watch, and afterward drove to the presumed landing area but found nothing before darkness ended the search. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_001, p. 147](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_1.pdf>))

**1957** — Mrs. Margaret Blunt of Richmond, Virginia reported that a year or so earlier, while on her way to work at the Richmond Quartermaster Depot, she observed a 'flying saucer' near Richmond and believed several other women employees also saw it; the FBI memo mainly documents her reluctance to respond to a civilian flying-saucer research group that had requested details about the incident. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 165–167](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1957, March 12** — On March 12, 1957, John Stuart Martin of Great Meadows, New Jersey reported observing an unidentified flying object in the Great Meadows vicinity and later filed written reports with military authorities and civilian groups; he then asked the FBI for an agent to review what he had. The FBI did not investigate the sighting itself as a UAP incident, but arranged for a Newark Office agent to casually visit Martin due to his personal standing with the Bureau. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, pp. 174–176](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1957, June 27** — 11:16 a.m. On 1957-06-27 near Richland/Pasco, Washington, two Hanford engineers traveling south from White Bluffs to the 200 Area saw a brilliant light appear about 20 degrees above the southern horizon, slightly east of south, and descend until it seemed to burn out before reaching the ground. A contemporaneous newspaper clipping and later FBI memo note speculation that it may have been a fragment of Explorer III, which was due to fall from orbit around that time. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1957** — In Kearney, Nebraska, grain buyer R. O. Schmidt reported seeing a translucent cigar-shaped craft in a field near the Platte River, said it had about six occupants, and claimed he was briefly inside the machine before it rose silently and disappeared; contemporaneous investigators quickly challenged his account and later suggested the story was weakening, noting green oil at the scene resembled commercial auto oil from Schmidt's car. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 7–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1957, July 17** — 3:30 a.m. On 1957-07-17, an RB-47 electronic reconnaissance/training flight over the south-central United States was repeatedly tracked by onboard radar receivers, an aircraft radar, and the Utah ground radar near Dallas, while multiple crew members also saw lights visually. The object was described as a luminous, sometimes bluish-white or red light that moved with the aircraft, changed position abruptly, mirrored maneuvers, and at times vanished from radar and sight; the report also mentions pulsed microwave emissions detected from the object's direction. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 17–19](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1957, August 27** — 8:30 p.m. On 1957-08-27 at about 20:30, Leonard Schey, an Air Force Reserve captain in Clifton, New Jersey, telephonically reported seeing an unidentified flying object in the sky due west of Clifton. He described it as resembling an underwater demolition bomb, with a cross-shaped light coming from within and apparent diamond-shaped holes visible through field glasses. A duty OSI agent was notified later that evening. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1957, October 10** — This FBI memorandum summarizes a spike in UFO reports after Sputnik, citing several sensational but uncorroborated incidents from October 1957. The only concrete example given is a report from Vernal, Utah, on October 10, 1957, where an object said to look like the moon and trail a blue pulsing flame was allegedly seen landing; the Air Force investigated immediately and found no object and no evidence of a fall. The memo states that other recent cases were discussed with Air Force Intelligence and were considered nonauthentic or lacking basis, with field investigations generally negative. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1957, November 3** — 1:30 a.m. In the Levelland, Texas area on the night of Nov. 2-3, 1957, multiple motorists and Sheriff Weir Clem reported a strange ECG-shaped object glowing like a blinding red sunset or neon light. Witnesses said it sat in or crossed the road, caused several car engines to die and headlights to go out, then rose vertically and disappeared in a flash of light. Authorities searched the area, found no burn marks, and Reese Air Force Base checked for a possible plane crash but reported nothing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, pp. 14–15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1957, November 5** — 4:45 p.m. On 1957-11-05 at about 16:45 local time over Glen Ridge, New Jersey, Mrs. Marino observed a low-flying unidentified object moving south to north. Her husband later relayed the report to the FBI, describing it as brilliant white, cigar-shaped, with delta wings toward the tail, and noting that no noise was heard. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 19](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1957, November 5** — On November 5, 1957, in the middle of the afternoon at Glen Ridge, New Jersey, Mrs. J.J. Henry Muller, III reported that she, her husband, and their two children were on their lawn when they saw a long, cylindrical dark-silver object with rear fins move from the southwest toward the northeast at low altitude and not very fast. The object had no wings or markings, later returned around dusk from the direction it had gone, then flew back to the southwest at a higher altitude and about the same speed, with what seemed to be motor noise on the return pass. Nearby aircraft were present during the first sighting, making the sound uncertain. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1957, November 5** — On 1957-11-05, while fishing on Alaska Way near the Union Oil Company docks in Seattle/Puget Sound, Albert Cressey reported seeing what looked like a disk in the western sky over the water. After obtaining binoculars from an unknown bystander, he said he saw one large disc-like object and six smaller objects fly away from it. No other witnesses were identified. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1957, November 5** — Associated Press report summarizing Dr. Donald H. Menzel's explanation that a rash of glowing aerial objects reported across the country and Gulf of Mexico, including one sighting by the Coast Guard Cutter Sebago in the Gulf, were likely natural mirages caused by heated air layers rather than UFOs. The Sebago reported an object like a brilliant planet moving at high speed for about three seconds and tracked by radar; Menzel suggested a false radar image from bubbles of hot air. Some reports also claimed stalled auto engines and fading radios, which Menzel dismissed as likely normal causes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 12](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1957, November 6** — On the evening of 1957-11-06 over Old Brookville, Long Island, New York, several residents and at least one member of the local police department reported two red lights hovering in the air at an even distance apart and appearing joined together; at one point the lights lit up and seemed to cast a red dust or fog into the surrounding air. The information was relayed by the village police chief, who also reported it to Mitchell AFB and Roslyn Air Field, both of which said they had received numerous reports and that aircraft were investigating. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 195](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1957, November 7** — 9:00 p.m. Two Illinois State Police officers, Calvin Showers and John Matulis, reported seeing a bright white light moving southwest near Danville, Illinois, around 9:00 PM on 1957-11-07. They pursued it by car for about twenty miles before losing sight of it; during observation it appeared bright white, amber, and orange, and briefly seemed to interfere with their FM two-way radio receiver and transmitter. They reported no impact on the car and no indication the object descended to the ground. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_008, p. 201](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_8.pdf>))

**1957, November 11** — In mid-November 1957, a Havana newspaper relayed a report from Pinar del Rio, Cuba, that Jose Maria Nieto and Carmelo Guzman, newly arrived from the Matahambre Mines area, said they saw a silent flying disc shaped like a man's hat hovering over Matahambre for several minutes before it sped away toward the sea. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1957, November 12** — 11:15 p.m. Willard Jones of Kearny, New Jersey telephonically reported seeing an unidentified flying object over New York City from 11:15 p.m. until his call at 11:25 p.m. on 1957-11-12. He described fast fluctuating red and blue lights and said the object remained stationary; he could not describe its shape and observed it with 7x50 lenses. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 18](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1957, November 22** — An Air Force official at Wright-Patterson said investigators had found no evidence that flying saucers were real after reviewing 5,700 reported sightings from 1947 to 1957, while a separate Kearney, Nebraska account involving Bethholdt Schmidt's alleged daytime encounter with a space ship and crew remained under Air Force investigation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1957, December 5** — 8:05 p.m. A July 1958 FBI cover letter from Electronics Service Unit 4 in Roseau, Minnesota, asks Mr. Domonic Gerardi of Torrington, Connecticut to describe a reported Unconventional Aerial Object he allegedly saw near Torrington on or about December 5, 1957 at about 8:05 p.m. in the company of friends. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 42–43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1957, December 5** — This FBI/Air Force correspondence concerns a request for information about an alleged unidentified flying object sighting near Torrington, Connecticut, on or about 5 December 1957. The underlying letter was reportedly from Delmaine H. Oveson, who said he was Director of Operations for Electronics Service Unit 4 in Roseau, Minnesota, asking about the reported sighting of an unconventional aerial object; however, the agencies noted they had no knowledge of the author. The documents are administrative referral letters rather than a direct eyewitness account. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 38–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1958, January 9** — This is not a sighting report but a CIA administrative memo about contacting Dr. Leon Davidson regarding a prior 'space message' and transmitter issue; the memo discusses destroyed records, prior statements to Davidson, and expectations of further correspondence, with no described UAP observation. ([CIA-UAP-004, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-004-CASE_17708_CLOSED_AND_DR_LEON_DAVIDSON.pdf>))

**1958, February 11** — 12:25 p.m. Mrs. Mary Jane Kaczynski reported that she observed an unidentified flying object from her kitchen window at her home in Union, New Jersey. She described it as bullet-shaped, silver, and without wings, traveling south at very high altitude with a vapor trail. She said it was much higher than commercial aircraft and that she heard no noise. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 20](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1958, March 17** — In March 1958, Mrs. Harold Ferguson of Elkhart, Indiana reported a series of sightings near her rural Michigan farm and later in the Elkhart area, describing silent, blinking objects that hovered over the road and garden, apparently adjusted their position in response to the family’s movements, and were associated with repeated nighttime taking of newspapers and magazines left out near the orchard. She also described a red round light in the backyard, a dog whose bark was abruptly cut off and who was found unconscious the next morning, and a later view of a dome-shaped transparent craft rising straight up from beyond the orchard; she believed occupants were collecting printed material and possibly photographing the family. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 28–37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1958, April 12** — 3:30 p.m. On 1958-04-12 at about 3:30 PM near the Perth Amboy Bridge on Route 35 in New Jersey, Mrs. Edward Barry and her husband reportedly saw an object descending from the sky that looked like a flaming rocket while they were driving north home from the shore. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1958, April 17** — 4:08 a.m. On 1958-04-17 at 4:08 A.M., David Weaver of Detroit, Michigan reported seeing a circular object with a crystal-type dome that reflected lights as it crossed the city from southwest to north, passing three blocks south of Six Mile at Lamphere Street. Weaver said he was familiar with CAP but not well versed in aircraft identification, and he telephonically reported the sighting to the FBI after unsuccessfully trying to contact Selfridge Field and the Air Force. ([65_HS1-101634279_100-DE-18221_Serial_844, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-101634279_100-de-18221_serial_844.pdf>))

**1958, August 12** — 10:00 p.m. Nine witnesses near I.M. reported a cluster of unidentified flying objects that remained clustered like airplanes for more than an hour between about 10:00 P.M. and 11:30 P.M., then broke apart and disappeared. Nearby military bases, airports, and the U.S. Weather Bureau reported no jets or weather balloons aloft at the time. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1958, October 13** — This is not a sighting report but a cover letter from the Aerial Phenomena Research Group asking for help locating witnesses to unidentified flying objects seen over the Seattle area on 1958-10-13; no direct observation details are provided in the document itself. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 62](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1958, December 1** — On 12/1/1958 in Bellingham, Washington, Mrs. Bertha Harrison appeared at the local Resident Agent office and said she had received a letter from a veterinarian in Kansas City who was interested in newspaper reports that she had spotted a second flying saucer over Bellingham. The memorandum also notes that she had previously given details of an earlier sighting to Mr. Hoover, who said he would pass the information to Air Force authorities in San Diego. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1958, December 14** — 12:43 a.m. On 1958-12-14, multiple witnesses in New Jersey reported bright fast-moving aerial objects with bluish-white flames/tails. Jersey City detectives George Langrehr and Edward Sherry, while driving near Communipaw and Pacific Ave. around 00:43, saw an object for about 30 seconds moving northwest at an estimated 300-400 mph and 1,000-3,000 feet altitude before it vanished behind buildings; another officer reportedly saw it too. Around 00:13, Herman Taubman and others in Woodbridge reported a large comet-like object over the Curtiss-Wright plant with a bluish-white flame, also visible for about 30 seconds, and Mrs. A.G. Dower separately reported a white-hot point of light with a large orange fan-shaped tail moving south-to-north over West Orange for 2-3 seconds. The FBI office later noted radio reports and Hayden Planetarium's view that the objects were extremely bright meteors and not aircraft. ([FBI-UAP-D012, pp. 22–23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1959, January 3** — 4:55 p.m. On 1959-01-03, William Knoerer of 14 Stony Brook Drive, Oldbridge, New Jersey, and his wife reported seeing an orange object about six feet long flying northeast from in front of their house around 16:55 local time. The object traveled for about five minutes, reversed course to the southwest for about five more minutes, and then disappeared. Knoerer said it was difficult to estimate speed or altitude, and at times it appeared to be two orange objects side by side with a patch of blue sky between them. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1959, February 25** — 8:45 p.m. On 1959-02-25, the pilot of an American Airlines DC-6, Capt. Peter Killian, reported that three bright whitish, shining saucer-like objects accompanied the airliner for about 45 minutes on a nonstop flight from Newark, New Jersey to Detroit, Michigan. Killian, the co-pilot, the rest of the crew, and 35 passengers reportedly saw the objects; they were first noticed around 8:45 p.m. while the aircraft was flying at about 850 feet between Philipsburg and Bradford, Pennsylvania, and were later lost in haze over Cleveland during descent. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1959, February 26** — 6:37 p.m. On 1959-02-26 at about 6:37 PM, Mrs. Robert Schrann of 65 Shetland Lane, Levittown, New Jersey phoned to report that she saw a UFO in the air outside her home. She was alone, frightened, and described the object only as a considerable-distance silver object. The FBI Newark office recorded the call and OSI was advised at 6:55 PM. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1959, April 19** — On 1959-04-19, Mrs. E. R. Withrow of Marysville, Washington reported that she and four neighbors observed an unidentified flying object over the Whidbey Island area the previous evening. She said she watched it for about 40 minutes through binoculars and later telephoned the report to authorities the next day. The memo records that the information was passed to the Air Force at Paine AFB, which said it would contact the witnesses to seek a logical explanation. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1959, April 22** — 10:15 p.m. On 1959-04-22 at about 10:15 PM, Oscar R. Graham of Fanwood, New Jersey reported by telephone that he was watching a series of lights moving out of the southern horizon. He said the lights traveled as fast as satellites launched by the United States, changed color, and split apart in the air before coming back together. He also reported calling local police, with a policeman present in his home at the time. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1959, May 3** — 9:43 p.m. On 3 May at 2143 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a bright white light moving from south to north about 10-15 degrees above the horizon; it was seen for about 10 seconds and described as very fast, estimated up to 1,000 mph, with no sound or other effects noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1959, July 6** — 10:15 p.m. On 1959-07-06 at about 10:15 pm, Thomas Scalza of Rt. #23, Pompton Plains, New Jersey telephonically reported seeing an unidentified flying object in the sky over the general area of his home. The office gathered further details about its location, distance, size, and distinguishing characteristics, then at 10:25 pm relayed the information to Harold N. Peck of 2nd Dist. OSI, U.S.A.F. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1959, July 7** — On July 7, 1959, while walking on a beach near La Push, Washington, Ruth Linhoff reported brilliant flashes apparently emanating from the sky, followed by a swishing/blast-like sound like Fourth of July sky rockets. She then saw a parachute floating in from over the ocean and believed a man on the beach in neat sun tans, carrying loops of rope, may have somehow attached the rope to the chute. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1959, October 10** — 4:00 a.m. On 1959-10-10 in Seattle, Washington, Mrs. Neil Gotschall reported seeing between 4:00 and 4:45 a.m. a very bright light toward the east from her home at 7525 28th S.W.; it appeared about dime-sized, had tremendous brilliance, moved vertically in and out of view without noticeable horizontal motion, and disappeared when two airplanes crossed the sky. Her husband, an administrator in Boeing's Bomarc Air Space section, also observed the light and had no explanation. The FBI forwarded the report to OSI and stated it was not conducting an inquiry. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1959, October 28** — 5:40 p.m. On 1959-10-28 around 5:40 PM to 6:45 PM, an office received multiple telephone reports of a very bright aerial object described as surrounded by twinkling lights and haze or cloud-like mist, with a large vapor trail that dissipated quickly. Callers said there were numerous witnesses. One caller later said the object was not the balloon released in Virginia that he had first reported, but some other object. ([FBI-UAP-D012, pp. 28–29](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1960, January 16** — 5:45 p.m. On January 16, 1960, multiple New Jersey witnesses reported a bright unidentified object in the sky around 5:45 PM. Stephen Klyce in Bloomfield heard a jet-like roar, then saw a bright stationary object with a hazy outline east toward New York City, apparently near 5,000 feet, disappearing after about five minutes. Ralph Lavella in West Paterson reported seeing a jet plane, hearing an explosion, and an object leave the plane and travel east with a bright light before seeming to drop near NYC. Albert Douglas in Springfield later reported a vapor trail in the southern sky ending in a bright object at jet speed that stopped, remained stationary for five minutes, and disappeared; an anonymous caller gave similar information earlier. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1960, February 1** — Joe Perry of Grand Blanc, Michigan, photographed a saucer-like object silhouetted against the moon using a homemade telescope-camera on the second night of the full moon in February 1960; the slide attracted FBI and Pentagon attention, but the image was later returned with the explanation that the object was caused by faulty development. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_SUB_A, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_sub_a.pdf>))

**1960, February 21** — 1:00 a.m. In Grand Blanc, Michigan, photographer Joseph Perry reported that while developing moon photographs taken through a homemade telescope around 01:00 on 1960-02-21, one image appeared to show an oval, flat-bottomed flying object with a fluorescent glow and a vapor trail between the telescope and the moon; he believed it was genuine and not a camera or development artifact. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 108–109](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, February 21** — 1:00 a.m. Joseph Perry of Grand Blanc, Michigan reported that while photographing the moon through a homemade telescope at about 1:00 a.m. on 1960-02-21, he later found one developed slide showing what appeared to be a saucer-like or flying object silhouetted against the moon. Perry, a professional photographer for about 30 years, provided two slides to the FBI, which forwarded the information to the Air Force (Selfridge AFB / OSI) rather than opening its own investigation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 116–119](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, February 27** — 6:35 p.m. On 1960-02-27 in Bloomfield, New Jersey, Mr. S. M. Kaplan of 19 Alfred Lane telephoned the FBI office at 6:35 PM to report that he and a group of neighbors had seen an UFO and had details about it. The memorandum records only the receipt of the report and a follow-up call to OSI agent La Belle, who declined the details and said he would obtain them directly from Kaplan. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 31](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1960, March 1** — Joseph F. Perry wrote to President Eisenhower on March 21, 1960 saying that earlier in March he had turned over to the FBI a colored transparency of the moon and an unidentified flying object for identification, but had not been able to learn its whereabouts or get the photograph returned. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 132](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960** — The report mentions recent sightings in Rehoboth, Massachusetts, where Police Chief Oscar Berghman said the FBI had questioned at least two witnesses. It also references a similar earlier case in Grand Blanc, Michigan, where amateur astronomer Joseph F. Perry photographed what he believed were unidentified flying objects on February 13, 1960; after contacting authorities, the photographs were routed through the FBI and then furnished to the Air Force for analysis. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 277–279](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, March 4** — A professional photographer in Flint, Michigan reported that after photographing the moon on 1960-03-04 and developing the film, he noticed what appeared to be a flying object in one of the images. He turned two slides over to the FBI and also provided information to the press; the FBI simply forwarded the material to the Air Force and stated no Bureau investigation was being conducted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 130](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, March 5** _(cluster ×2)_ — On March 5, 1960, Joseph Perry of Grand Blanc, Michigan, a restaurant proprietor and amateur moon photographer, reported that two of his photographs showed a foreign object he believed to be a flying saucer. FBI agents contacted him at his request, obtained the film, and transferred it to local Air Force OSI representatives; no further FBI investigative action followed. The FBI later clarified to NICAP and the press that it was not investigating the matter and that the photograph had not been examined by FBI laboratories. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 126–129](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, March 27** — 1:00 a.m. On or about 1960-03-27, Grand Blanc Township pizza merchant Joe Perry reported that a color photograph taken around 01:00 of the moon through his homemade telescope showed a saucer-like object silhouetted against the full moon. Perry did not see the object at the time of exposure; he noticed it only after processing the slide himself and later showed it to patrons. The slide was turned over to the Flint FBI office and reportedly forwarded to the Air Force UFO office, after which Perry said he could not learn its whereabouts and wrote to President Eisenhower seeking its return. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 123–125](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, May 20** — 3:58 p.m. The report concerns a 1960 FBI follow-up on media inquiries about Joseph Perry of Grand Blanc, Michigan, who had submitted two photographs/slides to the FBI and whose material was forwarded to the Air Force Office of Special Investigations at Selfridge AFB. The only described 'UFO' content is a claimed photograph near the moon that an Air Force letter said was actually a misdeveloped portion of the negative; the FBI reiterates that it did not investigate the case and only transmitted the photos to the proper agency. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 164–167](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1960, August 12** — 2:30 p.m. On 1960-08-12 in Spokane, Washington, Muriel Bass reported seeing an unidentified object from in front of her house in daylight while bomber and fighter aircraft were also visible. The object approached from the southeast at very high speed, looked football-shaped with a slight orange sheen and near-illuminated appearance, made no sound, left no trail or exhaust, and appeared to maneuver slightly before disappearing behind a hill. ([FBI-UAP-D013, pp. 41–55](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D013_WA-UFO-Investigation_1952-1960.pdf>))

**1960, September 5** — Alethea Charney of 464 Jefferson Ave., Elizabeth, New Jersey reported telephonically to the Newark office that on the early morning of 9/5/60 she saw a flying saucer/UFO. The FBI memo dated 10/7/60 records follow-up calls regarding whether a federal officer had contacted her; a later call the same day from Investigator Gray of the Federal Aviation Agency set an appointment, but the document provides no description of the object beyond the witness's claim. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 32](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1960, December 17** — A revolving disc-like object was reported hovering in the sky east of Kettle Point on the night before publication in the Wall Street Journal account; it remained visible for more than two hours and was described as rising, with a similar fireball report also mentioned in the article. The clipping is a press-style narrative with heavy OCR corruption and no investigative conclusion. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 14](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1960, December 18** — A Wall Street Journal article dated 1960-12-18 describes a reported 'orange fireball' or flying saucer seen by several people, with one witness saying it dropped into woods near a house; the piece also includes promotional captions for photographs attributed to Dr. Daniel W. Fry of saucer craft near Merlin, Oregon and Joshua Tree, California. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1961, February 5** — 7:00 p.m. John Murphy of Westfield, New Jersey reported that while driving home around 7:00 p.m. on 1961-02-05 he saw an extremely bright round object northwest of his residence that was much larger than a star; he said he saw the same object on Monday and Tuesday evenings, and later observed three similar objects to the northwest, west, and north, which appeared to change from round to lateral/spectrum-like shapes. He said his wife dismissed him, but his daughter, son, wife, and a neighbor later looked and said they verified what he saw; he reported the matter to local police and attempted to contact Civilian Defense before bringing it to the FBI. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1961, February 6** — 7:50 p.m. On 1961-02-06 at 7:50 pm, Mrs. Bernice Abrams of East Paterson, New Jersey, reported a very bright object in the sky west of Paterson that she said she had not seen before and did not think was a star. Her husband, Carl Abrams, independently observed the same object while driving on the Garden State Parkway from Newark, first seeing it west of him and then south of him. They contacted the FBI because they did not know whom else to call and wanted the government informed. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1961, September 13** — 8:00 p.m. On 1961-09-13 around 8:00 pm, multiple callers reported observing a very bright reddish/tangerine object in the sky slightly south of east, about midway above the horizon. The object increased in size as if from an explosion, with no apparent movement or noise, and the reports were forwarded to FBI/OSI contacts in Newark for verification. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 37](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1961, September 25** — 9:00 a.m. This FBI file is not a direct UAP sighting report but a record of civilian UFO researcher Leonard H. Stringfield and his comments about unidentified flying objects, including an anonymous phone call predicting a major wave of sightings after Russian nuclear explosions and a newspaper article expressing his belief that UFOs are from outer space. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 180–193](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1962, February 21** — Astronaut Glenn reported seeing small golden luminous particles during his flight near Cape Canaveral, apparently around sunrise. The particles moved at roughly 3-4 mph, were visible by binocular vision, seemed to surround the capsule and appear mostly aft of it, and were said to have nothing to do with the capsule's motion. Clear star images were also captured with the UV camera despite some motion blur. ([NASA-UAP-D015, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/NASA-UAP-D015_Astronaut-Scientific-Debriefings_1962-1963.pdf>))

**1962, February 21** — A NASA memorandum written after John H. Glenn Jr.'s MA-6 flight requested a more detailed scientific description of two visual observations reported in the press: thousands of luminous particles apparently travelling with Friendship-7 at sunrise, and the apparent altitude or angular view of the upper limb of the atmosphere. The memo does not record the sighting as an identified craft event, but as an in-flight visual phenomenon requiring follow-up from Glenn. ([NASA-UAP-D015, pp. 51–52](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/NASA-UAP-D015_Astronaut-Scientific-Debriefings_1962-1963.pdf>))

**1962, February 27** — During John H. Glenn Jr.'s MA-6 Mercury flight, he observed luminous yellow-green particles outside the capsule at sunrise on three separate orbits. The particles appeared static or slowly drifting in a field around the spacecraft, with a few turning white in the capsule's shadow; Glenn compared their brightness and color to fireflies and estimated they were spaced roughly 6 to 10 feet apart, with the nearest particles possibly tiny to about half an inch in apparent size. ([NASA-UAP-D015, pp. 20–33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/NASA-UAP-D015_Astronaut-Scientific-Debriefings_1962-1963.pdf>))

**1962, April 1** — NASA pilot Joseph A. Walker reported that during X-15 flights he was tasked with detecting UFOs and that in April 1962 he filmed five or six unidentified objects during his record-breaking fifty-mile-high flight; he said it was the second time he had filmed UFOs in flight. The report notes that the films had not been released to the public and that NASA had no UFO investigation program, while the Air Force had formerly investigated such reports for national security purposes. ([USG-UAP-D001, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1962, May 3** — 9:26 p.m. On 3 May at Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a light seen first at 2126 and again at 2140, moving ESE to NWN about 10°-15° above the horizon. The object was described at first as base-ball-diamond lights, then as slightly larger than firebox lights, white at 2126 and red at 2140, with no sound or other effects noted. Its motion was said to be at the same speed as an aircraft landing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1962, May 24** — 4:47 p.m. During MA-6, Lt. Cdr. Carpenter observed a luminous band around the horizon and later a separate Glenn effect of white snowflake-like particles near sunrise; the luminous band was identified instrumentally as the 5577 airglow layer at about 89 km altitude, while the drifting particles were judged to be debris/snow originating from the capsule rather than an external UAP. ([NASA-UAP-D015, pp. 57–63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/NASA-UAP-D015_Astronaut-Scientific-Debriefings_1962-1963.pdf>))

**1962, July 17** — On July 17, 1962, Major Robert White reported a grayish UFO during his X-15 high-altitude flight, estimating it was only thirty to forty feet away. His radio exclamation, "There ARE things out there! There absolutely is!", suggests a startling close aerial observation, though no additional details about shape, motion, or duration are provided. ([USG-UAP-D001, pp. 38–39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1962, September 15** — A subject employed as a guard for a detective agency reported by telephone on 1963-03-01 that he had observed flying objects/flying saucers in the air over Lodi, New Jersey, the previous night. He stated he had also seen flying saucers over Lodi on 1962-09-15, notified local police, and possessed documents and affidavits previously sent to the U.S. Air Force. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1962** — Astronaut John Glenn, during his Mercury-Atlas 6 flight, reported small luminous particles or "fireflies" streaming past Friendship 7's window. He turned the spacecraft 180 degrees on his second orbit to test whether the objects were paint or debris from the capsule, and they continued moving independently of the spacecraft. The report also mentions a luminous haze belt Glenn saw about 7 to 8 degrees above the horizon, which Soviet cosmonaut Gherman Titov had reportedly seen as well, though unexplained. ([NASA-UAP-D015, p. 201](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/NASA-UAP-D015_Astronaut-Scientific-Debriefings_1962-1963.pdf>))

**1962** — During a post-flight debriefing on Mercury-Atlas 7, astronaut Scott Carpenter Schirra described a broad, layered luminous band and patch seen through the spacecraft window while in orbit, especially around sunset and over Africa/Indian Ocean. He initially called it 'airglow' but clarified it was a bright brownish-gray smog-like belt or twilight layer with stars visible above and below it, not a self-luminous object. The discussion focused on whether the phenomenon was atmospheric/twilight, and Schirra repeatedly framed it as an observational issue through the window rather than a discrete craft. ([NASA-UAP-D015, p. 121](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/NASA-UAP-D015_Astronaut-Scientific-Debriefings_1962-1963.pdf>))

**1963, May 6** — 1:05 a.m. A single witness reported a very brief nighttime observation near Los Alamos, New Mexico, of a small round green object moving north to south about 5 degrees above the horizon, descending at an estimated 30-35 degree angle at very high speed, then disappearing west of the Jemez Mountains. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1963, May 15** — Major Gordon Cooper reported that during the final orbit of his May 15, 1963 Mercury flight, while over the tracking station at Much/Muchea near Perth, Australia, he saw a glowing greenish object ahead of his capsule rapidly approaching. The report also notes that Muchea radar reportedly picked up the object, and that press access to question Cooper about the sighting was restricted after landing. The source further recounts Cooper's earlier 1951 F-86 UFO sighting and later pro-UFO statements, but those are separate historical references rather than the space-flight event itself. ([USG-UAP-D001, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1963, July 18** — This is not a UAP sighting report but a July 18, 1963 National Aeronautics and Space Council memorandum by J. W. Hunt discussing policy implications if alien intelligence were ever discovered in space. It speculates about possible extraterrestrial life on Mars or from other star systems, references flying saucer claims only as a topic of discussion, and concludes that any response would depend on the nature of the intelligence encountered. ([59_214434_SP 16 (7.18.1963), pp. 1–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/59_214434_sp_16_[7.18.1963].pdf>))

**1963, November 22** — A 1963 letter from Barry Storm to NASA/OSTI discusses his personal history of seeing several unidentified flying objects, including one huge saucer-shaped craft that he says performed extreme high-speed maneuvers beyond known propulsion systems. The document is less a case report than a technical proposal about possible hydrogen-fusion propulsion inspired by those sightings. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 215–216](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1963, December 7** — On 1963-12-07 at Moorestown, New Jersey, Edward A. Stewart Jr. reported seeing an unusual object and behavior in the sky; he later argued it was a camouflaged missile laying air mines and linked it to the Pan American Boeing 707 crash near Elkton, Maryland on 1963-12-08. Harvard College Observatory later replied that the object was a bright meteor/fireball/bolide, with a similar report also received from Wilmington, Delaware. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 127–131](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1964, April 24** _(cluster ×3)_ — 5:50 p.m. On 1964-04-24 near Socorro, New Mexico, police officer Lonnie Zamora reported seeing a flame in the sky, then a whitish oval object in a depression about 800 feet away that resembled an overturned automobile. Two figures in white coveralls were initially seen beside it, but none were visible after he drove closer. He heard two or three loud thumps, then saw bluish and orange flames as the object rose vertically to about car height and departed at high speed in a straight line over a distant mountain. Investigators later noted four smouldering areas and four depressed ground impressions in a rectangular pattern. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 226–228](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>); [65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_438, pp. 1–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_438.pdf>))

**1964, April 24** — On April 24, 1964 in Socorro, New Mexico, a sober, dependable local police officer reported seeing an oval unidentified flying object about twenty feet long with a red insignia rise from the ground with a roar, emit bluish and orange flames, and disappear over a distant mountain. Local law enforcement searched the site afterward and observed four small irregular smouldering areas and four regular depressed depressions on the ground. The FBI note says the military was investigating and Albuquerque maintained liaison, while the FBI's role was only to ensure the Air Force was informed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, p. 232](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1964, April 24** — FBI memorandum dated 1964-05-08 summarizes the Socorro, New Mexico UFO incident of 1964-04-24, noting that Captain R. T. Holden of the Stallion Range Center and SA D. Arthur Byrnes Jr. examined the site, took measurements, interviewed Officer Lonnie Zamora, and produced statements and diagrams for authorities at the Stallion Site near Socorro. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 237–238](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1964, April 24** — FBI reporting on the Socorro, New Mexico incident dated 1964-04-24 records that Officer Lonnie Zamora described an unexplained object and associated ground markings; Kirtland AFB and FBI personnel later examined the scene, found several burned spots and indentations, noted no radio activity, and could not explain the observations. The same memo also discusses a separate reported incident at La Madera involving Orlando Gallegos, which Major Conner thought might have been a dump-fire or explosion. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 233–236](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1964, April 25** — The report references the well-known Socorro, New Mexico sighting dated April 25, 1964, noting that it was observed by several people in different towns and that the FBI was reportedly investigating it. The excerpt itself is mainly a declassified FBI cover note stating that a Socorro police officer was interviewed with a U.S. Air Force officer and that no additional FBI investigation was conducted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 250–252](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1964, April 26** — 1:00 a.m. A 1964 FBI teletype reports an incident near La Maderia, New Mexico, where Orlando Gallegos said he saw a silent, ground-level object shaped like a butane tank or telephone pole, about 12 to 14 feet high and long, emitting a blue-white flame from portholes. He watched it for about one minute until the flame went out, after which he later found a smoldering and scorched area with rectangular indentations and circular prints. A related Socorro, N.M. incident is also mentioned in the same report. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 221–225](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1964, April 26** — A witness report from La Madera, New Mexico, dated April 26, 1964, says Orlando Gallegos, age 35, told Officer Marvin Romero that he observed an unidentified object approximately 300 feet from his home. The report is cross-referenced in an FBI declassification note that appears to be discussing a different New Mexico UFO case near Socorro on April 24, 1964, where FBI Agent D. Arthur Byrnes interviewed Lonnie Zamora; the Bureau states it conducted no investigation beyond liaison with the Air Force and has no information identifiable with the correspondent. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 259–263](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1964, May 30** — 9:50 p.m. On the evening of 1964-05-30, 13-year-old Theodore John Rubitz reported seeing five unidentified flying objects while walking along 22nd Street in Bayonne, New Jersey, between Avenue A and Newark Bay. He described them as round, gray objects flying north to south in a diamond-shaped formation with a fifth object trailing behind and to the right, appearing very high, like lights, and moving faster than a plane. His mother said he was not prone to fanciful stories and believed he had seen what he described. ([FBI-UAP-D012, pp. 41–66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1965, June** — In June 1965, astronauts Ed White and James McDivitt reported seeing a weird-looking metallic object with long arms while passing over Hawaii in a Gemini spacecraft. McDivitt photographed the object with a cine-camera, but the images were never released. The report notes that many astronaut sightings were later explained as spacecraft debris or water droplets, and that NASA had no UFO investigation program. ([USG-UAP-D001, pp. 1–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1965, July 1** — 6:00 a.m. On 1965-07-01 near Valensole, Alpes-de-Haute-Provence, farmer Maurice Masse reported seeing a landed object in his lavender field at dawn, approached it, and observed two small beings before becoming temporarily paralyzed; French gendarmerie later investigated and documented ground depressions and dried lavender along the object’s apparent path. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 24](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1965, August 3** — University of Colorado UFO study co-ordinator Robert J. Low visited Santa Ana to review a controversial 1965 photograph taken by Rex Heflin just off the Santa Ana freeway on 1965-08-03. The Air Force had previously labeled the image a hoax and said it did not match Heflin's description of a round, saucer-like object; Low nevertheless described the photo as among the strongest photographic evidence of UFOs. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 85–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1965, December 5** _(cluster ×3)_ — During Gemini 7 in low Earth orbit on 1965-12-05, astronauts Frank Borman and Jim Lovell reported a 'bogey' and a debris field, describing hundreds of small particles drifting past about three to four miles away; Lovell also described a brilliant body in sunlight against a black background with trillions of particles on it, and mission control discussed whether the object was the booster or a natural sighting. ([USG-UAP-D001, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>); [NASA-UAP-D003, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_t_763_r1b_transcripts.pdf>))

**1966, January 13** — 6:05 a.m. At Holloman AFB, New Mexico, at 0605 on 13 January, three witnesses saw a star-like object about the apparent size of Venus move from east to west while erratically going up and down. It was white, then changed to green and red, showed no sound or other effects, remained visible only a short time, and disappeared with daylight. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1966, January 15** — 8:30 p.m. In mid-January 1966, residents and police around the Wanaque Reservoir in Ringwood/Wanaque, New Jersey reported a bright white oval or disk hovering, darting, and making sharp right-angle maneuvers over the reservoir and nearby roads and landmarks. The object was widely publicized, drew crowds of skywatchers and traffic congestion, and prompted a NICAP investigation; the Air Force initially suggested a helicopter with lights, though observers described repeated low dips, hovering, and apparent station-keeping over several nights. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, February 10** — A poorly preserved newspaper-style report dated Feb. 10, 1966, says two brightly lighted unidentified flying objects were seen in the area and that a plane named Burns was escorted by a mysterious UFO. The text is heavily garbled and provides almost no usable details beyond the apparent presence of lights and possible aircraft interaction. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, March 25** — This source is a newspaper-style interview with Gabriel Green, president of a flying saucer committee, discussing reported Martians, telepathic contacts, and general UFO beliefs rather than a specific observed incident. The only event-like material is a brief reference to Green's claimed sighting/contact history and a caption mentioning a film photographed in California the previous August, but no concrete sighting particulars are provided in the text excerpt. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, March 25** — The provided source is a newspaper/FBI-vault compilation page about UFO-related headlines and commentary rather than a single clearly described sighting. It mentions items such as "Airliner detours to chase U.F.O.", "40 See Flying Saucer", and references to FBI files and declassified UFO records, but gives no coherent single event with identifiable witnesses, object description, or location for this row. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, March 25** — A 13-year-old boy in Wichita reported seeing a flying object early Friday morning, claiming it had colored lights in red, orange, and green and appeared to move across the sky for about five minutes. He and his mother later examined unusual damage near the driveway and foundation, including broken concrete reinforcement and swirled sand, which was described as 'flying saucer damage.' ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, March 29** — 7:40 p.m. Multiple witnesses in the Detroit/Thorndale-Kintaro area reported a white, orange-yellow, and light-changing object or light in the evening sky, described as brightening and dimming and moving slowly or almost directly south over several minutes. The report is embedded in a 1966 press discussion of Air Force explanations for UFO cases, specifically the 'swamp gas' explanation, which the source says closed the books on the sightings as conventional misidentification. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 10](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, March 29** — Newspaper reports from Western Ontario described multiple nocturnal sightings of mysterious lights and objects over a wide district from Sariola to Kintaro northwest of Woodstock, with witnesses reporting orange, red, white, and blue lights, intermittent bursts of speed, hovering, and movement in line with a southbound track. A U.S. Air Force base in Michigan reportedly tracked an unidentified object briefly on radar, while a U.S. Army search found only conventional aircraft. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 10](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, April 7** — 10:00 a.m. A 1966 Associated Press report from North Bend, Ohio relays the Louis S. Smith family's claim that they observed a strange object on April 7 while traveling to Chicago. The object was described only in negative terms as 'not a star' and 'not an aircraft,' with no reliable details preserved about shape, size, motion, or duration due to the garbled source text. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 13](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, April 10** — The source is a garbled press clipping about a UFO report and includes unrelated text about a chase and a speaker named Green discussing flying saucers and possible extraterrestrial visitors. No coherent incident details, witness account, or object description can be reliably extracted from the provided text. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, May 4** — This report is not a discrete sighting account but a 1966 Special AFSCA Convention Message attributed to 'Master Kalen-Li Retan' of Korendor, a purported off-world source speaking through Bob Renaud. The text discusses Earth politics, Vietnam, and the Flying Saucer Movement, while also describing alleged prior contact between Renaud and a spacecraft crew, visits to bases, and piloting scout craft. No single incident, date, location, object description, or observation sequence is documented in the message itself. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, pp. 4–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, May 16** — On the night of 1966-05-16, scores of residents in the Pasadena/San Gabriel Valley area reported blinking lights moving across the sky. Witnesses described the lights as traveling fast, then slow, then stopping, and one resident said a smell of perfume was present as the lights appeared and moved. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 17](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, June 6** — The provided material is not an individual sighting report but a cover/summary page for FBI Declassified file 62-HQ-83894 concerning unidentified flying objects and flying discs documented between June 1947 and July 1968. It mentions investigative records, eyewitness testimonies, public reports, photographic evidence from sites such as Oak Ridge, TN, and technical proposals about propulsion systems, but gives no single incident details. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 14](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1966, July 11** — 10:00 p.m. At about 10:00 PM on July 11, 1966, Nancy Rauch and her friend Susan Shaw reported seeing three white objects in the sky over West Orange, New Jersey. Two airplanes approached the objects from opposite directions as if chasing them, and the objects disappeared as the planes drew near. ([FBI-UAP-D012, pp. 41–66](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1966, July 16** — The report is not a direct sighting but a 1966 letter from Steven G. Gray to J. Edgar Hoover asking whether a magazine photo was authentic: the photo allegedly showed two FBI agents leading a silver-clad man about 1.5 to 2 feet tall down an American street, first published in a Cologne, Germany newspaper. The writer suspected the image was faked and sought confirmation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 286–290](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1966, August 2** — 10:30 a.m. On Tuesday, August 2, 1966, eight-year-old Andrew Hyde reported seeing a round, silent flying object twice from his back garden at 59 Scotgate, Stamford, England, moving quickly above the clouds first to the northwest and then returning about ten minutes later to travel southeast. He described black circle-like markings around the middle, a ring around the center, small wheel-like protrusions on the bottom, and a cloud of yellow material coming from the rear. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 85](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1966, August** — The report concerns a photograph reproduced in Real Magazine (Volume 17, Number 3, August 1966) and described there as a 'Cladion occupant from a foreign plant,' with two men next to the figure identified by the magazine as FBI agents. The FBI file note says the enclosure was actually a photograph of an apparently emaciated child holding the hands of two male adults, and that due to the fictional nature of the Real magazine article no contact was made with the magazine about the alleged FBI agents. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_009, pp. 281–283](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_9.pdf>))

**1966, September 7** — 11:45 a.m. Fred Steckling reported filming an 'armada' of about 40 to 50 cigar-shaped UFOs/UAP over Germany on 1966-09-07 while riding a speeding train between Mannheim and Frankfurt. He later described seeing 6 groups of 10 to 12 white cigarette-shaped objects high in a cloudless sky, moving across the sky in formation and appearing/disappearing as he filmed them with an 8mm Revere power-zoom camera from an open window. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 84–87](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1966, October 3** — This FBI memo is not a sighting report itself but a cover note about an issue of Flying Saucers International, the official journal of the Amalgamated Flying Saucer Clubs of America, Inc. It mentions an article allegedly written by "Master Kalen-Li Retan" and received by special directional short-wave radio, but provides no firsthand UAP observation, date, location, witness account, or descriptive sighting details. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Serial_449, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_serial_449.pdf>))

**1966, October 9** — The provided text is a 1966 FBI correspondence letter from Paul L. Wright asking whether FBI agents have seen any UFOs; it does not describe a specific sighting event, only general references to reported UFO sightings and public controversy. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 22](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1966, October 16** — This source is a 1966 press-style roundup of many UFO reports and Air Force explanations, not a single case; it summarizes assorted sightings across the U.S. and abroad, along with official skepticism and the launch of the University of Colorado study under Dr. Edward Condon. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 29–30](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1966, October 19** — This FBI memorandum (dated 1966-10-19) does not describe a single firsthand sighting; instead, it summarizes press and book reports about UFOs, including claims of metallic objects, unusual lights, silent flight, hovering, and occasional burns or debris recovery. It also notes that the FBI is being informed because of public pressure for a civilian UFO investigative agency and because of the University of Colorado/Dr. Condon study. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 25–28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1966, November 29** — Police Chief Joe Thorn of Princeton, West Virginia wrote to J. Edgar Hoover on 1966-11-29 stating he had personally seen two UFOs: one in daylight under low cloud cover, and another at about 2,000 feet altitude and roughly one mile away. The letter was a public appeal for FBI attention rather than an incident report, and the FBI replied that UFOs were outside its authority and forwarded the letter to the Air Force. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 41](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1967, January 17** — 4:10 a.m. On January 18, 1967 at about 4:10 a.m., James C. Collins of Chesapeake, Virginia reported that while returning home from his television repair shop he saw a large oblong object land in the street in front of him. He alleged that he was taken into a transparent, glass-like craft occupied by several undersized humanoid beings wearing trousers and T-shirts, transported an unknown distance, and returned about an hour later. He also reported an unexplained loss of time between 8:00 p.m. and 4:00 a.m. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1967, January 21** — 7:00 a.m. On the morning of January 21, 1967, Peter M. Mackevich (age 11) reportedly saw an inverted-saucer-shaped object hovering 10 to 12 feet above the ground at the intersection of Deerfield Drive and Forest Drive near his home in Florham Park, New Jersey. The object was described as about 25 feet in diameter and topped with a turret. He reportedly photographed the object twice from his bedroom window, producing one image of a smoke cloud with the turret visible and another showing the full object. ([FBI-UAP-D012, p. 64](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D012_Newark-Field-Office_1952-1967.pdf>))

**1967, February 26** — In an EL HERALDO article dated February 26, 1967, three photographs are described as having been taken by Dr. Jose A. Lanuza from an airplane route between Mexico City and Guadalajara and above the Valley of Mexico. The object is described as a circular flying object with a transparent dome and aluminium-colored metal appearance; one caption says a photo was taken from about 100 metres away and only a few metres above the Mexico Valley surface, with power lines visible for scale. Photo experts reportedly examined the negatives and stated that no fake was involved. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1967, March 5** — 10:30 a.m. On 1967-03-05, at least 29 White Sands Missile Range personnel reported seeing one or more silvery disc-like UFOs over the White Sands/Holloman area between about 10:30 and 11:00 a.m. The objects were said to travel from near Ruidoso toward Holloman AFB, then south toward Orogrande and over the Sacramento Mountains; witnesses estimated an altitude of 20,000-25,000 feet and speed around 2,000 mph. Air Force aircraft in the area were notified but saw nothing unusual, and one source said the objects were tracked on radar. The incident was under official investigation by Maj. James Stephens at Holloman AFB, with information tightly restricted by Pentagon orders. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 87](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))
