**1967, March 20** — 1:15 a.m. On 20 Mar at 0115 in Clovis, New Mexico, a single witness reported a white spherical object with light blue coloration traveling from the WNW at above 12,000 feet. The object was visible for 1-2 seconds, made no sound, was estimated at about 2,000 mph, and then disappeared from view. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 69](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1967, March 24** — Two policemen reported seeing an oval, saucer-like object flash across the sky over Strood, Kent, with a tail of blue and silvery stars; a similar report was later received from Sevenoaks, Kent, and police at Rochester said the authorities had been informed. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 85](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1967, April 14** — 8:05 p.m. On 1967-04-14 around 8:05 p.m. in the Uplands area of Victoria, British Columbia, two 14-year-old boys on bicycles reported seeing stationary red and green lights over the Cadboro Bay Road/Uplands Golf Clubhouse area; about five to six minutes later, Ian Squire's grandmother independently reported a similar low, dark wing-like object with the same red and green lights passing over Cadboro Bay Road, with a tiny possible white light behind it and no sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 86–87](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1967, April 19** — A Soviet scientist, F. Zigel, wrote in a Soviet youth magazine that radar screens in the Soviet Union had been detecting unidentified flying objects for about 20 years and cited the 'Angel Echo' as a UFO consistently observed by scientists near Moscow. The report is primarily a discussion of radar-detected UFOs and possible explanations, not a single dated incident. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 88](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1967, August 26** — 1:45 p.m. At Davis-Monthan AFB, Arizona, on 26 Aug at 1345, a single witness reported a brownish hazy triangular object with round edges moving horizontally from SE to NW at an estimated 50,000 feet. It was said to appear about 1/4 inch across at arm's length, travel at a terrific rate of speed, and fade from view after about 60 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1967, August 29** — 10:30 a.m. On 1967-08-29 at about 10:30 a.m. on the high plateaus of Cussac in Cantal, France, two children watching cattle saw four small black humanoid beings beside an extremely bright spherical object; the beings hurried into the sphere, which then rose in a spiral at high speed with a hissing sound, while sulfur odor, animal agitation, and later physiological effects on the children were reported and investigated by GEPAN/SEPRA in a second inquiry starting in 1978. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 24–25](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1967, October 9** — An anonymous young white female appeared at the Dallas FBI Office on 1967-10-09 claiming interest in UFOs and relaying secondhand/claimed messages from non-earthly sources about multiple UFO incidents, including an alleged missile firing at a UFO over Africa, a radar detection 22,000 miles from Earth, a UFO over Antarctica, and a UFO shot down over the 'Dewline'; no firsthand sighting details of an object were provided. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, pp. 102–104](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1968, January 9** — 10:25 p.m. On 9 Jan at 2225 near Los Alamos, New Mexico, a single witness reported a greenish-white round object moving in a straight line at an estimated 25 degrees per second, about 75-80 degrees above the horizon and then behind the horizon, lasting about 3 seconds with no sound or other effects noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 63](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, January 30** — 5:54 p.m. On 30 January at 17:54 near El Paso, Texas, about 200 observers reported a green ball-shaped object traveling northwest to southeast at roughly 34° to 5° above the horizon. No sound or trail was reported, and the object was said to have broken into pieces before disappearing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, March 5** — 11:35 a.m. On 5 March at about 1135-1300, four witnesses in Vaughn, New Mexico observed a round, white object traveling straight on a 195-degree course. The object was described as about the size of a ping-pong ball at arm's length, with no sound or other effects reported, and observation ceased after 1 hour 25 minutes. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 67](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, March 25** — 9:50 p.m. CIA information report summarizing multiple bright-object sightings across Ladakh, northeast Nepal, Sikkim, and Bhutan between 19 February and 25 March 1968. Witnesses described fast-moving lights or luminous objects, often red/green/white or bluish, sometimes with smoke trails, thunder or blasting sounds, and in one case a circular path. One entry also mentions a reported crash in NW Nepal with a metallic disc-shaped object and fragments, but the document itself is confused and mixes several reports. ([CIA-UAP-016, pp. 3–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-016-Sightings_of_Unidentified_Flying_Ojbects_in_Ladakh_Nepal_Sikkim_and_Bhutan.pdf>))

**1968, April 6** — 12:05 p.m. On 6 Apr at 12:05, a single witness reported a very fast object near Los Alamos, New Mexico, seen toward the southeast at an estimated altitude of about 15,000 feet. The object was observed for roughly 3 to 5 seconds and described as between dark and light green; no sound was reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 42](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, April 30** — 10:15 p.m. At 2215 on 30 Apr, a single witness in Albuquerque, New Mexico reported a blue-green round object traveling east to west about 59 degrees above the horizon. The object was observed for about 2 seconds, appeared roughly one-tenth the size of the moon, and then went out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, May 3** — 9:26 p.m. On 3 May at Los Alamos, New Mexico, the witness reported three brief sightings of a large white object moving very fast from south to north about 10 to 15 degrees above the horizon, each pass lasting only a few seconds. The report is embedded in an FBI declassified UFO case file and appears to be a limited eyewitness account with no additional contextual details. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 44](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, July 11** — A July 11 newspaper report from the Azores, quoted in an FBI declassified file, describes a white cylindrical flying object seen over Santa Maria Island that allegedly stopped electro-magnetic watches at Santa Maria Airport as it moved slowly northwest at about 33,000 feet. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 82](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1968, July 28** — 10:59 p.m. At 2259 on 28 Jul in Killeen, Texas, a witness reported one unknown object seen moving south to north about 30 degrees above the horizon. Three round objects were noted, predominantly white, with one showing an orange glow at the tail; the objects were described as tennis-ball sized and fading out after short intervals. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, August 14** — 9:35 p.m. On 14 Aug at 21:35 near Alamogordo, New Mexico, a single witness reported a round reddish-orange object larger than Venus that appeared at 25 degrees and disappeared at 12 degrees, about 10-15 degrees off vertical, lasting about 2 seconds before vanishing. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, August 20** — 9:30 p.m. Two witnesses reported a single unidentified object near Douglas, Arizona, on 20 Aug at about 2130, traveling north to south at an estimated altitude of 8,000 to 10,000 feet. The object was described as round to oblong like an inverted saucer, about the size of a single-engine airplane, moving in a flat trajectory at roughly 3,500 to 4,500 mph before disappearing into the distance after 6 to 10 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 53](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, September 19** — 9:00 a.m. On 19 Sep at 0900 in Tucson, Arizona, an unidentified grayish-white object was reported at an apparent altitude of 4,000 ft, seen moving at about 600 mph before fading from view after roughly 2 minutes. The report gives no additional context on the witnesses or source circumstances. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, October 22** — 2:20 a.m. At 0220 on 22 Oct, a single witness in Los Alamos, New Mexico reported a small green object seen vertically at an estimated 50-100 ft. It was described as like a flare, lasted about one second, made no sound, and went out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 59](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, November 27** — 5:30 p.m. On 27 November at 1730, a single witness in Winslow, Arizona observed a bright blue-white egg-shaped object traveling east to west at roughly 30 degrees above the horizon. The object was held visually at arm's-length comparison size, remained visible for about 3 to 4 seconds, and dwindled out after apparently covering 15 to 20 degrees of horizon in 5 to 4 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1968, December 5** — 10:00 p.m. At about 2200 on 5 December in Albuquerque, New Mexico, two reliable observers reported a round green object at an apparent altitude of 10,500 feet. They noted no apparent movement, a parabolic course, and a visible trail. The object was seen for about 2 seconds before it faded out, and no sound was heard. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1969, May 16** — 12:50 a.m. A former CIA agent, Forrest E. Wood of Compton, California, telephoned the FBI at 12:50 a.m. on 1969-05-16 to report that he had recently been seeing flying objects and wanted the Director to call him about the matter. The report contains no description of the objects beyond the claim of seeing them, and the agent taking the call believed Wood was intoxicated and noted he had recently been discharged from a psychiatric ward. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 136](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1969, July 21** — Retrospective allegations claim Neil Armstrong and Edwin 'Buzz' Aldrin observed one or more UFOs/lights near or beyond a lunar crater during Apollo 11 on 1969-07-21, with supposed radio exchanges between the astronauts and Mission Control and later claims that Armstrong said they were 'warned off' by aliens. The report mixes hearsay, anonymous accounts, and later commentary; no contemporaneous, independently verified incident report is provided. ([USG-UAP-D001, pp. 1–40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1969, July 31** — During Apollo 11 en route to the Moon, the crew observed an unusual sizeable object one day out that was first seen out the window and then examined with a monocular and spacecraft optics. The object appeared variously as an L-shape, open suitcase, hollow cylinder, two connected rings, and an open-book shape as focus changed, and the astronauts briefly suspected it might be the Saturn V S-IVB stage or possibly something from the spacecraft such as a panel or Mylar from the LM. The sighting occurred in PTC, involved three astronauts, and was seen again only briefly with no firm conclusion as to size, distance, or identity. ([NASA-UAP-D004, pp. 2–5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d4-apollo-11-technical-crew-debriefing-1969.pdf>))

**1969, July 31** — Apollo 11 Lunar Module Pilot Buzz Aldrin reported a bright light seen from lunar orbit after ascent, initially tentatively attributed to a possible laser from Earth; on the return trip, the same type of light source was observed again and, when viewed through a monocular, appeared like sunlight reflected from a smooth body of water such as a lake. The crew revised the initial laser hypothesis to a more likely Earth reflection, though Aldrin still noted it was unusual to see such a bright source at that distance. ([NASA-UAP-D004, pp. 10–11](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d4-apollo-11-technical-crew-debriefing-1969.pdf>))

**1969** — During Apollo 11's translunar and transearth coast, Buzz Aldrin repeatedly observed small flashes of light inside the spacecraft cabin while the crew was trying to sleep with the lights out. The flashes appeared intermittently, sometimes as single flashes and sometimes as double flashes separated by about a foot, and Aldrin speculated they might be particles penetrating the spacecraft and causing brief emissions or impacts on interior structure. He briefly considered static electricity and noted a possible relation to the sun's direction through leaking window shades, but he was unconvinced. Armstrong said he had seen some light he attributed to sunlight leakage and later watched for it carefully. The observations were made aboard Apollo 11 during the 1969 lunar mission. ([NASA-UAP-D004, pp. 6–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d4-apollo-11-technical-crew-debriefing-1969.pdf>))

**1969, November 19** — 5:19 a.m. During Apollo 12 lunar operations, Astronaut Alan L. Bean reported seeing particles and flashes of light through the Lunar Module Alignment Optical Telescope (AOT) in the dark quadrant while on the Moon, describing them as sailing off in space and seemingly escaping from the lunar surface. ([NASA-UAP-D001, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d1-apollo-12-transcript-1969.pdf>))

**1969, December 7** — A teleconference summary dated 1969-12-07 reports an investigation of a Florida incident previously described as being reported in Bellefontaine. Witness tending and examination of burned remains led investigators to conclude the object was probably a very small pistol flare fired from the ground, likely by someone unwilling to admit firing it because of the publicity. The burned remains were sent to the materials lab for composition analysis. ([38_143685_box_Incident_Summaries_173-233, p. 94](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/38_143685_box7_incident_summaries_173-233.pdf>))

**1970, May 16** — 5:00 p.m. A single witness reported a black, round, solid, flat object seen near an aircraft hangar in Tucson, Arizona, moving horizontally from north to west at about 5,000 feet. The object was observed for 8-10 seconds and was estimated at 3-4 feet in diameter, traveling at 800-1000 mph with no sound or other effects noted. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1971, September 6** — This report is not a UAP sighting record but a summary of a Soviet scientific conference in Yerevan, Armenia, discussing speculative astrophysics papers by N. Kardashev and A. Sakharov on charged mass collapse, along with the attendee's comments that one Soviet committee dealt with 'flying saucers' in a largely non-scientific way. ([CIA-UAP-008, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-008_SPECULATIVE_PAPER_BY_N_KARDASHEV_AND_A_SAKHAROV.pdf>))

**1972** — 3:34 a.m. During Apollo 17 maneuvering near the S-IVB, astronauts Ronald Evans and Harrison Schmitt observed a field of very bright particles or fragments drifting past the spacecraft. The fragments were described as jagged, angular, flat, flakelike, and tumbling very slowly, with occasional higher-velocity pieces crossing the window. The crew speculated they might be ice chunks or paint peeling from the rocket stage, but no firm identification was made in the transcript. ([NASA-UAP-D002, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d2-apollo-17-transcript-1972.pdf>))

**1972, December 10** — 2:18 a.m. Apollo 17 mission transcript excerpt in which Commander Eugene Cernan, while in bed and sleep-hazy on the lunar mission, reported seeing peripheral horizon streaks, flashes lighting up the horizon, and a very bright flash like a headlight between his eyes; he said he had never seen it before that day and felt the lights were definitely there. ([NASA-UAP-D002, pp. 5–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d2-apollo-17-transcript-1972.pdf>))

**1972, December 14** — 2:20 a.m. Apollo 17 astronauts observed two widely separated, regularly flashing distant lights while in lunar orbit; Cernan described them as bright and dim flashes, with one near the earlier-called position and another far left of Earth in the window, and he suggested they could be SLA panels. ([NASA-UAP-D002, p. 11](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d2-apollo-17-transcript-1972.pdf>))

**1972, December 15** — 3:15 a.m. During Apollo 17 on the Moon, Lunar Module Pilot Harrison Schmitt reported seeing a bright flash on the lunar surface just north of Grimaldi crater and suggested a small impact might explain it; mission control acknowledged the report and said they would check seismometers and have Schmitt mark the location on the map. ([NASA-UAP-D002, p. 16](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d2-apollo-17-transcript-1972.pdf>))

**1972** — 6:49 p.m. During Apollo 17 on the Moon, Commander Eugene Cernan reported a bright physical object in the distance that flashed rhythmically as if rotating, not resembling nearby particles or a star. He estimated it was about 10 to 12 Earth diameters away and around 45 degrees pitched up when viewed through the center hatch window; the crew discussed using spacecraft attitude and optics to tie in the object. The observation was made in the context of multiple Apollo 17 sightings of distant flashing phenomena later tentatively attributed to spacecraft components. ([NASA-UAP-D002, pp. 7–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d2-apollo-17-transcript-1972.pdf>))

**1973, January 4** — During Apollo 17 transearth return, astronaut Harrison Schmitt reported seeing light flashes while dark-adapted and, during one interval, thought one flash was on the lunar surface; other crew remarks describe the windows as foggy and Evans describes a bright fireball-like scene through the rendezvous window after brightness decreased. ([NASA-UAP-D006, p. 2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d6-apollo-17-technical-crew-debriefing-1973.pdf>))

**1973, January 8** — During the Apollo 17 crew debriefing for science on 1973-01-08, NASA investigator Dick Henry discussed ultraviolet observations from lunar orbit/spaceflight context that produced an unexpected background signal across several high-galactic-latitude pointings. He noted that the measured spectrum resembled hot-star light even though no hot stars were in the field of view, and suggested a conservative explanation involving galactic-plane starlight reflecting off interstellar dust, while acknowledging that some spectral characteristics did not fit and that extragalactic radiation remained possible. ([NASA-UAP-D005](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d5-apollo-17-crew-debriefing-for-science-1973.pdf>))

**1973, February 22** — During Skylab missions in 1973-1974, crew members reported seeing lights flashing outside the station with definite motion relative to their own orbit. In the Skylab 4 excerpt, Commander Gerald P. Carr said they reported two or three such sightings on the dump tapes and presumed the objects were other pieces of Skylab or possibly other satellites. The broader report also notes similar observations by Skylab 2 and Skylab 3 crews, including flashing light phenomena and a bright reddish object that appeared to be a satellite in a similar orbit. ([NASA-UAP-D007, pp. 10–11](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d7-skylab-technical-crew-debriefing-1973.pdf>))

**1973, August 15** _(cluster ×2)_ — In late summer 1973 at Site 7 within the Sary Shagan weapons testing range in the USSR, a source watching television stepped outside and observed a bright green circular airborne phenomenon to the west at a high angle of sighting. Over 10 to 15 seconds the green circle widened and formed several green concentric circles before the coloring disappeared within minutes. No sound was heard, and the witness could not estimate size or altitude. ([CIA-UAP-011, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-011-The_Sary_Shagan_Weapons_Testing_Range.pdf>); [CIA-UAP-D001, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/CIA-UAP-D001_Intelligence_Information_Report_USSR_1973.pdf>))

**1973, October 4** _(cluster ×2)_ — Skylab crew members discussed several visual sightings during orbit, including light flashes, drifting debris-like lights, and two notable satellite observations. One sighting involved a bright reddish object first noticed by Jack Lousma out the wardroom window about a week to 10 days before recovery; Owen Garriott described it as brighter than Jupiter, with a reddish hue, slowly rotating with a 10-second brightness cycle, remaining within 10-20 degrees of its original position over about 10 minutes, and disappearing into darkness only 5-10 seconds after the spacecraft did, suggesting it was in a very similar orbit and roughly 30-50 nautical miles away. Another sighting was described as a sunrise/sunset-like view of thousands of star-like points drifting along the X-axis, later linked to an RCS leak in the command module. The document also notes other flash sightings during Skylab missions. ([NASA-UAP-D007, pp. 5–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/nasa-uap-d7-skylab-technical-crew-debriefing-1973.pdf>))

**1974, August 21** — 5:55 p.m. On 1974-08-21 in Milwaukee, Wisconsin, police reportedly recovered an unidentified metallic object that had fallen from the sky; the FBI was contacted by the National Military Command Center for information shortly after midnight on 8/22/74. The object was described by Milwaukee police as roughly 13 x 8 x 5 inches, jagged on one side, metallic in color and substance, and said to have an 'internal heat source.' The FBI had no information at its Intelligence Division and relayed the report back to military contacts. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_010, p. 172](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_10.pdf>))

**1976, March 3** — On 1976-03-03, a student fighter pilot flying solo at night in a T-33 on a Tours training route at 6000 m saw what first appeared to be a distant green flare, which rapidly climbed above his altitude, leveled off, then descended directly toward his aircraft. The object became a bright phosphorescent green glow that filled the cockpit windscreen before a small green sphere with a comet-like tail passed over the right wing at the last instant. Two other pilots later reported seeing the phenomenon from farther away, and the ground radar controller reported no radar contact. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 14–15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1976, September 18** — 11:00 p.m. On the night of September 18-19, 1976, multiple witnesses around Tehran, including airport controller Hossain Perouzi and Air Force General Youssefi, reported a brilliant stationary object over Shemiran. Two F-4 Phantom interceptions were launched; both crews experienced radio/intercom failures near the object, radar returns comparable to a Boeing 707, and one observed smaller luminous objects exiting the main craft, one approaching the jet and another descending to the ground and illuminating a large area before disappearing near a dried lake. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 19–20](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1976, November 18** — This report is primarily about Soviet Aeroflot aerospace medical research and pilot fatigue testing, but it includes a brief discussion of UFO phenomena in which Dr. Inal Georgiyevich Akoyev was told that 99 percent of reported occurrences were attributable to natural or man-made causes such as celestial movement, aircraft, or artificial satellites, with the remaining one percent unexplained and possibly hallucinations; no specific sighting event, date, location, or object description is provided. ([CIA-UAP-012, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-012-Combatting_Fatigue_in_Crewmembers.pdf>))

**1977, March 7** — 9:00 p.m. On 1977-03-07 around 21:00 local time, a Mirage IV returning to Luxeuil from a night mission over Dijon was observed by pilot Hervé Giraud and his navigator to be approached at the same altitude by an intensely bright glow at 3 o'clock. The glow moved on an apparent collision course, shifted behind the aircraft after a hard right bank, disappeared, then reappeared about 45 seconds later in an identical form at 3 o'clock and again mirrored evasive maneuvers before vanishing; ground radar at Contrexéville reported no contact throughout, and no sonic boom or other sound was heard in the region despite the apparent high-speed maneuvers. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 13–14](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1978, January 5** — Four policemen at different locations in St. Claire County, Illinois, reported a huge brightly lighted triangular craft flying and hovering at about 1,000 feet. One officer described extreme rapid motion that he felt could not be explained conventionally. Nearby Scott Air Force Base and the FAA reportedly had no explanation. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 91](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1979, March 10–11** — On the night of 1979-03-10 to 1979-03-11 in a small village in the Dombes region of France, four independent testimonies described a very bright bluish-purple luminous mass, slightly oval and about 15 meters long, which lit the village square like daylight, extinguished automatic street lighting, and in one account appeared to move along a road ahead of a car before vanishing after orange lights appeared on both sides; a fish farmer also reported a dull noise, a bluish glow, and subsequent death of fish in a tank beneath a 10 kV power line later found melted. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1979, December 9** — 9:15 a.m. On 1979-12-09 at about 09:15 near Dijon, former Air Force Lt. Col. Jean-Pierre Fartek and his wife observed a metallic gray and bluish saucer-like object hovering low over a field in front of a grove of trees near their home. The object was estimated at about 20 m across and 7 m thick, remained nearly stationary with slight balancing oscillations, then tilted slightly and departed horizontally at very high speed without noise, turbulence, or visible trace. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 22–23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1981, January 8** — 5:00 p.m. On 1981-01-08 around 17:00 in Trans-en-Provence, Var, France, a man building a small garden shed reportedly saw a silent ovoid metal object descend to a platform of earth below his house, rest for a few seconds, then depart rapidly. The case became notable because investigators found mechanical tracks and crown-shaped impressions, and later soil/plant analyses reportedly suggested an unidentified heavy metal object had landed there, producing persistent biological effects in the vegetation. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 25–26](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1981** — In 1981, while flying over Lake Michigan, TWA Captain Phil Schultz and his first officer observed a large round silver metal object with dark portholes spaced around its circumference descending from above into the atmosphere. Both pilots braced for a collision, but the object then made a high-speed turn and departed. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1982, October 21** — 12:35 p.m. On 1982-10-21 in Nancy, Meurthe-et-Moselle, France, a cellular biology researcher reported that a shiny oval craft came from the southeast and descended into his garden in excellent visibility and clear weather. The object hovered about 1 meter above the ground for roughly 20 minutes, then rose straight up and departed very rapidly. The witness later noted grass lifting as it left and a dried amaranth bush, with investigators considering possible strong electrical-field effects. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 26–27](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1985, January 24** — In late January 1985, the U.S. Embassy in Port Moresby relayed a Papua New Guinea intelligence inquiry about reports of high-altitude, high-speed aircraft over PNG on the night of January 24. Local residents in Wewak were reportedly frightened by the overflights, and the provincial premier held a public meeting attended by the prime minister. Reports described fast-moving unidentified aerial phenomena with lights, contrails, and noise; an Air Niugini pilot said his radar detected aircraft flying south-to-north at high altitude and high speed near Angoram, and other visual reports described one aircraft moving north-to-south around 1900 local and six to eight aircraft traveling south-to-north around 2200 local. ([State Department UAP Cable 001, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dos-uap-d1-cable-1-papua-new-guinea-january-1985.pdf>))

**1986, May 29** — 7:30 p.m. This is not a UFO sighting report but a Department of Energy letter from the Pajarito Astronomers announcing an upcoming club meeting on May 29, 1986, at Fuller Lodge in Los Alamos, New Mexico, where Dr. John Warren would speak on 'Why Should a Scientist be Concerned about UFO's?' The document notes the event was not officially hosted by Los Alamos and that the laboratory had no record of the subject matter discussed. ([DOE-UAP-D003, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOE-UAP-D003_Pajarito_Astronomers.pdf>))

**1986** — Japan Airlines 747 Captain Kenju Terauchi reported a prolonged UFO encounter over Alaska in 1986, describing two space-ship-like objects that stopped directly in front of the aircraft and emitted lights, causing the cockpit to brighten and the witnesses to feel warmth in their faces. The report was later preserved in a NASA-associated file folder and accompanied by references to an independent COMETA UFO study and a letter from Carol Rosin. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1988, September 29** — An auto mechanic driving on the Paris-Lille freeway on 1988-09-29 reported seeing an enormous red ball cross the road a few dozen meters ahead, descend below the roadway, and come to rest in a field amid reflections, dense smoke, and apparent light. Authorities sealed the freeway, took the witness and family for hospital examinations, and sent civilian/military security personnel with Geiger counters until the object was identified as a decorative sphere that had fallen from a truck bound for London. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 27–28](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1989, July 28** — 10:15 p.m. At a Russian army missile base near Kapustin Yar, Astrakhan region, multiple military witnesses from two centers reported seeing several UFOs on the night of 1989-07-28 to 1989-07-29 under good visibility. The first group, including Lieutenant Klimenko, two corporals, and two soldiers, observed up to three objects at 3-5 km range between 22:15 and 23:55; one object made silent jerky movements with abrupt stops and starts, and a fighter jet tried to intercept one UFO before it escaped at extreme speed. A second group, including Second Lieutenant Volochine and soldier Tichaev, watched a disk-shaped craft with a lit dome from about 23:30 to 01:30, sometimes hovering low and silently, sometimes moving abruptly, and at one point illuminating a missile depot with a green phosphorescent glow and a moving beam of light; Volochine also provided a sketch. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 23](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1989** — In 1989 near an Army missile base near Astrakhan, seven military members reportedly observed three bright disks performing extraordinary aerial maneuvers: the objects hovered, accelerated to high speed, then slowed back down again without making any noise. When a Soviet fighter jet approached, one disk allegedly escaped so quickly that it appeared to leave the fighter standing still. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1990, March 21** — Nighttime sightings in the Pereslavl-Zalesski region east of Moscow were summarized by Air Defense Forces commander General Igor Maltsev, who reported over one hundred visual observations collected by unit commanders and noted that combat aircraft were dispatched to intercept UFOs reportedly tracked on radar. Witnesses described a large disk-shaped object with two pulsating side lights, rotating about its axis, making S-turns in both vertical and horizontal planes, hovering, and then accelerating to speeds said to exceed modern jet fighters by two to three times. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, pp. 20–21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1990, March 21** — A 1990 Russian newspaper account describes a well-documented visual/radar case near Pereslav-Zalesski on the night of 1990-03-21, with multiple witnesses reporting a silent discoid object that hovered and then accelerated to roughly two to three times the speed of a modern fighter jet. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1994, January 27** — On 1994-01-27, a Tajik Air chief pilot, two American pilot colleagues, and American citizen Ed Rhodes reported encountering a bright, intensely luminous UAP while flying a Boeing 747SP at 41,000 feet over Kazakhstan near 45N 55E. The object approached from the east at high speed, appeared much higher than their aircraft, and was observed for roughly 40 minutes executing circles, corkscrews, and 90-degree turns under very high G-forces before departing on a horizontal high-speed course. Rhodes photographed the object and later reported contrails left at an estimated 100,000 feet. ([State Department UAP Cable 002, pp. 1–3](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dos-uap-d2-cable-2-kazakhstan-january-1994.pdf>))

**1994, January 28** — 1:14 p.m. On 1994-01-28, Air France Flight AF 3532 en route Nice-London was cruising near Coulommiers, Seine-et-Marne, when the chief steward, then the copilot, and finally the captain observed a large airborne phenomenon in excellent visibility and scattered altocumulus. The object appeared at about 10,500 m altitude and roughly 50 km away, changing from a brown bell shape to a chestnut-brown lens before vanishing almost instantaneously to the left of the aircraft. The sighting was reported to air traffic control; subsequent CODA radar review found a simultaneous 50-second track crossing the aircraft route, with no filed flight plan, and investigators ruled out a weather balloon. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 15](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1994** — NASA file 78078 summarizes an independent COMETA report and cites two airline encounters: a 1994 Air France sighting of a UAP that disappeared instantaneously on radar confirmation, and a 1995 Aerolineas Argentinas Boeing 727 encounter with a luminous object that extinguished airport lights during landing approach. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1995, July 31** — On July 31, 1995, Aerolineas Argentinas flight AR 674, a Boeing 727 approaching San Carlos de Bariloche in the central Andes, observed a strange star and then a large aircraft-like object with three lights, including a red center light, flying parallel to the plane. As the crew attempted to land, the object moved behind the aircraft, stopped, rose vertically, stopped again, moved back in front, and then disappeared toward the Andes. At the same time, the town and airport experienced repeated power failures, and a second airplane on standby, airport controllers, crew, passengers, and some residents also witnessed the phenomenon. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 21](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1996, September 10** — This document is a Department of War / Research Triangle Institute cover page for a Department of the Air Force report on modeling unlikely space-booster failures in risk calculations. It concerns historical launch failure modes and corrective actions, not a UAP sighting. ([DOW-UAP-D048](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d48-report-september-1996.pdf>))

**1997, March** — In March 1997, hundreds of people across Arizona reported large triangular objects hovering silently in the night sky; the event was later cited by Senator John McCain as never fully explained. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 89](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1997, March** — In March 1997, hundreds of people across Arizona reported seeing huge triangular objects descending silently in the night sky. The report is referenced in a NASA Brown file folder (78078) that also contains a later independent COMETA UFO study and a letter from Carol Rosin about her work with von Braun. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 91](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1997, August 10** — 9:45 a.m. While watching NASA TV’s nighttime shuttle mission coverage on Sunday, August 10, 1997 around 9:45am CDT, the witness saw a light rise vertically from the Earth’s horizon on the screen, then make an apparent 90-degree turn to the right while the shuttle camera tracked and zoomed in before NASA abruptly cut the feed to Houston. ([USG-UAP-D001, p. 33](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1997** — In 1997, a Swissair Boeing 747 over Long Island nearly collided with a glowing white cylindrical object moving rapidly toward the aircraft; pilot Philip Bober later told the FAA Civil Aviation Security Office that if the object had been any lower it may have struck the right wing. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 90](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**1998, February 22** — 9:11 a.m. The document is a congressional forwarding letter from Senator Olympia J. Snowe to NASA about a constituent from Portland, Maine who is interested in UFO sightings and abductions. The enclosed constituent letter does not describe a specific firsthand sighting by the correspondent; instead it lists multiple alleged UFO sightings/abductions from around the world and mentions claims about Roswell, Area 51, Hangar 18, MJ-12, and alleged alien contact at Holloman Air Force Base. ([USG-UAP-D001, pp. 84–86](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/USG-UAP-D001_Congress-WhiteHouse-UFO-Correspondence_1998.pdf>))

**1999, August 10** — 9:00 p.m. On 10 Aug at about 21:00 near Killeen Base, Texas, a single witness reported a bright orange reddish ball with a long fiery tail ascending at roughly a 30-degree angle above the horizon and continuing to climb until it disappeared after about 5 seconds; no other effects or explanations were recorded. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 51](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:10 p.m. An FBI-submitted redacted sensor still image from a U.S. military system shows a grainy monochrome scene with a targeting reticle and two dark objects: a structured object with an appendage near the reticle center and a smaller circular object below-right. The operator could not positively identify the UAP, and the image date is noted as incorrect because the system date/time was not set. ([FBI Photo B006, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b6.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:10 p.m. A U.S. military sensor image, later redacted and submitted by the FBI to AARO, shows a small dark circular object just below-left of a central targeting reticle against an indistinct mountainous background; the operator reportedly could not positively identify it, and no mission report accompanied the image. ([FBI Photo B009, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b9.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:11 p.m. An FBI-submitted, redacted monochrome sensor still from a U.S. military system shows a grainy image with a targeting reticle and a small dark circular object in the upper-right quadrant near the center; the operator reportedly could not positively identify the object, and the accompanying mission report was not provided. ([FBI Photo B001, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b1.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:12 p.m. An FBI-submitted redacted sensor still image from a U.S. military system shows a small dark circular object near the center-right of a grainy monochrome frame with a targeting reticle. The operator could not positively identify the object, and no accompanying mission report was provided. The source notes the image date is incorrect because the system clock was not set. ([FBI Photo B004, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b4.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:12 p.m. An FBI-submitted still sensor image from a U.S. military system was later provided to AARO as a redacted monochrome photograph with a targeting reticle. The operator could not positively identify any UAP, and the accompanying mission report was not provided; the image description notes no distinct object visible and only an indistinct background formation, possibly mountains, with an incorrect system date embedded in the image. ([FBI Photo B005, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b5.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:18 p.m. A redacted monochrome sensor image submitted by the FBI to AARO shows a small dark object centered in a targeting reticle; the operator reportedly could not positively identify it, and no mission report accompanied the image. ([FBI Photo B019, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b19.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** — 6:19 p.m. A redacted FBI-submitted sensor still image from a U.S. military system shows a grainy monochrome frame with a central targeting reticle and two small dark elongated objects near the center. The operator could not positively identify the UAP, and no mission report accompanied the image. The embedded date/time is acknowledged as incorrect because the system clock was not set. ([FBI Photo B022, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b22.pdf>))

**1999, December 31** _(cluster ×3)_ — 6:20 p.m. Redacted still sensor image from a U.S. military system submitted by the FBI to AARO shows a grainy monochrome scene with a central crosshair and two small dark circular objects near the center; the operator could not positively identify the UAP, and the image date/time was known to be incorrect because the system clock was not set. ([FBI Photo B014, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b14.pdf>); [FBI Photo B017, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b17.pdf>); [FBI Photo B015, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/fbi-photo-b15.pdf>))

**2000, January 5** — On or about 2000-01-05 in St. Clair County, Illinois, four policemen at separate locations reported a huge, brightly lighted triangular craft flying and hovering at about 1000 feet. One officer described extreme rapid motion that he felt could not be explained conventionally. Nearby Scott Air Force Base and the FAA reportedly said they had no knowledge of the event. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 89](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**2000, February 3** — This is not a sighting report but a cover page for a Vandenberg Air Force Base launch summary prepared by the 30th Space Wing Office of History, stating that it summarizes launches at the base between 1958 and 2000. ([DOW-UAP-D049](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d49-launch-summary-february-2000.pdf>))

**2000, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:00 p.m. On 6 Aug 2000, a single reliable observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a bluish-green, round object about the size of a thumbnail at arm's length, seen at an apparent altitude of 30 degrees above the horizon on a 200-degree heading. It moved in a long, slow curve toward the earth, left a trail, made no sound, and burned out after roughly one second. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 38–39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2000, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — 8:00 p.m. Single witness at White Sands, New Mexico reported a round white object about half the apparent size of a fingernail at arm's length, seen roughly 40 degrees above the horizon moving in a straight line toward the earth for about 1 second before disappearing behind a sand dune. No sound or trail was noted, and the observer was color blind. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 37–39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2000, August 6** — 8:05 p.m. At Alamogordo, New Mexico, a single unidentified white object was observed for about 2 seconds moving east to west at a very steep angle above the horizon; it appeared large as an automobile spotlight at arm's length and then disappeared behind a building. No sound or trail was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 37–38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2000, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 6 Aug 2000 in Las Cruces, New Mexico, a single witness reported observing a round object, about 6 inches in diameter, moving from east to west. It appeared at roughly 3°28" to 9°40" above the horizon, curved upward and then fell in an almost vertical direction, showing reddish-blue and green colors, before disappearing gradually after about 4 to 5 seconds. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2000, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 6 Aug 2000, a single observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a round bright white object with a slight reddish cast moving in a straight vertical flight path. It was seen for about 3 seconds at an apparent altitude of 2°20" to 7°35" above the horizon, had no train or sound, and was said to have exploded with the pieces dying out. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2000, August 6** — A single observer reported a brief UAP sighting over Las Cruces, New Mexico, on 6 Aug 2000: a round object seen for about 4–5 seconds moving E to W at an apparent altitude of 3°28" to 9°40" above the horizon, following a curved path upward before falling almost vertically. It appeared reddish blue and green, had a train or trail, made no sound, and disappeared gradually. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 39](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2000, November 25** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 25 Nov 2000, five reliable observers in Los Alamos, New Mexico, reported a yellow-ish green unidentified object moving E to W at about 15°-10° above the horizon and descending 10° below horizontal. The observation lasted about 2 seconds, with no sound, and the object disappeared behind natural terrain. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 60](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2001, January 27** — On 2001-01-27, Barnaul Airport in southern Siberia was shut down for about 90 minutes after crew on an Il-76 cargo aircraft reported a luminescent UFO hovering above the runway. A second cargo aircraft crew also refused to use the runway, and diverted to another airport; the object then departed and vanished. ([255_413270_UFO's_and_Defense_What_Should_we_Prepar, p. 93](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/255_413270_ufo's_and_defense_what_should_we_prepare_for.pdf>))

**2001, October 28** — The document is a U.S. diplomatic cable about alleged Russian air violations over Georgia on October 28-29, 2001. Georgian MFA officials, while denying Russian aircraft involvement, sarcastically suggested reports of planes in the area might as well have been 'UFOS.' The text is not a conventional witness sighting report; it is a political exchange in which the term UFO is used dismissively amid disputed bombing claims. ([State Department UAP Cable 003, pp. 1–5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/059uap00011.pdf>))

**2003, September 12** — On 2003-09-12 the Mexican Congress heard testimony on UAP as part of debate over an Aerial Space Protection Law. Witnesses/expert presenters including journalist Jaime Maussan and former U.S. Navy pilot Ryan Graves showed videos of Mexican pilots' encounters with fast-moving flying objects and also presented alleged alien corpses; Graves later criticized the display as an unsubstantiated stunt. The report is a political/hearing context rather than a direct observation account. ([State Department UAP Cable 005, pp. 5–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/059uap00013.pdf>))

**2004, November 12** — This diplomatic cable is not a UAP sighting report; it discusses the Union of UfOlogists of Turkmenabat, their NGO work, and notes that the group's president said Turkmen military and government authorities had consulted him about mysterious occurrences in Turkmen airspace, but there had been no confirmed sightings of UFOs in Turkmenistan. ([State Department UAP Cable 004, pp. 1–5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/059uap00012.pdf>))

**2005, August 6** — On 6 August 2000/2005, a single witness in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a white object moving from east to west about 2 ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**2008, May 8** — On 8 May 2008, at Camp Hood, Texas, a single witness reported a diamond-shaped object seen to the north and east at an estimated altitude of 1600 feet. It appeared reddish greenish white, had an estimated duration of about 9 minutes, moved/grew from about 2 mils width to 10 mils in 9 minutes, then dimmed and went out. The report is associated with FBI declassified materials referenced in the input, but no fuller investigative narrative is provided here. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 45](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>))

**2008, May 8** — On 8 May 2008, a single reliable observer reported a diamond-shaped UAP over Camp Hood, Texas, seen toward the north and east at an apparent altitude of 1600 feet. The object was described as reddish greenish white, with no train or trail, observed for about 9 minutes, moving at an apparent 10 miles in 9 minutes before dimming and going out. No sound was reported. ([DOW-UAP-D017, p. 34](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2008, July 2** — 2:00 p.m. In early July 2008, reporting from Zimbabwe described an unidentified disc-shaped object hovering at high altitude above Harare International Airport during the afternoon of 2 July. Witnesses reportedly saw it with possible radar and optical confirmation; the object had a hollow center, rotating lights underneath, and at one point emitted beams. After being observed from the ground, the lights changed color and the object rapidly ascended out of visual range, prompting Zimbabwe to be placed on high alert amid speculation it was either a foreign reconnaissance device or an extraterrestrial craft. ([CIA-UAP-017, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/CIA-UAP-017_Placement_on_High_Alert_Due_to_Perceived_Aggressive_Foreign_Posturing.pdf>))

**2010, June 20** _(cluster ×4)_ — A single reliable observer reported a brief daytime sighting over Los Alamos, New Mexico, of a round green object that turned orange-red and then vanished after about 3 seconds while moving west to east. No trail or sound was noted, and the event was logged in a military/UAP document collection from New Mexico. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 46](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 34–35](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2010, June 29** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 29 June 2010, a single unknown-reliability witness in Flagstaff, Arizona reported a bullet-shaped object moving from east to west at roughly 30 degrees from vertical. The object was described as yellow in front and red behind, with no train or trail, no sound, and an apparent size about half that of a small airplane. It was seen as relatively slow and disappeared over a hill. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 47](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 33–36](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2010, October 11** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 11 Oct 2010 near Roswell, New Mexico, six unknown observers reported a round, baseball-sized light that appeared light green and then turned orange, with a train or trail visible. They said it moved generally north while angling slightly east and maneuvered up and down for about 45 minutes, with no sound reported. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 57](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 48](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2015, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 6 Aug 2015, a single unknown observer in Alamogordo, New Mexico reported a round whitish-yellow object with a red trail moving southwest in a straight path at about 20 degrees downward, visible for about 1 second before disappearing behind a mountain; it was described as about twice the size of a normal falling star and extremely fast, with no sound. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 49](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 38](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2015, September 18** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 18 Sep 2015 at Sandia Base, New Mexico, a single unknown witness reported a small round dark yellow object, about the size of a baseball, seen 45 degrees above the horizon traveling from west to south. The object had no trail, made no sound, lasted only 3 to 4 seconds, and disappeared in an unknown manner. The report is included in a broader Department of War/National Security site collection concerning historical Sandia-area UAP sightings. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 54](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, pp. 41–43](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2016, November 18** — 1:10 p.m. A U.S. Navy P-8A monitoring activity in the Eastern Mediterranean observed an unidentified low-flying object east of the Mediterranean near Latakia, Syria, via EO/IR sensor at 18/1310Z on 18 Nov 2016. The object was described as possibly a missile, moving in sea-skim mode on a southeasterly outbound heading at about 500 knots; the aircraft was about 26 NM south of the detection point at 16,121 ft, and the object was lost from visual contact about two minutes later near 40 NM northwest of Latakia after passing between a Russian vessel and an unidentified vessel. ([DOW-UAP-D055, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d55-mission-report-syria-november-2016.pdf>))

**2020, May 14** — 8:40 p.m. On 2020-05-14 at 20:40 local time during an ISR tasking over the Arabian Gulf, crew observed a solid white object flying through the sensor field of view above water. The object was intermittently lost and then reacquired, appeared to make erratic movements, and was tracked at 4x zoom before being lost again due to poor track placement. The report describes one round-shaped contact with intermittent trackfile stability and no radar or electronic effects noted. ([DOW-UAP-D038, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d38-range-fouler-debrief-middle-east-may-2020.pdf>))

**2020, July 16** — 6:30 p.m. On 2020-07-16 at 18:30Z over the Arabian Gulf, a U.S. military operator using FMV observed an unidentified aerial phenomenon at about FL200 while the aircraft was heading 152 M at 98 KIAS. The report records the sighting as UAP and notes weather was not a factor. ([DOW-UAP-D065, p. 6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d65-mission-report-persian-gulf-july-2020.pdf>))

**2020, July 16** — 7:20 p.m. At 1920Z on 16 July 2020, a U.S. military aircraft (callsign 1,4a) observed an unidentified aerial phenomenon via FMV in the Persian/Arabian Gulf area near 39RUN70[1]14a while flying at FL190 and 90 KIAS; weather was not a factor. ([DOW-UAP-D065, pp. 6–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d65-mission-report-persian-gulf-july-2020.pdf>))

**2020, July 16** — 11:45 p.m. At 2345Z on 16 July 2020, a military aircraft crew using FMV observed an unidentified aerial phenomenon in the Arabian Gulf/Persian Gulf area near coordinates 39RUN6234236874 while flying at FL191 and 115 KIAS. The report records the event as a UAP observation with no further descriptive characteristics given. ([DOW-UAP-D065, pp. 7–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d65-mission-report-persian-gulf-july-2020.pdf>))

**2020, August 6** _(cluster ×2)_ — On 6 Aug 2020 in Albuquerque, New Mexico, a single observer reported a green, round-to-pear-shaped object descending vertically toward the ground at about 15° above the horizon. It appeared roughly the size of a 500-watt bulb at about 1/5 mile away, moved about 10° in 1.5 seconds at an estimated 2 miles distance, produced no sound or trail, and then dissipated after a 1 to 1.5 second observation. ([65_HS1-834228961_62-HQ-83894_Section_006, p. 50](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/65_hs1-834228961_62-hq-83894_section_6.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D017, p. 40](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/052226/release_02/documents/DOW-UAP-D017_General_Correspondence_Of_Sandia.pdf>))

**2020, August 8** — 7:26 a.m. At 0726Z on 8 Aug 2020 over the Arabian Gulf, a U.S. military observer using FMV reported one UAP transiting IVO 39RWL08. The report says dense cloud coverage intermittently affected the video collection and that there was no impact to mission. ([DOW-UAP-D060, pp. 4–5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d60-mission-report-persian-gulf-august-2020.pdf>))

**2020, August 24** — 12:04 a.m. On 2020-08-24 at about 00:04:30 local time over the North Arabian Sea, a US Navy O-3 pilot from HSM-73 reported three possible unidentified small air contacts during routine operations. The witness described the contacts as round/square with wings/airframe, at unknown distance, speed, and altitude, appearing to move westward and maintain relative course, speed, and altitude. One contact was initially tracked until lost behind a cloud; when reacquired, two additional contacts were seen east of the first. There was no ES, radar track, IFF track, or interaction. ([DOW-UAP-D056, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d56-range-fouler-debrief-arabian-sea-august-2020.pdf>))

**2020, August 27** — 3:27 p.m. During an AREC mission over the Arabian Gulf/Strait of Hormuz/Gulf of Oman, a 1.4a aircraft observed a formation of unknown flying objects traveling NE to NW along the coast for about two minutes at 1527Z on 2020-08-27. The crew tracked the formation by sensor but lost positive ID when light cloud cover prevented continuous tracking. ([DOW-UAP-D061, pp. 1–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d61-mission-report-persian-gulf-august-2020.pdf>))

**2020, August 31** — A U.S. military pilot from the 482 ATKS reported a dusk-range tracking incident over E/CM on 2020-08-31, with radar equipped and a stable trackfile noted as other/false. The witness saw an initial object fly through the screen and began tracking it; a second object of the same size and shape but much higher speed overtook it, and at one point three objects were visible on the screen moving among one another. ([DOW-UAP-D042, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d42-range-fouler-debrief-japan-2023.pdf>))

**2020, September 4** — 9:09 p.m. A U.S. military ISR crew from 172 ATKS reported tracking a round, cold object over the Gulf of Aden from 21:09Z to 21:17Z on 2020-09-04. Seen through black-hot IR as a bright white target, it was described as traveling about 168 degrees at 277 mph, with a few abrupt directional changes during the eight-minute contact. No audio, visual daylight observation, or other effects were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D057, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d57-mission-report-gulf-of-aden-september-2020.pdf>))

**2020, September 16** — On 2020-09-16 in the Strait of Hormuz, a U.S. military operator reported observing one UAP at an estimated altitude of 1,800 feet. The mission report states that weather was not a factor and provides no further description of the object's appearance, movement, or effects. ([DOW-UAP-D062, p. 9](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d62-mission-report-strait-of-hormuz-september-2020.pdf>))

**2020, October 2** — 6:29 p.m. An Air Force AREC ISR mission over the Arabian Gulf/Strait of Hormuz/Gulf of Oman reported a single UAP at 1829Z on 2020-10-02 while supporting NAVCENT. Earlier in the mission the aircraft observed identifiable traffic, including UI/U/I aircraft and a possible NASER WAP docked at Busherh IRIN boatyard. The UAP was not described in detail in the report beyond being a single observed object. ([DOW-UAP-D063, pp. 1–8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d63-mission-report-strait-of-hormuz-october-2020.pdf>))

**2020, October 15** — 2:18 p.m. On 15 Oct 2020 at 14:18:39Z, a U.S. military operator from 172 ATKS reported tracking a round, cold object in infrared over the Gulf of Aden/Arabian Sea area. The object was seen at about 19,073 HAT, moving roughly 319 degrees at about 20 mph, and made several abrupt directional changes during the one-minute observation. The sensor was black-hot and the object appeared bright white in the IR display. ([DOW-UAP-D044, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d44-range-fouler-arabian-sea-october-2020.pdf>))

**2020, October 27** — 1:12 a.m. On 2020-10-27 at 01:12 UTC? (night), a U.S. military aircrew from the 77 EFS reported a kingpin-directed identification of an unknown contact during a range fouler incident. Radar lock and target pod video were obtained, but the aircraft could not get closer than 16.9 NM. The target pod showed two IR-significant contacts; one range fouler circled the other, and both were gone within about 1/30 of a second. The observer described the pair as balloon-shaped, metallic, opaque, and reflective, with 2 red blinking strobes and reported noise jamming indicated by two chevrons. ([DOW-UAP-D058, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d58-range-fouler-debrief-na-october-2020.pdf>))

**2020, November 2** — 9:48 p.m. At 2148Z on 2020-11-02, a military observer using FMV reported an additional UAP traveling northwest in the vicinity of grid 39RWK60(1)1.4a over Iran. A prior UAP observation in the same report occurred at 2143Z. ([DOW-UAP-D064, p. 7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d64-mission-report-iran-november-2020.pdf>))

**2020** — 5:36 p.m. A heavily redacted U.S. Air Force mission report from the Arabian Gulf documents a UAP sighting during an ISR mission: at approximately 17:36Z on the observation date, four UAP were seen in the 100% field of view, including one observed at 17:36:22, two side by side at 17:36:30, and one at 17:36:49. Cloud cover obstructed follow-on tracking and prevented a clear visual, and the reporter described the objects as a line of dots followed by a trailing dot. ([DOW-UAP-D003, pp. 1–7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d3-mission-report-arabian-gulf-2020.pdf>))

**2021, November 1** — 5:00 a.m. An FBI interview in October 2024 documented multiple UAP sightings reported by a witness at a property where they have lived since 2021. The witness described an initial sighting in November 2021 around 5:00 a.m. while exercising indoors: lights appeared outside the back window, were first thought to be a person with a flashlight, then became a single light that split into two or three lights forming a triangular pattern with a central light and remained for about ten minutes before disappearing when a flash photo was taken on a smartphone. The witness also discussed a March 2022 video and said the lights are usually seen at dusk or night, can morph and move laterally, and often produce corrupted video recordings. The witness claimed hundreds of similar light sightings from the property and said others, including visitors and the witness's spouse, had also seen them. ([FBI-UAP-D005, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D005_FD-1057-04_Northeastern-US_2024.pdf>))

**2022, February early-February** — 1:30 p.m. In early February 2022, around 1:00-2:00 PM on Fort Carson, a soldier and four unit members walking toward a building near Cheyenne Mountain observed a matte white/off-white, non-metallic bean-shaped oval object hovering motionless in a saddle of the mountain. The object had intersecting ridges/lines forming an abstract polygon pattern, remained stationary for about 3-5 minutes in clear weather, and vanished when the witnesses briefly looked away to get a phone. ([FBI-UAP-D001, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/FBI-UAP-D001_FD-302_Unresolved-UAP-Report_ColoradoSprings_2022.pdf>))

**2022, February 15** — 9:35 a.m. Five U.S. Army service members at Fort Carson, Colorado reported an airborne object over and slightly behind the silhouette of Cheyenne Mountain on 15 February 2022 around 09:35 MST. They described it as a large-jet-sized, angular, non-symmetrical, potato-like object with uneven panels, stationary about 300-500 feet above the mountain for roughly 30-180 seconds, shimmering white and slightly translucent with defined edges, then suddenly disappearing. The report assesses the sighting as likely backscattering of sunlight off snow-covered terrain and low clouds, with low confidence due to uncertainty in witness viewing geometry and cloud cover. ([ICA-UAP-D001, pp. 1–4](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/ICA-UAP-D001_Analysis_Colorado-Springs-UAP-Incident.pdf>))

**2022, May 6** — 3:14 p.m. On 2022-05-06 during an ISR/XCAS mission over Iraq, a USAF aircraft (callsign 1.4a) observed multiple UAP on FMV while conducting target development; the report specifically notes one UAP at 15:14Z and another observation at 15:15Z IVO 38SMC531.4a961.4a, with the remaining objects later described as fitting the profile of possible birds. Dust hindered most FMV collection of the ground. ([DOW-UAP-D010, pp. 1–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d10-mission-report-middle-east-may-2022.pdf>))

**2022, May 20** — 8:43 p.m. A single UAP was observed by a military screener at 2043Z on 2022-05-20 in Iraq. The object was seen flying north to northeast and was followed as long as possible, but the observer could not obtain a positive identification. ([DOW-UAP-D012, pp. 5–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d12-mission-report-iraq-may-2022.pdf>))

**2022, May 30** — 1:17 a.m. A U.S. military operator in Syria reported observing one UAP at 0117Z on 2022-05-30 while at FL240 and 142 KIAS. The object was first seen near 37SBV28 1.4a19 1.4a, flown north to northeast, and followed as long as possible, but screener could not positively identify it. No signatures, effects, or additional descriptive characteristics were recorded. ([DOW-UAP-D014, p. 8](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d14-mission-report-iraq-may-2022.pdf>))

**2022, July 31** — 2:39 a.m. On 31 Jul 2022 at 0239Z, a TF CHOSIN observer at grid 37SFU27 in Syria reported a brief UAP event lasting less than a minute. The object moved from north to south while the friendly aircraft was at 19,359 ft and 116 knots; no weather factor or RF/signature effects were reported. ([DOW-UAP-D016, p. 7](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d16-mission-report-syria-july-2022.pdf>))

**2022, December 1** — 4:20 p.m. During an ISR mission near Baghdad, Iraq, a military aircraft observed one possible UAP/UAV at 1620Z on 2022-12-01 while flying west to east in the vicinity of 38S MB 42 1.4a 83 1.4a. The object was not pursued, no further events were observed, and the mission continued as tasked. ([DOW-UAP-D018, pp. 1–6](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d18-mission-report-iraq-december-2022.pdf>))

**2022** — In 2022, U.S. Central Command submitted to AARO a still image from a U.S. military sensor system showing an unidentified object over Iraq; the original reporter had added a red encircling mark before submission. The accompanying mission report (DoW-UAP-D12) described the object moving from north to northeast, and the operator said they could not positively identify it. ([DOW-UAP-PR020, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-pr20.pdf>))

**2023, February 21** — 12:25 a.m. During an F-15E DCA mission over eastern Syria near Shaddadi/ESSA, the crew reported radar jamming at FL270 and then observed three possible UAP at about FL240; the objects produced a white-something visible (WSV) and were not further tracked. Later in the mission they also reported one possible balloon near the same area at FL210, with cloudy weather throughout. ([DOW-UAP-D019, pp. 1–10](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d19-mission-report-syria-february-21-2023.pdf>))

**2023, March** — In March 2023, a civilian using a personal cellular device reported seeing a large blue, featureless triangular object at night in the Pacific Time Zone, apparently near a national security facility. The object was described as a solid, unwavering silhouette emitting whitish-blue light from multiple points along its perimeter. It hovered stationary for about three minutes, then moved higher in view while backing up in a jerking or jumping manner that the witness felt was unlike smooth jet propulsion. The observer watched it for roughly eight minutes and could not identify its control method, propulsion, or whether it had a front or rear. ([DOW-UAP-D051, pp. 4–5](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/dow-uap-d51-email-correspondence-pacific-time-zone-march-2023.pdf>))

**2023, September 1** — 7:15 a.m. In September 2023 at a restricted test area on a U.S. base, a witness driving with contractors to a special project site saw a silent cigar-shaped metallic bronze object with an intensely bright diamond-white light on its eastern end. The object was already present when first noticed, appeared to hover or move very slowly east to west, and was estimated at roughly two to three Blackhawk helicopter lengths long. A second vehicle occupant reportedly saw it too; no photos or video were taken. ([FBI September 2023 Sighting - Serial 004, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/serial-3_redacted.pdf>))

**2023, September 1** — 7:30 a.m. In September 2023, a witness interviewed by FBI agents via Facetime described seeing a metallic gray linear object with a super-bright white light on its east side while driving south near a gate during LiDAR testing with contractors. The object was estimated at about 5000 feet AGL, larger than a drone but smaller than a 737 and roughly one to two Blackhawk helicopters in length, moving east-to-west parallel to the ground for about 5-10 seconds before the light went out and it vanished; no wings, exhaust, vehicle interference, or sound were reported. ([FBI September 2023 Sighting - Serial 005, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/serial-4-redacted_redacted.pdf>))

**2023, September 1** — 9:00 a.m. In September 2023, a witness driving east to a test site for LiDAR data collection with colleagues saw a bright white light over the horizon. The light appeared stationary, then moved to the right and disappeared after about ten seconds. It remained the same size, was seen through the upper-right portion of the windshield, and was estimated to be 10 to 20 miles away. Another witness pointed it out, and later additional occupants said they saw it too. No vehicle interference was noticed. ([FBI September 2023 Sighting - Serial 003, pp. 1–2](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/serial 5 redacted_redacted.pdf>))

**2023, September 1** — An FBI composite-sketch-style page depicts a saucer-shaped object with a bright light hovering over a grassy field, alongside text describing corroborating eyewitness reports from September 2023 of an apparent ellipsoid bronze metallic object materializing out of a bright light in the sky, estimated at 130-195 feet long and disappearing instantaneously in the United States. ([FBI September 2023 Sighting - Composite Sketch, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/release_1/2024-04-30-composite-sketch.pdf>))

**2023, October mid-October** _(cluster ×2)_ — 7:00 p.m. Witness 3, a U.S. federal law enforcement special agent, reported two UAP incidents in mid-October 2023 in the western United States while on duty with another agent. In the first, near dusk under a clear sky around 1900, he saw a large orange orb moving eastward toward them, alternating between orange and clear, which then at about 45 degrees altitude mechanically expelled three smaller red orbs that traveled south in a straight line with no sound or exhaust. In the second, either that night or the next, around 2000-2030 in near full darkness while using NVGs, he observed four red lights moving south, stopping and hovering in a square pattern over an airfield, plus possibly two orange orbs near a ridgeline; meanwhile a vehicle below them flashed headlights in a slow pattern, then seemed to keep distance, float off the road into rough terrain, and disappear. When the vehicle vanished, the lights/orbs also ceased being seen. ([DOW-UAP-D081, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DoW-UAP-D081_Narrative-3_Western-US-Event.pdf>); [DOW-UAP-D079, p. 1](<https://www.war.gov/medialink/ufo/061226/release_03/documents/DoW-UAP-D079_Narrative-1_Western-US-Event.pdf>))
